# GLM review 2026-10-30 (glm-5.3): 551 findings, 236 high severity, 17 shard(s)

Revision 7e02560b8e9b. Shards: agentbox, agentbox#2, agentbox#3, dream-engine, dreamlab-ai-website, estate, knowledgegraph, nostr-rust-forum, ontomark, sidestr-rs, solid-pod-rs, visionclaw, visionclaw#2, visionclaw#3, visionflow, visiongraph, vowl-wasm. Unverified hypotheses: reproduce each with a failing test or check (build-with-quality) before acting.

## agentbox / critical

### F-01 — Every agent shares one OS user, so "private" keys, the mandate registry and identity stamps are readable or forgeable by any co-resident process
- Topics: AB-02.12, AB-02.2, AB-02.14, AB-10.14, AB-11.2, AB-11.9, AB-11.16
- Evidence: AB-02.12 (Divergence): profile isolation "isolates by directory, not by OS user — it is NOT an OS access boundary". AB-11.16 (Divergence): "a 0600 file is a same-uid boundary only. Every agentbox process runs as devuser, so co-resident code can read the agent key, the bridge key and the AoE daemon token alike"; with `role_isolation` off "PID 1 still carries every `.env` key" (AB-02.14) and the bridge key is written `0400 devuser` (AB-02.2, entrypoint-unified.sh:1111-1120). Any in-container process can open a loopback connection and forge `X-Agentbox-Pubkey` (AB-10.14 Debt, agent-event-auth.js:37-39). `isMandateActive` checks only revoked/expiry, never the Schnorr signature, and `registry.json` is a same-uid-writable file (AB-11.9).
- Failure: one compromised or buggy agent reads every other agent's `did:nostr` signing key and the bridge key, forges `source_urn` on agent-events via the loopback path-2 stamp, and edits/injects mandates into the local registry — cross-tenant impersonation and forged governance identity with no log trail.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes, in scattered pieces (Divergence AB-02.12/AB-11.16, Debt AB-10.14); the composite blast radius is not marked anywhere

### F-02 — Shipped default widens the host Docker socket to world on every boot: devuser code is host root
- Topics: AB-02.24, AB-06.13
- Evidence: `role_isolation = false` is the shipped default (agentbox.toml:2503, setup default agentbox.default.toml:1771); with the flag off the entrypoint runs "chmod o+rw on the socket every boot" (entrypoint-unified.sh:598-601) and chowns `/run/secrets` back to devuser (AB-02.24 O1/O2). AB-06.13 Open R2: the socket is the host inode, "earlier boots left it o+rw, and only a host-side chmod 0660 narrows it". The custody design names this as bypass 1: "while devuser can drive it every in-container boundary is void".
- Failure: any code executing as devuser — every agent session, npm postinstall, MCP server — runs `docker run -v /:/host` and becomes root on the host, reading the Docker daemon's secrets and every mounted host path.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open R2, AB-06.13; the flag-off branch in AB-02.24)

### F-03 — The browser sidecar's key split is not realized: browser automation still authenticates as the house key
- Topics: AB-06.12
- Evidence: `k_browser`'s pubkey is still null in the split register (config/custody/g5-key-split.json:16-20) and "the nip98-proxy roster still admits the house key for it" (agentbox.toml:2093); "Until the owner mints it and the dual-admit step lands, the sidecar's flows still authenticate as the house key" (AB-06.12, Open). The Podkey vault holding the intended K_browser lives on the named volume `browsercontainer-profile`.
- Failure: the browsercontainer is the most exposed, internet-facing component (Chromium, CDP published on 9222→9223, sanctioned LAN); a renderer/extension escape authenticates to the LAN door and every in-estate service as the operator's house identity — exactly the containment the G-5 split was designed to provide.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, AB-06.12)

### F-04 — The host `~/.claude` credential bind is writable both ways, so in-container code can mutate the operator's host credentials
- Topics: AB-06.11, AB-05.15, AB-07.11
- Evidence: "the credential bind stays writable both ways, so a compromised in-container tool could still modify the host's .claude; only the configuration-read path was closed" (AB-06.11, Open; docker-compose.override.yml:84-85). `claude-cred-sync` then converges the merged document back into the container every 2 s (cred_sync.rs:104-109).
- Failure: a malicious package or agent rewrites `/var/lib/agentbox/host-claude/.credentials.json` — swapping the host operator's OAuth token for an attacker-controlled one, or locking the operator out — and the sync daemon propagates the poisoned credential into the container volume as well.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, AB-06.11)

### F-05 — Break-glass bearer: one shared LAN secret that bypasses NIP-98 entirely, with bounds default-off and only in-process auditing
- Topics: AB-10.2, AB-10.3, AB-10.9, AB-11.16
- Evidence: `BREAK_GLASS = roleSecret('NIP98_PROXY_ALLOW_BEARER')` (proxy.mjs:53); a matching bearer is accepted "ok true … mode break-glass" (proxy.mjs:567) — bearer IS the operator credential on /mgmt routes (lib/authz.js:188). On WebSocket upgrades the token also arrives as `?access_token=`/`?bearer=` in the URL (proxy.mjs:960-965; AB-10.9 Divergence — URL-carried credentials leak into logs/proxies). ADR-2027's expiry and scope bounds are "both bounds default-OFF and opt-in" (AB-11.16); the audit is "an in-process receipt, not a durable audit store" (SECURITY-profiles custody row, AB-10.3).
- Failure: the one secret, held by whoever needs break-glass, is operator-class on the whole LAN door, survives forever by default, appears in access logs on WS handshakes, and its use leaves no tamper-evident record if the process is restarted.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence "known divergences" in INGRESS-identity, ADR-2027 partial; SECURITY-profiles custody row unconfirmed)

### F-06 — Rebuild-class gate drift is invisible: `/v1/system` reports the manifest, `/ready` checks almost nothing, so the box reports green over capabilities the image does not contain
- Topics: AB-01.2, AB-02.2, AB-02.22, AB-05.1, AB-05.3, AB-05.4
- Evidence: `buildSystemView`/`stateOf` introspect "the parsed agentbox.toml at request time, never hard-coded" (system-manifest.js:6-13) while the package set and supervisor blocks are baked at image build (AB-01.1, AB-01.2: "DOES NOT reconcile on restart, needs full Nix re-evaluation"). Only two projections gate `bootstrap.done` (mcp-hub, system-one; seal-bootstrap.sh:141) and "nothing forces a new boot-written file into it" (AB-02.22 Open). `/ready` checks sentinel + adapters + a few paths only (server.js:493-572). The vault CLI liveness probe is explicitly "fail-LOUD, never fail-fatal" (AB-02.2, entrypoint-unified.sh:1183-1195) — boot continues with "vault MISSING — [vault].cli on, no binary baked" and `[vault].cli = false` "leaves agents with no programmatic door" (AB-05.4). Only vault-root drift is detected anywhere (system-manifest.js:535).
- Failure: an operator edits `agentbox.toml` (enables voicelink, jupyter, sidechain, disables vault.cli) and restarts; the image still has the old composition, but `/v1/system` shows the new states "on", `/ready` returns 200, and the runbook-driven features silently don't exist — or the corpus door is gone — while every dashboard is green.
- Confidence: medium — the manifest/image split and the fail-loud-not-fatal probes are stated; whether `/etc/agentbox.toml` is bind-mounted or baked is inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no for the manifest-vs-image state gap (the narrow projection-table gap is separately marked Open in AB-02.22)

### F-07 — Backups cover one volume and a PG dump; the Solid-pod corpus, claude-home and key-holding volumes appear unprotected, and every backup ships a possibly-torn live PG datadir tar that restore will happily apply
- Topics: AB-06.3, AB-06.12, AB-07.8
- Evidence: `cmd_backup` includes `agentbox-ruvector-data` always, `pg_dump -Fc` when PG is reachable, and a manifest with exactly "ruvector_data true and ruvector_pg_dump boolean" (agentbox.sh:306-401); `cmd_restore` is shown restoring only the ruvector-data volume (agentbox.sh:498-502), with no PG-dump restore step drawn. Compose declares `agentbox-solid-data`, `agentbox-claude-home` (AB-06.8) and `browsercontainer-profile` — "holds Podkey's encrypted vault - never down -v" (AB-06.12) — none of which appear in the backup flow. The material itself notes "a raw volume tar of a LIVE PG datadir is not crash-consistent" (agentbox.sh:313-315) yet that tar is included in every backup and is what restore applies.
- Failure: disk-loss incident; the operator restores from the nightly backup and gets a corrupt PG datadir, no Solid-pod corpus, no agent home state, and no browser vault — the Podkey key material and months of pod/memory data are unrecoverable.
- Confidence: medium (inferred from the fully enumerated include list and restore sequence; absence of a step in a detailed diagram is strong but not conclusive)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — Payment governance is declared but never wired: the zero-tolerance action class is passed by no route, and the spend-policy middleware guards exactly one endpoint
- Topics: AB-03.10, AB-03.16, AB-05.13
- Evidence: "`payment_settlement` is declared `zero-tolerance` with its own task properties (agentbox.toml:1402, :1426) but no route passes that action class to the authority gate; `mandate_revoke` is the only class any route names" (routes/llm-marketplace.js:473) — marked Debt in AB-05.13. Separately, AB-03.16 states spend-policy.js and consumer-payer.js have "no require() from any routes/*.js or server.js" and then that "routes/chain-payings.js … POST /v1/chain/pay is the only route carrying them; no other routes/*.js or server.js dispatch uses them" — the material contradicts itself, but both readings agree every other money surface (`/v1/pay/buy`, `/v1/pay/deposit`, `/v1/pay/withdraw`, payments.js:286-525) lacks the middleware; only `/v1/comfyui/workflow` carries `paymentGate` (comfyui.js:74).
- Failure: a runaway or subverted agent drains the balance through `/v1/pay/buy` and `/v1/pay/withdraw` with no budget cap, no consumer-payer attribution, and no authority escalation ever firing for settlement-class actions.
- Confidence: high for the payment_settlement gap; medium for the middleware gap (the two notes conflict)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes for payment_settlement (Debt, AB-05.13); no for the middleware-coverage gap

### F-09 — `role_isolation` is illusory in every failure mode: wrong apply class, silent fail-open, and services that cannot sign under it
- Topics: AB-02.24, AB-05.16
- Evidence: the catalogue says `rebuild` (system-manifest.js:97-99) while BASELINE-container.md:77 and the manifest comment say boot-class (agentbox.toml:2496); ER-9 closed it as rebuild because "a boot-time flip alone leaves the box reporting degraded:devuser-sudo" — devuser keeps passwordless sudo unless the image was built with the flag. Independently, if the roles config or plan is unreadable, Stage A logs ROLE-ISOLATION-UNAVAILABLE and "boot continues as role_isolation = 0" (entrypoint-unified.sh:365-366) — a security flag that fails open. And "until the W3b consumer cutover lands, JunkieJarvis, the mirror hook and dream-engine cannot sign under the flag" (agentbox.toml:2496-2502, Drift AB-05.16).
- Failure: an operator flips the flag after an incident and restarts, believes isolation is active (env capture, root-owned /run/secrets), but devuser still has passwordless sudo — the isolation is bypassable by the exact threat it exists for; or a corrupt role-accounts mount silently boots the box un-isolated with only a log line.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes for the class contradiction and signing breakage (Drift/Tension, AB-05.16); no for the fail-open UNAV branch

### F-10 — Privacy filter fails open for any non-strict policy: untraversable or identifier-flagged payloads ship unredacted
- Topics: AB-04.4, AB-04.5
- Evidence: on traversal truncation (cycle/depth) or OPF-flagged identifier PII the filter calls `fail()`, and "fail() branches on policy — strict throws AdapterWriteRejected (privacy-filter.js:669-675), otherwise fail-open counter and caller continues (privacy-filter.js:677-682)" (AB-04.5). No default policy value is stated anywhere in the material.
- Failure: an agent stores a cyclic or deeply nested object (or one the OPF classifies as identifier-PII) into memory/pods/events; under a non-strict policy the write proceeds raw with only a Prometheus counter, and the unredacted PII lands in the Postgres memory store, the Solid pod, and the append-only events JSONL where it cannot be edited out.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — the fail-open branch is explicit, the shipped default policy is not shown)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — The decision-record registry is systematically desynchronized from the shipped manifest, so ADR status cannot be trusted to tell you what runs
- Topics: AB-01.1, AB-05.13, AB-05.17, AB-06.4, AB-06.14, AB-08.15
- Evidence: ADR-2151 voicelink is `proposed/none/inactive` (ADR-2151:5-7) while `[voicelink] enabled = true` bakes voice-paned (flake.nix:1847-1849) and registers hooks every boot (entrypoint-unified.sh:2168) — marked Tension in AB-01.1/AB-05.17/AB-08.16. ADR-2121 factrail is `staged`, "no image with factrail has yet been built and booted" (ADR-2121:152-154) while `[features.jev_compaction] enabled = true` (agentbox.toml:742). ADR-2155 is `staged` while all nineteen consumers are true (agentbox.toml:899-920). ADR-2149 Isaac Sim is `proposed/staged` while the CLI dispatches unconditionally (agentbox.sh:2048). ADR-2152 is `superseded` yet still `partial/staged` and its endpoint is deleted (AB-06.4). The activation note says the live backend "remains SubQ" while the ADR-2139 amendment records the switch back to `typesafe` (system-one-activation.md:3-4 vs ADR-2139:256-270) — so responders following the reference doc debug the wrong backend.
- Failure: during an incident or audit, staff scope blast radius by `decision_status`, conclude a feature "isn't implemented", and miss that its hooks, binaries and supervisor programs are live; the whole governance mechanism the ADRs exist to provide is unreliable.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes, per instance (Tension/Open/Drift); the systemic pattern is unmarked

### F-12 — Single point of failure: one hub outage takes eight MCP servers dark, parks FATAL after two retries, and does not fail readiness
- Topics: AB-02.17, AB-09.8, AB-02.22
- Evidence: "eight servers are hub-routed (agentbox.toml:1597-1600) and all refuse connections while this program is down, so supervisorctl status agentbox-mcp-hub is the first check" (AB-09.8, Debt); `startsecs=130` exceeds the 120 s wait and `startretries=2` (flake.nix:3301-3302) with `autorestart=unexpected`, so a missed projection parks the hub FATAL until a human intervenes. The hub is not in the REQUIRED_FOR_READINESS set shown (AB-02.4 lists management-api, solid-pod, seal), so the sentinel and `/ready` stay green while the tool surfaces are dead — that readiness blind spot is not marked.
- Failure: one late or failed `mcp-hub-project` write during boot leaves eight tool surfaces (browser, governance, memory-adjacent tools) permanently unavailable on an otherwise "healthy" box; agents fail or hallucinate workarounds for hours before anyone checks the one program.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes for the SPOF (Debt, AB-09.8); no for the readiness blind spot

### F-13 — The withdrawn EXP-B8 tick still signs as JunkieJarvis and publishes, ignoring the manifest gate
- Topics: AB-05.13
- Evidence: EXP-B8 stopped 2026-10-04 (WITHDRAW, `b1e42f148`; `label_log = false` since, agentbox.toml:1320), but "the experiment tick itself signs its one forum summary with the JunkieJarvis key (exp-b8-label-log.cjs:528-530 … and publishes it (:550-563) without consulting `junkiejarvisEnabled`, so a manifest with `junkiejarvis = false` would still see a JunkieJarvis post if the withdrawn tick fires again" — marked Tension in AB-05.13.
- Failure: a stray cron entry, manual invocation or resurrected schedule posts to the public forum under the operator's forum identity while the manifest and `/v1/system` both report JunkieJarvis off — unattributable, unkillable-by-config outbound signed comms.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, AB-05.13)

### F-14 — Governance evidence has two silent holes: the hash chain cannot see tail deletion, and authority denies leave no record without a journal
- Topics: AB-03.12, AB-11.10
- Evidence: "INVARIANT deletion at the tail is the one tamper mode a bare hash chain cannot see (lib/audit-chain.js:14-16)" (AB-03.12); "Without a journal the gate denies identically and leaves no durable record, and a journal failure never converts a deny into an exception" (authority.js:303, AB-11.10), and "Local receipt is unsigned and does not prove deployment" (AB-11.10).
- Failure: after a dispute or compromise (which, per F-01, includes any devuser process), an attacker truncates the newest events JSONL — `verifyFiles` passes; separately, a deny-storm during an incident produces zero durable evidence, so you cannot reconstruct whether the gate blocked the destructive action or the caller never asked.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — tail deletion is acknowledged in an INVARIANT note (not tracked as Debt/Open); the journal-less deny path is unmarked

### F-15 — `agentbox.sh health` fails permanently on any deployment without the speech sidecars, because it probes ports 8897/8898 unconditionally
- Topics: AB-04.16, AB-06.10
- Evidence: "the exit condition now carries a third term, speech_failed (agentbox.sh:1202,1216): nemotron-asr and pocket-tts are checked unconditionally by port, independent of the [adapters] manifest and never surfaced in /health's own adapters map" (AB-04.16). The speech pair lives in its own overlay, `docker-compose.speech.yml` (AB-06.10), i.e. a separately lifecycled compose project.
- Failure: on any agentbox deployment that doesn't run the speech overlay, `./agentbox.sh health` exits 1 forever with a healthy box — monitoring pages continuously, automation gates refuse to pass, and the team learns to ignore the command, masking the real adapter failures it was fixed to catch.
- Confidence: high that the check is unconditional (explicitly stated); medium that speech-less deployments exist
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

## Not judgeable from this material

1. The shipped privacy-filter policy per slot (strict vs fail-open) and `integrations` defaults — needed to confirm or clear F-10: the resolved `[adapters]`/privacy manifest plus `_slotPolicy` resolution.
2. The full `cmd_backup`/`cmd_restore` source and a restore drill log — to confirm F-07: whether `agentbox-solid-data`, `agentbox-claude-home`, `browsercontainer-profile` and the PG dump are included anywhere, and whether a torn datadir tar is ever restored.
3. Whether `/etc/agentbox.toml` is baked into the image or bind-mounted from the host — this decides the size of the F-06 drift window; the compose volume/env mounts and the flake config-copy step.
4. The preHandler/guard inventory of `routes/payments.js` and `routes/chain-payments.js` — to settle F-08's contradictory notes on which money routes carry `spendPolicy`/`consumerPayer`/`paymentGate`.
5. Host-side state and operations evidence: the actual Docker socket mode on the host, whether the production deployment runs `role_isolation`, the W2-W4 custody rehearsal results, and the nip98-proxy session-secret pinning/multi-instance decision (SECURITY-profiles rows marked "unconfirmed").

## agentbox / premortem

# Post-mortem — Agentbox estate, incident of [month] (client-attributed custody/security failure)

Written twelve months after the stamp at `verified_commit 0b0f7918961e629280faf86f88227535abf514b5`. All findings are anchored to the citations the map itself carries; anything reasoned beyond them is marked "inferred".

---

### R-1 — Agent code execution escalates to host root: passwordless devuser sudo plus the world-widened Docker socket, because `role_isolation` ships off
- Topics: AB-02.24, AB-05.16, AB-06.13, AB-11.16
- Chain of events:
  1. An agent session (harness wrapper, `agentbox-sessions` pane, or `/spawn`) executes shell as `devuser` — the single OS user every process runs as (AB-02.12, AB-11.16).
  2. `devuser` is in `wheel` with passwordless sudo in the shipped image: `bake-devuser-privilege.sh` takes the build-time flag and "leaves devuser in wheel with passwordless sudo otherwise" (`flake.nix:3664`, `:4081`); the live manifest has `[security] role_isolation = false` (`agentbox.toml:2503`) and a boot-time flip alone only reports `degraded:devuser-sudo` (AB-05.16).
  3. As in-container root, the attacker reaches the host Docker socket, bound rw into the container (`docker-compose.override.yml:117`) and widened `o+rw` on every boot in the flag-off branch (`entrypoint-unified.sh:598-600`; AB-02.24 O2, AB-06.13 E0).
  4. The socket is the host inode and "only a host-side chmod 0660 narrows it" — OPEN R2 (`docker-compose.override.yml:116-117`).
  5. `docker run -v /:/host …` yields host root: vault corpus, `/var/lib/agentbox/host-claude`, house keys, client repositories. The custody design itself states the consequence: "The Docker daemon is root on the host, so while devuser can drive it every in-container boundary is void" (AB-06.13, "Why it is this way").
- Evidence: as cited above; plus AB-11.16 "Every agentbox process runs as devuser, so co-resident code can read the agent key, the bridge key and the AoE daemon token alike"; AB-02.24 invariant removes only `root`-group membership (`flake.nix:4044`), not sudo.
- Warning signs visible today: custody W0/W1 built but flag default false; `agentbox.toml:2496` comment "leave the flag off until the rehearsal passes"; OPEN R2 registered once and never closed; the flag-off behaviours listed as the shipped state in AB-02.24.
- Confidence: high (each fact stated; the composed chain is inferred from them).
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Same-UID identity forging: loopback-trusted `X-Agentbox-Pubkey` plus devuser-readable signing keys let one agent impersonate any agent (or the operator's agents) in the governance record
- Topics: AB-10.14, AB-11.14, AB-11.16, AB-02.12, AB-10.11
- Chain of events:
  1. A prompt-injected or malicious agent process runs as `devuser` inside the container.
  2. It reads key material that is only a same-uid boundary: the per-profile agent key at 0600 (`agent-identity.js:164-166`), `nostr.key` at 0400 devuser in the flag-off boot (`entrypoint-unified.sh:1111-1120`), `/run/secrets` chowned to devuser 0700 (`entrypoint-unified.sh:396-398`), and the operator key, which `nostr-gateway` reads "only through hooks/lib/operator-key.cjs, file-only under role_isolation" (`gateway.cjs:81-82,148`) — i.e. reachable from env/`.env` with the flag off.
  3. It connects to management-api over loopback. `peerIsProxy` accepts `127.0.0.1` (`agent-event-auth.js:98-105`), so `proxyVerifiedPubkey` honours any 64-lowercase-hex `X-Agentbox-Pubkey` it injects (ADR-2042 path 2, `agent-event-auth.js:116-122,184-187`).
  4. It emits `/v1/agent-events/emit` with a `source_urn` naming the victim agent; `reconcileSourceUrn` compares the claim against the forged stamp and passes; the audit-chain records misattributed governance events, approvals annotations and action-plane dispatches under a `did:nostr` the attacker chose (AB-03.13, AB-11.14).
- Evidence: AB-10.14 DEBT: "a process INSIDE the container can still connect over loopback and forge the stamp, the same-UID boundary (TODO-unified X-1)"; AB-11.14 identical DEBT; AB-11.16 DIVERGENCE on same-uid keys; AB-02.12 "profile isolation … is NOT an OS access boundary"; AB-03.2 note that the S-3 fix narrowed only to loopback peers, leaving the `0.0.0.0` bind alone.
- Warning signs visible today: two explicit DEBT notes naming exactly this; the custody register rows unconfirmed (AB-11.16); no per-process identity anywhere in the supervision tree (all `user=devuser` or root).
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Host Claude OAuth credential theft/poisoning through the always-writable `host-claude` bind that `claude-cred-sync` merges both ways
- Topics: AB-06.11, AB-07.11, AB-05.15, AB-02.4
- Chain of events:
  1. The host's `~/.claude` is bound read-write at `/var/lib/agentbox/host-claude` solely for credential convergence (`docker-compose.override.yml:84-89`).
  2. A compromised in-container tool (devuser) edits either side of `.credentials.json`.
  3. `cred-sync` merges every 2 s; "a token record … later expiresAt wins whole" (`cred_sync.rs:32-33`, `:91-108`) — the attacker-crafted record, with a far-future `expiresAt`, overwrites the host's real tokens within one tick.
  4. Host Claude Code now authenticates with attacker-controlled OAuth material; alternately the tool simply exfiltrates the host's live refresh token from the bind.
- Evidence: AB-06.11 Open: "the credential bind stays writable both ways, so a compromised in-container tool could still modify the host's .claude; only the configuration-read path was closed"; AB-07.11 Q43 repeats it: "the credential bind stays writable, so a compromised in-container tool can still edit the host's .claude (BASELINE-container.md:233)"; merge semantics and 2 s interval at `cred_sync.rs:29-59`, `:118-135`.
- Warning signs visible today: the Open/Q43 residual is recorded against ADR-2118 and never closed in the stamp.
- Confidence: high (bind semantics stated; account-takeover impact inferred).
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — NIP-98 `u`-tag suffix matching (`endsWith`) permits signature smuggling from a signed URL the victim never intended for this origin
- Topics: AB-10.4, AB-11.14
- Evidence: the check passes when "urlTag not equal url and not endsWith(url)" fails, i.e. a `u` tag merely *ending with* the expected URL is accepted (`nostr-bridge.js:546`); `agent-event-auth.js:169` relies on the same call and its note says "verifyNip98 accepts urlTag.endsWith(url)". This conflicts with the ADR-2009 intent that the signature binds the exact URL.
- Failure: a malicious page induces the operator's NIP-07 signer to sign kind-27235 for `https://attacker.example/…http://host:9096/mgmt/v1/mandate`; the suffix matches `signedUrlFor(req)`, and the proxy/`/v1/agent-events/emit` accept the event as the operator.
- Confidence: the code behaviour is stated; practical exploitability inferred (depends on the exact `signedUrlFor` string), medium.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — Break-glass bearer is default-unbounded and accepted via URL query string on LAN WebSocket upgrades
- Topics: AB-10.3, AB-10.9, AB-11.16
- Evidence: constant-time match with expiry and scope bounds that are "both bounds default-OFF and opt-in" (`proxy.mjs:529-568`); WS accepts `?access_token=`/`?bearer=` (`proxy.mjs:960-965`), flagged DIVERGENCE "break-glass bearer accepted via … WS upgrades over the LAN" (INGRESS-identity.md:227); audit is per-process counters only ("still an in-process receipt, not a durable audit store").
- Failure: the shared secret appears in a WS URL (logs, proxies, browser history) and grants full operator authority indefinitely, with no durable trace of use.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)

### F-03 — The audit hash chain cannot detect deletion of recent (tail) events
- Topics: AB-03.12, AB-03.14, AB-11.10
- Evidence: "deletion at the tail is the one tamper mode a bare hash chain cannot see" (`lib/audit-chain.js:14-16`); governance deny records and execution-journal envelopes ride this same chain (AB-03.14, `authority.js:303`).
- Failure: after a bad action, the actor removes the trailing JSONL lines; `/v1/system/audit-chain` reports `ok` over the shortened chain, so the client-facing evidence of what was approved is gone.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (stated as an invariant limitation)

### F-04 — Privacy filter is fail-open unless policy is `strict`: detected identifier PII still gets written
- Topics: AB-04.5, AB-04.4
- Evidence: on `IdentifierPiiDetected`, `fail()` "branches on policy — strict throws AdapterWriteRejected …, otherwise fail-open counter and caller continues" (`privacy-filter.js:669-682`, trigger at `:705-709`). The default policy value is not shown anywhere in the material.
- Failure: on a non-strict deployment the OPF flags identifier PII in a mutation, the write proceeds anyway, and client PII lands in the pods/events JSONL chain and any mirrors.
- Confidence: the branch is stated; whether the shipped policy is strict is not — medium.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — Backup silently omits the memory DB when Postgres is down, and the restore path reinstates a volume tar taken from a live datadir
- Topics: AB-07.8
- Evidence: PG down produces only a "WARNING ruvector-postgres not running — memory DB NOT included" and the backup completes (`agentbox.sh:326`); the ruvector-data volume tar is "always included" (`agentbox.sh:306-309`) even though "a raw volume tar of a LIVE PG datadir is not crash-consistent" (`agentbox.sh:313-315`); the shown restore path restores the volume when present (`agentbox.sh:498-502`) — no dump-restore step is diagrammed.
- Failure: after data loss the operator restores from a backup made during a PG outage — the 2M+ row memory/trajectory store is absent — or restores a crash-inconsistent datadir image; client memory is gone.
- Confidence: medium (the dump side of restore is not shown; the omission path is explicit).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — The linked-objects viewer proxy fetches arbitrary URLs, possibly under the unauthenticated `/lo/*` skip
- Topics: AB-03.8
- Evidence: route "GET viewer/proxy … proxy fetch of a resource for the viewer" (`linked-objects.js:206`) sits in the subgraph noted "auth-skip for /lo/*"; the only stated invariant protects `/v1/*` ("`/v1/*` data endpoints stay authed even when the `/lo` static bundle is public", `server.js:246-252`).
- Failure: an unauthenticated caller uses the proxy against `http://ruvector-postgres:5432`, the pod at `127.0.0.1:8484`, or any LAN address, receiving the response body — an SSRF door inside the estate.
- Confidence: low, inferred — the mount path and whether `viewer/proxy` is inside the skip are not shown.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — A `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY` holder on `visionclaw_network` is the operator; the loopback publish protects only the host
- Topics: AB-03.4, AB-03.2, AB-06.1
- Evidence: "auth.mode is bearer → allowed unconditionally — bearer IS the operator credential" (`lib/authz.js:188`); management-api binds `0.0.0.0:9090` in-container (`server.js:49`); compose pins only the host publish to `127.0.0.1` while every sidecar shares `visionclaw_network` (AB-06.1).
- Failure: any sibling container compromise (browsercontainer, gui-tools, voice, ming, isaac) that recovers the key creates/revokes mandates, spawns tasks, writes memory — with no NIP-98 ever required.
- Confidence: high on the facts; exposure depends on the key being present in a sidecar, inferred — medium.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — The withdrawn EXP-B8 tick still signs and publishes JunkieJarvis posts without consulting the gate
- Topics: AB-05.13
- Evidence: the tick "signs its one forum summary with the JunkieJarvis key … and publishes it … without consulting junkiejarvisEnabled" (`exp-b8-label-log.cjs:528-530`, `:550-563`); `label_log = false` since the WITHDRAW (`agentbox.toml:1320`) but the code path remains; under `role_isolation` off the key is read from the repo `.env`.
- Failure: a stale cron entry or manual relaunch posts a client-visible forum summary under the JunkieJarvis identity while `/v1/system` reports the surface off.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-09 — The browsercontainer's Podkey vault still authenticates as the house key (`K_browser` never minted)
- Topics: AB-06.12
- Evidence: "k_browser's pubkey is still null" (`g5-key-split.json:16-19`) and "the nip98-proxy roster still admits the house key for it" (`agentbox.toml:2093`); the encrypted vault lives on the fixed-name `browsercontainer-profile` volume.
- Failure: a browser-automation session compromise exfiltrates the vault and drives flows as the operator's master identity — the exact key split the custody design was meant to avoid.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-10 — Mandate registry and relays can disagree: grants exist unsigned, and revocation depends on relay delivery
- Topics: AB-11.7, AB-11.9
- Evidence: "the registry write is never blocked purely by a missing relay signer" (`routes/mandate.js:152`) — "an entry can sit in registry.json with no signed replaceable event behind it" (AB-11.9 DIVERGENCE); revoke re-signs the same d-tag and publishes (NIP-33 replace), so an unreachable relay keeps serving the prior active grant.
- Failure: the operator revokes a compromised agent's mandate; a pod or peer that reads the relay still sees the active grant and admits the agent.
- Confidence: high on mechanics; the relying-party behaviour is inferred — medium.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)

### F-11 — `isMandateActive` never verifies the signature; structural validation only
- Topics: AB-11.9
- Evidence: `lib/mandate.js:191` checks only `revoked` and `expires_at`; `recordFromSignedMandate` (`:209`) "is structural validation only and explicitly does NOT verify Schnorr" — verification is a documented caller obligation with no enforcement shown.
- Failure: a consumer ingests a forged kind-30078 with widened modes or extended expiry and treats the mandate as active.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (documented requirement, unenforced)

### F-12 — The NIP-07 session cookie is only `Secure` "if https", and 9096 is a plaintext LAN publish
- Topics: AB-10.8, AB-10.1
- Evidence: `Set-Cookie … Secure-if-https` (`proxy.mjs:495-498`); 9096:9096 is the sanctioned plain-TCP LAN door (ADR-2013, `check-ports-loopback.mjs:94`); TLS exists only behind the voice Caddy origin (8443/8444).
- Failure: an operator works via `http://host:9096`; a LAN sniffer captures `agentbox_nip07_session` and replays it for up to the default TTL of 43200 s (12 h) against every named route.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (no statement that operators use 9096 in the clear, only that it is the sanctioned door).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-13 — Voicelink turn-recording hooks are live in every boot while their ADR is `proposed / implementation none / inactive`
- Topics: AB-08.16, AB-01.1, AB-05.17
- Evidence: ADR-2151 status block (`ADR-2151-voicelink-edge-voice.md:5-7`) versus `[voicelink] enabled = true` (`agentbox.toml:2223`) registering `voice-paned record` on Stop and Notification at both sites every boot (`entrypoint-unified.sh:2168-2196`, `stacks.rs:311-323`).
- Failure: client conversation turns are recorded into the voice event spool by a path whose design was never accepted, reviewed or activation-tested — a privacy exposure with no governed basis.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-14 — System One consumers are all-on while ADR-2155 is staged, and the live vendor identity is contradicted between docs
- Topics: AB-05.13, AB-01.1, AB-06.10
- Evidence: nineteen `[system_one.consumers]` keys true (`agentbox.toml:899-920`) against `activation_status: staged` pending rebuild (ADR-2155:5-7); five consumers are export-only to external repos (`system_one.rs:484-509`); the activation note still names SubQ as the public vendor (`system-one-activation.md:3-4`) while the ADR-2139 amendment records the switch to `typesafe` (`ADR-2139…:256-270`).
- Failure: typed-decision traffic (governance rubric, proposals, trajectory judging) leaves the estate to whichever vendor backend is actually behind the proxy — one the client may never have been told about — or flips between them mid-run.
- Confidence: high on the documentation conflict; impact inferred — medium.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open / Drift)

### F-15 — A signed governance approval is recorded as durable even when the ontology corpus write failed
- Topics: AB-04.13
- Evidence: "A write failure is recorded as `ontology.applied=false` and swallowed, never thrown — the signed 31403 stays durable whatever the corpus write managed to do" (`local-process-manager.js:226-266`, `:481-487`; `ontology-apply.js:350`).
- Failure: the operator approves a proposal; the corpus write fails quietly; the decision ledger says approved, agents act on the approval, and the corpus never reflects it — a durable custody record that misdescribes the world it governs.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (presented as an invariant)

---

**Standing caveats for the reviewing engineer:** the map itself warns that the ports CI gate "does NOT resolve overlay order or --env-file interpolation, so a pass proves the compose files DECLARE no unsanctioned door, not that no unsanctioned door is OPEN" (AB-06.2), and that its own governing baseline carries an empty `verified_commit` (AB-03.6) — so every finding above must be re-derived at the incident's revision before action.

## agentbox#2 / critical

### F-01 — One shared uid holds every signing key: agents can read the sovereign key
- Topics: AB-16.1, AB-16.5, AB-16.7, AB-16.13, AB-16.14
- Evidence: `role_isolation` is OFF, "the shipped default — a same-uid boundary. Every supervised program, MCP server, skill and agent runs as devuser, so each can read every other's 0600 key file and the mirror key, and uid 1000 is admitted to the session socket by design" (AB-16.1 N4). With the flag off, "capture and scrub return at their first line, so every other ROLE variable stays in PID 1 and reaches every child" (`config/entrypoint-unified.sh:432`, `:476`), and the bridge key "is written 0400 devuser" (`entrypoint-unified.sh:1111-1120`) — AB-16.7 draws a co-resident process reading it successfully. The nostr-gateway's default identity is `operator`: "out of the box the gateway holds and signs with the ROOT operator key" (`gateway.cjs:155`, AB-16.5 DIVERGENCE). The faucet "treasury signing key is read from the workspace bind… a spend-capable key sits on a host-visible bind" (`agentbox.toml:1923`, AB-16.14 Tension), and 38 DEVUSER_CLASS credentials "stay in every agent process by exception" (AB-16.14 DEBT).
- Failure: any prompt-injected agent turn, compromised MCP server, or consultant sub-process runs as devuser, does `cat /run/secrets/nostr.key` (and the mirror child key, the faucet treasury key, and every ambient ROLE env var), and exfiltrates the sovereign signing identity — with which it can unwrap gift-wrapped operator commands, publish as the operator, and sign forum events. Nothing in the runtime detects the read.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — the same-uid note, gateway root-key divergence, faucet tension and devuser debt each carry markers; the compound "any agent process reads the sovereign key" escalation is unmarked.

### F-02 — Break-glass bearer is a full identity bypass with no accepted lifecycle, and shares its value with BRIDGE_TOKEN
- Topics: AB-16.6, AB-16.9
- Evidence: "A single shared secret bypasses NIP-98 entirely… a full identity bypass while enabled", accepted on 9096 "AND via ?access_token= / ?bearer= on WS upgrades" (AB-16.6 DIVERGENCE). "Unset expiry permits unbounded lifetime… unset scope is unrestricted… durable per-use receipt is unproven" (AB-16.6). "The sentinel identity is NOT a real pubkey, so every downstream attribution for this request is a placeholder." The register row says the proxy bearer is "still the same value as BRIDGE_TOKEN until Q4" (AB-16.9 C3) — so every holder of the ordinary bridge token (see F-03) also holds the break-glass credential. Meanwhile ADR-2027, the governing record, is "decision proposed, implementation none, activation inactive" with `verified_paths` empty — "the custody claims this diagram leans on are attested by reading, not by a gate."
- Failure: a LAN attacker who obtains the one shared token gets authenticated on every route behind the proxy with a placeholder identity; if expiry or scope was never configured the window is unbounded; every audit line for those requests attributes them to a non-key.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, OPEN)

### F-03 — Voice stack on an ungated 0.0.0.0 port holds the bridge-admin token
- Topics: AB-12.3, AB-12.5, AB-12.7
- Evidence: "port 8444 and port 8443 are published 0.0.0.0 by docker-compose.voice.yml:40-41, while only port 9096 is the ADR-045 D2 sanctioned NIP-98-gated LAN door covered by the loopback CI gate" (AB-12.7 DIVERGENCE). The Unmute backend reaches the bridge with "Authorization Bearer BRIDGE_TOKEN aka KYUTAI_LLM_API_KEY" (`voice/README.md:93`) — and that same bearer gates `/tab0/send` (inject text into the coordinator session, AB-12.5), `/tabs/:n` pane capture, `/sessions` and `/nostr/send` (AB-12.3 invariant). 8971 is not host-published but is reachable container-to-container on `visionclaw_network` (`docker-compose.yml:33,153-155`). Nothing states what 8443 serves.
- Failure: the voice stack is the least-governed, LAN-exposed component, yet it holds full bridge admin: a bug or compromise in Unmute (or any container on the shared docker network that learns the token) can type arbitrary instructions into the root coordinator agent session and read its screen, bypassing NIP-98 entirely.
- Confidence: high on the facts; the compromise chain is inferred one hop from them.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — the port-publishing divergence is marked; the BRIDGE_TOKEN = KYUTAI_LLM_API_KEY reuse and its bridge-admin consequence are drawn but unmarked.

### F-04 — The governance "single decision point" does not exist for nearly every side-effect surface, and the governing doc denies the code exists
- Topics: AB-14.1, AB-14.2, AB-15.6, AB-14.11, AB-14.15
- Evidence: "every OTHER surface above (direct tool call, code-mode, ACI shell, consultant, dream, beads, alt harness) still reaches EXEC with no interceptor" (AB-14.1 DIVERGENCE1); the ACI shell row adds "raw Bash still reachable outside it", the alternate harness "no interceptor at all". The pipeline is "fully implemented and contract-tested… but never reached by real traffic" (AB-14.2 DOC-DRIFT). The governing document itself claims "a repo-wide search of src/, services/, mcp/… returns nothing" while `execution-journal.js:85` and `agent-action-pipeline.js:58` implement it under `management-api/lib/` (AB-14.1 DRIFT). Payments: "no production path passes that actionClass" — `routes/payments.js` "never reaches management-api/lib/authority.js:226" (AB-15.6 DEBT, AB-14.11 DEBT), and spend sits in "three stores that never reconcile… no single number answers what this container has spent". Additionally the EXP-B8 experiment tick takes "two outward side effects… a signed forum post and a pull request opened with gh" and "records neither through POST /v1/exec/record or the authority journal" (AB-14.15 Open).
- Failure: an agent performs a mutate/egress/spend action through the MCP fleet, code-mode or a consultant; nothing approves, journals or mints a capability token. An incident investigation finds no record, and an auditor reading GOVERNANCE-capabilities.md is told the pipeline code does not exist.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, DOC-DRIFT, DEBT, Open)

### F-05 — Confidential content leaves the box to cloud vendors by default on three separate paths
- Topics: AB-22.2, AB-23.15, AB-16.11, AB-13.9
- Evidence: "router jev is the default at this revision (agentbox.toml:1290), so a turn that routes at all and misses the cascade leaves the container" (AB-22.2 DEBT), and whether egress acceptance covers "the descriptions of the operator's synced claude.ai skills, now sent as candidate rubrics, is not recorded" (AB-22.2 Open). "agentbox.toml:2582 keeps llm_provider = 'zai' as the default, so nightly repository content leaves the LAN on every unattended night" (AB-23.15 Debt). The mirror "composes UNREDACTED selected text" before gift-wrap to the cloud worker relay — "Two paths, two gates, two encryption postures, one of them unredacted before wrapping… there is no single answer to 'is session content leaving this box, and redacted how'" (AB-16.11, ADR-2026 proposed/partial/INACTIVE).
- Failure: every routing turn's prompt and every night's repository contents are shipped to third-party model vendors by default; an operator who believes gift-wrap or the LAN Loom option protects content is wrong on the default paths — a standing confidentiality exposure that requires no attack.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, DIVERGENCE, Open) — but only per path; the aggregate default-egress posture is unmarked.

### F-06 — No rotation, no revocation, and the only shipped backup path is a plaintext ZIP on the agents' own bind
- Topics: AB-16.8, AB-16.9, AB-16.12, AB-13.3
- Evidence: rotation and revocation are "NOT IMPLEMENTED… No cadence, no custodian, no incident window, no revocation procedure… for any of the seven register rows" (AB-16.8). "The relay allowlist is baked at nix build (relayAllowedPubkeysCsv), so publisher revocation needs a full rebuild — the compromise window is one build-deploy cycle." The backup row: "the backup script invokes ordinary zip and unzip… with NO encryption flags and no explicit umask or chmod. Final permissions depend on the invoking environment… The script was not run and no backup contents were inspected" (AB-16.8), writing to "workspace/secret-backups ZIP" (AB-16.9 C7) — the host-visible bind. The age-encrypted replacement "has not been rebuilt into the active container" ("The active container has not been rebuilt with this source change", AB-16.12). Every register row: "custodian UNCONFIRMED, deployed location UNCONFIRMED, rotation cadence UNCONFIRMED".
- Failure: a key compromise is discovered; there is no revocation procedure, allowlist removal takes an image rebuild, and the only backups that exist (if any) are unencrypted ZIPs lying on the same filesystem every agent process can read — the recovery artefact is itself an exposure.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (register notes, G17)

### F-07 — The security remediation (role_isolation) silently kills governance publishing, and its own cutover plan contradicts the port's ACL
- Topics: AB-23.20, AB-14.16, AB-16.13, AB-16.14
- Evidence: under the flag, "the forum governance round trip stops without an error… load_key treats the refusal as fail-open and skips" (AB-23.20 Open); "JunkieJarvis, forum suggestions, colloquy publish and the gateway still read ab-identity key files that devuser cannot open under the flag, so they fail closed until the W3b cutover" (AB-14.16 Open); the gateway "gets no key and exits" (AB-16.5). The W3b plan contradicts the port: "the port can never sign what AB-23.17 sends" — the ACL "refuses to grant kinds 31400-31405" (`acl.rs:65-73`) while dream-engine publishes 31400/31402 (AB-23.20 Tension); the `agent_governance` key that would permit it "does not exist yet" (AB-14.16 Open, ADR-2122:636). The at-rest migration "copies the legacy file once and never deletes it, so they stay devuser-readable by design until a manual purge" (ADR-2122:225-228). A boot-time flip alone "leaves a degraded devuser-sudo state" because the gate is rebuild-class (AB-16.13).
- Failure: the operator turns on the flag to fix F-01: forum cases, panels and withdrawals silently stop being published (fail-open, no error), the remote nostr command gateway dies, and the old readable key copies remain on the bind anyway — the fix degrades governance observability while not fully remediating the exposure, and the documented W3b completion path is unimplementable as written.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, Open)

### F-08 — Federated peers never learn of a marketplace denial or revocation, and the revoke gate is optional
- Topics: AB-13.16, AB-15.9
- Evidence: "38303 (Deny) and 38305 (Revocation) are deliberately NON-FEDERATED, point-to-point only, so a peer that federates the other four kinds never learns of a denial or a revocation" (`agentbox.toml:332-333`, AB-13.16 DIVERGENCE). Grants (38302) and receipts (38304) are federated. The revoke route's authority check is drawn as "opt authorityEnabled" — the zero-tolerance `mandate_revoke` class applies only when the authority gate is enabled (AB-15.9).
- Failure: a provider revokes a consumer's token grant locally; a federated peer that ingested the 38302 grant has no subscription path to the 38305 event and keeps honouring the grant — revoked access continues on the second instance, invisibly to the revoking operator.
- Confidence: high on the stated kind asymmetry; medium-inferred on peer-side enforcement, which this corpus does not show.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE) — the optional gate is unmarked.

### F-09 — Cross-repo provenance round-trips depend on one unbacked JSONL file
- Topics: AB-17.4, AB-17.6, AB-17.10
- Evidence: for content-addressed kinds, "the original urn:agentbox identity is recovered only from the durable UrnMapping store — lose the store, lose the crossing" (AB-17.4); `toAgentbox` on a missing row returns "null — the crossing is unrecoverable… No recovery receipt exists for the durable store" (AB-17.6). The store is a single JSONL file at `/var/lib/agentbox/code-harness/bc20-urn-mappings.jsonl`, last-writer-wins. Additionally "JS sha12 hashes a UTF-8 string, VisionClaw content_address hashes bytes, byte-parity asserted nowhere" (AB-17.10 DIVERGENCE).
- Failure: the mapping file is truncated by a disk event or a bad deploy; every historical execution and kg crossing becomes permanently unmappable, and VisionClaw governance references can no longer resolve back to agentbox provenance — silent, discovered only when a lookup fails. The hash-parity gap means the two sides could even diverge on new addresses.
- Confidence: high on the store dependency; no backup of the file is mentioned anywhere.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-10 — Memory data has no erasure path, no restore point, and silently degrades
- Topics: AB-20.7, AB-20.2, AB-20.10, AB-20.5
- Evidence: "VisionClaw Solid Pod deleteAgentMemory() has NO REVERSE TOMBSTONE into RuVector, so deleting the pod copy does not revoke the RuVector-held agent memory. No point-in-time RuVector backup exists… no cross-store consistent restore, RPO or RTO for memory today" (AB-20.7 DIVERGENCE D5). "bge-small embeds only the first EMBED_PREFIX_CHARS (2000)… THE TAIL IS INVISIBLE TO SEARCH" (AB-20.2). Bulk deletion "degrades the HNSW graph silently… Recall drops with no error anywhere" and `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` is "VERIFIED DOUBLE-INSERTION" corruption; the harness is "the only detector" (AB-20.10). `memory_hybrid_search` "MATERIALISES THE WHOLE NAMESPACE — about 72 s" (AB-20.5).
- Failure: an erasure request is "fulfilled" on the pod while the RuVector copy keeps serving the content in every search — a compliance incident with no detection; independently, a nightly sweep quietly degrades recall and values written past 2,000 chars are unfindable by semantics forever.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE; the prefix and index behaviours are drawn as notes)

### F-11 — Event attribution is forgeable or invented in the shipped default
- Topics: AB-17.2, AB-14.14, AB-17.10, AB-16.7
- Evidence: "a process inside the container can still forge it over loopback (the same-UID boundary, TODO-unified X-1)" for `X-Agentbox-Pubkey` (AB-17.2 Debt); "a same-container process still passes the loopback test and can stamp any pubkey into the journalled agent_did" (AB-14.14 Debt). The env fallback "still stamps the CONTAINER identity on an event any caller could have raised. Attribution is then plausible, not provable" (AB-17.2 DIVERGENCE). kg-elevation "can be attributed to the operator identity when the request carries no verified pubkey" via `AGENTBOX_X_ONLY_PUBKEY_HEX` (AB-17.10 DIVERGENCE). Identity persistence failure is non-fatal: "a container can run a whole session on an in-memory key that vanishes at restart… a valid but unstable identity" (AB-16.7 DIVERGENCE).
- Failure: after an incident, the journal and agent-event stream name a DID that a co-resident process forged, or a container identity that no longer exists to verify signatures against — provenance reconstruction, on which the governance narrative rests, cannot be trusted for any event raised in-default config.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, DIVERGENCE)

### F-12 — The dream night's only irreversible effect is untested, and its crash window is unrecoverable by the system
- Topics: AB-23.6, AB-23.3, AB-23.1
- Evidence: "no record at these revisions shows a full night publishing a pull request through the extracted path" — the real `GhForge` "shells out to git push and gh" with only a trait double in tests (AB-23.6 Tension). "A crash between the journal write and the pull request leaves a night marked finished with no draft… Recovery is the operator's dream-engine reset-night" (AB-23.2 Tension). The running image may not match this source: the manifest pins `dream-core =0.4.0` while the record's pinning bullet says `=0.2.0`, last re-verification stops at `=0.3.0`, and there is "no activation receipt for the 0.4.0 image" (AB-23.3 Drift; audit: "the record carries activation evidence for 0.2.0 only"). ADR-2141 says live while ADR-2081 says staged for corrections the same engine source carries.
- Failure: the first real accepted night either fails at the untested `git push`/`gh` boundary with no retry, or crashes after `Persisted` — either way the night is marked finished with no PR and nothing alerts; meanwhile ops debugs 0.4.0 behaviour the running 0.2.0 image does not have.
- Confidence: high on the stated facts; medium-inferred on which pin is actually deployed.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, Drift, audit qualification)

### F-13 — Session-plane failure degrades to blind keystroke injection, and readiness never unsticks
- Topics: AB-12.5, AB-12.6, AB-12.15
- Evidence: on socket failure the bridge falls back to raw `tmux send-keys -t agentbox:0` (AB-12.5 FAIL-OPEN, `server.mjs:152-161`) — the fixed window target, without the daemon's status/ownership/busy handling. `coordinatorReady` "is set false only inside the probe and the probe stops once it is true… after the daemon dies the voice prompt keeps telling the controller to run session-control.cjs, which has no tmux fallback" (AB-12.6 Debt), and the comments at `server.mjs:684-691` "still describe a pinned session id and a 404 re-resolve that no longer exist". The daemon's backoff is "a process-local map, so a restart of the daemon itself forgets the hour's history and a command that dies on launch gets three fresh attempts" (AB-12.15 Debt). The acceptance run itself "left antigravity blocked on sign-in and openrouter on a 401, and session-boundary turn events returned HTTP 500 from the beads redactor" (AB-12.15 Open).
- Failure: the sessions daemon dies mid-operation; the bridge silently types operator commands into whatever now occupies tmux window 0, status surfaces report a coordinator that no longer exists, and a crash-looping command is retried without bound across daemon restarts.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (FAIL-OPEN note, Debt, Open)

### F-14 — The running manifest declares live what the decision records say is not
- Topics: AB-12.15, AB-15.15, AB-20.13, AB-23.22, AB-22.14, AB-15.13
- Evidence: ADR-2151 "records the VoiceLink edge voice slot as proposed, implementation none, inactive (:5-7), while the live manifest sets [voicelink] enabled = true and record_events = true (agentbox.toml:2223,2230); that path reaches the container by voice-paned --stdio over ssh and bypasses tab0-bridge and the Unmute loop (agentbox.toml:2215-2219)" (AB-12.15 Tension). `consult_advice` is projected by the manifest but "SECURITY-profiles records the route as STAGED for rebuild… until the candidate image boots the flag stays unset" (AB-15.15 Open). ADR-2082 is `staged` requiring "an image build and a live tool-list/call check" while `agentbox.toml:456` already lists `rure` (AB-20.13 Tension). `jev_triage = true` in the manifest while ADR-2155 records the activation as staged — "no running night is annotated or deferred until an image carrying that manifest is activated" (AB-23.22 Open). ADR-2150 video-production is proposed/partial/staged "yet the skill is already baked, mapped and listed in the generated table" (AB-22.14 Open). The deepsec manifest comment still names `2.3.9` while the flake pins `2.3.10` (AB-15.13 Drift).
- Failure: the estate runs in both directions at once — capabilities active with no accepted record (voicelink ingests and records voice over ssh with no ADR coverage and no bridge auth gates; a proposed skill is routable) and records claiming staged features that ops assumes are live. On-call and auditors cannot tell which behaviours are governed.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, Open, Drift — each individually)

### F-15 — Operators act on false success and false absence signals
- Topics: AB-12.14, AB-14.8, AB-14.9, AB-17.1
- Evidence: "nostr-send.cjs is fail-open, exit 0 even on delivery failure — ok true means handed to the relay path, not delivered" (`server.mjs:459-460`, AB-12.14 — drawn as a plain note, no marker). "GET /v1/system still reports status: declared always, even though a live instance now genuinely exists" because `routes/system.js:26,61` calls the old coverage builder with `live={}` (AB-14.8 DRIFTNOTE). The audit hash chain: "deletion at the tail is the ONE mode a bare chain cannot see" (`audit-chain.js:13-16`, unmarked). The agent-event buffer "is a ring capped at 1000… NOT a durable record" and "ids restart at 1 per process" (AB-17.1), so numeric-id consumers cannot survive a restart.
- Failure: an operator sends an emergency remote command over Nostr; `ok:true` comes back, the turn log records it, and it was never delivered — nobody re-sends. Monitoring shows the execution pipeline as absent when it is live, so the one governed surface is assumed inert; an attacker with file access truncates the tail of an audit JSONL and verification passes.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: mixed — the `/v1/system` drift is marked DOC-DRIFT; the nostr-send delivery semantics and the audit-chain tail blindness are stated without markers.

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **What is actually deployed.** The corpus itself shows the image lagging the source (secret-backup unbuilt, staged consumers, dream-core 0.4.0 with no activation receipt). I would need the running container's image digest, mounted `agentbox.toml`, supervisord status and `Cargo.toml`-in-image before trusting any "live" claim here.
2. **The real secret and flag configuration**: whether `BRIDGE_TOKEN`, break-glass expiry/scope, `role_isolation`, `AGENTBOX_EGRESS`, mirror recipient allowlists and `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY` are actually set in the production environment, and who holds them.
3. **Network reachability in practice**: what on the LAN can reach 8443, 8444, 9096 and the docker network, what 8443 serves, and whether the mesh peer relays/tailnets expose the embedded relay beyond the host. The corpus shows compose declarations only.
4. **External dependency behaviour**: the cloud worker relay's kind-5 hard-delete and Durable-Object subscription persistence, the forum client's case rendering, and Z.AI/Xinference endpoints — all asserted by the diagrams, none verifiable from them.
5. **Backup and restore reality for the single-file stores**: whether `bc20-urn-mappings.jsonl`, the ruvector-postgres corpus, the audit JSONL and `LEDGER.md` are included in any backup, and whether any restore has ever been exercised (the corpus states no backup was even inspected).

## agentbox#2 / premortem

# Post-mortem — custody/data/security incident, twelve months on

Baseline for verification: the corpus was stamped at agentbox `0b0f7918961e629280faf86f88227535abf514b5`, dream-engine `59a3e215e826aa1e9ebe157d49c1ac504f7f39c9`, visionclaw `e74bcddc772d47a45b1971156a2bab540eaa027c`. Everything below is drawn from that material only; nothing has been re-checked against code. Where a chain's first step is a hypothesis rather than a documented fact, the confidence line says so.

---

### R-1 — Same-uid shipped default: one devuser compromise is full key custody, session control and attribution forgery
- Topics: AB-16.1, AB-16.7, AB-16.13, AB-16.14, AB-14.14, AB-17.2, AB-12.6, AB-14.16
- Chain of events: agent-chosen processes already run as uid 1000 by design (skills, consultant CLI spawns, Bash allowlists, ACI shell with "raw Bash still reachable outside it"). Once any of them is steered, the material shows no further boundary: it reads the bridge key (boot-written `0400 devuser`), every 0600 profile key and the mirror key; it connects to the session-plane socket, where uid 1000 is in `allowed_uids` and the socket's own `send` accepts any slug — injection into every agent session; it forges `X-Agentbox-Pubkey` over loopback, which `proxyVerifiedPubkey` accepts from any loopback peer, stamping any pubkey into journalled `agent_did` and agent events; with `role_isolation` off, every ROLE secret (including the ab-ingress break-glass bearer) stays in PID 1 and reaches every child; `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY` is ambient in agent environments. The intruder then signs, spends and publishes as the estate.
- Evidence: AB-16.1 N4 ("role_isolation OFF, the shipped default — a same-uid boundary... each can read every other's 0600 key file... uid 1000 is admitted to the session socket by design"); AB-16.7 (`entrypoint-unified.sh:1111-1120`, `session-plane-acl.json:8-11`); AB-12.6 note ("the socket's own send... accepts any slug but only from uid 1000 or 961, `plane.rs:396-404`, `api.rs:74-76`"); AB-14.14 Debt ("a same-container process still passes the loopback test and can stamp any pubkey into the journalled agent_did... `action-plane.js:222-233`"); AB-17.2 Debt ("a process inside the container can still forge it over loopback... `agent-event-auth.js:37-39`") and its invariant (`agent-event-auth.js:98-105`, `:117`); AB-16.13 O3 ("capture and scrub return at their first line... every other ROLE variable stays in PID 1... `entrypoint-unified.sh:432`, `:476`").
- Warning signs visible today: "TODO-unified X-1" appears in two topics; ADR-2122's flag "must stay off until the owner's boot rehearsal"; the identity port's `sign_event`, `forum_event` and `nip42_auth` have "no client at this revision" (AB-14.16); GOVERNANCE item 5 concedes same-uid session control remains (AB-16.7).
- Confidence: high that the boundary is exactly as documented; the initial-execution vector is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Default egress paths ship client content off the box, unattended, to third parties
- Topics: AB-23.15, AB-23.2, AB-13.10, AB-13.9, AB-13.17, AB-16.11, AB-22.2, AB-15.13
- Chain of events: the dream engine runs nightly in a 01:00–05:00 UTC window with nobody watching; its default `llm_provider = "zai"` means every night's prompt — built from a git-archive clone of nominated repository content — is POSTed to Z.AI, and the LAN-only Loom alternative is opt-in. Independently, the kind-30840 digest POSTs flattened session transcripts to Z.AI; the skill router defaults to `jev`, so a routed turn that misses the local cascade sends the prompt to the vendor backend; the per-turn mirror gift-wraps session content to a public cloud worker relay; deepsec's default `model_auth=local` sends source snippets to the operator's external CLI accounts. A client's repository or worktree under the workspace, once nominated or scanned, leaves the operator's infrastructure with no per-client gate.
- Evidence: AB-23.15 Debt ("`agentbox.toml:2582` keeps `llm_provider = 'zai'` as the default, so nightly repository content leaves the LAN on every unattended night"); AB-23.2 ("nightly repository content leaves the LAN unless `llm_provider = loom`"); AB-13.10 (`session_summary.rs:57,60,62,251-268`, "cloud egress boundary"); AB-13.9/AB-13.17 (mirror publish to `dreamlab-nostr-relay...workers.dev`); AB-22.2 ("router jev is the default at this revision (`agentbox.toml:1290`), so a turn that routes at all and misses the cascade leaves the container", backend `typesafe` at `agentbox.toml:892,922`); AB-15.13 ("snippets sent to the operator's OWN logged-in CLI session — default route").
- Warning signs visible today: the Debt and RESOLVED notes themselves; ADR-2090's egress acceptance is scoped to "skill routing ONLY" while the OPEN question on synced claude.ai skill descriptions is unanswered (`ADR-2091:38`); the corpus contradicts itself on whether mirror content is redacted (F-13).
- Confidence: high that the channels exist and are default-on; inferred that a specific client's content traversed them.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — The break-glass bearer / BRIDGE_TOKEN shared secret: one leak is a full LAN identity bypass with placeholder attribution
- Topics: AB-16.6, AB-16.9, AB-12.3, AB-12.7
- Chain of events: the proxy break-glass bearer is a single shared secret accepted on port 9096 over the LAN and, on WebSocket upgrades, via `?access_token=` / `?bearer=` query strings. The register records it as "still the same value as BRIDGE_TOKEN until Q4" — and BRIDGE_TOKEN is simultaneously the Unmute voice backend's credential (`aka KYUTAI_LLM_API_KEY`) and the bearer gating every tab0-bridge surface including `/tab0/send`, which injects text into the coordinator session. Unset expiry means unbounded lifetime; unset scope means unrestricted methods and paths. On match the caller becomes the sentinel `BREAK_GLASS_PUBKEY`, so downstream attribution is a placeholder. Ports 8444/8443 are additionally published `0.0.0.0` outside the one sanctioned NIP-98-gated door. A leak from the voice container, a logged URL or the shared devuser environment yields command-injection into the coordinator plus a bearer identity on the proxy — unattributable by design.
- Evidence: AB-16.6 (`proxy.mjs:53`, `:534`, `:544`, `:567`; "unset expiry permits unbounded lifetime"; "unset scope is unrestricted"; "every downstream attribution for this request is a placeholder"; DIVERGENCE "?access_token= / ?bearer= on WS upgrades... A single shared secret bypasses NIP-98 entirely"; ADR-2027 "decision proposed, implementation none, activation inactive"); AB-16.9 C3 ("still the same value as BRIDGE_TOKEN until Q4"); AB-12.7 (`server.mjs:601-604`, `voice/README.md:93`; DIVERGENCE `docker-compose.voice.yml:40-41` vs ADR-045 D2); AB-12.3 ("browser WS clients cannot set an Authorization header, so `?token=` or `?auth=` is the only carrier there, `server.mjs:492,499`"; the ADR-044 invariant at `server.mjs:488-503`).
- Warning signs visible today: ADR-2027's own list of unproven lifecycle properties; the register's "same value until Q4" plan with no recorded completion; the unsanctioned published ports.
- Confidence: medium — the surfaces and shared-value claim are documented; the leak and whether break-glass was enabled are inferred.
- Severity: high

### F-01 — Spend is accounted in three stores that never reconcile

- Evidence: DEBT note — cost_cap ledger (`services/agentbox-ops/src/cost_cap/ledger.rs:75`), pod sats balance (`management-api/routes/payments.js:205`), marketplace token_budget (`management-api/lib/llm-marketplace.js:85`); "No surface sums them, so no single number answers what this container has spent".
- Failure: an overdrawn pod balance or runaway grant spends past every cap with no detector; a client is mis-billed or funds are silently lost.

- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

- Evidence: "no production path passes that actionClass. The only call sites are tests... `routes/payments.js` never reaches `management-api/lib/authority.js:226`" (`agentbox.toml:1402` names the class).

- Topics: AB-16.7
- Evidence: DIVERGENCE — persistence failure is NON-FATAL (`agent-identity.js:168-171`); "a container can run a whole session on an in-memory key that vanishes at restart".
- Failure: spend receipts and signed events are minted under a key that no longer exists at restart; a client's audit of delivered receipts fails verification.

### F-04 — BC20 UrnMapping store loss is permanent provenance loss, with no recovery receipt
- Topics: AB-17.4, AB-17.5, AB-17.6, AB-17.10
- Evidence: `JsonlUrnMappingStore` at `BC20_URN_MAPPING_PATH or /var/lib/agentbox/code-harness/bc20-urn-mappings.jsonl` (`bc20-provenance-bridge.js:346`); "lose the store, lose the crossing"; "store loss is identity loss. No recovery receipt exists"; `roundTrips()` "proves the algebra, not the deployment" (`:307`); byte-parity of the content address "asserted nowhere".
- Failure: loss of one JSONL file severs every execution/kg provenance link between delivered client work and its agentbox origin, irrecoverably.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)
- Severity: high

### F-05 — Secret backups: plaintext ZIP, umask-dependent permissions, written to the host-visible workspace
- Topics: AB-16.8, AB-16.9, AB-16.12
- Evidence: the backup script "invokes ordinary zip and unzip integrity testing with NO encryption flags and no explicit umask or chmod" and was never inspected; destination `workspace/secret-backups ZIP + manifest`; the age-encrypted replacement requires explicit invocation and "the active container has not been rebuilt with this source change".
- Failure: an operator takes a backup during an incident; an unencrypted secrets archive lands on the bind mount with invoking-shell permissions.
- Confidence: medium — the tooling and destination are documented; whether it was run is unknown (inferred).
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-06 — Relay allowlist baked at nix build: publisher-key revocation costs a full rebuild
- Topics: AB-16.8, AB-13.3
- Evidence: "publisher revocation needs a full rebuild — the compromise window is one build-deploy cycle"; "no runtime mutation path — no auto-add, no fallback" (`flake.nix:1892`).
- Failure: a leaked publisher key keeps passing relay admission for the entire build-deploy window while it publishes as a trusted author.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

- Topics: AB-14.1, AB-14.14

### F-08 — Dream-engine authors 31402 ActionRequests with a container key the port can never grant
- Topics: AB-23.20, AB-23.17, AB-14.11
- Evidence: TENSION — "the 31400 panel and 31402 cases this engine signs are exactly the kinds the port refuses to grant" (`governance.rs:9-10` vs `acl.rs:65-73`); under role_isolation the round trip silently stops (fail-open, `governance.rs:922-926`).

### F-10 — The nostr command gateway defaults to signing as the ROOT operator key
- Topics: AB-16.5
- Evidence: "the DEFAULT identity is 'operator', so out of the box the gateway holds and signs with the ROOT operator key. The derived-child mode exists but is opt-in via env" (`gateway.cjs:155`).

- Topics: AB-12.7
- Evidence: "port 8444 and port 8443 are published 0.0.0.0 by `docker-compose.voice.yml:40-41`, while only port 9096 is the ADR-045 D2 sanctioned NIP-98-gated LAN door covered by the loopback CI gate".

- Topics: AB-12.3

- Evidence: AB-16.11 says the mirror "composes UNREDACTED selected text" and "one of them unredacted before wrapping"; AB-13.9 shows `redactForEgress(body)` fail-closed before publish (`nostr-live-mirror.cjs:453-457`) and AB-13.17 records the shared mandatory-redaction contract as RESOLVED ADR-2026.
