Verification note for the reviewing engineer: R-1's escalation steps, R-2's channel defaults and R-3's shared-value claim are each independently checkable in one sitting (entrypoint scrub functions, `agentbox.toml:2582`/`:1290`, and the proxy/bridge token plumbing respectively). F-13 requires deciding which of two corpus drawings matches the deployed hook; treat that check as blocking for any statement about mirror redaction made to the client.

## agentbox#3 / critical

### F-01 — Host Docker-socket bypass is open in the shipping configuration: every agent is host-root-equivalent today
- Topics: AB-36 (intro), AB-36.7
- Evidence: AB-36's intro states "A fourth bypass, the host Docker socket, only closes under the flag, and only fully once the host narrows the socket's mode" — and `[security].role_isolation` ships **false** (`agentbox/agentbox.toml:2503`). AB-36.7 Open (R2): the socket is "a bind of the host inode that earlier boots widened" and "stays world-accessible until the host chmods it" (`docker-compose.override.yml:116-117`); a flag-on boot can only record `degraded:docker-socket` (`entrypoint-unified.sh:614`); rehearsal check (f) expects "no docker socket" for devuser only under the flag (`role-isolation-rehearsal.sh:25-26`).
- Failure: with the flag off (the shipped state), any process running as devuser — every agent tool, MCP server, and hook — reaches the host Docker daemon, spawns a privileged container or mounts the host filesystem, and becomes host root. This voids every container-level boundary in AB-36 and the entire custody programme at a stroke.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — Open (R2) frames it as a host follow-up; the live off-mode exposure is stated in the intro sentence but not flagged as a standing finding.

### F-02 — Every process in the container can read every secret, and the legacy key copies are never purged
- Topics: AB-36 (intro, AB-36.3, AB-36.6), AB-34.3, AB-34.6
- Evidence: AB-36 intro: "Until this change every secret in the container reached every process… It is switched off today" (`agentbox.toml:2503`). AB-36.6 Debt: 37 DEVUSER_CLASS names stay ambient in every devuser process, including `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY`, `BRIDGE_TOKEN`, provider keys and the ssh-agent socket (`env-classes.json:90`, `:92`, `:195-211`). AB-34.6 off-mode: the chain signer key, parent RPC credential and faucet treasury key are "read straight from their at-rest paths by devuser" (`run-producer.sh:70-71`, `run-faucet.sh:27`). AB-34.3 Tension / AB-36.3: `ab_custody_migrate` copies the legacy workspace twin once and never deletes it — "a spend-capable key stays on the host-visible bind in both modes until a manual purge" (ADR-2122:225-228).
- Failure: one compromised or mistaken agent tool reads the environment and the secrets volume in a single pass and exfiltrates the sovereign Nostr identity material, the forum house key, both sidechains' signer and treasury keys, and the management API key. No per-principal boundary limits the blast radius; this is the default posture, not a misconfiguration.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, Tension, Open markers across AB-34/AB-36; the shipping-off state is stated throughout).

### F-03 — The P21 real-value gate is a field-presence check; nothing resolves the receipt, forbids a mainnet-parent chain, or refuses at the node
- Topics: AB-32.3, AB-32.2, AB-31.9
- Evidence: AB-32.3 Debt: genesis test case 3 only requires that a mainnet parent alias carries a `p21Receipt` field — "Resolving the receipt is NOT checked"; ADR-2103 D4's second CI check (no level-1 chain on a mainnet parent) and a node that refuses to open one — "none is built" (ADR-2103:229-230). AB-32.2: the coinbase `pin:` record that would commit parent and containment into the genesis is UNBUILT (ADR-2103:210-214), so the binding is the committed JSON document plus its hash only.
- Failure: a mainnet-parent chain sealed with a placeholder or unresolvable receipt passes CI. PRD-024's own invariant — P4 "cannot start until the gate exists AS CODE" (`sovereign-settlement.md:329-330`) — is therefore not satisfied by what exists; the containment the whole plan leans on for real value is an assertion, and the tests that should enforce it cannot fail on the failure that matters.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high (latent: bites exactly when real value is introduced, which is what this gate exists to prevent)
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt in AB-32.3; Tension and the ADR-2103 amendment in AB-32.2).

### F-04 — The prompt-egress register's line citations have rotted, and its checker is structurally unable to detect the rot
- Topics: AB-24.14
- Evidence: the register is stamped `0919dc39a` (`config/egress-policy.json:51`) and its loom-door and zai-night rows cite manifest lines 2179 and 2163 and flake line 3438 (`egress-policy.json:231-232`, `:355`). At this revision line 2179 is a session seed's `worktree = true`, line 2163 a seed's `tool = "claude"`, and flake 3438 an empty line; `llm_provider` is now `agentbox.toml:2582` and the `LOOM_BASE_URL` default `flake.nix:3957`. The renderer's `--check` "only tests that a cited line falls inside the file" (`scripts/ci/render-egress-register.js:110-119`).
- Failure: the artifact that records which prompts may leave the estate and under whose acceptance points auditors at unrelated lines. Any future manifest change that moves an egress-relevant key passes the check with stale evidence, so prompt-egress compliance is asserted by a document that is silently false and cannot self-correct.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift).

### F-05 — The Loom door serves a superseded corpus generation, and the two topics that own the door contradict each other about why
- Topics: AB-24.13, AB-24.15
- Evidence: AB-24.13 Debt (2026-10-02): generation `visionGraph@015ca2c1f` is built, promoted and verified "but the door still serves `visionGraph@ae913f93…`", and "the `vault` 0.1.0 baked into the agentbox image predates the portable-records file, so it cannot produce a promotable bundle" — fixed in source (`flake.nix:31-32`) "but that reaches a running `vault` only at the next image rebuild" (ADR-141:141-150). AB-24.15, at the same verified commit: ADR-2140 is `accepted`/`complete`/`live`, with an every-boot background reconcile that validates, builds and promotes HEAD to the door (`hp-loom-reconcile.sh:119-155`, `entrypoint-unified.sh:1209-1219`).
- Failure: both statements cannot quietly be true — either the reconcile fails for the same stale-tooling reason (fail-open, timeout 1800, "never stop a container", `hp-loom-reconcile.sh:13-15`, so nothing alerts beyond a receipt) or the debt note is itself stale. Meanwhile every ontology-grounded consumer (retrieval brain AB-24.3, condense AB-25.7, dream loom nights) answers from an outdated corpus for an unbounded period.
- Confidence: medium (the contradiction is certain; which statement describes the current runtime is not)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt in AB-24.13); the inter-topic contradiction is unmarked.

### F-06 — The condense consumer hand-rolls chat-completions against the facade with a pinned model, contradicting both ADR-2084 and the model-swap invariant
- Topics: AB-25.7, AB-24.9, AB-24.10, AB-24.7
- Evidence: the refresh shell script itself does `POST /v1/chat/completions` (`ontology-condense-refresh.sh:108`) with "model qwen3.8-27B style openai max_concurrency 2" (`agentbox.toml:1118-1120`); AB-24.9's register shows the condense consumer holding `model qwen3.8-27B`. ADR-2084's invariant: "hand-rolled chat-completions construction against a facade is PROHIBITED — a sixth caller takes the crate" (ADR-2084:37; the five crate callers listed in AB-24.10 are all Rust services). ADR-2023's invariant (AB-24.7): swapping the deployed model "must NOT touch any consumer" — yet this consumer pins a concrete model while `[dream_machine].loom_model` went empty at `878f23311`.
- Failure: on the next model swap behind the facade, condense requests a model that no longer exists or silently targets the wrong one; as a shell client it carries none of the crate's trap knowledge — a "one retrieval sentence" ask is exactly the shape that hits the sub-1536 empty-content trap (AB-24.10 K3) and reads empty output as success, every scheduler tick.
- Confidence: medium-high (the direct POST and pinned model are stated; whether ADR-2084 intended to scope only the five Rust callers is not)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no.

### F-07 — The dream engine sends an empty model name and depends on undocumented facade leniency
- Topics: AB-24.7
- Evidence: "the supervisor exports LOOM_MODEL empty (`flake.nix:2900`) and llm_config passes it through as the request model (`estate.rs:106`), so a loom night sends an empty model name and depends on the Loom answering an empty name with its loaded model. Observed by a live request… it answered with a loom.generation block. The serde default qwen3.8-27B (`config.rs:171`) applies only when the key is absent" — an exported empty string bypasses the default (classic empty-vs-absent env bug).
- Failure: any facade update that validates the model field, or a backend swap to an engine requiring an explicit model, breaks every loom night with a request-shape error; nothing in the client distinguishes "empty model accepted" from configuration drift, and the only contract is one observed response.
- Confidence: medium (the mechanism is stated; the failure requires a facade change not shown)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (presented as an invariant/observation, not a risk).

### F-08 — Durable stores receive compressed payloads whose originals expire in 30 minutes or 1000 entries
- Topics: AB-26.1, AB-26.2, AB-26.3, AB-26.9
- Evidence: slots `pods = true` ("compress pod writes") and `beads = true` ("compress bead payloads") at `agentbox.toml:2465-2468`; compression "rides inside" the adapter-dispatch middleware chain wrapping every store write (AB-26.9 N3); originals live only in the CCR store with `ttl_minutes = 30`, `max_entries = 1000` LRU (`agentbox.toml:2460-2461`), and `headroom_retrieve` errors on expiry or eviction (`headroom-mcp-tools.js:34`). No re-expansion on read is shown anywhere.
- Failure: a pod write (the durable personal-data store) or a work-DAG bead created today and read back after the TTL — or after 1000 newer entries evict it — yields content with irreversible CCR sentinels. Blocked beads can legitimately sit for days (AB-26.5), so the full payload is unrecoverable by design.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (the write path and TTL are stated; the read path is not shown)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no.

### F-09 — A TUI save silently resets the model router's provider, quality bar and cost ceiling to literals
- Topics: AB-28.10, AB-29.2
- Evidence: the `[model_routing.neural]` renderer emits "every OTHER key a FIXED literal (provider 'openrouter', quality_bar 0.50, cost_ceiling_usd_per_mtok 0, … `tui_sections.rs:193-200`)" and "a TUI save therefore RESETS provider/quality_bar/cost_ceiling/trajectory/assets_dir… there is no read-back-and-preserve path" — unlike the consultants preserve rule (ADR-2031, AB-28.3). That ceiling is projected into the router console as `AGENTBOX_MODEL_ROUTER_COST_CEILING_USD_PER_MTOK` (`entrypoint-unified.sh:2320-2327`) and is the only spend bound on an external billing key (`router.sh:69-75` refuses an empty key; nothing refuses an unbounded run).
- Failure: an operator hand-lowers the ceiling, later flips an unrelated boolean in the TUI, and the ceiling returns to the literal `0` (whose semantics — zero budget or no limit — the material never defines) before the next OpenRouter-backed execution: a silent removal of the sole cost control on a paid vendor path.
- Confidence: high on the reset behaviour; low on what `0` means
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT).

### F-10 — The chain's genesis does not commit its parent or containment, although the decision record claimed it did
- Topics: AB-32.2, AB-31.6
- Evidence: upstream's `buildGenesis` commits only the chain id, pegs, `genesisTime` and the signer's witness; "parent… every containment field" are NOT committed (ADR-2103:207-208); the `pin:` record that would close this is UNBUILT and must be an upstream proposal (ADR-2103:210-214). ADR-2103 D3 nonetheless states parent, headerProfile, currencyPin and cashOut are "committed as a `pin:` record in the genesis coinbase" (ADR-2103:73-74) — the amendment at :208 admits the mismatch.
- Failure: the binding of a sealed chain to its parent and its containment promises is only the committed JSON document; a document re-issued with different containment validates against the same `genesisHash`. In any future multi-party use (the federation stage), a presented document cannot be checked against the chain itself — the identity the "chain as ledger of record" design leans on is weaker than the record asserted.
- Confidence: high (facts stated); the exploit path requires multi-party signing that does not exist yet
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension; ADR-2103 amendment).

### F-11 — The XR sidecar runs a no-password VNC server with no gate, note or warning
- Topics: AB-27.13
- Evidence: "x11vnc (priority 20) mirrors :3 on VNC 5904, -nopw (`xr-runtime/supervisord.conf:41-49`)". Nothing in the topic marks it as a risk, gates it, or states its network exposure.
- Failure: anything that can reach port 5904 — other containers on the shared network, or the LAN if the port is published — gets unauthenticated view-and-control of the XR compositor session with no audit trail. The material never states the publish topology, so reachability is unconfirmed but the unauthenticated server itself is unconditional.
- Confidence: medium (the `-nopw` server is stated; network reachability is not)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no.

### F-12 — A `NOPASSWD: ALL` sudo grant is baked into the shipped image and is inert only by two independent accidents
- Topics: AB-36.7, AB-36.2
- Evidence: built with role_isolation off (the shipping state, `agentbox.toml:2503`), devuser stays in `wheel` (`flake.nix:4045`) with a `NOPASSWD: ALL` drop-in (`bake-devuser-privilege.sh:44-47`, called at `flake.nix:4059`). "In the shipped image the grant is inert only because `no-new-privileges` is set (`docker-compose.yml:129`) and no setuid sudo ships" (`flake.nix:3812-3816`); check (f) probes `sudo -n true` (`role-isolation-rehearsal.sh:591-592`); "Reverting either guard on a flag-off build turns devuser back into root, past every role boundary in this topic."
- Failure: running the image under any orchestrator or compose override that drops `no-new-privileges`, or adding any setuid sudo, instantly converts every agent process into container root with the baked grant waiting. The related trap — the flag was designed boot-class but is catalogued rebuild-class (`system-manifest.js:97-98`) — means a flag flip without rebuild records `degraded:devuser-sudo` rather than failing.
- Confidence: high on the baked grant; the trigger requires a configuration change
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension).

### F-13 — The scaffold opt-out fails toward grounding: anything but a literal boolean `false` re-enables it, and the failure mode has a recorded incident
- Topics: AB-24.11, AB-24.6, AB-24.10
- Evidence: "absence of the key, or any value other than the boolean false, leaves the scaffold ON (ADR-139:31)". The dangerous direction is the one with precedent: on 2026-09-09 "a packet about a test script scored the blockchain class Node at 42 and was served from the corpus in 40 ms with zero completion tokens" (ADR-139:13-16) — a confident wrong answer returned as a success, which AB-24.11 marks as Debt.
- Failure: any caller that sends `"false"` as a string, varies casing, or loses the field gets ontology grounding applied to a subject the corpus does not cover — a wrong answer with `corpus_backed` labels, not an error. Every non-crate consumer of the facade (the condense shell script, the email gateway's `REASONER_BASE_URL`, deepsec's `ai_base_url`) is exposed to exactly this default.
- Confidence: high on the rule; medium on which consumers ever set the field
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — the 2026-09-09 incident is marked Debt; the fail-toward-grounding default itself is presented as specification, not as a defect.

### F-14 — The ontology monitor sends session content to a vendor in its default (dryrun) mode, and no accepted egress record covers it
- Topics: AB-25.8, AB-24.14
- Evidence: once `AGENTBOX_ONTOLOGY_MONITOR=1` and a Z.AI key exist, the detached child gathers "the session's concept-bearing work" and makes "ONE review call" to the "Z.AI GLM worker"; the dryrun/publish switch gates only relay egress of the signed proposal (`ontology-monitor.cjs:195-204` vs `:213-241`), not the vendor call. The egress register's only Z.AI row is `zai-night`, marked `no-accepted-record` (`config/egress-policy.json:346-361`); no row shown accepts this session-content path.
- Failure: an operator enables the monitor believing dryrun is local-only; every session's concept-bearing work leaves to a third party with no acceptance record, and the local proposals ledger looks entirely normal because the egress happens before the mode switch matters.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (the vendor call is stated; the absence of an accepting register row is inferred from the rows the material shows)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no.

### F-15 — The compliance surface systematically lags the built system, and the one checker shown cannot see the lag
- Topics: AB-24.1, AB-24.7, AB-24.15, AB-30.2, AB-34.5, AB-28.8, AB-27.11, AB-28.1
- Evidence (each mismatch individually marked; the pattern is not): GOVERNANCE-capabilities still describes a scaffolded `slug="loom"` seed and a raw `:8085` path (`GOVERNANCE-capabilities.md:312-314`) and names Qwen3.8-27B as current (`:316`) though `loom_model` is empty since `878f23311`; it never mentions the live boot reconcile or the retired HP agentbox (`:309-311`) although ADR-2140 is accepted/complete/live. `docs/user/quickstart.md:75-84` still teaches the removed python wizard while the launcher exits 1 (`start-agentbox.sh:444-447`). Three sidechain places still say GitHub Pages and the README says the txbt4 table ships `enabled = false` while the manifest sets it true (`config/sidechain/README.md:188` vs `agentbox.toml:1934`). The default hub roster still names the deleted precedent-bridge (`flake.nix:231-234`). The catalogue advertises `live` isaac-sim and voice-console capabilities whose ADRs are proposed/inactive (`system-manifest.js:178-180` vs ADR-2149:5-7; `agentbox.toml:2206`/`:2230` vs ADR-2151:5-7 — the voicelink hooks are already written per `stacks.rs:311-320`). The only citation checker shown is bounds-only (`render-egress-register.js:110-119`).
- Failure: an operator or auditor working from the governing documents misconfigures the estate (points a consumer at the raw :8085 port, expects a pinned model, follows the removed wizard) or signs off compliance against text describing a system that no longer exists; meanwhile capabilities their own records have not accepted are already advertised as live.
- Confidence: high (each mismatch is stated); the systemic reading is mine
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes, individually (Drift/Tension/Open markers); the aggregate pattern and its check-gap are unmarked.

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Runtime flag and receipt state at takeover.** Everything here is a snapshot at stamped commits: `role_isolation` false with one STAGED rehearsal receipt, sidechain gates on, monitor off. Whether a newer PASS receipt, a flag flip, or a host socket chmod has happened since is not visible, and most security findings above change severity on it.
2. **Actual network exposure.** The material binds many services to `0.0.0.0` or leaves them unauthenticated (VNC 5904 `-nopw`, jupyter 8888, code-server 8080, voice 8443/8444, isaac 9890, openmed 9093, the LAN facade 8084), but never shows which ports are published past the compose network, or the host firewall. F-11 and the bridge-reachability notes cannot be sized without this.
3. **The out-of-repo halves of every contract.** The loom facade implementation, the sidestr-rs crates (including the mirror-trust and level-2 codec logic), the ruflo router internals, and the Unmute stack are cited as external and unverifiable from here — the deployment contracts' other sides are taken on faith.
4. **Backup, snapshot and key-escrow story.** Nothing shows backups for the `agentbox-secrets` volume (sovereign and both chain signer keys — losing it loses the identities and the chains), `blocks.dat` host binds, the beads sqlite store, RuVector namespaces or pods. Single-signer chains with a single key file and no shown escrow are a single point of failure I cannot confirm or rule out.
5. **Current test and CI status.** Contract specs, golden tests, the genesis test and the egress/env inventory gates are cited, but no pass/fail state at HEAD is shown — so I cannot tell which of the marked drifts are already failing a gate somewhere and being ignored, versus unguarded.

## agentbox#3 / premortem

# Post-mortem — custody, data-egress and grounding failures at the Loom/agentbox estate

Basis: the verified diagram set AB-24 through AB-36 at the stamps recorded in each topic (`agentbox 0b0f7918961e629280faf86f88227535abf514b5` and siblings). Every claim below is stated or directly implied by that material; where I reason past it, the Confidence line says "inferred".

---

### R-1 — Role isolation never activated: every process in the container can still read every key, including the sovereign identity and spend keys
- Topics: AB-36 (all sections), AB-34.3, AB-34.6
- Chain of events:
  1. `[security].role_isolation` ships **false** (`agentbox.toml:2503`); activation requires the owner's rebuild plus a flag-on PASS rehearsal receipt, and the only committed receipt is **STAGED with the flag false** (`docs/estate-closeout/x1-rehearsal-20261003T090708Z.json:4`, `:8`) — and that receipt "predates the rule it would now fail" (AB-36.7 Drift).
  2. With the flag off, "every long-running program ran as devuser (uid 1000), so a 0600 key file protected nothing from a co-resident agent" (AB-36, For developers). The chain programs "run as devuser anyway" (AB-34.6).
  3. A tool an agent installs or updates (an MCP server, a SessionEnd hook spawning a detached child — AB-25.8, or a skill binary) runs as devuser and reads the sovereign Nostr key, the JunkieJarvis forum house key, the sidestr signer keys and the faucet treasury key from their at-rest paths.
  4. The treasury key's legacy workspace twin is "never deleted and stay[s] devuser-readable by design until a manual purge" (AB-36.3; ADR-2122:225-228 via AB-34.3) — a spend-capable key on the host-visible bind **in both modes**.
  5. The same environment carries `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY`, `BRIDGE_TOKEN`, provider keys and "the ssh-agent socket path that grants use of every key the agent holds" (AB-36.6 Debt), so the tool can also drive the management API as admin and authenticate over ssh.
  6. The identity port's receipts never see any of this — the attacker bypasses the port by reading the files directly.
- Evidence: as cited above; AB-36 business section: "One mistaken or compromised tool could therefore impersonate the organisation or move value. … It is switched off today." Adjacent bypasses that survive even a flag-on flip: the host Docker socket stays world-accessible with detection-not-gating (AB-36.7 Open R2), and the `NOPASSWD: ALL` drop-in is baked on flag-off builds (AB-36.7 Tension).
- Warning signs visible today: flag false at the verified stamp; only a STAGED receipt; key twins never purged; 37 ambient credential names including the admin key; the rehearsal receipt failing its own current rule.
- Confidence: high (the exposure is documented as the present state; the triggering tool is inferred)
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Client-derived corpus and session content leave to an external vendor by default, with no accepted egress record
- Topics: AB-24.7, AB-24.9, AB-24.14, AB-25.8
- Chain of events:
  1. The dream engine's default provider is Z.AI, a vendor; `loom` is the opt-in (`agentbox.toml:2582`, `config.rs:184` — AB-24.7).
  2. A nightly run therefore sends prompts built from estate memory/corpus to the vendor. The egress register itself records this row with "marker no-accepted-record, nothing accepts the corpus leaving" (`config/egress-policy.json:346-361`, via AB-24.14).
  3. Separately, the SessionEnd ontology monitor sends "the session's concept-bearing work" to "Z.AI GLM worker" in one review call whenever its gates pass (AB-25.8) — session content, i.e. client work product, leaving by a second path.
  4. The register that should catch this has rotted: its loom-door and zai-night rows cite manifest/flake lines that now point at unrelated keys, and its renderer "--check only tests that a cited line falls inside the file" (`scripts/ci/render-egress-register.js:110-119`) — so no review artifact alerts.
  5. A client audit or a vendor-side discovery finds their material outside the estate; the operator cannot show an acceptance record because none exists.
- Evidence: as above; AB-24.9 N1 confirms the Z.AI default is "by deliberate choice" and only "names the egress posture" rather than accepting it. The public-only privacy gate exists on the model-router console (AB-29.6) but not on the dream engine default.
- Warning signs visible today: the register's own `no-accepted-record` marker; the rotted citations; `ZAI_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` ambient in every devuser process (AB-36.6).
- Confidence: high (default and unaccepted marker are stated; the client-discovery step is inferred)
- Severity: high

### R-3 — A stale or empty ontology corpus keeps grounding client-facing answers, served as corpus-backed
- Topics: AB-24.8, AB-24.11, AB-24.12, AB-24.13, AB-24.15, AB-24 audit qualification
- Chain of events:
  1. The corpus door serves `visionGraph@ae913f93…` while `visionGraph@015ca2c1f` is built, promoted and verified, because the `vault` 0.1.0 baked into the image "predates the portable-records file, so it cannot produce a promotable bundle" (AB-24.13 Debt) — repair waits on an image rebuild.
  2. Nothing enforces freshness: "a generation past stale_after is STILL SERVED, honestly labelled — staleness is carried, never enforced" (`loom-domain/src/model.rs:459-462`); the boot reconcile is background, timeout 1800, fail-open (AB-24.15), and its failures "never stop a container" (AB-34/AB-24.15 N2).
  3. `/health` stays 200 through all of this — and even with a fully unloaded index, "the facade still starts and /health still returns 200" (the empty-floor trap, AB-24.8, `loom/README.md:73-76`).
  4. A non-agentic consumer — the email gateway at `http://loom:8080/v1` (AB-24.1) — asks a client question; the injection plane gives single-turn consumers "one static scaffold merge per request" (AB-24.12 note).
  5. The ADR-139 precedent shows the failure shape: grounding on an uncovered subject "is a WRONG answer, not a weak one" — served from the corpus "in 40 ms with zero completion tokens", `corpus_backed true` (AB-24.11 Debt; ADR-139:13-16).
  6. The stricter generation-verifying client is staged, not live: "the live façade was probed and still reports lexical generation 2026-08-22 versus semantic 2026-08-17, without loaded identity/embedding fields" (AB-24 audit qualification), so nothing at the door discriminates the stale serve.
  7. The client receives a confident, corpus-attributed answer about their own subject matter and acts on it.
- Evidence: as above; AB-24.15 Drift adds that the governing document "still reads as if the generation were staged by hand", so an operator following it during recovery would not check the reconcile.
- Warning signs visible today: the promoted-but-unserved generation; fail-open reconcile; carried-not-enforced staleness; the 2026-09-09 wrong-subject incident already on record; staged verification client.
- Confidence: medium (each mechanism is documented; whether the email gateway declines the scaffold is not stated — that composition is inferred)
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Spend-capable chain keys remain readable on the host-visible bind in both isolation modes
- Topics: AB-34.3, AB-36.3
- Evidence: "the migrate step copies that legacy file once and never deletes it, so a spend-capable key stays on the host-visible bind in both modes until a manual purge" (ADR-2122:225-228); "Those twins are never deleted and stay devuser-readable by design until a manual purge" (AB-36.3).
- Failure: with the flag on or off, any devuser process reads the faucet treasury key and spends outside policy; the manual purge never occurs.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-02 — The identity port exports a private key (`mirror_key`, secret true) instead of wrapping
- Topics: AB-36.5
- Evidence: the ACL grants devuser "mirror_key with secret true (identity-port-acl.json:42)"; the design specified a `mirror_wrap` operation; ADR-2101:230-234 "records it as the one key export, with a `mirror_wrap` operation as the retiring condition".
- Failure: a compromised devuser tool obtains live signing key material usable off-receipt and off-host, defeating "the identity keys never leave one dedicated signing service".
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (recorded as a tension with ADR-2101 and AB-16)

### F-03 — Host Docker socket world-accessible; the rehearsal records the mode but does not gate on it, while the ADR counts it a failure
- Topics: AB-36.7
- Evidence: "the host Docker socket inode stays world-accessible until the host chmods it … The host half records the mode for the owner without gating on it, while ADR-2122 counts `degraded:docker-socket` as a failure" (`scripts/activation/role-isolation-rehearsal.host.sh:18-19`; `docker-compose.override.yml:116-117`).
- Failure: any in-container process reaches the host Docker daemon — root on the host — voiding every container boundary including the secrets volume; the flag-on boot only records the degradation.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-04 — `NOPASSWD: ALL` sudo drop-in baked into flag-off images, inert only behind two independent guards
- Topics: AB-36.7
- Evidence: flag-off builds keep devuser in `wheel` (`flake.nix:4045`) and bake the drop-in (`config/bake-devuser-privilege.sh:44-47`, called at `flake.nix:4059`); "the grant is inert only because `no-new-privileges` is set (`docker-compose.yml:129`) and no setuid sudo ships … Reverting either guard on a flag-off build turns devuser back into root, past every role boundary".
- Failure: a compose edit drops `no-new-privileges` (or an image variant ships setuid sudo) and every agent silently gains passwordless root in the container; check (f) probes this only during rehearsals.
- Confidence: high (grant is baked; the reverting step is conditional)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-05 — Ambient agent environment retains admin, vendor and agent-forwarding credentials
- Topics: AB-36.6
- Evidence: "37 DEVUSER_CLASS names, among them provider API keys, `BRIDGE_TOKEN`, `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY`, the System One vendor keys … and the ssh-agent socket path that grants use of every key the agent holds, remain in the agent environment by exception Q8" (`env-classes.json:90`, `:92`, `:195`–`:211`).
- Failure: any tool exfiltrates the management admin key or authenticates via the ssh-agent to hosts — including the HP the reconcile reaches "over ssh on the 25G rail" (AB-24.15).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-06 — The prompt-egress register's citations have rotted and its renderer only checks bounds
- Topics: AB-24.14
- Evidence: register stamped `0919dc39a` (`egress-policy.json:51`); its rows cite manifest lines 2179/2163 and flake 3438 which "at this revision" are a session seed's `worktree = true`, a `tool = "claude"` key and an empty line; `llm_provider` is now `agentbox.toml:2582`; "the renderer's `--check` only tests that a cited line falls inside the file" (`render-egress-register.js:110-119`).
- Failure: egress review passes while pointing at unrelated lines; moved or new egress paths escape review; during an incident the register cannot be trusted as evidence.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-07 — The Loom empty-floor trap: `/health` 200 with the lexical index unloaded
- Topics: AB-24.8
- Evidence: "The facade still starts and /health still returns 200, but the log reads 'lexical index NOT loaded … empty floor'. That is a STAGING bug, not a dead container — check the mount before you check the process" (`loom/README.md:73-76`).
- Failure: a mis-mounted `loom-data` after a host change leaves retrieval returning nothing while all healthchecks stay green; consumers answer without grounding and nothing alarms.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (diagram note, not a formal marker)

### F-08 — Stale corpus served indefinitely: reconcile fail-open plus a baked `vault` that cannot promote
- Topics: AB-24.13, AB-24.15
- Evidence: staleness "carried, never enforced" (`loom-domain/src/model.rs:459-462`); reconcile failures "recorded in the receipt and ex[it] 0 … never stop a container" (`hp-loom-reconcile.sh:13-15`); boot Phase 5d "background, timeout 1800, fail-open" (`entrypoint-unified.sh:1209-1219`); the baked `vault` 0.1.0 "cannot produce a promotable bundle" while the `vaultSrc` pin "reaches a running `vault` only at the next image rebuild" (`flake.nix:31-32`).
- Failure: the door serves a months-old corpus after reconcile silently fails; every grounded consumer degrades together with no signal.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-09 — The generation-verifying retrieval client is staged, not live; the live facade reports no identity fields

A coordinated bundle/server rollout is required before activating the stricter client"; the design keys the cache on a verified generation discriminator (`ontology-retrieval.js:82` via AB-24.4).

## dream-engine / critical

### F-01 — The darwin sandbox "refusal" is evidenced only by a report-only code path
- Topics: DE-01.2 (K4 and "Invariant (darwin)")
- Evidence: The material asserts "darwin entrypoints are still refused by evaluator readiness as a safety check" and K4 says "refused at admission, a read-only check" — both citing `../dream-engine/crates/dream-core/src/readiness.rs:228-232`; the same citation says "`assess` only appends an `Unusable::DarwinSandboxMissing` problem for the report and changes nothing". No code that converts the appended problem into an actual refusal is shown; the only cited mechanism mutates nothing. It is also the only sandbox gate the material mentions, and it names one platform.
- Failure: On a macOS evaluator started without a sandbox flag, readiness records a problem and the run proceeds; candidate code compiled and run by dream-core's compile/runner executes unsandboxed overnight; nothing fails until after the damage.
- Confidence: medium — a refusal may live in an unstated consumer of the report; the tension between "refused" and "changes nothing" is in the material itself.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (K4 lists the check as an Open limit but frames it as an enforced refusal; the enforcement gap is not marked)

### F-02 — The licence fence's only shown enforcement bans two crate names, not licences
- Topics: DE-01.1 (DENY and FENCE nodes)
- Evidence: The fence invariant is "a permissive crate must never depend on an AGPL crate" (`../dream-engine/docs/adr/ADR-DE-002-licence-and-provenance.md:34`), but the enforcement cited bans exactly two crates, `nostr-bbs-core` and `sqlx` (`../dream-engine/deny.toml:29-30`). No licence allowlist appears anywhere in the material.
- Failure: A contributor adds any other AGPL dependency — or a renamed fork of nostr-bbs-core — cargo-deny passes, and MIT OR Apache-2.0 crates are published to crates.io containing AGPL-derived code; every downstream consumer inherits a licence breach found only by audit.
- Confidence: medium — deny.toml beyond lines 29–30 is not shown; the gap is between the fence claim and the enforcement evidenced.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — Publish outcomes are at-most-once and never reconciled after a crash
- Topics: DE-01.2 (K1, LIMITS subgraph)
- Evidence: "publish outcomes are not reconciled after a crash, at-most-once" (`../dream-engine/docs/adr/ADR-DE-007-a-night-is-identified-by-its-directory.md:34`).
- Failure: The daemon crashes mid-publish. Either the outcome is lost — the night's result silently absent from the ledger forever — or a draft already accepted by the forge exists with no local record, an untracked artifact. Restart reconciles neither, so both states persist undetected.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, K1)

### F-04 — Nothing attests which engine version the production daemon runs; only the 0.1.0-era activation is recorded
- Topics: DE-01.2 (LIVE node, "Resolved (activation)")
- Evidence: LIVE records image `9fcd1eef9` activated 2026-10-16 (`../project/agentbox/docs/adr/ADR-2141-extract-the-dream-engine-core-into-a-standalone-permissive-repo.md:47`); the same paragraph concedes "the later releases (0.2.0 to 0.4.0) are adopted in `Cargo.toml` and their image activation is not recorded in the ADR", while the adapter pins `dream-core =0.4.0` (`../project/agentbox/services/dream-engine/Cargo.toml:26-27`).
- Failure: The running daemon may predate ADR-DE-008 (requested sources honoured first, 0.2.0), ADR-DE-009 (candidate formatted before the gate, 0.3.0) and screening (0.4.0). Operators debug against documented behaviour the image does not have — e.g. requested-source ordering silently absent — and no record exists to resolve the doubt during an incident.
- Confidence: high (that the attestation is absent; the staleness itself is unknown)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (the missing activation record is stated in passing, unflagged)

### F-05 — The verification stamp contradicts itself and the remote head has moved past it
- Topics: DE-01 (frontmatter and re-check paragraph)
- Evidence: The body says the 2026-10-29 re-check was made "against this stamp (`61581d3`)" and that `git ls-remote origin main` reads 59a3e21, "which is not the stamped head" — yet the frontmatter's `verified_commit` is `dream-engine: 59a3e215…`. Three heads appear across the document (5578a24 on 10-16, 61581d3 stamp, 59a3e21 remote).
- Failure: The estate cannot say which commit the cited lines (`Cargo.toml:11`, `readiness.rs:228-232`, `engine.rs:1774`) describe. Commits after the stamp — including whatever produced the published 0.4.0 — are unverified by this record, while the frontmatter asserts verification of a commit the body says was not the stamp.
- Confidence: high (the contradiction is explicit)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (stated as fact, not flagged)

### F-06 — Roster, manifest generator and gate are locked into the stale "imported fork" provenance

- Evidence: Roster row "provenance imported, role fork of ruvnet/dream-machine" (`scripts/estate-health/roster.json:21`); the generator seeds the same row (`scripts/generate-release-manifest.sh:102`); the gate "asserts exactly two imported repositories" (`tests/gates/release-manifest.test.sh:68-69`) — against a repository the material elsewhere describes as first-party, clean-room (`../dream-engine/Cargo.toml:3,6`).

- Severity: medium

### F-08 — The forge merge step has never run against real GitHub, and policy applies only in persist::publish
- Topics: DE-01.2 (K2)
- Evidence: "the automerge forge step was not exercised against real GitHub, and only persist::publish applies the policy" (`../dream-engine/README.md:130-135`).
- Failure: The merge path is first exercised in production (host migration, config change); untested GitHub API behaviour yields partial merges, wrong drafts merged, or silent no-ops, with no test history to consult.

- Severity: medium

### F-09 — Workspace releases are not atomic: the published CLI is two minor versions behind the libraries

- Evidence: Workspace version is 0.4.0 (`../dream-engine/Cargo.toml:11`) while crates.io serves `dream-cli` 0.2.0 and both leaf crates 0.1.0; the material itself notes "the shell crate is not released with the library crates".
- Failure: Anyone installing the `dream` binary from crates.io gets 0.2.0-era behaviour without the 0.3.0 gate-formatting change or 0.4.0 screening, so CLI gate verdicts diverge from library consumers'. Inferred: the published CLI's own dependency requirements are not shown.

### F-10 — The only browser journey for this area probes the archived fork's Pages site; the new repository has nothing to probe
- Topics: DE-01.2 (STALE R4)

### F-12 — The 0.4.0 pin landed on adapter main with no record of any kind
- Topics: DE-01.2 (M node, "Resolved (activation)")
- Evidence: The only merged adapter change recorded is PR 28 (aa9b094c3, 2026-10-16, the extraction-era pin); the ADR's last re-verification stops at `=0.3.0` (`ADR-2141:55`); activation records stop at the 10-16 image; yet `Cargo.toml:26-27` reads `=0.4.0`.

### F-13 — Persistence is filesystem-and-directory based with no shown concurrency control: safe for one writer, unsafe for two
- Topics: DE-01.1 (DENY node, DC node), DE-01.2 (K1, K3)
- Evidence: Database crates are banned from the engine (`../dream-engine/deny.toml:29-30`); dream-core carries `ledger`, `persist` and run-state modules (`../dream-engine/crates/dream-core/Cargo.toml:17-18`); "a night is identified by its directory" (ADR-DE-007); the material speaks of a "first host", implying others exist, and `dream-cli` exists to run the same engine.
- Failure: A second writer — the CLI against the same night directory, or a second host — interleaves with the daemon; with directory-identity and no reconciliation (K1), updates clobber each other or a night is processed twice, with no database constraint to catch it.
- Confidence: low, inferred — the ban and the identity rule are stated; no locking story appears

### F-14 — "The agentbox GitHub Actions run again" implies an undated CI outage that may span the entire 0.2.0→0.4.0 adoption window
- Topics: DE-01.2 ("Resolved (CI)")
- Evidence: The resolution says the workflows "run again … green on 2026-10-29" — "again" concedes they had stopped; no stoppage dates are given; the only undated window in the material (2026-10-16→10-29) is exactly when releases 0.2.0–0.4.0 were published and adopted in `Cargo.toml`.
- Failure: The dependency bumps to 0.3.0/0.4.0 were merged while adapter CI was down; the adapter's first tested run with 0.4.0 is production itself.
- Confidence: low, inferred — the outage's timing relative to the bumps is not shown
