### F-15 — The dream-gate single-version invariant is maintained by hand and fails as a confusing build break
- Topics: DE-01.1 (PIN node)
- Evidence: "both pins stay on one dream-gate release, dream-core 0.4.0 needs dream-gate 0.3.0, and a mismatch links two copies of dream-gate whose types do not unify" (`../project/agentbox/services/dream-engine/Cargo.toml:22-27`).
- Failure: An urgent single-crate bump — say a security fix in dream-gate that dream-core has not been re-pinned for — breaks the adapter build with cross-crate type-unification errors; the patch is delayed while both pins and the lockfile are reconciled by hand under pressure.
- Confidence: medium — the coupling is stated; the scenario assumes the two pins can drift independently, which per-crate exact pins permit
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no (labelled an invariant, not a problem)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. The admission code that supposedly turns `Unusable::DarwinSandboxMissing` into a refusal — the caller of `readiness.rs:228-232` — and whether any sandbox requirement exists at all for non-darwin evaluators.
2. The full `deny.toml`: whether a licence allowlist exists beneath the two named crate bans, which decides whether F-02 is a real gap.
3. Deployment or registry records mapping image digests (9fcd1eef9 and any successor) to crate versions — i.e. whether the daemon was rebuilt after the 2026-10-16 activation.
4. The published `dream-cli` 0.2.0 manifest (which dream-core/dream-gate it actually resolves) and whether the adapter enables dream-core 0.4.0's opt-in screening (`../dream-engine/crates/dream-core/src/lib.rs:57`).
5. Credential scope and isolation for the autonomous path: what the forge publish token can do, who or what can merge the accumulated drafts, and how compiled candidates are contained when run via the adapter's `SshRunner` on remote hosts.

## dream-engine / premortem

# Post-mortem — dream-engine extraction estate

Basis: the estate distillation DE-01 and the citations it carries; no code beyond the cited lines was available to the investigator. Every chain that goes beyond a stated fact is marked inferred.

---

### R-1 — A crash between draft publication and the ledger write loses the publish outcome permanently; ledger and forge diverge with no reconciliation and no merge
- Topics: DE-01.2, AB-23
- Chain of events: 1) The supervised engine runs a night — live since the 2026-10-16 activation, including the finished-night rule (ADR-2141:47). 2) The adapter publishes the night's result through `Forge::publish_draft` (`../project/agentbox/services/dream-engine/src/engine.rs:1774`). 3) The daemon crashes or is restarted after the forge accepts the draft but before the publish outcome reaches the ledger. 4) Publish outcomes are not reconciled after a crash and publishing is at-most-once (`../dream-engine/docs/adr/ADR-DE-007-a-night-is-identified-by-its-directory.md:34`), so nothing retries and nothing discovers the orphan draft. 5) The finished-night rule now evaluates a night whose forge state and ledger state disagree. 6) Nothing ever merges the orphan draft either: the `publish_draft` path never merges whatever `autoMerge` says (ADR-DE-006:44-48), and the only path that applies the merge policy is `persist::publish` (`../dream-engine/README.md:130-135`). 7) The night's work exists only as an untracked draft, or a re-run emits a second divergent draft; the divergence is permanent and client-visible.
- Evidence: ADR-DE-007:34 states the limit verbatim; the engine is live under supervision with the finished-night rule (ADR-2141:47); the adapter's publish path is `Forge::publish_draft` (engine.rs:1774); that path never merges (ADR-DE-006:44-48); only `persist::publish` applies the policy (README:130-135).
- Warning signs visible today: the limit is recorded as OPEN on a component already activated in production; a supervised daemon implies restarts are expected; the later releases 0.2.0–0.4.0 were adopted into `Cargo.toml` with no recorded activation, so upgrade/restart events around them are untracked.
- Confidence: high that the defect exists (stated); the crash-to-divergence chain is inferred from the stated pieces.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — The estate cannot establish which commit is head or which image is running: three head claims disagree and activation records stop at 0.1.0
- Topics: DE-01.2, AB-23
- Chain of events: 1) On 2026-10-16 the record states `main` at 5578a24 with the five crates at 0.1.0. 2) By 2026-10-29 crates.io reads dream-core 0.4.0 and dream-gate 0.3.0 — three releases published. 3) That re-check was performed "against this stamp (`61581d3`)", yet `git ls-remote origin main` reads `59a3e21`, "which is not the stamped head"; the front-matter `verified_commit` is `59a3e215…`, whose short prefix equals the remote reading — so the document carries three different head claims (5578a24, 59a3e215, 61581d3) and exactly one matches the remote. 4) The adapter consumes the published registry artifacts by exact pin with lockfile checksums (`../project/agentbox/services/dream-engine/Cargo.toml:26-27`), so what runs is what was published, while the relationship between those artifacts and any recorded commit is unestablished. 5) The only recorded image activation is the 0.1.0-era image (source 9fcd1eef9, ADR-2141:47); the record itself states the 0.2.0–0.4.0 adoptions have no recorded activation. 6) During an incident, responders using this file's path:line citations inspect a source state that may not correspond to the deployed crates and cannot tell which image is live; a fix or rollback is prepared against the wrong state, or unaudited code is confirmed as audited.
- Evidence: the re-check paragraph (three head claims, crates.io `max_version` readings, "not the stamped head"); front-matter `verified_commit: {dream-engine: 59a3e215…}`; `Cargo.toml:26-27`; ADR-2141:47; the sentence "the later releases (0.2.0 to 0.4.0) are adopted in `Cargo.toml` and their image activation is not recorded in the ADR".
- Warning signs visible today: the re-check recorded the head mismatch as a neutral observation, not as a drift entry; three releases shipped within thirteen days of extraction with no activation trail.
- Confidence: high that the stamp disagreement and the missing activation records are facts (stated); inferred that the deployed artifacts diverge from the recorded head — the material does not establish which stamp, if any, matches the publishing commits.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — `autoMerge` is a documented option that is inert on the only path the adapter uses, and the merge policy lives in exactly one never-exercised path
- Topics: DE-01.2, AB-23, AB-23.6
- Chain of events: 1) `autoMerge` exists as an option (ADR-DE-006). 2) The adapter publishes through `Forge::publish_draft` (engine.rs:1774). 3) That path never merges, "whatever `autoMerge` says" (ADR-DE-006:44-48). 4) The only path that applies the merge policy is `persist::publish`, and the automerge forge step has never been exercised against real GitHub (README:130-135). 5) Drafts accumulate unmerged; delivery silently degrades to out-of-band human merges that bypass the policy only `persist::publish` would apply; combined with R-1, any draft orphaned by a crash is never merged and never re-emitted.
- Evidence: engine.rs:1774; ADR-DE-006:44-48; README:130-135; the Drift paragraph "the permissive repository's option exists but the adapter publishes through `Forge::publish_draft`"; cross-references ES-05 and AB-23.6.
- Warning signs visible today: the configuration surface exposes a knob documented to do nothing on the live path; the policy-applying path has zero real-forge coverage.
- Confidence: high that the knob is inert on the adapter path (stated three times); the stall and out-of-band-merge scenario is inferred.
- Severity: medium

---

### F-01 — The published `dream-cli` is three minor versions behind the engine it shells
- Topics: DE-01.2
- Evidence: the workspace version is 0.4.0 (`../dream-engine/Cargo.toml:11`) and the record states "the shell crate is not released with the library crates"; crates.io `max_version` reads dream-cli 0.2.0. Release 0.3.0 changed gate ordering — a candidate is formatted before the gate judges it (ADR-DE-009) — so the published CLI predates that change.
- Failure: anyone installing dream-cli from the registry gets 0.2.0-era behaviour — candidates judged before formatting, no 0.4.0 screening — and verdicts reproduced with the CLI disagree with the supervised engine on the same candidate.
- Confidence: high (version facts stated; the usage scenario is inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — ADR-2141 contradicts the adapter's pins twice, and both of its readings are wrong
- Topics: DE-01.2, AB-23
- Evidence: the ADR's Pinning bullet names `=0.2.0` for both crates (ADR-2141:31), its last re-verification adopts `=0.3.0` for both (ADR-2141:55), while the adapter pins dream-core =0.4.0 and dream-gate =0.3.0 (`../project/agentbox/services/dream-engine/Cargo.toml:26-27`).
- Failure: during response an operator follows the ADR: rolling both to `=0.3.0` silently drops the 0.4.0 hypothesis screening (`../dream-engine/crates/dream-core/src/lib.rs:57`); mixing readings (core =0.4.0 with gate =0.2.0) violates the one-dream-gate-release invariant and "links two copies of dream-gate whose types do not unify" (PIN node, `services/dream-engine/Cargo.toml:22-27`).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-03 — Roster, generator and gate hard-code a false provenance and must change in lockstep, so the false value keeps shipping
- Topics: DE-01.2
- Evidence: the roster row says provenance imported, role fork of ruvnet/dream-machine (`scripts/estate-health/roster.json:21`); the generator seeds the same row (`scripts/generate-release-manifest.sh:102`); the gate asserts exactly two imported repositories (`tests/gates/release-manifest.test.sh:68-69`); the record says row, generator and test "must move in one change".
- Failure: release manifests continue to attribute first-party clean-room code ("built clean-room", `../dream-engine/Cargo.toml:3,6`) to a third-party TypeScript fork; a client audit or licence scan reading the manifest concludes the engine is derived code; any piecemeal correction turns the gate red and blocks releases.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-04 — Estate monitoring probes the archived fork's Pages site; the new repository has no probe
- Topics: DE-01.2
- Evidence: the browser journey probes the fork's Pages site (`scripts/estate-browser-journeys.mjs:24`); the fork was renamed and archived (DE-01.2, P1) and "the new repository has no such page".
- Failure: the journey reports either perpetual failure (which is trained to be ignored) or success against an archived page; availability or content problems in DreamLab-AI/dream-engine itself are invisible to estate journeys.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-05 — Exact registry pins cannot take up compatible patches, and the PIN invariant forbids moving one pin alone
- Topics: DE-01.1, AB-23
- Evidence: both dependencies are "registry dependency, exact pin" (`services/dream-engine/Cargo.toml:26-27`); the PIN invariant requires both pins to stay on one dream-gate release (`services/dream-engine/Cargo.toml:22-27`).
- Failure: a fix shipped as dream-gate 0.3.1 cannot be taken by a lockfile refresh; uptake requires a coordinated source change to both pins plus rebuild and reactivation — the slowest path at the moment time is shortest.
- Confidence: inferred (exact-pin semantics are standard; the material states "exact pin" but no uptake policy)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — The licence fence's cited mechanism is a two-name ban list, not a licence assertion
- Topics: DE-01.1
- Evidence: the fence invariant is "a permissive crate must never depend on an AGPL crate" (ADR-DE-002:34); the cited enforcement bans exactly nostr-bbs-core and sqlx by name (`../dream-engine/deny.toml:29-30`).
- Failure: any AGPL dependency other than the two banned names — a rename, a fork, a new crate — passes the cited checks and crosses the fence into the permissive workspace; the contamination ships under `MIT OR Apache-2.0` (`../dream-engine/Cargo.toml:8`).
- Confidence: inferred — only the crate bans are cited; a licence allow-list may exist in deny.toml outside the cited lines
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — The darwin sandbox refusal is shown only as a report annotation; the enforcement site is not shown
- Topics: DE-01.2
- Evidence: the limit says darwin evaluators are "refused at admission, a read-only check" and that `assess` "only appends an `Unusable::DarwinSandboxMissing` problem for the report and changes nothing" (`../dream-engine/crates/dream-core/src/readiness.rs:228-232`).
- Failure: if any consumer path selects evaluators without acting on the appended problem — or a future path forgets — an unsandboxed darwin evaluator runs candidate compilation and evaluation (dream-core carries compile, testfmt and screen modules) with host-level effect.
- Confidence: low, inferred — the material asserts refusal at admission, but the only cited code is the annotation
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, K4)

### F-08 — A night is identified by its directory while more than one host is implied, and no reconciliation is cross-host
- Topics: DE-01.2
- Evidence: ADR-DE-007 makes the directory the night's identity (cited at :34 of the same file for the publish limit); ADR-DE-006:44-48 speaks of "the first host" publishing through publish_draft, implying at least a second; the only reconciliation limit stated (ADR-DE-007:34) concerns crashes, not hosts.
- Failure: two hosts writing nights under the same directory name alias into one identity — cross-host double-apply or a corrupted ledger; a moved or renamed directory orphans a night's history; nothing reconciles either case.
- Confidence: inferred (multi-host is implied by "the first host", not asserted as architecture)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — The 0.4.0 safety screen is opt-in, and adopting the version does not show it is enabled
- Topics: DE-01.2
- Evidence: "0.4.0 adds opt-in hypothesis screening in dream-core (`../dream-engine/crates/dream-core/src/lib.rs:57`)"; the adapter's adoption evidence is the version pin only (`services/dream-engine/Cargo.toml:26-27`); no cited source shows the screen turned on.
- Failure: the supervised engine runs dream-core 0.4.0 with the screen off; a candidate the screening would have rejected reaches the gate and the forge; the operator believes the screen is active because the version is current.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (the material does not state the adapter's configuration)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — The one-dream-gate-release rule is enforced by prose only; nothing mechanical couples the two pins
- Topics: DE-01.1
- Evidence: the PIN invariant states "a mismatch links two copies of dream-gate whose types do not unify" and cites the pins' location (`services/dream-engine/Cargo.toml:22-27`); no test or lint enforcing the coupling is cited.
- Failure: a partial bump lands in a single edit (core moved, gate unchanged, or the reverse); the build fails late with type errors across two dream-gate copies, or — where types do not cross a boundary — two divergent gate implementations coexist and verdicts or receipts from one are consumed by the other.
- Confidence: medium, inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — The verification method cannot see yanks and records head mismatches without classifying them
- Topics: DE-01.2
- Evidence: the re-check reads crates.io `max_version` for the published versions and records `git ls-remote` reading `59a3e21` as "not the stamped head" as a plain observation; the document also carries two different dream-engine stamps (front matter `59a3e215…`, body `61581d3`).
- Failure: a yanked 0.4.0 still reads as `max_version` 0.4.0, so "published at 0.4.0" can hold in the record while the registry no longer serves it; a rolled-back or force-pushed `main` is recorded but never escalated, so later readers trust citations against a head that no longer exists.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (the blind spots are properties of the named APIs, not stated in the material)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — The gate's judging leaves have never been republished across three engine releases
- Topics: DE-01.1, DE-01.2
- Evidence: pairwise-gate and failure-fingerprint remain at 0.1.0 on crates.io while the workspace shipped 0.2.0, 0.3.0 and 0.4.0 (re-check paragraph); dream-gate depends on both leaves (`../dream-engine/crates/dream-gate/Cargo.toml:23-24`, DAG).
- Failure: if a defect is found in pairwise judging or failure fingerprinting, the demonstrated release history — three parent releases, zero leaf releases — means consumers of published dream-gate and dream-core keep the defective leaf until a leaf release process that has never been exercised is run under pressure.
- Confidence: low, inferred (the leaves may simply be unchanged)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

## dreamlab-ai-website / critical

### F-01 — One unsplit key is both forum primary-admin and external governance publisher, and rotating it requires a five-location manual change including a console-only secret
- Topics: DW-03.4, DW-03.3, DW-01.8, DW-06.6 (I9)
- Evidence: `dreamlab.toml:39-72` comment records that `visionclaw-server` (11ed6422...663c) is BOTH primary admin AND `[governance].agent_pubkeys` publisher; the split is "staged, not applied" (`admin-key-split-runbook.md`); the auth-worker `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` CF secret "cannot be rotated from this repo or CI"; `deploy.yml:62-73` bakes `VITE_ADMIN_PUBKEY` — the operator's current working admin key — into the client as INTERIM, making the split a five-location atomic change (TOML, relay vars, search vars, CF secret, client mirror).
- Failure: compromise of the external VisionClaw server yields full forum admin (ban/mute/reset-db/whitelist). The response requires five coordinated edits, one of which is a Cloudflare-console-only step outside CI, plus re-pointing the client-baked key or signups route to a dead key. During the window the attacker retains admin.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open/staged, DW-03.4; INTERIM noted at `deploy.yml:62-73`)

### F-02 — A plaintext admin private key remains in git history, unrotated
- Topics: DW-07.2
- Evidence: three root seeders embedded `ADMIN_PRIVKEY_HEX` from commit `c0bcece` (`CLOSEOUT-SECURITY-AUDIT.md:113-114,300`); deleted by `3baf2fa4` (2026-10-07) but "key not rotated, remains in history" (`CLOSEOUT-SECURITY-AUDIT.md:116`); "whether it is live cannot be decided from this repo". The repo is deployed via GitHub Pages and mirrored to a second org (`deploy.yml:428-439`).
- Failure: anyone with a clone of the history extracts an admin private key; if it is still valid in the static set, D1, or any worker env, that is a standing full-admin compromise with no rotation planned.
- Confidence: medium (key liveness and repo visibility are unknown)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, DW-07.2)

### F-03 — The repo cannot tell you what production runs: kit pin and third-party egress have no deployment receipts, and every gate checks files, not deployments
- Topics: DW-05.8, DW-03.8, DW-01.5, DW-01.3 (D4)
- Evidence: `kit-compatibility-record.md:38-43` says of the current pin "this pin update is not a deployment receipt"; the record's deployment row is self-inconsistent (SHA column `8a5cf817`, branch column still "`main` at ebcfaa2", `kit-compatibility-record.md:26,30`) and pin-parity compares only the SHA; System One/SubQ egress is configured in the overlay but "no source here records as done" (`SYSTEM-ONE.md:3-4`, wrangler tomls); the post-deploy check is only a `/health` probe (`workers-deploy.yml:367-370`); branded worker domains are the documented end-state but "not provisioned in DNS" while `*.workers.dev` hosts are baked into clients (`deploy.yml:49-61`).
- Failure: a repin lands without a worker redeploy (or from an SHA the record misdescribes) and the client/worker skew class that "wiped the forum on 2026-06-15" (`BASELINE-architecture.md:121-124`) recurs; or prod workers silently begin calling a third-party SubQ endpoint with no record of when or whether; during an incident nobody can state what version or config production runs.
- Confidence: high that receipts are absent (stated); medium on consequences
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — System One is marked OPEN (DW-03.8, DW-04.5 T4) and the receipt quote appears in DW-05.8, but the resulting unverifiable-production-state problem is not marked

### F-04 — The only backup path has no restore procedure, misses Durable Object state, lands on a single NAS copy, and the 24h RPO covers messages that can never be regenerated
- Topics: DW-07.5, DW-03.10, DW-03.11, DW-02.7
- Evidence: `forum-backup.sh:26-35` covers D1 (relay, auth), R2, KV only; `NostrRelayDO` "manages its own SQLite schema at runtime" (`migrations/001_init.sql` note, DW-03.11) and appears in no backup list; single `DEST_ROOT` default `/mnt/dell/shared/backups`, `KEEP_NIGHTS=14`; "there is no corresponding restore script"; contact-form success is gated only on relay OK-true (`Contact.tsx:489-517`) with the message stored server-side until the admin reads it.
- Failure: whatever the relay DO persists is in no backup; NAS failure destroys the only copy of everything; a destructive D1 incident loses up to 24h of contact/signup/chat messages whose senders already saw success toasts and whose ephemeral identities were discarded — they cannot resend; when recovery is needed, engineers improvise D1/R2/KV reassembly under incident pressure.
- Confidence: high for restore-absence and 24h RPO (stated); medium, inferred, for the DO-state gap
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — "no restore script" is marked; the DO gap and single-copy destination are not

### F-05 — Anonymous ingress has no server-side abuse controls: 10 events/s/IP is the only limit, kind-1059 bypasses moderation, and LLM-cost throttling is client-side only
- Topics: DW-04.5
- Evidence: `SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:282` — "10 events/s/IP relay limit only — no PoW/CAPTCHA/per-recipient throttle; kind 1059 bypasses content moderation"; `:284` — mitigation for LLM-cost abuse is "serialised sends (one in-flight/session), client-side throttle, ops kill-switch `JUNKIEJARVIS_ENABLED=0`".
- Failure: a script ignores the client-side throttle (it is client-side) and drives junkiejarvis at the relay's per-IP cap across IPs — a cost incident resolved only by the kill-switch, which is also a feature outage; concurrently, unmoderatable kind-1059 spam floods the admin and monitor inboxes and the nightly digest.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (threat register T1/T3, DW-04.5)

### F-06 — Data-subject erasure is cryptographically impossible and the backups that hold the PII are retained 14 nights on a readable NAS
- Topics: DW-04.5 (T2), DW-02.7, DW-07.5
- Evidence: `SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:283` — erasure is operator-side D1 purge only, "self-service NIP-09 deletion is cryptographically impossible (author key is a discarded throwaway)"; the contact form collects name/email/message (`Contact.tsx:47-53`); `forum-backup.sh:16-18` says the NAS copy is readable content and `KEEP_NIGHTS=14`.
- Failure: an erasure request is satisfied against live D1 but the message persists in two weeks of NAS backups; for anonymous chat copies there is no user identity to locate them all; regulatory exposure with no deletion path for the backup tier.
- Confidence: high for D1-purge-only (stated); medium, inferred, for the backup-retention conflict
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — T2 marks the D1-only fact; the backup-retention contradiction is unmarked

### F-07 — Admin bootstrap re-opens whenever admin rows are lost, and an admin action (`reset-db`) is one documented way to lose them
- Topics: DW-04.6, DW-04.9
- Evidence: admin = static ∪ D1 (`whitelist.is_admin` then `members.is_admin`, `SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:259-270`); "first-user-is-admin bootstrap: GET /api/setup-status reports needsSetup when no is_admin=1 row exists"; `POST /api/admin/reset-db` drops whitelist and registry rows (`ADR-2011:58`, `provision-website-info.mjs:19-20`).
- Failure: any event that empties `is_admin` rows — reset-db, a partial/older D1 restore, a bad migration — flips `needsSetup` and the first caller of the setup path claims admin. The material does not state the setup path's authentication requirements, so exploitability is unconfirmed.
- Confidence: medium, inferred
- Severity: high (if the chain holds)
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — The live encryption regime for zones 2–4 cites a decision record (ADR-2016) that was never written, and the governing invariant says the opposite
- Topics: DW-03.1, DW-06.6 (I7), DW-06.7 (D15)
- Evidence: `dreamlab.toml:34,123,137,148-149` — zones 2/3/4 `encrypted=true`, zone4 `agent_keys=true`, citing 'ADR-2016'; "no ADR-2016-*.md exists in docs/adr/ at HEAD — the record was never written"; `IDENTITY-zones.md:180-181` invariant still says only zone3 is encrypted; the master gate `[encryption].enabled=false` "suppresses every zone's flag (keys already held still decrypt)" (`dreamlab.toml:33,37`).
- Failure: nobody can establish who decided zones 2/4 are encrypted, what key distribution was intended, or what gate-off semantics should be; an operator "restoring" the documented state (only zone3 encrypted) flips privacy flags on live zones; any gate toggle yields mixed old-encrypted/new-plaintext content in one zone.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT/D15; I7 marked "BREACHED")

### F-09 — Zone4's private-zone encryption key is distributed to the agent cohort — LLM-driven bots that process untrusted input
- Topics: DW-03.1, DW-03.3, DW-04.9
- Evidence: ADR-2011 keeps `website-info` cohortless precisely because "the agent cohort would receive the zone4 key" (`ADR-2011:35-37`) — implying the other agents do receive it; `dreamlab.toml:149` sets zone4 `agent_keys=true`; the roster runs eleven agents on agentbox, including junkiejarvis (answers arbitrary visitor questions) and the poker bots.
- Failure: every agent holding the agent cohort possesses the zone4 E2EE key; prompt injection or compromise of any one bot — they consume untrusted user content by design — exfiltrates the nominally-private DreamLab zone in plaintext. The website-info ADR proves the authors know the cohort is key-receiving, yet zone4 opts in with no surviving record of why.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (assumes the other agents carry the agent cohort; ADR-2011's wording implies it)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — Seven hand-synced mirror groups, one entirely unchecked (`[nip05]`), and D1-promoted admins invisible to the search worker
- Topics: DW-03.5, DW-03.6, DW-03.10, DW-04.6
- Evidence: seven mirror groups enumerated (`config-mirrors.mjs:1-18`); `[nip05]` "still not compared at all" (`config-mirrors.mjs:360-361`); search-worker honours only its own `[vars] ADMIN_PUBKEYS` copy (`search-worker.wrangler.toml:46`) so D1-promoted admins are invisible to it (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:272`); rotation extends to five locations including the console-only CF secret (DW-03.4).
- Failure: a rotation under time pressure misses a mirror or the unchecked nip05 block — stale admin keys stay live on some worker, or a newly promoted admin is granted moderation on auth but denied on search; CI stays green throughout because it compares files within the repo, not deployed state.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (ADR-2005 deferred generator; Gap 2; nip05 marked Debt)

### F-11 — The KV provisioning job "resolves or creates" the shared admin namespace; the create path silently drops every ban and mute
- Topics: DW-03.8, DW-03.9
- Evidence: `workers-deploy.yml:60` provision-kv "resolves or creates a SINGLE shared `dreamlab-admin-kv` namespace" specifically because "a per-worker title would split it and silently drop bans/mutes"; the repo still ships `REPLACE_WITH_NEW_ADMIN_KV_ID` placeholders in both auth and pod wrangler files.
- Failure: if resolution fails — renamed namespace, token scope, transient API error — the job creates a fresh namespace and binds auth `ADMIN_KV` and pod `ADMIN_KV_RO` to the empty one; all existing bans/mutes/admin KV state disappears with no error anywhere, banned users return, and subsequent writes accumulate in the orphan's successor.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (fallback behaviour on failed resolution is not shown)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — Content generators fail soft: a missing input directory ships an empty workshop catalogue and empty testimonials with a green build
- Topics: DW-06.1, DW-06.2
- Evidence: "a missing `public/data/workshops/` writes an empty `workshop-list.json` instead of failing the build" (`generate-workshop-list.mjs:121-124`); same for a YAML parse error and testimonials (`generate-testimonials.mjs:22-25`); the postbuild discovery check asserts canonical/schema/sitemap structure (`check-discovery.mjs:32`), not content presence.
- Failure: a path rename, case mismatch or checkout quirk in a deploy commit publishes the live site with no workshops and no testimonials; prerender happily renders the empty routes and the discovery assertions pass, so the regression is discovered by visitors.
- Confidence: medium (fail-soft is stated; the detection gap is inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (stated as design, not flagged as risk)

### F-13 — The kit-compatibility History table calls a six-releases-old pin "Current (canonical)", and the gate cannot see prose rot — a rollback source that lies
- Topics: DW-01.5, DW-05.8, DW-06.7 (D9)
- Evidence: `kit-compatibility-record.md:354` marks `931898a` (beta.10) "Current (canonical — matches `CANONICAL_KIT_SHA` above and the `KIT_REF` pins)" while the canonical is `8a5cf817`; "pin-parity compares only the SHA, so this does not fail the gate"; `BASELINE-architecture.md:117` cites yet another stale SHA; `CLAUDE.md:14` still says "static dual-SPA".
- Failure: during an incident an operator reads the History to pick a rollback or verify what runs and acts on beta.10-as-current; given a repin/rollback mistake previously "wiped the forum" (2026-06-15), a wrong-version restore is data-loss-grade; CLAUDE.md misleads any human or agent following it.
- Confidence: high (staleness stated); medium on the rollback harm
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift entries) — but the operational use of the stale table as a rollback source is not drawn by the authors

### F-14 — Talk-to-AI has no request/reply correlation and its reply path depends on third-party public relays
- Topics: DW-04.3
- Evidence: AIChatFab closeout comment — "the DM transport carries no protocol-level correlation"; a late answer "can otherwise read as the answer to whatever was asked most recently" (`ChatMessage.lateForQuestion`, `AIChatFab.tsx:9-19`); replies are readable only from open relays (`relay.damus.io`, `relay.primal.net`) because the primary relay rejects kind-1059 addressed to the ephemeral session key; invariant I8 requires reply relays stay a subset of the agent's fan-out (`IDENTITY-zones.md:182-183`).
- Failure: users act on wrong AI answers attributed to the wrong question; and if the agent's publish fan-out or the public relays change, replies silently stop while question publishes keep succeeding — a first-party feature whose return leg rides infrastructure the operator does not control.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (closeout comment; invariant I8)

### F-15 — agentbox is a single host carrying the backup cron, the only monitoring, the chat backend and the poker house seats
- Topics: DW-07.5, DW-07.8, DW-01.8, DW-03.3
- Evidence: `forum-backup.sh` cron (`crontab:6`, 03:10) and inbox-watch cron (`seo-watch/crontab:17`, 07:15) run on agentbox via supercronic; junkiejarvis (Talk-to-AI recipient) and the poker house seats `poker-citizen`/`poker-citizen-blakes7` are agentbox-resident (`dreamlab.toml:386-406`, DW-01.8); seeding keys are read from `agentbox/.env` (DW-07.3).
- Failure: one host outage simultaneously stops backups (silently — the failure mode of a cron is that it doesn't run), blinds the nightly watch, freezes the DREAM poker table mid-session, and kills chat replies; compromise of agentbox's `.env` harvests the jarvis/agentbox/admin-adjacent private keys in one file.
- Confidence: medium (colocation is stated; the SPOF framing is inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Repo visibility and mirror exposure** — whether `dreamlab-ai-website` and `TheDreamLabUK/website` are public determines both the live severity of the historic admin key (F-02) and the AGPL-3.0-only source-offer obligations for the kit-derived WASM and Workers (`forum-config/Cargo.toml:6-9`).
2. **`workers-deploy.yml`'s full trigger list** — only `forum-config/Cargo.lock` and `KIT_REF` triggers are stated (DW-01.5); whether a `dreamlab.toml` zone/admin edit alone redeploys workers decides whether deployed `ZONE_CONFIG` can drift from the repo while every file-parity gate stays green.
3. **What `NostrRelayDO`'s runtime SQLite actually persists** — events versus ephemeral session state decides whether the backup gap (F-04) is total or cosmetic, and whether the single DO is a throughput/availability ceiling for the whole forum.
4. **The setup/bootstrap path's authentication** — the requirements of whatever consumes `needsSetup` (F-07) decide whether admin-row loss is an inconvenience or a takeover vector.
5. **Whether the SubQ third-party egress is live in production today, and what it transmits** — no deployment receipt exists (F-03); the data classification of search-rerank/triage payloads to that endpoint is unstated.

## dreamlab-ai-website / premortem

# Post-mortem — dreamlab-ai-website

**Incident class:** loss of a client through a data/custody/security failure (not availability cosmetics). The three root causes below are reconstructions ranked by likelihood; every claim carries the citation the distillation gives, and confidence is graded because the engineer verifying this will find at least one step the repository itself cannot prove. Latent defects follow.

---

### R-1 — An admin private key committed to git history was never rotated, and the one credential store CI cannot verify still honours whatever it was last given
- Topics: DW-07.2, DW-03.4, DW-03.5, DW-04.6, DW-04.9
- Chain of events:
  1. A plaintext `ADMIN_PRIVKEY_HEX` was committed at `c0bcece` inside three root-level seed scripts. The scripts were deleted 2026-10-07 (`3baf2fa4`), but the key "not rotated, remains in history" and "whether it is live cannot be decided from this repo" (`CLOSEOUT-SECURITY-AUDIT.md:116`).
  2. The admin/governance key split that would retire legacy admin material is staged, never executed: the visionclaw-server key `11ed6422...663c` is simultaneously primary admin and governance publisher (`dreamlab.toml:39-72` comment; DW-03.4).
  3. The admin pubkey set lives in three readable sites plus the auth-worker Cloudflare secret; that secret "cannot be rotated from this repo or CI" and is marked `verifiable: false` (DW-03.5 M1; DW-03.4 stateDiagram note; `set-worker-secrets.yml:12-13` is `workflow_dispatch`-only).
  4. A later rotation updates the TOML and relay/search `[vars]` — all CI-checked — while the unverifiable secret silently retains the old pubkey.
  5. The historical key (or the shared visionclaw key, compromised on its external server) authenticates as admin: admin = static ∪ D1 (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:259,268-270`), with NIP-98-bound moderation endpoints kinds 30910-30914 (`docs/api/MODERATION_API.md:9,15`), writes into `moderation_actions`/`hidden_events`/`whitelist` (`migrations/001_init.sql:89,408,266`), `/api/whitelist/set-admin`, and `POST /api/admin/reset-db`.
  6. reset-db wipes whitelist and agent_registry rows that only the 07:15 UTC nightly watch would discover the next morning (DW-04.9 Debt; `scripts/seo-watch/crontab:17`).
- Evidence: `CLOSEOUT-SECURITY-AUDIT.md:113-116,300`; DW-03.4 (split "Open, staged"); DW-03.5 M1 (`verifiable: false, pushed by set-worker-secrets.yml`); DW-04.6 (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:259,268-270`); DW-04.9 (`ADR-2011:58`); DW-03.11 table anchors.
- Warning signs visible today: the material itself records the unrotated historical key, the unexecuted split, and the one mirror site no gate can read.
- Confidence: medium — every mechanism is stated; whether the historical key still authenticates is precisely the fact this repo cannot show.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Every client still addresses raw `*.workers.dev` hosts because the branded domains were never provisioned; the exact configuration that previously "severed every client API call"
- Topics: DW-01.3, DW-01.2, DW-02.7, DW-04.3, DW-04.4
- Chain of events:
  1. The branded Worker domains (`relay./api./pods./search./preview.dreamlab-ai.com`) are the documented end-state but "not provisioned in DNS"; clients instead receive raw `*.workers.dev` hosts baked into the build env (`deploy.yml:49-61`).
  2. The last DNS check was 2026-06-09 (`ERR_NAME_NOT_RESOLVED`), explicitly "not re-checked as of 2026-08-31" (DW-01.3).
  3. Twelve months on, either the workers.dev route for a worker is disabled/retired (account-level change, custom-domain enablement) or DNS is finally cut to the branded domains without rebuilding the baked env — the prior incident: "shipping the branded domains baked in previously severed every client API call" (`BASELINE-architecture.md:91-97`).
  4. Every env-derived endpoint fails at once: contact form, signup DMs, Talk-to-AI, forum, pods. `publishGiftWrap` "never rejects, resolves false on transport failure, OK-false, or timeout" (DW-04.4).
  5. Visitor-submitted data is lost at the point of failure — the contact form has no queue or retry and success is gated strictly on relay OK-true (DW-02.7, `Contact.tsx:489-491` comment) — so the outage window silently discards enquiries and signup DMs.
- Evidence: `deploy.yml:49-61`; `BASELINE-architecture.md:91-97`; DW-04.4 (`src/lib/nostr.ts:226` PublishResult semantics); DW-02.7; DW-01.2 (React Vite vars, `window.__ENV__` injection, `BASELINE-architecture.md:66-70`).
- Warning signs visible today: the material documents the prior severance, the unprovisioned DNS, and the stale verification date in one bullet.
- Confidence: high that the hazard exists exactly as documented; medium that it is this incident's cause rather than R-1 — inferred, since the trigger is external.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — PII entering via contact/chat cannot be completely erased: discarded author keys, a second copy under the monitor key, and nightly NAS exports with no purge path
- Topics: DW-04.5, DW-02.7, DW-04.9, DW-07.5, DW-01.6
- Chain of events:
  1. Contact form fields (name, email, projectType, message — `src/pages/Contact.tsx:47-53`) are packaged into NIP-17 gift wraps signed by a per-submission ephemeral key that is then discarded (DW-02.7).
  2. Every submission is additionally wrapped to the monitor key `4ed429aa...aad7` (`publishMonitorCopy`, `src/lib/nostr.ts:352`; `deploy.yml:75-80`) — a second, differently-addressed copy of the same plaintext, existing because "the relay serves a kind-1059 wrap only to its addressee" (`src/lib/nostr.ts:342-347`).
  3. Self-service deletion is "cryptographically impossible (author key is a discarded throwaway)"; erasure is operator-side D1 purge only (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:283`, DW-04.5 T2).
  4. Enumerating all copies is structurally hard: reads are force-rewritten to the viewer's `#p` (DW-04.5 admission rule), so admin- and monitor-addressed copies must be purged separately — and the operator's ability to track monitor copies depends on whitelist/registry rows that `POST /api/admin/reset-db` itself destroys (DW-04.9 Debt).
  5. The nightly backup exports D1/KV to a NAS whose own header warns "the NAS copy is readable content — keep it on the trusted segment only" (`forum-backup.sh:16-18`), retained `KEEP_NIGHTS=14` (`forum-backup.sh:21`), with no restore or purge tooling for those exports (DW-07.5).
  6. An erasure demand or client audit finds a live-D1 purge left monitor-addressed copies and up to 14 nights of NAS exports intact, and/or plaintext kind-40/42/0 events readable outside the trusted segment.
- Evidence: `SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:283`; `src/lib/nostr.ts:352,342-347`; `deploy.yml:75-80`; `forum-backup.sh:16-18,21`; `docs/adr/ADR-2011-website-information-identity.md:58`; `scripts/seed/provision-website-info.mjs:19-20`.
- Warning signs visible today: the threat register carries "PII in DM content" with E2E as the only mitigation and an explicitly impossible deletion path; the co-copy design and the backup header warning are both documented.
- Confidence: medium — every mechanic is a stated fact; the incident is the inferred consequence.
- Severity: high

### F-01 — The compatibility record's History table names a six-releases-old pin as canonical
- Topics: DW-05.8, DW-01.5, DW-05.4
- Evidence: `kit-compatibility-record.md:354` marks `931898a` (v1.0.0-beta.10) "Current (canonical — matches `CANONICAL_KIT_SHA` above and the `KIT_REF` pins)" while `CANONICAL_KIT_SHA` is `8a5cf817` (`kit-compatibility-record.md:30`) and the deployment row's branch column still reads "`main` at ebcfaa2" (`kit-compatibility-record.md:26`); "pin-parity compares only the SHA, so this does not fail the gate".
- Failure: an operator rolling back or repinning consults the History table, repins the four sites to beta.10, and the client/worker skew that "wiped the forum on 2026-06-15" (`BASELINE-architecture.md:121-124`) recurs.
- Confidence: high (facts); failure inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-02 — The only backup has no restore path
- Topics: DW-07.5
- Evidence: "there is no corresponding restore script, only the raw D1/R2/KV export"; retention is `KEEP_NIGHTS=14` (`forum-backup.sh:21`).
- Failure: D1 or DO storage lost; the operator holds exports that have never been restored even in test, and recovery is ad hoc against a 14-night window.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — The auth-worker `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` Cloudflare secret can drift silently because no CI can read it
- Topics: DW-03.5, DW-03.4, DW-05.1
- Evidence: M1 is "3 readable sites + 1 secret" with "verifiable: false, pushed by set-worker-secrets.yml"; the split note records the secret "cannot be rotated from this repo or CI"; `set-worker-secrets.yml:12-13` is manual-only.
- Failure: a rotation updates TOML + relay/search vars, passes every mirror check, and the manual secret push is forgotten — the auth-worker rejects the new admin and keeps authorizing the retired one during an incident.
- Confidence: medium (mechanism stated; occurrence inferred).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open/staged)

### F-04 — D1-promoted admins are invisible to the search worker
- Topics: DW-04.6, DW-03.10
- Evidence: "the search-worker honours only its own `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` `[vars]` value — D1-promoted admins are not visible to it" (`SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:272`); its separate copy is `search-worker.wrangler.toml:46`.
- Failure: an admin promoted mid-incident via `/api/whitelist/set-admin` moderates the forum but cannot act on search; offending content stays retrievable via the search API until a redeploy.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes ("Known gap")

### F-05 — `provision-kv` "resolves or creates" can fork the shared namespace
- Topics: DW-03.8, DW-03.9
- Evidence: the pre-job "resolves or creates a SINGLE shared `dreamlab-admin-kv` namespace"; the material's own stated consequence of a namespace split is that it would "silently drop bans/mutes"; both wrangler files ship the placeholder id substituted at deploy time.
- Failure: a transient lookup failure makes the job create a second same-titled namespace; both workers bind the new empty one and every ban/mute vanishes without an error.
- Confidence: low, inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — The E2EE zone rollout has no decision record and contradicts its governing doc
- Topics: DW-03.1, DW-06.6, DW-06.7
- Evidence: `dreamlab.toml:123,137,148` set zone2/3/4 `encrypted=true` and zone4 `agent_keys=true` citing 'ADR-2016' (`dreamlab.toml:34,149`) but "no ADR-2016-*.md exists in docs/adr/ at HEAD — the record was never written" (I7); `IDENTITY-zones.md:180-181` still says only zone3 is encrypted; the master gate's semantics: "false suppresses every zone's flag (keys already held still decrypt)".
- Failure: an operator following IDENTITY-zones mishandles zone2/4 content in backups/erasure, or flips `[encryption].enabled` during an incident believing it scopes to one zone — new posts in all encrypted zones go out plaintext while the client still renders them per `ZONE_CONFIG` as encrypted.
- Confidence: medium-high.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (D15 / breached invariant I7)

### F-07 — The Jev/System One egress opt-in is armed and fires on the next routine Worker deploy
- Topics: DW-03.8, DW-04.5
- Evidence: the overlay opts in `JEV_TRIAGE`, `JEV_SEARCH_RERANK`, `JEV_RATIONALE_CHECK` with a 5-minute relay cron, "effective only on the next Worker deployment" (`SYSTEM-ONE.md:3-4`; `relay-worker.wrangler.toml:16,99-100`; `search-worker.wrangler.toml:33`); the third-party egress "is not in the threat table above" and no source records the deployment as done (T4).
- Failure: an unrelated workers deploy months later silently activates external calls to the SubQ endpoint from the relay and search workers, egressing data to a third party never reviewed in the threat model.
- Confidence: medium (config stated; activation inferred).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN)

### F-08 — The mirror completeness sweep recognizes only six governed key names
- Topics: DW-03.7, DW-03.6
- Evidence: the sweep scans wrangler `[vars]` against `MIRROR_BEARING_KEYS` = ADMIN_PUBKEYS, ZONE_CONFIG, POD_BASE_URL, RELAY_URL, JARVIS_PUBKEY, ENCRYPTION_ENABLED (`config-mirrors.mjs:41-47`); before 2026-09-05 CI compared only one of seven mirrors.

### F-09 — `[nip05]` mirrors are unchecked

- Evidence: "`[nip05]` is still not compared at all … 'still elided from source views'" (`config-mirrors.mjs:360-361`, Debt).

### F-10 — No documented deploy trigger for wrangler `[vars]` edits

- Evidence: `workers-deploy.yml` is documented to fire on `forum-config/Cargo.lock` and `KIT_REF` changes (DW-01.5); the mirror checks validate repository files, not deployed Worker state; `test-and-lint.yml` runs on main only inside deploy/workers-deploy path filters (DW-05.2 drift).

### F-14 — Anonymous ingress has no per-recipient throttle and bypasses moderation
- Topics: DW-04.5

### F-15 — reset-db destroys monitoring identity state discovered only the next morning
- Topics: DW-04.9, DW-07.8
- Evidence: "the registry row and the whitelist row are both lost on `POST /api/admin/reset-db`, and only the nightly watch reports it" (`ADR-2011:58`; `provision-website-info.mjs:19-20`); the watch runs 07:15 UTC (`scripts/seo-watch/crontab:17`).
- Failure: a reset late in the day leaves the monitor unregistered for up to ~24h; contact/signup monitor copies in that window go unmonitored, and the operator depends solely on the admin inbox.
- Confidence: high (fact); window consequence inferred.

- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

## estate / critical

### F-01 — Shipped compose inverts every fail-closed default and is exposable to the public internet through an unauditable tunnel

- Topics: ES-10.3, ES-10.6, ES-10.1
- Evidence: The code defaults are fail-closed (`RBAC_PUBLIC_READS` defaults to false at `rbac_gate.rs:141`, `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS` absent refuses boot at `project/src/main.rs:809-819`). But the shipped `docker-compose.unified.yml` inverts them: `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS: ${RBAC_PUBLIC_READS:-1}` (line 121), `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS: ${RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS:-1}` (line 122), and `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE: ${VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE:-1}` (line 105). When `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE=1`, `dev_full_bypass_active()` returns true and the bypass is "TOTAL — no NIP-98, no token, no peer check, across REST verify_access, the settings extractor and the WS handshake" (`src/utils/auth.rs:116`). The standalone `docker-compose.cloudflared.yml` "fronts WHATEVER profile the backend booted" and "anonymous /api reads reach the public internet via the Cloudflare tunnel" (`docker-compose.cloudflared.yml:3-8`). The tunnel route is "configured as a Cloudflare dashboard Public Hostname — no local config.yml, so the exposed mapping is NOT reviewable from this repo" (`docker-compose.cloudflared.yml:5-6`).
- Failure: An operator brings up the stack with default compose values and starts the standalone cloudflared. The result is a publicly reachable API with zero authentication — anonymous reads, anonymous ownerless boot, and the total dev-mode bypass — with no way to audit the exposure from the repository.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, Open, Divergence — but the compound effect is not flagged as a single finding)

### F-02 — The human-approval rationale gate is structurally a no-op for all VisionClaw-originated governance cases

- Topics: ES-05.12, ES-05.13, ES-05.14
- Evidence: VisionClaw's 31400 carries "only a d tag and a PanelDefinition with no tier or task-property field" (`events.rs:211-217`) and its 31402 carries "d, priority, category, subject-kind, subject-id and title" (`events.rs:308-323`) — no tier. The relay folds every VisionClaw case to "the advertised default, medium" (`nostr-bbs-core/src/governance.rs:769, wrangler.toml:61`). `check_rationale` "only bites at high or critical" (`nostr-bbs-core/src/governance.rs:525`). Therefore "NEITHER rationale gate fires on a VisionClaw case." The host gate takes "the stricter of the case's DECLARED tier and an optional effective tier the caller forwards" (`enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:382-390`), but "no VisionClaw writer records a declared tier." The material says this is "OPEN" and requires "the operator declares the triple on VisionClaw's panels."
- Failure: Any governance decision originating from VisionClaw (ontology elevation, KG proposal, agent action approval) can be executed with zero, one-character, or AI-generated rationale because both the host gate and the relay gate return `Ok(())` immediately at `medium` tier. The entire "human approval" governance architecture silently does nothing for a whole class of cases.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, Divergence)

### F-03 — Deleting agent memory from the pod leaves the RuVector embedding permanently searchable (no reverse tombstone)

- Topics: ES-07.9, ES-08.9, ES-07.1
- Evidence: `deleteAgentMemory` in the pod (`agentMemory.ts:196-204`) "has no reverse-tombstone dispatch to mcp claude-flow memory_delete or equivalent." The material states this is "the Largest erasure hole" and "No point-in-time RuVector backup exists, so there is no cross-store consistent restore, RPO or RTO." The deleted data's "embedding row persists and stays semantically searchable" (`docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md` Known divergences). Additionally, "Invariant 6 of docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md requires a dropped secondary write to be logged, not swallowed — this call path does not even attempt the secondary write, let alone log its absence."
- Failure: A user exercises their erasure right (GDPR or equivalent) by deleting agent memory. The pod resource is removed but the vector embedding remains in RuVector and is returned by every semantic search hitting that content. There is no backup to restore from and no way to identify which embedding rows correspond to which deleted pod resources.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence, Debt)

### F-04 — Default pod writes are unsigned and use a `did:nostr:local` placeholder identity

- Topics: ES-08.2, ES-04.4
- Evidence: "nip98 is null unless integrations.solid_pod_rs.sign_requests=true (pod-signer.js:73) — default keeps prior unsigned behaviour byte-identical (pod-signer.js:34-35)." The `did:nostr:local` placeholder fallback "that agent-identity.js:41-43 says the entrypoint still runs unconditionally." The material states a degraded boot "can silently produce a NON-SOVEREIGN identity." While ADR-2064 closes the hole when `sign_requests=true`, it "remains reachable only with the flag off, where unsigned is the declared baseline." The material does not confirm what the running manifest sets for `sign_requests`.
- Failure: An agent boots in a degraded state (key file missing, decryption failure). It writes to the pod with a placeholder identity and no signature. These writes are accepted by a permissive WAC ACL and are permanently attributed to `did:nostr:local`, making them unattributable and unerasable by the real identity. The pod signer also "caches and never retr[ies]" a key-load failure (`pod-signer.js:106-116`), so this persists until restart.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence, Debt)

### F-05 — Any process inside the agentbox container can forge `did:nostr` attribution for any pubkey

- Topics: ES-02.1, ES-04.1
- Evidence: Path 2 of `verifyAgentEventRequest` accepts the proxy-stamped `X-Agentbox-Pubkey` "without a signature" (`agent-event-auth.js:184-186`). At this stamp the header is "honoured only from a loopback TCP peer" (`agent-event-auth.js:98-106,117`). But the material acknowledges: "a process inside the agentbox container can still stamp any pubkey over loopback (TODO-unified X-1), so attribution on the session and break-glass hop is bounded by the same-UID boundary" (`agent-event-auth.js:36-39`). The same applies to `action-plane.js:228-232` `resolveAgentDid`.
- Failure: A compromised or malfunctioning agent process inside the container emits events attributed to another agent's or the operator's `did:nostr`, which VisionClaw then ingests as attributed and renders in the governance trail. There is no cryptographic proof of origin within the container boundary.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-06 — Content address hashes can diverge between the JS and Rust implementations

- Topics: ES-03.6
- Evidence: "JS sha12 hashes the raw string via crypto.createHash on the URN or payload string, Rust src/uri/mod.rs content_address hashes caller-supplied bytes directly — equivalence depends on callers feeding byte-identical canonical input on both sides, not enforced by the type system." A golden fixture test exists (`provenance_writer.rs:620-630`) but only covers one specific payload. The `_stableStringify` in JS (`uris.js:471-477`) sorts "object keys" and uses `JSON.stringify` primitives; the Rust `stable_stringify` (`provenance_writer.rs:231-233`) says "same rule — keys sorted, ASCII keys so scalar sort matches JS UTF-16 order" — the "ASCII keys" qualifier means non-ASCII keys would sort differently.
- Failure: A URN is minted on the agentbox side with a payload containing non-ASCII keys or a differently-serialized value. VisionClaw computes a different sha256-12 for the "same" content. The cross-repo identifier contract (`cross_from_agentbox`, `toVisionclaw`) produces a mismatch: bead URNs fail to round-trip, provenance records point at the wrong content, and the fixture test doesn't catch it because it only tests one fixed payload.
- Confidence: medium
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)

### F-07 — Agent-event hub drops the oldest buffered actions silently past 256

- Topics: ES-02.4, ES-02.1
- Evidence: `MAX_PENDING_ACTIONS=256` — "past MAX_PENDING_ACTIONS=256 the OLDEST buffered action is evicted and dropped_total incremented — never a silent drop-and-spam" (`agent_beam_actor.rs:119`). The hub itself is `HUB_CAPACITY=256 tokio broadcast channel` where "a lagged receiver observes RecvError::Lagged(skipped)." The coalescer also caps at `MAX_COALESCE_PER_FLUSH=256`. No queueing or persistence exists between the hub and the beam actor.
- Failure: Multiple agents emit a burst of >256 actions in one flush interval (e.g., a swarm completing a task). The earliest actions are silently dropped from the client render and from any consumer that lags. Under sustained load, the drop rate is continuous and unbounded in total. There is no replay, no dead-letter, and no alert beyond a rate-limited warn every 10 seconds.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (the drop-oldest behavior is documented as an INVARIANT but the capacity limit as a production risk is not flagged)

### F-08 — A build launched from inside the container silently bakes stale code

- Topics: ES-09.1, ES-09.2
- Evidence: "a build launched from INSIDE this container resolves bind paths against the HOST filesystem and silently bakes stale code" (`docker-compose.unified.yml:158-195`). The trap: "container starts, health check passes, edits never take effect." The correct path is exclusively "host tmux tab 6." The material states this is an "ENV-FACT (operator-verified, not repo-committed)."
- Failure: An operator or CI system runs `docker compose build` or `docker compose up` from inside the Claude Code container (which has the host Docker socket mounted). The image builds successfully, health checks pass, but the running container serves the image-build-time source, not the current code. A "fixed" security patch is deployed, verified healthy, and is not actually live.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Trap, ENV-FACT)

### F-09 — Three unsynced sats ledgers, one deposit handler returns 501, version pins skew

- Topics: ES-03.12, ES-03.11
- Evidence: "the estate runs THREE independent did:nostr-keyed sats ledgers and NONE of them is synced with the others" (`sovereign-settlement.md:61`). The host `FsPaymentStore` deposit handler "returns 501 so no value ever entered it" (`ADR-2111:22-25`). Version pins: "the forum record still reads the host at solid-pod-rs 0.4.0-alpha.15 while the forum resolves 0.5.0-alpha.7" and "no gate compares them." The `payment_settlement` authority class "currently GATES NOTHING" (`economy-loop.md:270`).
- Failure: A user attempts to deposit sats through the host's payment route and receives a 501. If they deposit through one of the other two paths, the balance exists in one ledger only and is invisible to the others. There is no reconciliation, no gate, and no way to determine the authoritative balance for any identity.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence, Open, Doc-Drift)

### F-10 — Docker socket cannot be narrowed after enabling `role_isolation`; earlier boots left it `o+rw`

- Topics: ES-10.12
- Evidence: "under `role_isolation` the entrypoint stops widening the Docker socket but cannot narrow it: the socket is the host's inode and earlier boots left it `o+rw`" (`entrypoint-unified.sh:605-621`). The boot records `degraded:docker-socket` but "only a host-side `chmod`" can fix it. The read-only proxy at `/run/docker-ro.sock` only applies to devuser's docker CLI, not to any process that can reach the original socket.
- Failure: An operator enables `role_isolation` and believes the container's Docker access is now read-only. In fact, the original socket is still `o+rw` on the host, and any process that knows the socket path (not just via the docker CLI) retains full Docker access — including the ability to launch privileged containers, mount host paths, and escape the container entirely.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, InvARIANT with caveat)

### F-11 — Browsercontainer exposes CDP and VNC on 0.0.0.0 with no stated authentication

- Topics: ES-01.3, ES-10.8, ES-10.11
- Evidence: The browsercontainer overlay publishes "0.0.0.0 5903 VNC / 8931 MCP SSE / host 9222 to container 9223 CDP" on the "SANCTIONED" list (`check-ports-loopback.mjs:97-99`). CDP (Chrome DevTools Protocol) allows arbitrary JavaScript execution in the browser instance. VNC allows full desktop interaction. The material provides no evidence of authentication on any of these ports. They are described as "DECIDED exposures, not a breach" but the decision record does not state what controls exist beyond network-level access.
- Failure: Any host on the LAN connects to port 9222, uses CDP to execute arbitrary JavaScript in the browsercontainer (which has authenticated sessions to internal services), exfiltrates cookies and tokens, or uses the browser as a pivot into the docker network. VNC on 5903 similarly allows full visual and input access.
- Confidence: medium (the material doesn't state authentication is absent, just that none is described)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (listed as "sanctioned" without flagging the security implications of unauthenticated CDP/VNC)

### F-12 — Pod write URL signature may fail for all LAN clients because nginx drops the port from the Host header

- Topics: ES-09.9, ES-10.6
- Evidence: The `/solid/` and `/pods/` nginx blocks "still send host without port" (`nginx.dev.conf:216, nginx.production.conf:177,222`). The pod NIP-98 check "also rebuilds its URL from X-Forwarded-Host" (`solid_proxy_handler.rs:207-222`). The material states: "Whether a LAN client signing host:3001 for a pod write is refused is untested." The fix for other routes (`auth.rs:170-189`) was to rebuild the URL from `X-Forwarded-Host` which includes the port, but this was not applied to the pod routes.
- Failure: Every pod write from a LAN client (where the dialled URL includes `:3001`) fails NIP-98 verification because the reconstructed URL lacks the port. Users cannot write preferences, ontology contributions, or agent memory to their pods. Alternatively, if the check is lenient (not asserted in the material), it could silently accept mismatches.
- Confidence: medium (the material says "untested" but the mechanism is clear)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-13 — NIP-98 replay cache full refuses all new authentication — a single valid key can DoS the estate

- Topics: ES-04.3
- Evidence: `REPLAY_CACHE_MAX_ENTRIES` triggers a prune at 4096 entries (`nip98.rs:183`). On a FULL cache, "the code REFUSES new auth (ReplayCacheFull) and must NOT evict the oldest live entry" (`nip98.rs:189-197`). Entries live for `REPLAY_CACHE_TTL` which is "2x the freshness window" (120 seconds, `nip98.rs:178`). An attacker with one valid key can generate 4096 unique signed tokens within 120 seconds (~34/second), fill the cache, and block all other users from authenticating for the TTL window. Repeating this maintains the denial indefinitely.
- Failure: A malfunctioning or malicious client with a valid NIP-98 key generates unique tokens at moderate rate. Within seconds, the replay cache fills. All other users' authentication attempts return `ReplayCacheFull` and are rejected. The system is effectively unavailable to everyone except the attacker (whose tokens are also in the cache but who controls which ones).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (presented as a deliberate INVARIANT without flagging the DoS implication)

### F-14 — No point-in-time backup exists for the pod store; only SQLite is covered

- Topics: ES-08.8, ES-07.9
- Evidence: "no point-in-time backup for the pod store, scripts backup-sqlite.sh covers SQLite only" (`docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md` Known divergences). There is "no cross-store consistent restore, no declared RPO or RTO." The pod is deployed as a supervised `solid-pod-rs-server` on port 8484 backed by `FsBackend` filesystem storage.
- Failure: The pod filesystem (a Docker volume or bind mount) is corrupted, accidentally deleted, or lost in a host failure. All user preferences, ontology contributions, agent memories, and WAC ACLs are irrecoverable. There is no backup to restore from and no documented recovery procedure. The SQLite backup covers a different store entirely.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)

### F-15 — Cross-repo federation contract fixture check was governed but never executed by any CI workflow

- Topics: ES-09.22, ES-03.5
- Evidence: "the federation fixture check was governed by a record but never run by anything, found in a script audit" (`invariants.yml:172-175`). The fixture's "whole point is that both repositories assert the SAME table rather than two tables that happen to agree, which an ungated check cannot deliver." The check has since been wired (`invariants.yml:176-177`) but the material also states the ungated period means the two sides could have diverged silently during that window.
- Failure: During the period when the check was not running, one side's `federation-kinds.json` interpretation changed (e.g., a kind mapping was added or a crossing was flipped). The other side was not updated. Cross-repo URN translation now produces asymmetric results: agentbox maps a kind that VisionClaw refuses, or vice versa. Durable identifiers are minted that the other system cannot resolve. The wired check now catches future drift but does not detect existing divergence.
- Confidence: medium (the current state is resolved, but historical divergence is unknown)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

---

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Actual running manifest values for security-critical flags** — the material describes code defaults and compose defaults but does not confirm what `agentbox.toml` sets for `sign_requests`, `role_isolation`, or `RBAC_*` variables in the currently deployed instance. I need to see the actual running configuration to confirm whether F-01 and F-04 are live or latent.

2. **Authentication on sanctioned LAN-exposed ports** — the material lists VNC (5903/5904/5905), CDP (9222), and MCP SSE (8931) as "sanctioned" 0.0.0.0 publishes but never states whether authentication (password, token, TLS client cert) is required on any of them. I need the browsercontainer, gui-tools, and xr-runtime configurations.

3. **Actual Cloudflare tunnel exposure** — the material states the standalone cloudflared route is configured in the Cloudflare dashboard and "NOT reviewable from this repo." I need the Cloudflare dashboard configuration or DNS records to confirm whether a tunnel is actually active and what hostnames it exposes.

4. **Pod NIP-98 behaviour on port-less URLs** — the material says LAN pod writes are "untested" for the `/solid/` route. I need an actual test result (pass or fail) to determine whether this is a live defect or works correctly by accident.

5. **The `multi-agent-docker_workspace` volume's actual content and permissions** — two aliases mount it with different modes (read-write from agentbox, read-only at `/vault` from VisionClaw). I need to verify what data is in it, whether the read-only mount is actually enforced by the kernel, and whether any path traversal from the read-write side could affect what VisionClaw reads.

## estate / premortem

# Post-mortem

Reconstructed from the estate documentation set (ES-01 – ES-12, ES-90) only; no runtime telemetry, logs or client correspondence were available to me. The three root causes below are the highest-probability candidates consistent with a client-costing data, custody or security failure, ranked by how completely the material itself evidences each chain. Every claim carries the citation an engineer can re-check; where a step is not stated in the material it is marked inferred. The further-findings list records every latent defect I met while tracing these chains, including ones I could only suspect.

### R-1 — Shipped dev-compose defaults (anonymous reads + total DEV_MODE bypass) fronted to the public internet by the standalone Cloudflare tunnel
- Topics: ES-10.3, ES-10.6, ES-09.8, ES-09.5, ES-10.4
- Chain of events:
  1. The `visionclaw` dev service is brought up with compose defaults: `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE:-1`, `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS:-1`, `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS:-1`, default role editor (`docker-compose.unified.yml:105,121,122,129`).
  2. The dev build compiles the `dev-auth` feature by default (`scripts/rust-backend-wrapper.sh:42`), so `dev_full_bypass_active()` returns true and every request — REST, settings extractor and WS handshake — is granted the `dev-mode-local-admin` identity with no NIP-98, no token, no peer check (`src/utils/auth.rs:116`, ES-10.6 "BYPASS IS TOTAL").
  3. The standalone `docker-compose.cloudflared.yml` container is also up on `visionclaw_network`; it fronts whatever profile booted and reaches nginx :3001 as an ordinary peer (`docker-compose.cloudflared.yml:3-8`).
  4. The public hostname → service mapping lives only in the Cloudflare dashboard, so nothing in the repo reviews or blocks it (`docker-compose.cloudflared.yml:5-6`).
  5. ADR-2119's `VISIONCLAW_INGRESS=lan` does not stop this hand-started standalone tunnel (`ADR-2119-prod-ingress-is-declared-lan-or-tunnel.md:64-66`).
  6. Anonymous reads — and with DEV_MODE, writes and admin — on the client's graph are reachable from the public internet.
- Evidence: "anonymous /api reads reach the public internet via the Cloudflare tunnel, not just the LAN" (ES-10.3, CFT note); "Net shipped posture (visionclaw dev service) = demo-open" (ES-10.3); the tunnel is "invisible to the ADR-2013 compose port-audit" because it publishes no port (`docker-compose.cloudflared.yml:6-8`); profiles do not enforce mutual exclusion and both dev and production publish :3001, so an operator avoiding collision may run dev alone on a host (ES-09.8, GATE).
- Warning signs visible today: the material states the exposure and the review gap explicitly; the only mitigations named (profile intent checks in `security_profile.rs`, release hygiene in `main.rs:161`) apply to release/dev-auth boundaries, not to a dev-profile container behind a tunnel.
- Confidence: high — every link is stated in the material; only the simultaneous presence of the dev service and the standalone tunnel on one host is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Client erasure is a no-op against RuVector: pod deletion leaves embeddings live and semantically searchable, with no backup or restore point
- Topics: ES-08.9, ES-07.9, ES-07.3, ES-08.8
- Chain of events:
  1. A client's data is deleted via `SolidPodService.deleteAgentMemory` (`SolidPodService.ts:363`) → `agentMemory.ts:196-204` → LDP DELETE → 204.
  2. This call graph never reaches RuVector: "no reverse-tombstone dispatch to mcp claude-flow memory_delete or equivalent" (ES-08.9).
  3. The embedding row persists and stays semantically searchable — "Largest erasure hole" (ES-07.9, D5).
  4. A later `memory_search` (≈100 ms semantic path, ES-07.3) — including the global cross-namespace `namespace "*"` search (ES-07.3 note) — returns the deleted content, and it flows into an agent answer shown to another party.
  5. Remediation is impossible within stated controls: "No point-in-time RuVector backup exists, so there is no cross-store consistent restore, RPO or RTO" (ES-07.9, D5); the pod store itself has no point-in-time backup either — `scripts/backup-sqlite.sh` covers SQLite only (ES-08.8 note).
- Evidence: `agentMemory.ts:196-204` (no secondary write); DATA-authority-erasure "Known divergences" naming the gap; Invariant 6 requires a dropped secondary write to be *logged* — "this call path does not even attempt the secondary write, let alone log its absence" (ES-08.9 note); "live erasure acceptance remain[s] outstanding" and the reconciliation journal "has no production destructive adapters" (ES-08.11 closeout note).
- Warning signs visible today: the hole is documented in two topics and in the governing erasure doc; nothing in the material shows a fix shipped, only that the gap is known.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Shipped-default Docker-socket widening turns any in-container agent compromise into host root and estate-wide key custody
- Topics: ES-10.12, ES-01.5, ES-05.11, ES-10.10, ES-05.15
- Chain of events:
  1. An agent session (default `bypassPermissions`, and deny rules that "a `sh -c` wrapper evades", `agentbox.toml:698-713`, ES-10.10 D6) executes attacker-influenced code as devuser inside the agentbox container; the task journal does not mediate shell commands ("does not establish complete mediation of shell commands or nightly egress", ES-05.11).
  2. With `role_isolation = false` — "the shipped default" — the entrypoint widens the host Docker socket `o+rw` for devuser (`agentbox/config/entrypoint-unified.sh:598-602`); the override also adds the socket gid (`group_add 965`, ES-01.5 N2).
  3. devuser drives host Docker without sudo → mounts the host filesystem → host root.
  4. Host root reads the plaintext `.env` ("SOPS never executed… VisionClaw .env is PLAINTEXT today", ES-10.10 D5) and key material (`/run/secrets/nostr.key`, ES-04.5 K1).
  5. The house key `11ed6422` "stays admitted for every use until its roles are listed as withdrawn, and that list is empty" (`agentbox/config/custody/g5-key-split.json:32`, ES-05.15) — the compromise can sign governance and federation events estate-wide.
- Evidence: as cited above, plus ES-05.10 "Same-UID agents are not isolated by application-level policy"; ES-10.12 OPEN2 — earlier boots already left the socket `o+rw` and a host-side `chmod` is owed (`entrypoint-unified.sh:598-621`); role-isolation changes are rebuild-class and the rehearsal was still pending (ADR-2122:616).
- Warning signs visible today: the degraded-socket marker, the empty withdrawal list, the unconfirmed custody register ("Every custodian, deployed location, rotation cadence… is UNCONFIRMED", ES-10.10).
- Confidence: high that the surface exists exactly as described; the exploit chain from agent code-execution is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — The raw-email "capability gate" checks a public value, so tier 2 adds no secret
- Topics: ES-06.3, ES-06.2
- Evidence: tier 1 is the bearer token ("gates ANY call"); tier 2 checks the `nostr_pubkey` *argument* against an allow-list — and the material itself states a Nostr public key "is the publishable half… not a secret. Passing it as a tool argument is the design" (ES-06.3). Anyone holding the bearer and knowing the operator's public key passes tier 2.
- Failure: a compromised session or token holder calls `fetch_email_raw` with the operator's public hex and reads full raw mail of both Proton accounts, bypassing the egress sanitizer entirely.
- Confidence: high — the implication is direct from the material's own note.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (presented as design)

### F-02 — Pod writes are unsigned by default, and degraded boots mint `did:nostr:local`
- Topics: ES-08.2, ES-04.4
- Evidence: "nip98 is null unless integrations.solid_pod_rs.sign_requests=true… default keeps prior unsigned behaviour byte-identical" (`pod-signer.js:34-35,73`, ES-08.2); "the did:nostr:local placeholder fallback that agent-identity.js:41-43 says the entrypoint still runs unconditionally" — "a degraded boot can silently produce a NON-SOVEREIGN identity" (ES-04.4). ADR-2064 closed only the flag-on path.
- Failure: with the flag off (the default), agent pod writes go out unsigned and, on a degraded boot, are attributed to a placeholder identity — custody and attribution of pod data silently lost.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence, ES-04.4)

- Evidence: "OPF_MODE soft and sidecar unreachable → continue unredacted, fail-open, warn plus counter" (ES-03.9). The deployed mode is not stated anywhere in the material.
- Failure: if soft is the running mode, an opf-router outage silently ships unredacted payload content to adapters and any downstream store.
- Confidence: medium — the branch is stated; that soft is deployed is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — Nothing verifies a *built* release binary omits dev-auth
- Topics: ES-09.5, ES-09.11
- Evidence: "ADR-2037 (proposed, implementation_status none): no CI or image-build assertion yet verifies a shipped release binary omits dev-auth — a mis-targeted pipeline could promote the stubbed-hygiene binary to production undetected" (ES-09.5); the `dev-auth-release-gate` job is a hermetic *text* assertion over committed Dockerfiles/entrypoints, not over artefacts (`ci.yml:146-155,163-168`, ES-09.11). Dev builds include `dev-auth` by default (`rust-backend-wrapper.sh:42`).
- Failure: a mis-wired build target promotes a binary carrying the Bearer dev-session bypass and the no-op hygiene stub; the boot hard-fails that catch `SETTINGS_AUTH_BYPASS` are themselves stubbed.
- Confidence: high — the gap is stated verbatim.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence, ES-09.5)

### F-05 — Any same-UID process can stamp any `did:nostr` over loopback
- Topics: ES-02.1, ES-04.1, ES-04.5, ES-05.10
- Evidence: "a process inside the agentbox container can still stamp any pubkey over loopback (TODO-unified X-1)" (`agent-event-auth.js:36-39`, ES-02.1 Debt); the session-plane ACL "authorises a UID, not a process" (`session-plane-acl.json:6`, `api.rs:32`, ES-04.5 D3); "Same-UID agents are not isolated by application-level policy" (ES-05.10).
- Failure: one malicious or hijacked process in the container emits agent-events, task-journal entries or session-boundary calls under another agent's or the operator's `did:nostr`; attribution and audit become unverifiable.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, ES-02.1; Divergence, ES-04.5 D3)

### F-07 — NIP-98 URL checking trusts a client-forgeable header, and the pod path is untested

- Evidence: `nip98_request_url` "rebuilds the URL from X-Forwarded-Host" (`auth.rs:170-189`, ES-09.9/ES-10.6); the backend publishes :4000 on the dev service (ES-01.3), i.e. reachable without the proxy that sets the header; separately, OPEN: "`/solid/` and `/pods/` blocks still forward host without the port. Whether a LAN client signing host:3001 for a pod write is refused is untested" (`solid_proxy_handler.rs:207-222`, ES-09.9 XFHOPEN).
