## knowledgegraph / critical

### F-01 — The privacy/publication invariants (I1, I8, I9) are enforced only by archived code; nothing in the live publish path is stated to enforce them
- Topics: KG-07.1, KG-07.2, KG-05.3, KG-01.1
- Evidence: I1 ("vc:public is the ONLY publication gate — strictly boolean, re-checked in every output stage"), I8 (zero-rejected input accounting) and I9 ("No public artefact contains a private identifier — visibility.py at every output, release gate re-scans") are all attributed to files that now live only under `archive/logseq-era-2026-09-22/pipeline/` (census.py:61, visibility.py:133, release_gate.py — KG-07 sources; knowledgeGraph/README.md:64). The live path's stated steps are `ontomark validate --vault all`, `ontomark build`, certificate closure, and three domain contracts (visionGraph/.github/workflows/publish.yml:127-135, 158-163, 309-313) — no visibility re-scan, no vc:public boolean check, no input census is stated anywhere in it. The baseline itself now disclaims: its invariants "do not assert that the archived workflow remains active" (BASELINE-narrativegoldmine.md:29).
- Failure: a vault note containing private identifiers with a missing or mistyped visibility field publishes to narrativegoldmine.com, is ingested by VisionClaw into the reasoned graph, and is then served to agents via the Loom facade and ontology-bridge MCP tools — a public leak with persistent downstream copies, undetected by any gate.
- Confidence: medium — the gap is directly readable from the material's silence; `ontomark validate` could conceivably implement these checks, but nothing states it does (inferred from absence)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — the baseline's historical-tense drift is marked (Drift, KG-07.1), but the live-path enforcement gap is not

### F-02 — The deploy trigger was widened from "owner's push or by hand" to any push to visionGraph main, and the record that asserted the tighter control is now the stale side
- Topics: KG-05.5
- Evidence: the Drift note records the earlier claim "Agents never push to visionGraph main — publish.yml runs only on the owner's push or by hand" versus the workflow header, which "now says it runs on a push to main or by hand, with estate deployment authorised during the ontomark resumption" (visionGraph/.github/workflows/publish.yml:17-18). That workflow is the sole writer of the production site via `external_repository=knowledgeGraph` (publish.yml:515-523).
- Failure: anyone (human or agent) with push access to visionGraph main triggers a full production deploy of the corpus and site; the documented control an auditor or on-call operator would rely on no longer exists, and nothing in the material states branch protection on visionGraph main.
- Confidence: high on the trigger change (both texts quoted); medium that it is exploitable (depends on branch protection, unstated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes — Drift (KG-05.5), though framed as documentation drift rather than a control change

### F-03 — Production site content is written by another repository's CI; this repo has no remaining workflow and no stated post-deploy verification of what lands on gh-pages
- Topics: KG-05.3, KG-01.1, KG-05.5
- Evidence: publish.yml deploys with `peaceiris/actions-gh-pages, external_repository=knowledgeGraph` (publish.yml:515-523); this repo's only workflow is archived under `archive/github-workflows/build.yml` (its `ontology/` inputs already moved, so it "cannot run again even if triggered" — knowledgeGraph/README.md:58); the CNAME "is read by Pages, not written by any workflow here". All quality gates (validate, certificate, domain contracts) run pre-deploy inside visionGraph; nothing cited runs in or against knowledgeGraph after content lands.
- Failure: the gh-pages branch — the sole source of the production site and of the artefacts VisionClaw reasons over — is writable only from outside this repo; a compromised or mis-scoped deploy secret in visionGraph, or tampering with gh-pages directly, changes production content with no in-repo detection, since no gate or audit exists on the serving side.
- Confidence: medium — the cross-repo write is stated; the credential requirement and the absence of any serving-side check are inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — The published Turtle snapshot is frozen at 7 August 2026 while consumer docs still direct fetches at it
- Topics: KG-05.5, KG-06.2, KG-01.1
- Evidence: "the committed `dist/data/` snapshot is the 7 August 2026 generation, not the served one" (knowledgeGraph/README.md:87, cited in KG-05.5); `dist/data/` is explicitly "last local pipeline build" (KG-01.1). Yet KG-06.2's live sequence has VisionClaw fetch "markdown + ontology" from this repo's `dist/data/ontology.ttl` (258,200 triples; ecosystem.md:87-88), and that reasoned graph is what agentbox's Loom facade and MCP tools ground against. The path is now "secondary" (ADR-2114 defaults CorpusSource to the visionGraph vault), but the docs describing it as the seam are unchanged.

### F-05 — Exactly one publication path exists: a sha-pinned external binary with no build-from-source fallback, while this repo's own builder is archived with its inputs gone
- Topics: KG-05.3, KG-05.5, KG-01.1
- Evidence: ontomark is "fetch[ed] ... pinned by tag and by sha256, no build-from-source fallback" and "a run that cannot fetch it fails" (publish.yml:46-51, 98-117; KG-05.5 note1 frames this as the INVARIANT "one vault, one build"). This repo's pipeline and build.yml are archived and inert (knowledgeGraph/README.md:58; archive/github-workflows/build.yml:1), and the governing doc still claims a "reproducible half" running six gates (build-and-gates.md:1-3 — marked DIVERGENCE in KG-05.3). Nothing in the estate can rebuild the site except that one external release.
- Failure: the ontomark release is yanked, the org transfer breaks the URL, or Actions cannot reach it — every publish run fails at step one with no fallback; the site freezes at its last generation and recovery requires resources entirely outside this repo, whose own reconstruction path is archived beyond re-triggering.
- Confidence: high on the single-path facts; the risk reading is mine
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — the stale governing doc is marked (DIVERGENCE, KG-05.3); the no-fallback dependency is presented as an invariant, not a risk

### F-06 — The class-count and identity lockstep gates (I6, I10) retired with release_gate.py; the live gates cannot catch silent class loss or identity drift
- Topics: KG-07.1, KG-07.2, KG-05.3
- Evidence: I6 ("EXPECTED_CLASSES ... must equal the true class count") and I10 ("Count and identity move together ... same commit") are invariants of the archived build (BASELINE-narrativegoldmine.md:186-219); KG-07.2 notes `release_gate.py check_identity (line 127)` "was the mitigation, never a closure" and then moves the whole state machine to Archived. The live path's stated gates are certificate closure (publish.yml:158-163, 210-227) and three domain contracts (publish.yml:309-313) — neither is a census or identity check.
- Failure: a vault refactor silently drops or renames classes; the certificate still covers the closure of the surviving premises, so the build passes; the corpus publishes with classes missing and no signal — consumers diff against a phantom corpus, and the count-equality divergence the baseline itself said "cannot prove equal identities" goes entirely undetected.
- Confidence: medium-high — the absence of any stated census gate in the live path is directly readable
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — KG-07.2 marks the archived-era divergence, not the live-path gap

The tree that actually ships enumerates nine DOMAIN_SLUGS (visionGraph WasmVOWL/modern/src/types/scope.ts:76-86, cited in KG-04.6 note2); this repo's copy has six (explorer/modern/src/types/scope.ts:73-80).

### F-09 — Over-budget scopes throw a RangeError at runtime and no gate ties tier sizes to the caps
- Topics: KG-04.2, KG-04.3, KG-07.1
- Evidence: `assertScope()` "throws RangeError on any over-budget scope" against MAX_NODES 1500 / MAX_EDGES 4000 / FOCUS_MAX 300 (explorer/modern/src/types/scope.ts:144-155, 30-36), and the authors frame rejection (never truncation) as the contract. No build or publish step stated anywhere checks tier sizes against these caps — the publish gates are certificate closure and three domain contracts (publish.yml:158-163, 309-313).

- Severity: medium

The publish.yml steps cited include no binary-format fixture check.
- Failure: an ontomark release changes the tier layout, or the v0.2.0 reader changes interpretation; every stated gate passes (they check triples and contracts, not binary layout) and the shipped site renders garbage or blank graphs — the exact failure class the golden fixture existed to prevent, now unguarded.
- Confidence: medium, inferred — compatibility is plausibly maintained by intent ("same outputs"), but no enforcement is stated
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — No stated licence covers the artefact actually served: the grants attach to pre-archive paths, and the current corpus originates in a vault whose licence is nowhere stated

The served content is gh-pages built by ontomark from visionGraph's Obsidian vault (README.md:13, 46-49; publish.yml:515-523), including Quartz notes under /notes/ — none of which appears in any cited grant, and visionGraph's own licence is not stated anywhere in this material.

### F-12 — The page-content fallback chain fetches a TITLE-form path while the build produces a SLUG-form mirror the client never requests
- Topics: KG-04.4, KG-05.3
- Evidence: pageService fetches `/api/markdown/${title}.md` (pageService.ts:133) with a raw.githubusercontent fallback (pageService.ts:134), while "jsonld_to_page_api.py writes a SLUG-form mirror (api/markdown/SLUG.md) that pageService.ts never requests" (KG-04.4 note). `isValidMarkdown` is stated to reject only HTML content-type, sessionStorage bodies, and a DOCTYPE — nothing rejects a JSON error body.
- Failure: a page whose title differs from its slug, or contains URL-hostile characters, 404s on the title path, falls through to raw GitHub; a rate-limit or JSON error body that is not HTML content-type can pass the heuristic and render as page prose — while the correct mirror file sits at the slug path, unrequested. (Caveat: this is the never-ships tree; whether the shipping fork shares the defect is unstated.)
- Confidence: medium — the mismatch is stated; the failure modes of the heuristic are inferred
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes — DIVERGENCE (KG-04.4 note, cross-referenced to KG-05.3)

### F-13 — The accepted estate-canon ADR's central claim is false and its review trigger is premised on a build that does not exist
- Topics: KG-05.3
- Evidence: ADR-2013 (accepted, partial, staged) "names `VisionClaw/crates/vault` as the only implementation that parses, reasons over or builds the corpus" (docs/adr/ADR-2013-sovereign-corpus-is-ecosystem-canon.md:52-53), while "the sole producer in the workflow is now the released `ontomark` CLI" (publish.yml:91-97); the ADR's "review trigger names a second build" that is not the one that runs.

- Confidence: high — both facts quoted in the material
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes — Tension (KG-05.3)

- Topics: KG-01.2, KG-06.2
- Evidence: "GitHub licence detector reads root LICENSE and labels the WHOLE repo 'AGPL-3.0' — wrong for explorer/ and ontology/" (LICENSING.md:32-34); the scope table still lists pre-archive paths (LICENSING.md:22, 29); and ecosystem.md:121-127 asserts VisionFlow's licensing doc "still says" MPL 2.0 for VisionClaw when the table was already corrected to "AGPL-3.0-only" at line 8 — "the stale side is now this repo".
- Failure: a downstream relies on the GitHub label and forgoes legitimate MIT/ODbL use of explorer/ and the data, or mis-attributes obligations; an editor dispatched to fix the "stale" VisionFlow line finds nothing to fix, because the stale text is here — triage effort burned on the wrong repo in a dual-licensing estate where licence accuracy is the product.

### F-15 — The OntoCast integration doc still instructs operators to run an archived importer into a retired promotion target
- Topics: KG-06.4
- Evidence: docs/integrations/ontocast.md "describes pipeline.ontocast_import as the live seam" (ontocast.md:1-4), but `import_graph` lives only at archive/logseq-era-2026-09-22/pipeline/ontocast_import.py:140, and its promotion target `ontology/pages/` "is no longer built from or accepted into" (knowledgeGraph/README.md:15, 58).
- Failure: an operator follows the doc as written, resurrects or runs the archived importer, generates candidate .md files into review/ — and the promotion step lands them in a tree nothing consumes; either the work is silently lost or, worse, someone recreates ontology/pages/ to make the doc true and resurrects a zombie path parallel to the real vault.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes — DOC-DRIFT (KG-06.4)

## Not judgeable from this material
1. Branch protection and actor set on visionGraph `main`, and the scope/rotation of the deploy credential used by `external_repository` — F-02 and F-03 severity hinge entirely on these.
2. What `ontomark validate --vault all` actually checks — whether it enforces any equivalent of I1/I8/I9 (visibility booleans, input census, private-IRI rescans) or only schema. This decides whether F-01 is a live hole or a documentation hole.
3. The diff between this repo's `explorer/modern` and the shipping WasmVOWL/modern tree — whether pageService, assertScope, error boundaries, and the caps are identical, fixed, or worse in the fork (affects F-08, F-09, F-12).
4. The licence and rights position of the visionGraph vault content and of the gh-pages/`/notes/` output — nothing in the material states who holds rights in the currently served corpus (decides F-11).
5. Whether the post-deploy corpus-sync dispatch to VisionClaw has retry, reconciliation, or idempotence on the source sha — i.e., whether site and reasoned graph can silently diverge when the dispatch step fails.

## knowledgegraph / premortem

# Post-mortem — client-loss incident (data / custody class)

**Scope note.** This document is reconstructed solely from the KG topic set at its verified commits (knowledgegraph `1edcfc9`, visiongraph `fd5269a`, visionflow `124512b`). Nothing else about the system's state at incident time was available to me; every finding below must be re-verified against current code and tests before action. Where a diagram does not assert order or concurrency, I have not assumed it. The three sections R-1…R-3 are competing hypotheses for the root cause, ranked by likelihood; R-section claims about what the *new* pipeline lacks are inferences from the steps the material enumerates, not from any statement of absence.

---

### R-1 — The only privacy/publication gates ever documented were archived with the Logseq pipeline; the sole active build lists no equivalent
- Topics: KG-05.3, KG-05.5, KG-07.1, KG-07.2, KG-06.2
- Chain of events: (1) On 2026-09-22 the entire Logseq-era pipeline — `census.py`, `visibility.py`, `manifest.py`, `release_gate.py` — is archived under `archive/logseq-era-2026-09-22/`. (2) Authoring moves to visionGraph's Obsidian vault with "typed frontmatter"; nowhere does the material state that the strict-boolean `vc:public` flag or the private-identifier scan has an equivalent there. (3) The sole producer becomes the pinned `ontomark` release inside visionGraph's `publish.yml`. (4) The run's asserted steps are: fetch pinned binary → validate → build → certificate-closure check → three named domain-contract gates → Quartz → deploy gh-pages → dispatch corpus-sync to VisionClaw. No step in that enumeration re-scans artefacts for private identifiers, runs an input census, or enforces a publication flag. (5) The trigger is now a push to `main` or by hand, with "estate deployment authorised" — the earlier "agents never push to visionGraph main" guarantee is explicitly drifted. (6) A push publishes vault content to narrativegoldmine.com; the same run syncs it into VisionClaw, where agentbox's Loom facade and ontology-bridge MCP tools ground answers against the reasoned graph — the leak propagates into agent output with no independent gate. (7) The client discovers non-public material in the public corpus or in agent answers.
- Evidence: I1 (`vc:public` the only publication gate, strictly boolean), I8 (input census, zero rejected), I9 (no public artefact contains a private identifier — `visibility.py:133` at every output, release gate re-scans) — BASELINE-narrativegoldmine.md:186-219 via KG-07.1; `census.py:61`, `release_gate.py:127` (KG-07.2); archive status (`knowledgeGraph/README.md:58,64`, KG-01.1); the enumerated new-build steps with no visibility gate (`visionGraph/.github/workflows/publish.yml:46-51, 98-117, 127-135, 158-163, 210-227, 309-313, 515-523, 525-531` via KG-05.3); trigger drift (`publish.yml:17-18` via KG-05.5); governing doc still claiming six active gates (`build-and-gates.md:1-3`); Loom/MCP grounding against VisionClaw's graph (KG-06.2).
- Warning signs visible today: KG-05.3's own DIVERGENCE bullet on the retired gates; KG-07.1's Drift (invariants stated present-tense over archived machinery); KG-05.5's Drift bullet on the agent-push trigger; the ADR-2013 tension showing the canon-producer record already lags reality.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — retirement, enumerated steps, and widened trigger are documented; the absence of an ontomark-side privacy gate is inferred from the enumeration)
- Severity: high

### R-2 — The explorer that ships is a diverged tree nothing in this repo builds, tests or gates; every documented explorer guarantee attaches to a copy that never deploys
- Topics: KG-04.1, KG-04.6, KG-04.3, KG-04.2, KG-01.1
- Chain of events: (1) Incident hits narrativegoldmine.com's `/graph`. (2) Responders follow `BASELINE-narrativegoldmine.md:14` and `architecture/explorer.md:10`, both pointing at `explorer/rust-wasm/src/ngg1.rs` — a crate that does not exist at HEAD. (3) The audited tree pins `@dreamlab-ai/vowl-wasm` as the v0.1.1 release tarball (`explorer/modern/package.json:20`); the shipping tree (`visionGraph/publishing-tools/WasmVOWL/modern`) pins v0.2.0 and adds `@dreamlab-ai/ontomark-graph` (`WasmVOWL/modern/package.json:24-25`) — the one shared dependency is version-skewed. (4) Domain surface diverged since 2026-10-01: six `DOMAIN_SLUGS` here vs nine in the shipping tree (`scope.ts:73-80` vs `WasmVOWL/modern/src/types/scope.ts:76-86`). (5) This repo's `build.yml` is archived, inert, and "never builds explorer/" even before retirement; the only builder of an explorer SPA is `publish.yml:410` — of the *other* tree. (6) None of this repo's verified contracts — the 183-byte golden fixture over the 24-byte node record (I2), the scope caps with `RangeError` (`scope.ts:144-155`), the SAB hard-disable at `protocol.ts:153`, the markdown 404 sniffing — is established for the code serving the client, and any patch landed here cannot reach production. (7) If the shipping tree retains `assertScope`, upstream corpus growth eventually throws `RangeError` on `/graph`; if it does not, production silently violates the documented cap contract. Either way, incident response audits the wrong code.
- Evidence: KG-04.6 note1 "built by NOTHING in this repo"; KG-04.6 BUILDYML node; version pins (`explorer/modern/package.json:20`, `WasmVOWL/modern/package.json:24-25`); domain divergence (KG-04.6 note2); DOC-DRIFT on rust-wasm (KG-04.1); caps and rejection-not-truncation contract (`scope.ts:30-36, 144-155`); the prior blanked-view failure in the same lineage (`protocol.ts:153` note, KG-04.2).
- Warning signs visible today: KG-04.6's own note labels this DOC-DRIFT and "DIVERGED"; the v0.1.1-vs-v0.2.0 skew is flagged; a render-corruption failure (~1e20 amplification) already occurred once in this lineage.
- Confidence: high (the divergence and the governance void are documented; the concrete production failure mode is inferred)
- Severity: high

### R-3 — The repository's tracked dataset froze at the 7 August 2026 generation while the served corpus moved on; the ODbL "published export" and the residual VisionClaw ingest path serve stale data
- Topics: KG-05.5, KG-05.3, KG-06.2, KG-01.1, KG-01.2
- Chain of events: (1) After the sovereignty transition, nothing in this repo computes the served artefacts. (2) Committed `dist/data/` (12 tracked files, "last local pipeline build") remains the 7 August 2026 generation, explicitly "not the served one" — over a year stale at incident time. (3) The repo is nonetheless the published ODbL corpus and the COMMERCIAL track-2 data-licensing surface, so a data client's deliverable is this tree. (4) VisionClaw's GitHub-fetch path (markdown + Turtle over GitHub; `dist/data/ontology.ttl` at 258,200 triples) still exists as the secondary `CorpusSource` and fires whenever the primary visionGraph source is unavailable or a deployment pins the legacy default. (5) Classes, identities and counts known to have changed (8,138 pages; domains 6→9) are absent from what the client integrated; answers grounded via VisionClaw/agentbox on the stale path contradict the live site. (6) The deliverable is discovered to be wrong; the client is lost.
- Evidence: "the committed `dist/data/` snapshot is the 7 August 2026 generation, not the served one" (`../knowledgeGraph/README.md:87` via KG-05.5); `dist/data/` described as last local pipeline build (KG-01.1 DIST); "Secondary path, not default" and the still-accurate GitHub-fetch transcription (KG-06.2, `ecosystem.md:87-88`); 258,200 triples (KG-06.2); ODbL/COMMERCIAL scope (KG-01.2, `COMMERCIAL.md:7-8`); domain growth (KG-04.6 note2).
- Warning signs visible today: the staleness is already documented at audit time; the repo's entire purpose as the "published export" rests on a snapshot older than the archive event itself.
- Confidence: medium (the snapshot's staleness and consumability are documented; that a client actually consumed it is inferred)
- Severity: high

### F-01 — Estate deployment authorisation removed the human gate between a vault edit and production data
- Topics: KG-05.5, KG-05.3
- Evidence: KG-05.5's Drift bullet — earlier text read "Agents never push to visionGraph main — publish.yml runs only on the owner's push or by hand"; the header now runs on push to `main` or by hand, "with estate deployment authorised" (`publish.yml:17-18`), and the run writes this repo's gh-pages via a cross-repo credential (`publish.yml:515-523`).
- Failure: an agent-authored commit to `main` executes the entire publish → deploy → corpus-sync chain with no human review; a hallucinated vault edit becomes live client-facing data.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-02 — The shipping SPA's wasm dependency is a GitHub-release tarball with no hash pin documented, unlike ontomark

- Failure: a retagged or replaced release swaps wasm code into the client-facing bundle; deleting the release breaks all future builds of both explorer trees.

### F-03 — SharedArrayBuffer transport remains in code on both sides, disabled only by one boolean
- Topics: KG-04.2, KG-07.1
- Evidence: `canUseSharedMemory()` unconditionally returns false (`protocol.ts:153`); the prior failure — "a half-written frame amplified to ~1e20 and blanked the view" — and invariant I7 requiring a double-buffered SAB with Atomics-gated flip before re-enabling.
- Failure: a performance change flips the flag without the double buffer; intermittent corrupted or blank client views recur, with positions at ~1e20.
- Confidence: high (the dormant path is documented; occurrence requires a change)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE note; invariant I7)

### F-04 — Over-budget graph scopes fail by client-side `RangeError`, with no degradation path

- Evidence: MAX_NODES 1500 / MAX_EDGES 4000 / FOCUS_MAX 300; `assertScope` throws `RangeError` (`scope.ts:30-36, 144-155`); the caps are contract — "rejected, never silently truncated"; the corpus demonstrably grows (7,874→8,138 pages, KG-07.3; six→nine domains, KG-04.6 note2).

### F-12 — 4,383+ referenced-but-undeclared property targets ship as unredacted stub concepts
- Topics: KG-07.3
- Evidence: the STUBS open item — "object-property targets referenced but never declared — ship as skos:Concept stubs, deliberately NOT redacted".
- Failure: clients querying the corpus receive placeholder entities indistinguishable from declared ones; if any dangling target string derives from a non-public title, the no-redaction choice publishes the reference.
- Confidence: medium on the stubs-as-data harm (documented); the leak reading is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-13 — Every deploy auto-propagates the corpus into VisionClaw with no documented retraction path
- Topics: KG-05.3, KG-06.2
- Evidence: after deploy the run "dispatches corpus-sync to VisionClaw with the source sha" (`publish.yml:525-531`); VisionClaw ingests, reasons (Whelk) and serves agentbox Loom/MCP grounding (KG-06.2); no step or mechanism anywhere in the material reverses a sync.
- Failure: a bad or leaked publish is amplified into the reasoned graph and into agent answers within one run; retracting the site does not retract VisionClaw.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — the absence of a retraction mechanism is not stated)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — No generation manifest is documented for the served artefacts
- Topics: KG-07.1, KG-05.3
- Evidence: I11 (source rev, generation id, counts, SHA-256 per artefact — `manifest.py:137`) belonged to the archived pipeline; the ontomark run's enumerated outputs name `api/`, `data/`, `context/`, `okf/` and a certificate, no manifest (KG-05.3); the repo's own tracked dist is the 7 August 2026 generation (KG-05.5).
- Failure: after any bad publish, neither operator nor client can establish which generation an artefact is or verify its per-file integrity; custody is unprovable.
- Confidence: medium (inferred from the enumeration)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — The baseline declares itself historical yet states its invariants as present-tense rules
- Topics: KG-07.1, KG-01.1
- Evidence: KG-07.1's Drift — `BASELINE-narrativegoldmine.md:29` ("do not assert that the archived workflow remains active") vs `BASELINE-narrativegoldmine.md:186-219` (present-tense invariants); `README.md:13, 64`.
- Failure: engineers verify against retired machinery, or skip verification believing the invariants are enforced somewhere; the record of what was ever guaranteed degrades precisely when it is needed for audit.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

## nostr-rust-forum / critical

### F-01 — Preview worker's SSRF guard is denylist-only in the shipped configuration, with no fetch timeout
- Topics: NF-07.6, NF-10.7 (S3)
- Evidence: The material states the Workers runtime "exposes no resolve-then-pin primitive, so without PREVIEW_ALLOWED_HOSTS the guard is denylist-only and DNS-rebinding-vulnerable (`nostr-bbs-preview-worker/src/ssrf.rs:13`)", that the allowlist is "the real mitigation" (`ssrf.rs:100`, `ssrf.rs:114`), and that the template never sets it — `wrangler.toml:13` declares only `ALLOWED_ORIGIN`. Separately: "there is NO wall-clock timeout — only redirect-count and body-size limits bound a fetch" (NF-07.6 N3). Redirect hops are re-checked, capped at 3 (`ssrf.rs:33`), but re-checking a hostname does not pin its DNS.
- Failure: Any member posts a link; the preview worker fetches an attacker-controlled domain that rebinds to `169.254.169.254` or an internal address after the denylist check passes, and the unfurl response exfiltrates the result back through the preview card. Independently, a deliberately slow origin holds worker subrequests open with nothing bounding wall-clock time.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in NF-07.6 N4; S3 in NF-10.7)

### F-02 — No static admin bootstrap exists in a by-the-book deployment, and the only template that ships `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` ships a *real* stranger's key as default admin
- Topics: NF-07.2, NF-08.7, NF-10.6 (DD3), NF-10.7 (S2)
- Evidence: `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` is read by the relay (`nostr-bbs-relay-worker/src/auth.rs:185`) and the auth worker (`nostr-bbs-auth-worker/src/admin.rs:71`) but declared in exactly one template — the search worker (`nostr-bbs-search-worker/wrangler.toml:41`) — where it is "a REAL 64-hex pubkey", "the only non-placeholder value in any template". The relay template asserts the opposite of the code ("there is no ADMIN_PUBKEYS reader in src/", `nostr-bbs-relay-worker/wrangler.toml:18-21`), and `SETUP.md:147-151` never lists the variable. NF-08.7 N5 states the consequence for deployment: "a by-the-book deployment has NO static admin bootstrap on relay or auth". The search worker uses this variable as its *only* admin source, with no D1 fallback (`nostr-bbs-search-worker/src/auth.rs:54`, `auth.rs:8`).
- Failure: Two failures follow. (a) Every unmodified deployment of the search worker grants `/ingest` (and `allowPrivate` vector retraction) to whoever holds the shipped key — a third party can poison or delete the semantic index of any deployment that keeps the default. (b) Operators following SETUP.md have zero static admins on relay and auth; admin functions either fail closed or force undocumented hand-D1 surgery, and the template comment actively denies the variable matters.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT DD3, S2); the shipped-key-as-implicit-backdoor implication is stated as a fact but not flagged as a compromise risk

### F-03 — Governance read endpoints are effectively public: NIP-98 with no membership check, and the admin-gated reviewer aggregate is reconstructable from them
- Topics: NF-10.11 (KF-2, KF-2a), NF-02.6
- Evidence: `docs/security/known-findings.md:23-24`: "the governance READ endpoints verify a NIP-98 signature with no membership check, so authenticated is effectively anonymous, and the admin-gated reviewer aggregate can be reconstructed from the un-gated decisions read". The route table confirms cases and decisions are `require_authed`, not admin (`governance_api.rs:561`, `governance_api.rs:626`), while reviewer telemetry is admin-gated (`governance_api.rs:1407`).
- Failure: Anyone who can generate any keypair — no whitelist membership, no invite — enumerates every broker case, decision, rationale and delegation, including moderation and appeal content; the admin-only reviewer aggregate is then rebuilt from the public decisions read, so its gate protects nothing.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt KF-2/KF-2a, recorded as "an operator decision")

### F-04 — `ENCRYPTION_ENABLED` is undeclared in every wrangler.toml, so an "encrypted" zone silently runs as plaintext end to end
- Topics: NF-08.2, NF-08.9, NF-05.16
- Evidence: "ENCRYPTION_ENABLED is read from env at zone_config.rs:86 but declared in NO wrangler.toml [vars] block in any worker — unlike DEVICE_KEYS_ENABLED it has no documented deployment default, so a deploy that forgets it silently leaves encryption OFF" (NF-08.2 N4). The relay gate is `is_encrypted = gate AND zone.encrypted AND NOT is_public_read` (`zone_config.rs:133`), so with the gate off the NIP-44 ciphertext shape check (NF-08.9) never runs. Client-side, grant sync and gift-wrap re-opening are dormant "until encryption_enabled() is true" (`app.rs:796-800`, `zone_crypto/mod.rs:99`).
- Failure: The operator marks zones `encrypted = true` in forum.toml; nothing anywhere reminds them to set the master gate. Relay accepts plaintext into the encrypted zone (shape check skipped), the client composes plaintext (gate off), and members post into a board the config — and the rendered tiles — describe as encrypted, with everything sitting readable in the events table.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT, NF-08.2 N4)

### F-05 — Self-review and original-signer guards compare pubkeys case-sensitively: the same key in two casings bypasses them
- Topics: NF-10.11 (KF-6), NF-06.15
- Evidence: `docs/security/known-findings.md:29`: the guards "compare pubkeys case-SENSITIVELY while NIP-98 returns the pubkey verbatim". NF-06.15 N1 establishes the enabling fact: "NIP-98 accepts case-insensitive hex and returns event.pubkey verbatim, and the event id is recomputed over that string, so the SAME key can legitimately present in two casings and both verify". The registry lookup was fixed (`governance_api.rs:108`) but the core aggregate guards at `governance.rs:1709` (`record_decision`) and `governance.rs:1806` (supersession) were not.
- Failure: A registered agent publishes its 31402 under one casing and its deciding 31403 under the other. Registry admission canonicalises and passes; the core self-review check (`CaseError::SelfReview`) and the original-signer supersession-authority check compare raw strings, miss the match, and the agent decides its own case or supersedes a decision it was not authorised to supersede.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt KF-6)

### F-06 — With `DEVICE_KEYS_ENABLED` off (the shipped default), a known device-to-owner mapping is ignored and revocation has no effect at AUTH
- Topics: NF-02.7, NF-10.7 (S4), NF-08.8
- Evidence: NF-02.7 DIVERGENCE note: "with the gate off a known device to owner mapping is IGNORED and revocation has no effect at AUTH". Both templates ship `"false"` (`nostr-bbs-auth-worker/wrangler.toml:68`, `nostr-bbs-relay-worker/wrangler.toml:29`); the gate enables only on the exact string `"true"` (`devices.rs:100`, `devices.rs:112`). The `device_keys` table is created idempotently on first use in the relay D1 (`devices.rs:123`).
- Failure: A deployment that ever ran with device keys on (or has hand-seeded rows), then redeployed from the default templates, silently stops resolving device keys to owners: a device key whose row says `revoked = 1` still authenticates, and sanctions recorded against the owner do not bind the device at AUTH.
- Confidence: medium — the ignored-mapping behavior is stated; the exploitation path (rows present while the gate is off) is inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in NF-02.7; S4)

### F-07 — Moderation gates key on the raw signing pubkey while the write gate resolves device keys to owners; gift wraps have no author to moderate at all
- Topics: NF-10.11 (KF-4, KF-9), NF-03.4, NF-03.5
- Evidence: `docs/security/known-findings.md:27` and `:44`: "the write gate resolves a device key to its owner, but the moderation gates that follow key on the RAW signing pubkey, and a gift wrap has no author to resolve at all". The pipeline resolves the principal once at step 6 (`nip_handlers.rs:1121`) and deliberately excludes gift wraps from author-keyed gating (`nip_handlers.rs:56-65`, exclusion at `:69-75`, NF-03.5 N2). Note the internal tension: NF-03.4 step 9 claims suspension/silence is "checked for the device key AND its owner" (`nip_handlers.rs:1189`) — the material asserts both things about the same path without reconciling them.
- Failure: An owner under ban or mute publishes ban-gated kinds through their device key wherever the later checks use the raw signing pubkey; the gift-wrap path is structurally unmoderated by author, bounded only by recipient whitelisting.
- Confidence: medium — the finding is marked live, but the material is internally inconsistent about which gates key on which identity
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt KF-4/KF-9); the contradiction with NF-03.4 step 9 is unmarked

### F-08 — The material contradicts itself, same date, about whether the trust sweep can commit a false audit row: NF-03.9 says the residual defect remains, NF-10.10 says it is fixed and tested
- Topics: NF-03.9, NF-10.10, NF-11.11, NF-11.12
- Evidence: NF-03.9 (DemotionDecision note), stamped 2026-09-07: "Remaining: its unconditional audit INSERT can commit when the optimistic UPDATE changes zero rows; the conflict is detected after the batch. See NF-11.12 for the mechanism and NF-10.10 for the residual case." NF-10.10, same date: "The audit INSERT is conditional inside the transaction; both result counts must equal one. Five exact-SQL trust tests cover concurrent level/activity/admin edits, missing rows, nullable snapshots, rollback and idempotency", with a diagram showing "Conditional audit SELECT inserts zero rows → Conflict, no false audit fact". NF-10.8 N1 and NF-11.12 also disagree in flavour about what remains open for ADR-2006.
- Failure: One of the two statements is false. If NF-03.9 is right, a concurrent admin-grant or level change during a sweep writes an `admin_log` row recording a demotion that never happened — a false fact in the same table every admin mutation is audited into (`audit.rs:25`) — and the sweep's outcome counters misreport. If NF-10.10 is right, the area doc misleads any operator or auditor reading NF-03.9 about a live defect class. Either way, the audit trail's reliability cannot be established from the record.
- Confidence: high — the contradiction is textual; which half is true is not judgeable
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — The entire realtime relay plane is one singleton Durable Object
- Topics: NF-03.1, NF-11.1, NF-11.5
- Evidence: "The DO is a SINGLETON: every socket goes to get_by_name main" (`nostr-bbs-relay-worker/src/lib.rs:230`, NF-03.1 N1). NF-11.1 shows all sessions, rate limits and caches live in that one object, and NF-11.5 shows every saved event's broadcast iterate every session with per-session `protected_read_permitted` and `resolve_viewer_context`. No sharding or second instance appears anywhere in the material.
- Failure: All WebSocket traffic, admission and fan-out for the deployment funnel through a single object: it is a single point of failure (an unhealthy DO takes down the whole relay plane, not one channel), a hard throughput and memory ceiling, and a blast radius that cannot be partitioned per channel or per tenant. Adding a second relay instance does not relieve it — both would still be singletons of their own.
- Confidence: medium — the singleton is stated (high); the failure ceiling is inferred from stated architecture
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — The account root key (nsec) is distributed in a printable sheet, a QR, and a URL fragment
- Topics: NF-05.8, NF-12.1
- Evidence: The recovery sheet renders "nsec QR generated in-WASM (`recovery_sheet.rs:66`, `:294`)" and "a /connect magic link with the nsec in the URL FRAGMENT (`recovery_sheet.rs:290`)". The material's defence is only that "the nsec travels in the fragment (#k=), which browsers do not send to the server" — fragments are nonetheless persisted in browser history on the device, readable by any script on the origin via `location.hash`, visible to extensions and screen-sharing, and re-leaked wholesale if the "magic link" is shared or pasted. `nip19.rs:85` is where the nsec is encoded.
- Failure: A user recovers via the magic link on a shared or compromised machine, or forwards the link; the full root key — from which every subkey is recoverable (K6, `keys.rs:240`) — is in the history entry or the pasted URL, and the account is taken over with no relay-side signal.
- Confidence: medium — mechanism stated; the leak vectors beyond "not sent to server" are inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (presented as a mitigation, not a risk)

### F-11 — Schema bootstrap fails silently and the auth worker runs its DDL storm on every request
- Topics: NF-08.5, NF-08.6, NF-10.11 (KF-3), NF-09.5
- Evidence: NF-08.6 Debt: "the migration cannot repair an existing table — SQLite has no ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS… the 0009 file… leaves the two ALTERs to… ensure_schema, which discards their result (let _ =), so a real failure there is silent (`migrations/0009_events.sql:12-17`, `lib.rs:835`)". KF-3 drift note: the relay now latches once per isolate, but "the auth worker still bootstraps on every request (`nostr-bbs-auth-worker/src/lib.rs:163`)", with the original row describing ~75 DDL statements per request (`known-findings.md:26`).
- Failure: An ALTER that fails on a legacy table produces no error anywhere; the first symptom is that `save_event` — which writes `d_tag` and `received_at` on every insert — fails every write, the exact 66ca8d8 failure class returning invisibly. Independently, every auth-worker request (including each passkey ceremony step) pays the per-request DDL round-trips against D1, multiplying latency and row quotas for the most security-sensitive worker.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt in NF-08.6; DRIFT in KF-3)

### F-12 — An unlabelled consequential request folds to `medium`, below the rationale gate: nothing requires task properties to be present
- Topics: NF-06.12, NF-06.13, NF-08.8
- Evidence: "an entirely unlabelled request folds to the relay's advertised ESCALATION_DEFAULT_TIER" (`governance.rs:525`, `nostr-bbs-relay-worker/wrangler.toml:61` = medium). The rationale requirement fires only for High or Critical (`governance.rs:730-733`), and "nothing ever FILLS IN a rationale — absence is refused" (`governance.rs:761`) — but absence of *labels* is not refused, it is defaulted. ADR-2011's stated intent is that "the OPERATOR's task properties set the escalation boundary" (NF-06.12 N1); tightening-only merge cannot raise a floor that was never declared.
- Failure: An operator deploys a panel without `tp-*` tags (or an agent omits them and the panel is unlabelled): every approve/reject/promote/demote on that panel is admitted at medium — no rationale required, calibration sampling at medium's rules — and the human-accountability floor ADR-2011 exists for is silently absent rather than loudly refused.
- Confidence: high for the mechanism (stated); the operational gap is an inferred consequence
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (the fold is stated as a fact under DEF; the accountability gap is not flagged)

### F-13 — Shipped manifests turn the advisory Jev flags ON by default: member search queries and post snippets leave for a third-party endpoint, and one flag is on with no endpoint at all
- Topics: NF-07.9, NF-08.8, NF-02.2, NF-06.19
- Evidence: "Since eb5f232 the search wrangler.toml sets JEV_SEARCH_RERANK = "1" with the cloud endpoint, so the shipped manifest is ON and needs a SUBQ_API_KEY Worker secret (`nostr-bbs-search-worker/wrangler.toml:32-36`)" and "With the flag on, the query text and up to ten public snippets leave the Worker (`docs/jev-option.md:35`)". NF-08.8 N8: the relay ships `JEV_TRIAGE=1` (`relay wrangler.toml:11`) pointed at the same cloud endpoint, and the auth worker ships `JEV_RATIONALE_CHECK=1` while "declar[ing] no endpoint at all (`wrangler.toml:54-58`), so the route reports unavailable". The library default is off (`jev-option.md:4-5`) — the shipped manifests contradict it.
- Failure: Every default deployment exfiltrates member search text and public post content to an external service inside the request path (with a remote-call latency/availability dependency that only falls back on failure, not before sending); the relay triage flag is on with its data flow unspecified in this material; and the auth rationale route is live-but-unavailable by default, shipping a dead endpoint configuration as the checked-in state.
- Confidence: high for the search egress (stated); the relay triage data flow is not described
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partly — the ON status is stated in prose and the missing-uplift is marked Open; the egress-by-default itself is not framed as a defect

### F-14 — solid-pod-rs moved from the mandated exact pin to a caret range on an alpha, and the pod's authorization-critical policy is delegated to it
- Topics: NF-01.4, NF-04.3, NF-04.9, NF-10.1 (I6)
- Evidence: "ADR-2007 and BASELINE invariant 6 say solid-pod-rs stays an EXACT (=) pin (`docs/adr/ADR-2007...:30`, `docs/BASELINE-architecture.md:192`), but the manifest now declares a caret range under estate register ER-35 (`Cargo.toml:181-185`)" — `0.5.0-alpha.14`. ADR-2012 decision 4 still speaks of "the exact pin at Cargo.toml:155" (`ADR-2012...:76-80`). Meanwhile the sidecar escalation guard "delegates to the SHARED upstream policy effective_acl_target (`acl.rs:34`, `acl.rs:75-77`)" and the payments vocabulary is "re-exported from solid_pod_rs::payments rather than reimplemented (`payments.rs:34`)".
- Failure: Any fresh resolve can pull a different `0.5.0-alpha.*` — code that decides who gets `acl:Control` over a pod and how payments are credited — without review, and the ADRs that mandated the pin now describe a manifest that no longer obeys them, so no rule is enforceable. The previously-documented control (empty `allow-git` making the exact pin meaningful, NF-09.2 N1) no longer binds anything.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION, NF-01.4 note and NF-04 closing paragraph)

### F-15 — Every link preview embeds a Google favicon, leaking members' reading to a third party in an otherwise egress-minimal design
- Topics: NF-07.7, NF-07.5
- Evidence: "favicon via Google's s2 service, not the target site (`parse.rs:172`)" and the note: "The favicon choice is an outbound third-party dependency in an otherwise LAN-shaped design — every preview embeds a google.com favicon URL (`parse.rs:172`)".
- Failure: Every rendered preview card sends each reader's browser to `google.com` with the previewed site's domain in the request — a permanent, always-on correlation of member IPs and content interests to a third party that the preview worker's own SSRF posture otherwise tries to avoid; there is no configuration to switch it off shown anywhere in the material.
- Confidence: high for the mechanism; the privacy impact is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (described in a note as a dependency observation, not marked Tension/Debt/Drift/Open)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Whether the NIP-98 replay table is ever purged.** The material gives only `REPLAY_CACHE_TTL_SECS` as a *cache* bound (`nip98.rs:71`); nothing states a retention or deletion job for the D1 replay rows that every pod LDP request, admin call and ingest inserts. I would need the replay schema and any cron in `nostr-bbs-rate-limit`/worker crons before signing off on unbounded-write behavior.

2. **Whether the search worker's hash-embedding fallback registers as a distinct model in the RVF Meta segment.** NF-07.3 says inference failure falls back to a deterministic hash embedding, and separately that model mismatch returns 409; whether a fallback *query* against a real-model index yields 409 or silently wrong neighbours is unstated. I would need `embed.rs`/`store.rs` Meta handling.

3. **Actual D1 headroom.** The material documents the historical free-tier exhaustion that motivated ADR-2018 caching (`read_cache.rs:5-15`) and the auth worker's per-request DDL, but no current row-read/write measurements; whether present traffic sits near D1 limits is not judgeable.

4. **The consistency semantics of the KV-backed rate limiters.** `check_rate_limit` against `SESSIONS`, `RATE_LIMIT` and `SEARCH_CONFIG` KV is described, but not its read-modify-write pattern; whether bursts can exceed the advertised budgets under KV's eventual consistency needs the limiter implementation.

5. **Who owns the shipped real `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` key** (`nostr-bbs-search-worker/wrangler.toml:41`). Whether it is the kit maintainers' key (an implicit backdoor on default deploys) or a well-formed placeholder needs the key's provenance and commit history.

## nostr-rust-forum / premortem

# Post-mortem — nostr-rust-forum production incident (reconstructed from the verified diagrams-as-code record at commit `eb5f232`)

Scope note: the material below is the only source. Every claim carries the citation the record gives; where I reason past the record I say so. Ordering inside a chain is only asserted where the record asserts it.

---

### R-1 — "Encrypted" zones run in plaintext because `ENCRYPTION_ENABLED` is declared in no template, no SETUP step and no validator
- Topics: NF-08.2, NF-08.8, NF-05.16, NF-09.4
- Chain of events:
  1. Operator marks `encrypted = true` on the family/business zones in `forum.toml`. The validator's only encryption rule rejects `encrypted && visibility = public` (`nostr-bbs-config/src/validate.rs:250`) — it does not check the master gate, so the config passes.
  2. `ENCRYPTION_ENABLED` appears in no `[vars]` block of any wrangler.toml and in no SETUP step (`nostr-rust-forum/crates/nostr-bbs-relay-worker/src/zone_config.rs:86`; NF-08.2 N4, NF-08.8 N7). The relay env var is unset, so the master gate is false.
  3. `is_encrypted = gate AND zone.encrypted AND NOT is_public_read` (`zone_config.rs:133`) is therefore false for every zone. The encrypted-zone ciphertext shape check (`zone_config.rs:208`, `relay_do/nip_handlers.rs:1458`) never fires; the relay treats the zone as ordinary.
  4. Client side: the estate's stated convention gates "encrypted" on the same flag — the BBS client's `board_is_encrypted` gates on `ENCRYPTION_ENABLED` **and** the zone (`nostr-bbs-bbs-client/src/screens.rs:906-918`) and forum grant-sync is dormant until `encryption_enabled()` (`nostr-bbs-forum-client/src/app.rs:796-800`, `zone_crypto/mod.rs:99`). Inferred: the forum `ZoneWriter` likewise treats the zone as non-encrypted and passes content through (`zone_crypto/store.rs:480-491`).
  5. Members post sensitive content into a zone they and the operator believe is encrypted. Plaintext lands in the `events` D1, is broadcast, and is matched by NIP-50 text search — the zk-tag discard is **client-side** (`nostr-bbs-forum-client/src/components/global_search.rs:733-742`) and plaintext carries no zk tag.
  6. Discovered months later: the whole period's history is plaintext, was searchable by any member the whole time, and enabling the gate now does not encrypt stored rows.
- Evidence: NF-08.2 N4 ("a deploy that forgets it silently leaves encryption OFF"); NF-08.8 N7 (lockstep gate "undeclared in every wrangler.toml"); `zone_config.rs:77`, `:86`, `:133`; `validate.rs:250`; `store.rs:480-491`; `screens.rs:906-918`; `app.rs:796-800`.
- Warning signs visible today: the gate is absent from all five templates and from SETUP.md; nothing warns at validate time; the BBS client already demonstrates that "zone.encrypted" alone is not what clients test.
- Confidence: medium — the relay-side silence and the undeclared gate are stated; the forum-client plaintext passthrough is inferred from the BBS client's stated gating convention.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — preview-worker SSRF guard is denylist-only by default (acknowledged DNS-rebinding-vulnerable, no timeout) and its 10-day preview cache turns a hit into persistent in-app phishing
- Topics: NF-07.5, NF-07.6, NF-07.7
- Chain of events:
  1. Deployment from the templates: the preview template declares only `ALLOWED_ORIGIN` (`nostr-bbs-preview-worker/wrangler.toml:13`); `PREVIEW_ALLOWED_HOSTS` is unset, so the allowlist installed at `lib.rs:511`/`ssrf.rs:153` is empty.
  2. The guard runs denylist-only, which the code itself says is DNS-rebinding-vulnerable because the Workers runtime exposes no resolve-then-pin primitive (`nostr-bbs-preview-worker/src/ssrf.rs:13`).
  3. A member posts an attacker URL; the hostname passes the literal/IP checks (`ssrf.rs:294-466`) on its first resolution; the fetch resolves again at request time (rebind). There is no wall-clock timeout — only `MAX_REDIRECTS 3` (`ssrf.rs:33`) and body-size caps (`ssrf.rs:38`, `:44`) — so hangs are bounded only by size and hop count.
  4. The attacker-controlled "OpenGraph" response is cached under the synthetic internal URL (`lib.rs:149`, `:157`) for 10 days (`lib.rs:32`) and rendered as a preview card inside the forum to every member who later posts the same link. The CORS origin restriction (`lib.rs:95`, `:102`) does not constrain the card's content.
  5. Spoofed title/image/domain on a trusted surface → credential phishing of members; per-IP 30/60s rate limit (`lib.rs:519`) does not bound the cached blast radius. (Minor adjacent leak: every preview embeds a google.com favicon URL, `parse.rs:172`.)
- Evidence: `ssrf.rs:13`; template `wrangler.toml:13`; `lib.rs:32-34`, `:149`, `:157`, `:519`; NF-07.6 N3 (no timeout), N4 (denylist-only without allowlist); NF-07.5 N2 (SSRF check before cache).
- Warning signs visible today: the code carries its own vulnerability acknowledgment; the shipped template guarantees the vulnerable configuration.
- Confidence: medium — the vulnerability class and default exposure are stated by the code; the rebinding-and-cache exploit chain is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Admin authority is undeclared where it is read and pre-seeded with a real third-party key where it is not: inherited admin on search `/ingest`, zero-admin lockout on relay/auth
- Topics: NF-08.7, NF-07.1, NF-07.2, NF-10.6 (DD3), NF-10.7 (S2)
- Chain of events:
  1. Operator deploys per SETUP.md, which never lists `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` (`SETUP.md:147-151`).
  2. The relay reads `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` at `nostr-bbs-relay-worker/src/auth.rs:185` and the auth worker at `nostr-bbs-auth-worker/src/admin.rs:71`, but neither template declares the var — and the relay template falsely asserts "there is no ADMIN_PUBKEYS reader in src/" (`nostr-bbs-relay-worker/wrangler.toml:18-21`).
  3. Relay authority is therefore static ∪ `whitelist.is_admin` only (`auth.rs:185`, `:194`); auth unions three sources (`admin.rs:57`). With no static set and no hand-made whitelist row, a by-the-book deployment has no admin at all — and `admins/add`, governance register and every admin-gated surface refuse the operator (lockout twin).
  4. Simultaneously, the search-worker template ships a **real 64-hex pubkey** as `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` (`nostr-bbs-search-worker/wrangler.toml:41`) — the only non-placeholder admin value in any template. Every deployment cloned from it inherits that key's owner as an admin.
  5. That holder passes NIP-98 into `POST /ingest`, the only mutating route and admin-gated (`nostr-bbs-search-worker/src/lib.rs:716` via `auth.rs:63`), and curates member-visible search results and public snippets — an in-app misinformation/phishing surface on any inheriting deploy — or retracts legitimate vectors.
- Evidence: NF-08.7 N4, N5; NF-10.6 DD3; NF-10.7 S2; NF-07.2 N2, N3; `admin_shared.rs:91`; `search-worker/src/auth.rs:54`, `:86`.
- Warning signs visible today: the false template comment, the SETUP gap, and the real pubkey are all recorded as live at this commit.
- Confidence: medium — every fact is stated; the incident is the material's own predicted consequence ("a by-the-book deployment has NO static admin bootstrap", NF-08.7 N5).
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — A decided governance action is not a applied action: relay OK certifies storage only, and no consumer receipt exists
- Topics: NF-03.10, NF-06.10, NF-10.8, NF-10.12, NF-11.10
- Evidence: ADR-2010 ledger `proposed / partial / staged` (`docs/adr/ADR-2010-durable-governance-outcome-receipts.md:5-7`); "the relay OK certified STORAGE ONLY… accepted but NOT applied" (`nip_handlers.rs:1630-1642`), surfaced by log (`nip_handlers.rs:1635`); consumer implementations remain separate and absent (`relay_do/receipts.rs` OPEN note; NF-11.10 N4 "a relay receipt still cannot prove external application"); exactly one live consumer (`README.md:120`); `Demote` has no confirmed downstream consumer (NF-10.12 N1).
- Failure: a human approves a promote/demote; projection fails or the consumer never reads it; the estate acts on the old corpus state while the decision card shows a signed decision. Twelve months of ontology elevation run on a stage machine whose last mile is unwitnessed.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open / Tension)

### F-02 — The signing key is persisted in browser storage; any script execution on the forum origin is total custody loss
- Topics: NF-05.4, NF-05.8, NF-05.17
- Evidence: session key persisted in `localStorage` or `sessionStorage` by remember-me (`auth/session.rs:156`, `:163`); ADR-2023 itself notes "the nsec lives in localStorage" (`ADR-2023…:25`); the recovery sheet renders the nsec as a QR and puts it in a `/connect` fragment (`recovery_sheet.rs:66`, `:290`, `:294`). In-memory hygiene (StoredValue, Zeroizing, pagehide — `auth/mod.rs:107`, `:232`, `:831`) covers only RAM. The pod's CSP sandbox protects the pod origin only (`pod-worker/src/lib.rs:207`).
- Failure: one stored-XSS or hostile script on the forum origin reads the persisted key; because derived subkeys are root-recoverable (`keys.rs:240`), the whole identity plane including device keys falls with it. No XSS vector is stated in the material — the persistence is.
- Confidence: medium (persistence stated; vector inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — Moderation gates key on the raw signing pubkey while the write gate resolves the device principal; gift wraps have no author at all
- Topics: NF-03.4, NF-03.5, NF-02.7, NF-10.11
- Evidence: KF-4/KF-9 — "the write gate resolves a device key to its owner, but the moderation gates that follow key on the RAW signing pubkey, and a gift wrap has no author to resolve" (`docs/security/known-findings.md:27`, `:44`). Note the conflict: NF-03.4 step 9 claims suspension/silence are "checked for the device key AND its owner" (`nip_handlers.rs:1189`) — the diagram and the findings ledger disagree; an engineer must determine which is true.
- Failure: once device keys are enabled (they ship off, NF-02.7), a suspended or banned member publishes through a freshly registered device key; separately, gift wraps are deliberately excluded from ban gating (`nip_handlers.rs:56-65`), so banned authors keep publishing wraps.
- Confidence: medium (the finding is recorded; the diagram conflict is unresolved in the material)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, KF-4/KF-9)

### F-04 — Governance read endpoints treat "authenticated" as anonymous: any keyholder reads cases and decisions, and can reconstruct the admin-gated reviewer aggregate
- Topics: NF-02.6, NF-06.9, NF-02.10, NF-10.11
- Evidence: KF-2/KF-2a — "the governance READ endpoints verify a NIP-98 signature with no membership check, so authenticated is effectively anonymous, and the admin-gated reviewer aggregate can be reconstructed from the un-gated decisions read" (`known-findings.md:23`, `:24`); list agents/cases/decisions/roles are `require_authed` only (NF-06.9 note); decisions read endpoint `governance_api.rs:619`; reviewer telemetry admin-only `governance_api.rs:1407`.
- Failure: an outsider with any key enumerates the full governance record — case subjects, summaries, decisions, reviewer behaviour — on a forum whose zones and moderation are otherwise membership-scoped.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, KF-2/KF-2a)

### F-05 — NIP-42 relay-tag check silently skipped while `RELAY_URL` is blank (the shipped default)
- Topics: NF-03.2, NF-03.3, NF-08.8
- Evidence: `nip42.rs:168-172` skips the relay-tag check when `own_relay_url` is blank; the template ships it blank (`nostr-bbs-relay-worker/wrangler.toml:48-51`) with only a console error per AUTH (`nip42.rs:230`, `nip_handlers.rs:2054`); SETUP requires it only in prose (`SETUP.md:158`).
- Failure: AUTH events minted for a different relay are accepted here. The per-session challenge still binds (`nip42.rs:191-196`), so the practical loss is the defence-in-depth relay binding — a weakened, not broken, gate. Report as a checkable gap, not a breach claim.
- Confidence: high (skip stated; impact bounded by the challenge binding)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — Two divergent hardcoded relay-URL fallbacks: with `window.__ENV__` missing, the client talks to the wrong relay
- Topics: NF-05.7, NF-05.5
- Evidence: O8 "STILL LIVE" — `relay.rs:1045` reads `window.__ENV__` inline instead of the shared helper, and the two fallbacks diverge (`nostr-bbs-forum-client/src/relay.rs:22` vs `utils/relay_url.rs:9`).
- Failure: on a mis-injected deploy the socket connects to fallback A while REST bases resolve to fallback B: signed events and NIP-98 tokens (whose `u` tag binds the canonical URL, `nip98.rs:468`) are minted against the wrong origin. If a stale fallback domain later lapses and is re-registered, events and tokens are handed to a stranger's server. Domain re-registration is inferred; the divergence is stated.
- Confidence: medium (divergence stated; capture scenario inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (O8, anomaly live)

### F-07 — The NIP-50 SEARCH path's viewer authorization is unstated, and ciphertext matches are discarded client-side only
- Topics: NF-05.10, NF-05.14, NF-11.14, NF-11.15
- Evidence: the REQ path's per-event `authorize_event`/`protected_read_permitted` gates are documented (`nip_handlers.rs:1883`, `:2280`); the material documents NIP-50 handlers (`profiles.rs:251`, advertised `nip11.rs:207`) and relay text search returning matches (`global_search.rs:744-753`) but never states that SEARCH runs the same viewer gates; zone-encrypted hits are dropped only in the client by zk tag (`global_search.rs:733-742`).
- Failure: if SEARCH skips viewer authorization, withheld or membership-gated content is discoverable by any client; independently, any non-forum client performing NIP-50 receives ciphertext events (author, timestamps, existence of encrypted posts) because the discard is client-side.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (authorization unstated; ciphertext-serving is stated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — Banned members keep delivering gift-wrapped DMs to members
- Topics: NF-03.5, NF-03.6
- Evidence: "Gift wraps are DELIBERATELY excluded from ban gating… The recipient-whitelist gate is what bounds them instead" (`nip_handlers.rs:56-65`, exclusion visible `:69-75`); the ban-gated kind list (`nip_handlers.rs:68-75`) does not include 1059; recipient gate only requires the recipient be whitelisted (`nip_handlers.rs:1142`).
- Failure: a banned harasser continues DMing every member indefinitely; each wrap comes from a fresh throwaway key, so the recipient-whitelist bound does nothing to stop it. Presented as design in the record — report as a defect to check, since ban enforcement is the client-facing promise.
- Confidence: high (behaviour stated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — The solid-pod-rs exact-pin invariant is broken to a caret range in the settlement path
- Topics: NF-01.4, NF-04.9, NF-04.11
- Evidence: TENSION — ADR-2007 and BASELINE invariant 6 require an exact `=` pin (`docs/adr/ADR-2007…:30`, `docs/BASELINE-architecture.md:192`) but the manifest declares `^0.5.0-alpha.14` under ER-35 (`nostr-rust-forum/Cargo.toml:181-185`); the payment surface re-exports upstream payments vocabulary (`payments.rs:34`) and `derive_deposit_address` is frozen as live (`deposit_address.rs:120`); ADR-2012 D5 freezes derivation as a published contract (`keys.rs:251-265`).
- Failure: a routine `cargo update` pulls a later alpha whose derivation or ledger semantics shift; deposits are credited under a mismatched derivation or addresses diverge between stack halves. `Cargo.lock` holds today; nothing enforces the invariant the ADRs demand.
- Confidence: medium (tension stated; the drift event is inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-10 — The trust sweep may still commit an audit fact for an unchanged row — the record contradicts itself
- Topics: NF-03.9, NF-10.10, NF-11.11, NF-11.12
- Evidence: NF-03.9's note says "Remaining: its unconditional audit INSERT can commit when the optimistic UPDATE changes zero rows; the conflict is detected after the batch", pointing at NF-10.10 as "the residual case"; NF-10.10's own text says the audit INSERT is conditional and both counts must equal one; NF-11.12 declares the OFFSET/ignored-write defects historical.
- Failure: if the conditional does not actually bind to the UPDATE's row count in a D1 batch, `admin_log` records demotions that never committed and the sweep's counters misreport — false audit facts in the trust system. The material itself cannot decide; the SQL must be checked.
- Confidence: medium (self-conflicting record)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt note in NF-03.9)

### F-11 — `ensure_schema` discards ALTER results: a failed column add is silent and every subsequent save fails
- Topics: NF-08.5, NF-08.6
- Evidence: "the bootstrap loop… discards its result (let _ =), so a real failure there is silent" (`nostr-bbs-relay-worker/src/lib.rs:835`); the two column additions live only as a header comment in the migration (`migrations/0009_events.sql:12-17`); `save_event` inserts `d_tag`/`received_at` on every write (`relay_do/storage.rs:80`); `SCHEMA_READY` latches on CREATE success, not ALTER success (`lib.rs:200-203`).
- Failure: a deploy whose `events` table predates the columns takes a silent ALTER failure, reports schema ready, and then fails every EVENT save — a full publishing outage with a green-looking bootstrap.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-12 — The cross-stack `derive_subkey` parity proof runs in CI but does not gate merges
- Topics: NF-09.1, NF-09.3, NF-10.7, NF-12.3
- Evidence: the drift-gates job runs `identity-vector-parity.mjs` and the anti-drift lint (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:293`, `:296`) but is absent from the `ci-pass` needs list (`ci.yml:306-314`, S6); the Rust known-answer vector is `keys.rs:478`/`:483` and both halves read one fixture (`identity-vector-parity.mjs:6`); the contract is byte-for-byte with agentbox's JS mirror (ADR-2003).
- Failure: a keys.rs change that drifts the HMAC derivation merges on a red-but-ignored drift job; agentbox-minted subkeys and the forum's identity plane silently diverge across the estate.
- Confidence: medium
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (S6)

### F-13 — Jev advisory flags ship ON in all three manifests: search queries egress to a cloud endpoint by default; the rationale route is on with no endpoint
- Topics: NF-07.9, NF-02.2, NF-06.19, NF-08.8
- Evidence: `JEV_SEARCH_RERANK = "1"` with the cloud endpoint (`nostr-bbs-search-worker/wrangler.toml:32-36`), "the query text and up to ten public snippets leave the Worker" (`docs/jev-option.md:35`); `JEV_TRIAGE=1` (`relay wrangler.toml:11`); `JEV_RATIONALE_CHECK = "1"` with no endpoint declared, so the route answers unavailable (`auth wrangler.toml:53-58`; NF-06.19).
- Failure: member search text and content silently leave the deployment's boundary in a default configuration for a "proposed research candidate with no measured uplift" (`jev-option.md:11`); the advisory rationale check is dead weight that reports unavailable.
- Confidence: high (all stated)
- Severity: low

### F-14 — RiskTier parsing fails open to Medium, and an unlabelled consequential request folds to the relay's advertised medium default
- Topics: NF-06.12, NF-06.13, NF-11.14
- Evidence: "parse of an unlabelled or unrecognised tier falls back to the Medium default" (`governance.rs:186`, `:204`); "an entirely unlabelled request folds to the relay's advertised ESCALATION_DEFAULT_TIER" (`governance.rs:525`, default medium at `relay wrangler.toml:61`); the rationale gate fires only at High/Critical (`governance.rs:730-733`); member suppression only ever hides Low (`governance.rs:217`).

A human still signs the 31403, so this weakens audit and sampling rather than autonomy (autonomy thresholds are unstated).

PanelState, PanelUpdate and PanelRetired exist as kind constants and doc rows only" (README.md:111-116 vs `governance.rs:102`, `:1109`, `:1135`); validation requires only kind-in-range plus a non-empty d tag (`governance.rs:1216`, `:1222`); the client registry records the newest 31401/31404 per address (`panel_registry.rs:199`, `:461`).

## ontomark / critical

### F-01 — The running VisionClaw pipeline still ships the unfixed fail-open redactor and the frontmatter credential-scan gap
- Topics: OM-00.2, OM-00.3, OM-05.5, OM-06.5
- Evidence: OM-00.3 records that "VisionClaw's vault build job still ships S1.1, S1.2, S1.5, S2.3 and S3.1 unchanged until VC-60 retires" (ADR-OM-005-estate-alignment…md:11). S1.1 is "prose redactor failed OPEN on a regex compile error", inherited from `vault/src/projection.rs:230`; S1.2 is the body-only credential scan while render publishes frontmatter (`vault/src/build/publish.rs:159-160, 196`). The job is live today: `ontology-publish.yml:167-175` builds the in-tree `vault` crate and `ontology-publish.yml:247` feeds its output to `pack-pod-resources.py`; ADR-OM-005 decision 2 retires it only "gated, so it keeps running". The 8d654d1 fixes protect the ontomark path only.
- Failure: a credential in a frontmatter value (title, sources) on a public page is published by the VisionClaw job into pod resources consumed by agentbox; a regex compile error in the legacy redactor publishes private names unredacted. ontomark's register fixes do not cover this producer.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (EXTERNAL)

### F-02 — ADR-OM-009 says "every published closure carries a derivation certificate"; the default build writes and checks nothing
- Topics: OM-03.8, OM-04.1
- Evidence: ADR-OM-009 is titled "every published closure carries a derivation certificate" and OM-04.1 says it "decides every published closure carries one" (ADR-OM-009…md:14). But `certificate_out` "defaults to absent and the build writes and checks nothing then" (`build/mod.rs:730`, `ontomark-cli/src/main.rs:401`), and the C3 artefact listing "names no certificate output" (`sovereign-corpus-contracts.md:111`) while certificate files go to the `--certificate-out` directory, "never under out" (`build/mod.rs:715-717`).
- Failure: any build without `--certificate-out` (local rebuild, a second consumer, disaster recovery) publishes a bundle with no warrant and no parity check; a classifier regression ships undetected, and a consumer relying on the ADR's promise finds nothing to verify. Only the visionGraph workflow passes the flag.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open in OM-03.8; DRIFT in OM-04.1)

### F-03 — Public and machine bundles share content_digest, ontology_digest and generation id while containing different pages
- Topics: OM-06.1, OM-06.5
- Evidence: OM-06.1's invariant: "data/ (bar the markers) and okf/ are byte-identical across scopes … ontology_digest and content_digest are equal" (test `machine_and_public_builds_share_every_ontology_artefact`, `machine_scope.rs:143`), while the scopes differ in `api/pages/working/`, the search index, page_count and markers. `generation_id` is `prefix@commit(+dirty)` (`build/generation.rs:150-169`) — identical for both scopes. C3 only warns: "a consumer cache keyed on ontology_digest must also key on scope" (`sovereign-corpus-contracts.md:148-152`).
- Failure: a consumer (Loom mirror, site cache, pod pack) keys on digest or generation id; a machine-scope and a public build of the same commit collide on both; the cache serves machine artefacts — working-vault pages and the working-inclusive search index — as the public site, or stale public data to machine consumers. Nothing in the bundle identity distinguishes them; only the consumer remembering the rule does.
- Confidence: high (facts stated); the cache confusion is the stated obligation failing — inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (stated as a consumer obligation in C3, not tracked as a defect)

### F-04 — The compat projection flag is a shipped CLI option that publishes held-back content and skips link redaction
- Topics: OM-00.2 (S3.4), OM-02.3, OM-03.4, OM-05.5
- Evidence: "the compat path never forces misc/ pages private (held_back is only applied in the link path `ir/projection.rs:616`); a public misc/ page stays public there, while stage_redacted skips it `build/publish.rs:220`" (OM-02.3 n1). The path is reachable via `--no-redact-private-links` (`main.rs:434-441`, overlaying the profile at `main.rs:815`, `profile.rs:422`) and OM-05.5 records the legacy parity target "kept private links". Authors' disposition is KEEP for byte parity, pinned by `conformance.rs:230,245`.
- Failure: an operator passes the flag (runbook copy, legacy comparison): every machine artefact — page API, search index, markdown mirror — includes pages the publication rule holds back, publish/ and the API disagree about what is published, and the projection runs without the Step-1 private-link rewrite that the default path applies.
- Confidence: high on the held-back divergence; medium on prose-link exposure under the flag (not fully specified)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (KEEP, S3.4, DIVERGENCE)

### F-05 — `--vault working` builds and publishes the unreviewed working vault as the public corpus, contradicting the code's own comments
- Topics: OM-03.2, OM-05.2
- Evidence: comments claim "the projections are knowledge/'s by definition" (`main.rs:786-787`, `build/mod.rs:76-77`), yet "`--vault working` alone makes the WHOLE projection come from working/" (`build/mod.rs:76-77` context; `main.rs:796-803` sets `vault_root` to the working folder with `with_working_publish` false). `root_of` refuses only a missing folder (`profile.rs:561-576`).
- Failure: on the estate layout, `ontomark build --vault working --out … --publish-out site` succeeds and publishes the working vault's content as the public ontology and site. Nothing refuses it — the machine-scope guard refuses `--vault working` only for `--scope machine`.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT; the behaviour itself is stated as an invariant)

### F-06 — `publish_out` is written after `promote`, so a failure there leaves two live generations
- Topics: OM-03.5, OM-03.1
- Evidence: "write_to runs AFTER promote, so a failure there leaves the NEW bundle beside the PREVIOUS publication tree that swap_in kept, the two no longer one generation" (`build/publish.rs:356`, `build/mod.rs:1018-1020`, OM-03.5 N2). The S2.3 fix made each destination atomic; the pair is not.
- Failure: the publish-tree write fails (disk full, permissions) after `out` is already swapped in: `out` holds generation N, the live site tree holds N−1, the run returns an error but does not roll `out` back. Site visitors and bundle consumers (rvdb promotion) act on mixed generations with no marker reconciling them.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-07 — The promote swap has a window with no `out` at all, and the module doc still says "atomic"
- Topics: OM-03.5
- Evidence: "swap_in is two renames, not one: between them out does not exist" (`build/mod.rs:310-313`); rollback restores only "on error" (`mod.rs:320-327`), i.e. on a returned error, not on process death. The module doc still says "promote atomically" (`build/mod.rs:12`) while the `promote` doc no longer claims it (`mod.rs:409-410`).
- Failure: crash, kill or power loss between the two renames leaves the deployment with no `out` directory; the previous bundle sits in `.publication-retired-PID` with no shown automatic recovery. Stale `.publication-PID` staging directories from crashed runs also accumulate — only the retired directory is cleared (`mod.rs:306-312`).
- Confidence: high on the window; no-auto-recovery-on-process-death is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT); the crash-loss consequence is unmarked

### F-08 — content_digest does not cover inputs that change the emitted bytes: the attribution roster (stated) and the profile (implied)
- Topics: OM-03.6, OM-03.2
- Evidence: "the attribution roster shapes the Turtle and the page API but is not an input to content_digest, which hashes pages, journals and the vocabulary only" (`build/mod.rs:680-685`, `mod.rs:963-983`, `generation.rs:234-243` — an enumeration that also omits the profile). Debt: the roster is read from `vault_root.parent()/ontology` while dirtiness reads `repo_root/ontology` — "two roots for one directory" (`mod.rs:933`, `attribution.rs:32`; flat layout at `profile.rs:514-518`).
- Failure: a roster or profile change (domain aliases reshape NGG1 tiers; roster reshapes Turtle and page API) yields different bundle bytes with an identical content_digest and generation id; digest-keyed consumers serve stale artefacts. On a flat vault the roster is read from above the repository — a directory the build neither hashes nor dirty-flags.
- Confidence: high for the roster; the profile omission is inferred from the enumerated digest inputs
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE + Debt)

### F-09 — The held-back rule exists in two shapes, so the published census, the redaction identity set and the staging filter count different pages
- Topics: OM-02.5, OM-03.3, OM-03.4, OM-00.1
- Evidence: staging and the link path use `held_back` (`_misc` and `misc`, any depth: `build/publish.rs:134,138-148`) while "the census and excluded_identities_of still use the top-level-only is_unpublished (`projection.rs:114,231`)" (OM-03.3). The authors leave it Open: "is the held-back misc rule meant to stay in HELD_BACK_DIRS beside the UNPUBLISHED_DIRS it no longer restates?" (OM-00.1).
- Failure: a vault with a public page under a nested `_misc/` or any `misc/` folder: `api/census.json` counts it as considered/public while `publish/` drops it, and its identity never enters the excluded/marker set; under the compat path (F-04) it is published everywhere. Reconciliation of census against the staged tree fails, and the "every skip is reported" guarantee does not cover this class.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE + Open)

### F-10 — Three wikilink resolvers give three different answers for the same link
- Topics: OM-00.1, OM-02.1, OM-01.5
- Evidence: "THREE wikilink-target resolvers. Corpus::build resolve `ir/model.rs:336`, PrivateLinks::resolve `build/redact.rs:210`, and shacl Resolver::page `shacl.rs:1201` (no slug key, case-sensitive id match at `shacl.rs:1203`)". PrivateLinks strips `#fragment` and `.md` (`redact.rs:219,227`); Corpus::build passes the target whole, "so a fragment link redacted as private is resolved here to namespace + slugify of the whole string" (`model.rs:338`); curated links are slug-verbatim (`model.rs:424`) against the fallback's slugify (`model.rs:342`).
- Failure: `[[Page#Section]]` is withheld in the default path, becomes a phantom `namespace + slugify("page-section")` target in the compat and migrate paths, and resolves differently again in the SHACL export; a page differing only in case resolves in build and not in shacl, so gate verdicts and published Turtle disagree about the corpus's own edges.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-11 — NGG1 tiers collapse all twelve relations to one edge type, merge distinct relations in dedup, and silently drop edges to unselected targets
- Topics: OM-04.4, OM-06.5, OM-00.2 (S3.1)
- Evidence: "Every one of the 12 relation keys becomes the SAME type byte EDGE_RELATION (`graph_tiers.rs:307-316`)"; "edge_set keyed src,tgt,type: two different relations between one pair collapse to one edge (`:283,313`)"; "Edge to a target that is not a selected node is silently dropped (`:297-298,309-310`)".
