KEEP because `EDGE_RELATION` is a published wire value (`ontomark-ngg1/src/lib.rs:144`); per-relation types need an NGG2 bump undecided in ADR-OM-005.
- Failure: the explorer consumer cannot distinguish part-of from related-to; domain tiers lose cross-domain edges with no per-edge notice (only `stats.json`'s declared-vs-shipped delta hints at it); any consumer reasoning over tiers gets a strictly weaker graph than `ontology.ttl` with nothing failing.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (KEEP / DIVERGENCE / Open)

### F-12 — `retrieve`, `tree` and `edit` read only the first vault under `--vault all`
- Topics: OM-05.1
- Evidence: "retrieve, tree and edit call .next() on the loaded vaults, so under all they read knowledge alone (`main.rs:923-926,948-951,971-974`)" while "all means BOTH for validate, gate, conflicts, find and build" (`main.rs:650,686,745,901`).
- Failure: with `--vault all`, `retrieve`/`tree` report a working-only page as missing; `edit` on a working-only page takes its not-found path and, with the `--expect` guard satisfied ambiguously, creates a duplicate page in knowledge — silent bifurcation of one concept across vaults.
- Confidence: high on the `.next()` behaviour; the edit-duplicates scenario is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-13 — Relation identity is hard-coded in three places beside the vocabulary, so vocabulary changes silently degrade emitters
- Topics: OM-04.2, OM-04.6, OM-04.5, OM-00.1
- Evidence: the Turtle stub list is a "hardcoded array of 13 property names (`turtle.rs:446-460`) … a relation the vocabulary adds is declared but never stubbed"; WebVOWL maps relations "from a hardcoded table (`webvowl.rs:30-42`), while Turtle takes each relation IRI from the vocabulary (`turtle.rs:656-673`); the two agree only while the vocabulary does"; four hand-kept orderings of the same twelve relations (`indexes.rs:30`, `page_api.rs:18`, `ir/model.rs:36`, `webvowl.rs:30`).
- Failure: the vocabulary adds or re-IRIs a relation: Turtle declares it but leaves its foreign targets unstubbed, WebVOWL keeps labelling it with the stale IRI, and each artefact orders it differently — no build error, every published surface disagrees.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-14 — The fail-closed redactor makes total publication availability a function of private-corpus size
- Topics: OM-00.2 (S1.1, N2), OM-02.4
- Evidence: "a marker alternation past REDACTOR_SIZE_LIMIT is PublicationBlocked::Redactor and the build refuses" (`ir/projection.rs:285,313-315,583`); the marker set is built from the ids, names and http projections of every private page (`projection.rs:558-583`) and grows with the private corpus. The limit's value is not given in the material.
- Failure: the vault accumulates enough private pages (or long private names) that the alternation passes the limit: every build — public and machine — refuses to publish anything until the limit is raised or pages are made public. Fail-closed, so no leak, but publication stops wholesale with no bypass shown.
- Confidence: medium — the refusal is stated; that ordinary growth reaches the limit is inferred (value unknown)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (the fail-closed fix is marked; the availability ceiling is not)

### F-15 — Nothing shown serialises two builds writing the same `out` or `publish_out`
- Topics: OM-03.5, OM-03.1
- Evidence: `Staged` "holds bytes in memory" per process (`build/mod.rs:340-347`); disk staging is PID-namespaced (`.publication-PID` `mod.rs:415-430`, `.publish-staging-PID` `publish.rs:341-356`); the write-once checks `ensure_single_claims` (`mod.rs:957,1017`) are per-process; no cross-process lock on `out` or `publish_out` appears anywhere in the material.
- Failure: CI and a local run (or two CI jobs) build into the same destinations: each stages cleanly in its own PID directory, each swaps in wholesale, last rename wins; the overwritten build's Report claims success and the surviving bundle can be the stale-commit one. Collision detection cannot fire because claims never meet.
- Confidence: low, inferred — absence of a lock in the material is not proof of its absence in deployment
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

## Not judgeable from this material

1. The value of `REDACTOR_SIZE_LIMIT` and the current marker-alternation size on the real corpus — needed to say whether F-14 is live today or theoretical.
2. What `stage_redacted` receives under `--no-redact-private-links` (a `None` link set or not) — i.e., whether publish/ markdown keeps private wikilinks verbatim under the compat flag; this decides F-04's worst case.
3. Whether actual downstream caches (Loom mirror, the site, the agentbox pod pack) key on scope as C3 instructs — this decides whether F-03 has already fired.
4. Build serialization in deployment: whether CI or a wrapper guarantees single-writer access to `out` and `publish_out`, and whether crashed-run `.publication-PID` directories are cleaned by anything outside the code shown.
5. The eleven credential-scan patterns (`input/secrets.rs:115-157`) versus the estate's real secret formats, and whether the deliberate exclusion of `$` (env references) matches what deployment actually stores in pages — the scan's coverage cannot be assessed from the distillation.

## ontomark / premortem

# Post-mortem — ontomark publication estate

**Basis.** Everything below is drawn from the OM-00–OM-06 register as verified at ontomark `07f4c1c`, VisionClaw `e74bcdd`, Loom `2fad3e0`, agentbox `0b0f791`, and the cited contract/ADR text. Citations are the ones the register gives. Where I reason past what the material states, Confidence says "inferred". The three root causes are the publication-boundary defects the register itself rates highest and that still had live production paths at the verified commit.

---

### R-1 — The retired-but-still-running VisionClaw `vault` build kept publishing into Pod resources with the unfixed SEV-1 redaction and credential faults
- Topics: OM-00.3, OM-00.2, OM-05.5, OM-01.4
- Chain of events: A knowledge page that should not have published (or a public page carrying a credential in a frontmatter value) goes through the VisionClaw `ontology-publish.yml` job, which builds with the in-tree legacy `vault` crate, not ontomark. That binary still ships S1.1 (redactor fails **open** on a regex compile error — private prose publishes), S1.2 (credential scan is body-only while render publishes frontmatter, so a credential in `title` or `sources` ships unscanned), S1.5 (`--vault all` gates read only the first vault) and S2.3 (remove-then-write `publish_out`). The output feeds `pack-pod-resources.py`, so the exposure lands in client-visible Pod resources. The retirement (VC-60) was gated, so the job kept running.
- Evidence: "VisionClaw's vault build job still ships S1.1, S1.2, S1.5, S2.3 and S3.1 unchanged until VC-60 retires under its four gates" (ADR-OM-005-estate-alignment…md:11). S1.1 inherited at `vault/src/projection.rs:230`; S1.2 at `vault/src/build/publish.rs:159-160,196`, with d5c1188 wrapping redaction around render, "not the scan"; S2.3 at `vault/src/build/publish.rs:284`; S1.5 at `vault/src/main.rs:595-598` (all in OM-00.3). The job builds `-p vault` (`ontology-publish.yml:167-168`), runs `vault --repo vault-source build --vault knowledge --out output/vault` (`ontology-publish.yml:172-175`), and its "output feeds pack-pod-resources.py" (`ontology-publish.yml:247`); ADR-OM-005 decision 2 retires the job "gated, so it keeps running" (ADR-OM-005…md:11). The fixes exist only in ontomark at 8d654d1 (`ir/projection.rs:460,583`; `check/validate.rs:323`; `build/publish.rs:240`).
- Warning signs visible today: The register states the exposure outright as EXTERNAL; the legacy job is wired into CI at the verified commit; the retirement is conditional, not scheduled; the legacy build also "kept private links" (OM-05.5), so its bundles differ from ontomark's redaction contract by design.
- Confidence: high that these faults ship on a running production job at the verified commit; that this path produced the incident is inferred (whether VC-60 landed in the interim is unknown from this material).
- Severity: high

### R-2 — `--no-redact-private-links` (the compat projection) silently disables the held-back rule, so held content and private-name-derived IRIs ship in the machine artefacts while `publish/` omits them
- Topics: OM-02.3, OM-03.3, OM-03.4, OM-02.1, OM-00.1, OM-00.2
- Chain of events: Someone runs the build with `--no-redact-private-links` — the exact flag the estate parity test passes (`conformance.rs:245`), so it is a normalised, CI-exercised invocation. The build takes the compat path, where `held_back` is never applied at projection: a public page under `misc/` or `_misc/` (content held back as site-only) stays **public** in the projected corpus and therefore enters `api/pages/`, the search and prose indexes, Turtle and NGG1 (every emitter reads the projected corpus, OM-04.1), while `stage_redacted` skips it in `publish/` — so the site tree shows nothing missing and the exposure is invisible from the published pages. Additionally, with Step-1 link redaction off, wikilinks carrying `#fragment` or `.md` resolve through the fallback `namespace + slugify(raw target)` (`ir/model.rs:338-342`), publishing IRIs whose slugs embed the raw target text — including targets naming private pages — which the Step-2 marker redactor cannot recognise because they are synthetic IRIs, not any record's IRI. Credential gate note: held pages are skipped before scanning (`build/publish.rs:220`), so on this path their content also reaches the record-derived artefacts unscanned.
- Evidence: "the compat path never forces misc/ pages private (held_back is only applied in the link path `ir/projection.rs:616`); a public misc/ page stays public there, while stage_redacted skips it `build/publish.rs:220`" (OM-02.3 n1). Path selection at `build/mod.rs:661-676`, profile default true `profile.rs:428`, flag overlay `ontomark-cli/src/main.rs:815`, `build/mod.rs:84`, `profile.rs:422` (OM-05.6). "a fragment link redacted as private is resolved here to namespace + slugify of the whole string" against `build/redact.rs:219,227` (OM-02.1 n2). Held-list depth divergence: `is_unpublished` top-level-only at `input/page.rs:47,641` vs `held_back` any-depth at `build/publish.rs:134,138-148`; census and `excluded_identities_of` still use the narrow one (`ir/projection.rs:114,231`) — "the divergence that remains" (OM-03.3). S3.4 kept as the byte-parity mechanism, pinned by the golden that passes the flag (`conformance.rs:230,245`); the register leaves an Open question on the misc rule's home (OM-00.1 Open).
- Warning signs visible today: the register marks H4 "DIVERGENCE kept" and S3.4 permanently open; the flag's name advertises one behaviour (no link redaction) but disables a second, unrelated policy (hold-back at projection); CI exercises the unsafe path on every conformance run.
- Confidence: high that the compat path behaves as stated (explicitly labelled); medium, inferred, that an operator invoked it in production.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — `publish_out` is written *after* the bundle is promoted, so a failure leaves two generations live at once: new data beside the previous public site tree
- Topics: OM-03.5, OM-03.1, OM-05.5
- Chain of events: A site publish runs `build --publish-out quartz/content` (the visionGraph invocation, `publish.yml:165-171`). `promote(out)` succeeds — the new bundle is swapped in. Then `publish::write_to(publish_out)` fails (disk, permissions, path conflict). The CLI now exits 1, but the new bundle is already promoted while `write_to`'s own swap never ran: the public markdown tree keeps the **previous** generation's pages. Retracted, renamed or privatised pages remain live on the client-facing site beside new-generation data, and the two destinations are "no longer one generation". The failure is retryable but nothing in the stated mechanism rolls the bundle back to match the site.
- Evidence: "write_to runs AFTER promote, so a failure there leaves the NEW bundle beside the PREVIOUS publication tree that swap_in kept, the two no longer one generation — `build/publish.rs:356`, `build/mod.rs:1018-1020`" (OM-03.5 N2). Order asserted in the run sequence: promote at `mod.rs:1018`, `write_to` at `mod.rs:1019-1021` (OM-03.1). `write_to` stages in `.publish-staging-PID` then `swap_in` (`publish.rs:341-356`); `swap_in` restores only on a rename **error** (`mod.rs:320-327`). Consumer exposure: visionGraph builds with `--publish-out quartz/content` (`publish.yml:165-171`) and treats a missing tree as wrong (`publish.yml:189-195`).
- Warning signs visible today: recorded as a DIVERGENCE in the register; the module doc still calls step 6 "atomic promotion" (`build/mod.rs:12`) while the `promote` doc no longer claims it (`mod.rs:409-410`) — the code and its own documentation disagree.
- Confidence: high on the mechanism (explicitly stated); medium, inferred, that a mid-sequence failure of this kind produced the incident.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Public and machine bundles share identical `ontology_digest` and `content_digest`; consumers keyed on the digest alone cannot tell them apart
- Topics: OM-06.1, OM-06.5, OM-06.3
- Evidence: "ontology_digest and content_digest are equal" across scopes (test `machine_and_public_builds_share_every_ontology_artefact`, `ontomark-compile/tests/machine_scope.rs:143`, OM-06.1); C3 records the obligation "a consumer cache keyed on ontology_digest must also key on scope" (`sovereign-corpus-contracts.md:148-152`, OM-06.5 L4); Loom's readers never read `scope` (`loom-facade/src/mirror.rs:266-285`) and `promote_vault_build` rewrites the marker preserving it (`loom-vector-ruvector/src/artifact.rs:168-175,255-260`).
- Failure: A mirror or cache keyed on the digest serves machine-bundle content (including `api/pages/working/` and the working-inclusive search index) where the public bundle was expected, or treats a machine bundle as a public one — working pages surface on the public side with no digest mismatch to signal it.
- Confidence: high (equality is stated); the consumer mistake is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (recorded as an invariant obligation on consumers, not as a defect)

### F-02 — The credential gate scans only the staged `publish/` bytes; record-derived artefacts are covered by coincidence, not by the gate
- Topics: OM-03.4, OM-01.4, OM-02.3
- Evidence: "the markdown mirror and page API are built from projected records, not from the scanned bytes, so only publish/ is covered by the gate by construction — `build/mod.rs:869`" (OM-03.4 N2). The gate lives in `stage_redacted` over the rendered page (`build/publish.rs:228,240`); the mirror independently re-redacts the raw body (`build/mod.rs:887`); projection keeps frontmatter values through `frontmatter_value` (`ir/projection.rs:630-642`) while render re-renders frontmatter by its own path (`build/publish.rs:169-175`) — two independent frontmatter pipelines; held pages are skipped before scanning (`build/publish.rs:220`).
- Failure: Any content whose record form diverges from the staged render — a frontmatter value kept by `frontmatter_value` but dropped by render, or a held page public in the compat projection (R-2) — reaches `api/`, the search and prose indexes and the mirror without ever being scanned; a credential ships in an index rather than in `publish/`.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (the publish-only coverage is stated; the content divergence between the two frontmatter paths is not established in the material).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (presented as an invariant)

### F-03 — `retrieve`, `tree` and `edit` take the first vault, so under `--vault all` a guarded write lands in knowledge regardless of intent
- Topics: OM-05.1, OM-05.2
- Evidence: "retrieve, tree and edit call `.next()` on the loaded vaults, so under all they read knowledge alone — `ontomark-cli/src/main.rs:923-926,948-951,971-974`" (OM-05.1 n2), while validate/gate/conflicts/find loop every selected vault (`main.rs:650,686,745,901`).
- Failure: An operator runs `ontomark edit --vault all` intending a working page; the guard (`--expect`) validates against knowledge and the write is applied to the knowledge vault — a custody failure on the wrong store, with exit 0.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-04 — The attribution roster is read from `vault_root.parent()`, which on a flat layout is a directory *above* the repository; the vocabulary dirty-check uses a second, different root
- Topics: OM-03.2, OM-03.6
- Evidence: "the attribution roster is still read from `vault_root.parent()` (`build/mod.rs:680-684`), and a flat layout puts the vault root at `repo_root` itself (`profile.rs:514-518,549-550`), so for a flat vault the roster is looked up one directory above the repository while the dirtiness check reads `repo_root/ontology` (`build/mod.rs:933`)" (OM-03.2 Debt); "the vocabulary directory is hard-coded to repo_root/ontology for dirtiness, and the roster is read from vault_root.parent()/ontology, two roots for one directory" (OM-03.6 N3, `build/attribution.rs:32`).
- Failure: On a flat or custom layout, published Turtle and page API carry attribution read from outside the repository — uncontrolled content shaping published artefacts — and the dirty flag can read an ontology directory that is not the one the build loaded.
- Confidence: high on the two roots; medium, inferred, on the wrong-tree read occurring in a real layout.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt and DIVERGENCE)

### F-05 — The roster shapes Turtle and the page API but is not an input to `content_digest`
- Topics: OM-03.6
- Evidence: "the attribution roster shapes the Turtle and the page API but is not an input to content_digest, which hashes pages, journals and the vocabulary only — `build/mod.rs:680-685`, `build/mod.rs:963-983`" (OM-03.6 N1); Loom keys its promotion on the marker whose `content_digest` "stays the source-corpus digest" (`loom-vector-ruvector/src/artifact.rs:154-157`, OM-06.3).
- Failure: An attribution change produces different published Turtle with an unchanged `content_digest`; a consumer keyed on the digest (including the rvdb pipeline) serves the stale artefact and records no mismatch.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-06 — NGG1 silently drops every edge whose target is not a selected node, and reports the dropped edges as "declared"
- Topics: OM-04.4
- Evidence: "Edge to a target that is not a selected node is silently dropped — `graph_tiers.rs:297-298,309-310`"; "`declared_backbone` and `declared_relations` count edges the tiers then discard — `graph_tiers.rs:285-286,297,309`, so `stats.json` reports declared against shipped" (OM-04.4).
- Failure: The explorer client's graph is silently missing relations; only a stats field distinguishes declared from shipped, and nothing refuses the build on the gap.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-07 — All twelve relations collapse to `EDGE_RELATION` and the dedup key merges distinct relations between one pair (S3.1, open)
- Topics: OM-04.4, OM-06.5, OM-00.2
- Evidence: `graph_tiers.rs:283,306-316`; `EDGE_RELATION` is a published wire value (`ontomark-ngg1/src/lib.rs:142-144`); kept at v1 pending an NGG2 format bump, per-edge property IRI undecided in ADR-OM-005 (OM-06.5, OM-00.2 S3.1).
- Failure: A downstream consumer reads `part-of` or `disjoint-with` as a generic relation and acts on wrong semantics; the TTL keeps the distinction (`turtle.rs:337-341`) so the two artefacts disagree.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-08 — Turtle's stub list is a hard-coded array of 13 property names; relations added by the vocabulary are declared but never stubbed
- Topics: OM-04.2, OM-00.1
- Evidence: "a relation the vocabulary adds is declared but never stubbed" — `turtle.rs:446-460`, a "third copy of the relation set" beside the vocabulary-driven declarations at `turtle.rs:678-691` (OM-04.2); "hard-coded 13 stub names, misses vocabulary-added relations" (OM-00.1 R5).
- Failure: The vocabulary gains a relation; its out-of-graph targets get no `skos:Concept` stub, leaving dangling objects in the published TTL that consumers cannot resolve.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-09 — WebVOWL labels relations from a hard-coded table while Turtle takes the IRI from the vocabulary; they agree only while the vocabulary does not change
- Topics: OM-04.6, OM-00.1
- Evidence: `webvowl.rs:30-42` (`related-to`→`skos:related`, `part-of`→`vc:isPartOf`) vs `turtle.rs:656-673`; "the two agree only while the vocabulary does" (OM-04.6); OM-00.1 R4 marks the WebVOWL map DIVERGENCE.
- Failure: A vocabulary rename or addition leaves `ontology.ttl` correct and the explorer mislabelling or dropping the relation — two published views of the same corpus disagree.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-10 — Wikilink resolution diverges on `#fragment`, `.md` and dash-edge targets; curated links bypass the index entirely
- Topics: OM-02.1, OM-01.5, OM-02.6, OM-00.1
- Evidence: three resolvers (`ir/model.rs:336`, `build/redact.rs:210`, `shacl.rs:1201` — the third with no slug key and case-sensitive id matching); PrivateLinks strips fragment/`.md` (`build/redact.rs:219,227`) while `Corpus::build` passes the target verbatim into `names.get` (`ir/model.rs:338`); `target_slug` takes kebab targets verbatim, keeping leading and doubled dashes (`input/slug.rs:65-75`) against `slugify` at `ir/model.rs:342` (OM-01.5 note3, OM-02.6 R5).
- Failure: Fragment or `.md` links resolve through the fallback to synthetic IRIs whose slugs embed the raw target text — dangling edges that NGG1 then silently drops (F-06) — and on the compat path those slugs can derive from private page names (R-2); curated links with dash-edge targets publish IRIs that match no record.
- Confidence: high on the divergences; medium, inferred, on which input shapes reach production.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-11 — The derivation certificate is optional and C3 lists no certificate artefact, so builds without `--certificate-out` publish unwarranted closures
- Topics: OM-03.8, OM-04.1
- Evidence: "certificate_out defaults to absent and the build writes and checks nothing then (`build/mod.rs:730`, `ontomark-cli/src/main.rs:401`)" — the register's own Open question (OM-03.8); "the C3 file listing names no certificate output, `sovereign-corpus-contracts.md:111`, while ADR-OM-009 decides every published closure carries one" (OM-04.1 NC).
- Failure: Any build path that omits the flag (a local or hotfix build, a consumer other than visionGraph) publishes a closure no certificate covers, and the parity refusal that would have caught an emitter defect never runs.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, Drift)

### F-12 — The promote is two renames with a window where `out` does not exist; restore covers rename errors only, and the module doc still says "atomic"
- Topics: OM-03.5, OM-03.1, OM-00.2
- Evidence: "swap_in is two renames, not one: between them out does not exist — `build/mod.rs:310-313`; on error restore retired and delete stage — `build/mod.rs:320-327`" (OM-03.5 N1); DOC-DRIFT: module doc "promote atomically" (`build/mod.rs:12`) vs the two-rename swap (OM-03.5, OM-00.2 F5).
- Failure: The process dies between the renames: `out` is absent, the staged and retired directories are orphaned, and every consumer reading the bundle path fails until manual recovery. The material states the error-path restore only; crash recovery is not stated anywhere.
- Confidence: high on the window; inferred on the absence of crash recovery.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-13 — Public working content that shares an identity with public knowledge is silently omitted from the machine bundle, with only a count as signal
- Topics: OM-06.2, OM-06.1
- Evidence: "public working beside PUBLIC knowledge: yields to knowledge, counted" (twin rule, `ir/projection.rs:725-731`); "yields = public working records whose identity a cleaned public knowledge record holds — `ir/projection.rs:785-806`"; `Report.machine` carries the yielded count (`build/mod.rs:190-196`).
- Failure: The pod bundle serves the older knowledge version of a shared identity while working holds the newer content; nothing names which pages were yielded, so a consumer cannot detect the omission without diffing.
- Confidence: high on the behaviour; inferred that it causes a stale-content incident.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (stated as the designed twin rule)

### F-14 — Census and load read each page twice; a file edited between the reads is classified on one text and published from another
- Topics: OM-03.3
- Evidence: "each page is still read twice, census then load, so a file edited between the two is classified on one text and parsed from another, a time-of-check gap the digest no longer widens — `ir/projection.rs:118`, `input/page.rs:493`" (OM-03.3 N2).
- Failure: A mid-build edit yields census counts and validity verdicts from one text and published content from another; the digest attests only the load-time bytes, so the mismatch is undiscoverable after the fact.
- Confidence: high on the double read; low on exploitability (validate re-checks the parsed page for fence and public-flag faults, `check/validate.rs:342-348,354-367`).
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-15 — The furniture-folder list is written twice across the two producers
- Topics: OM-00.1, OM-01.2
- Evidence: "EXCLUDED_DIRS = [templates, bases, assets, journals] (`ontomark-compile/src/input/page.rs:58`) and VisionClaw's FURNITURE_DIRS with the same four names (`../project/crates/vault-core/src/page.rs:50`), both introduced when the pages/ folder was retired (ADR-OM-007)" (OM-00.1 Debt).
- Failure: One list is updated and the other is not — the legacy producer (R-1's job, still running) starts treating a new furniture folder as pages, or excludes a folder ontomark publishes; the two producers' bundles diverge on folder policy with no shared definition.
- Confidence: high on the duplication; inferred on the drift occurring.
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

## sidestr-rs / critical

### F-01 — The live peg is authorised by a single signer
- Topics: SR-01.4
- Evidence: The estate deliberately maintains single-key peg control via local patch: "`patches/peg-script.patch` keeps single-signer peg announcements (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:150-153`, `../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:162-165`)". Meanwhile estate policy makes the sidechain the value boundary: "Estate policy therefore treats EVM as in-chain execution rather than a second value rail (`../project/agentbox/docs/adr/ADR-2096-sidestr-sidechains-are-the-sole-value-instrument.md:116`)".
- Failure: whoever holds that one peg-announcement key can, alone or under coercion, authorise peg movement for every chain running this baked engine. No quorum, no second control is stated anywhere in the material. In production with real value, compromise of one key is a direct custody loss.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (the single-signer fact is stated; what an "announcement" alone authorises versus what additionally requires block-signer approval is not shown).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — The deployed engine is patched, but the compatibility ladder certifies against unpatched pins
- Topics: SR-01.2, SR-01.4
- Evidence: SR-01.4 says the producer runs "the upstream JavaScript engine baked at the upstream pins" *and* the local `peg-script.patch` (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:117`, `:150-153`). SR-01.2 says CI checks the Rust workspace "against those sources" at the fixed upstream revisions (`../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:99`, `:207`) and ADR-0001 makes that reference "the compatibility contract" (`../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0001-reference-oracle-is-the-compatibility-contract.md:27`). Nothing in the material says CI applies the estate patch to the reference checkout before regenerating fixtures.
- Failure: the peg script is consensus-visible (it decides which peg transactions are accepted). Rust components and fixtures are proven equivalent to the *unpatched* reference; the engine actually running is the reference *plus* a patch. A peg transaction the deployed engine accepts, the certified Rust path may reject (or vice versa), producing a stuck peg or a follower/producer divergence that the whole oracle ladder was built to prevent.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (the patch's presence in the deployment is stated; whether CI's reference includes it is not).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — A known-exploitable version of `sidestr-hitch` is published
- Topics: SR-01.1, SR-04 (header), SR-04.1
- Evidence: Hitch is one of the eight published crates (`../sidestr-rs/Cargo.toml:43`, `../sidestr-rs/README.md:77`). The material states the 0.1 flaw plainly: "the 0.1 key let a commitment's owner spend its own revocation leaf, which is the flaw this release fixes" and "a party could cheat with its own penalty path" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/CHANGELOG.md:45`, `:52`).
- Failure: 0.1 is on crates.io with a defect in the money-bearing script path. Any third party who adopted it — or any inherited deployment an operator takes over — holds channels where a counterparty can spend via a path that was supposed to be unusable by the commitment's owner. There is no stated advisory or yank, only a changelog entry.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (documented in the CHANGELOG, but not flagged as a security exposure of a published release)

### F-04 — Nothing in this estate has ever run in production
- Topics: SR-01.3, SR-01.4, SR-03.4
- Evidence: "the Rust workspace is currently a validated library component. No Rust producer is deployed on either chain; adoption stays under the estate board's open row (`../project/docs/TODO-unified.md:101`)"; both live chains are "test-only… produced by the upstream JavaScript engine"; the material's own invariant is "source evidence may promote implementation status; it cannot promote activation status" (`docs/adr/ADR-2012-sidestr-settlement-is-ecosystem-canon.md:115`).
- Failure: accepting production responsibility means operating a system whose every consensus, custody and recovery behaviour has been exercised only against fixtures and test chains. The first production incident will be the first time most paths run under load.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, SR-01.4)

### F-05 — Hitch's kernel has no host: every money-protecting duty is unimplemented
- Topics: SR-04.3, SR-04.4, SR-04.5
- Evidence: "no estate host supplies the complete service boundary"; the host must supply wallet funding, relay I/O, atomic snapshot storage, chain lookups, broadcasting, and the retry/timeout scheduler (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:49`, `:70`, `:105`). Time-sensitive duties depend on it: the machine takes a claimable HTLC to chain only "`delay + CLAIM_MARGIN` blocks before expiry" via its tick (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/machine.rs:1306`), and snapshots containing signing keys "must be protected as wallet key material" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:24`).
- Failure: there is no scheduler to fire the tick before HTLC expiry and no watcher to run `on_spend`. If anyone builds a partial host, the failure mode is silent: HTLCs expire unclaimed and revoked closes go unpunished — direct fund loss, not an error message.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, SR-04.5)

### F-06 — Version-1 snapshots cannot be restored, forcing operators to close legacy channels with the vulnerable 0.1
- Topics: SR-04 (header), SR-04.3
- Evidence: "only version 2 snapshots restore; a 0.1 (version 1) snapshot is refused… such a channel must be closed with 0.1 (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:29`, `:182`)".
- Failure: an operator inheriting any 0.1-era channel must provision and run the exact release that contains the F-03 flaw, with live keys, to wind the channel down. If 0.1 tooling or its pin becomes unavailable, the channel funds are unreachable with the supported software.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — No stated timeout resolves a peer that withholds the final revoke
- Topics: SR-04.2, SR-04.3, SR-04.5
- Evidence: during a pending update "nothing is forwarded, settled or announced and no further update is accepted or proposed" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:27`); set-aside states bind HTLCs "until that state number is revoked or the funding is spent" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/machine.rs:561`). The host scheduler's enumerated duties are "retries, proposal, funding and unfunded timeouts" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:70`) — no revoke-withholding or pending-update timeout appears anywhere.
- Failure: a malicious or buggy peer sends an update, receives the ack, then never sends the revoke. The channel freezes indefinitely with balances in limbo; the router's protective force-close is described only for the set-aside case (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:578`), not for a plain stalled handshake. Cheap griefing, funds locked.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (absence of a timeout in an enumerated list; not an explicit statement that none exists).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — `sidestr-evm` deliberately violates the reference-oracle contract it is certified under
- Topics: SR-01.2, SR-03.2
- Evidence: "The crate deliberately commits only an applied block, unlike the reference's `prepare` (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/lib.rs:84`)". The governing contract says the reference is the compatibility standard and a Rust-only suite proves nothing by itself (`../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0001-reference-oracle-is-the-compatibility-contract.md:27`).
- Failure: state-commit semantics are exactly the kind of thing the oracle ladder exists to hold equal, and here they differ by choice. If the reference engine and the Rust crate are ever run together (producer and follower/validator), their durable EVM state after a refused-but-prepared sequence can disagree at the same height — a divergence the certification story claims cannot happen.
- Confidence: high on the stated divergence; the runtime consequence is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — One fail-stop producer is a chain-halt single point of failure
- Topics: SR-01.4
- Evidence: "an append or index-write failure latches `SIDING_PERSISTENCE_FAILED` and the producer exits" (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:150-153`); agentbox "supervises the producer, mirror and faucet" as a single producer (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:111`). No failover producer, no producer rotation, and no supervisor recovery semantics are described.
- Failure: a transient disk error — full disk, slow fsync, permissions — halts block production on the chain. The exit-before-advertise behaviour protects safety, but nothing in the material describes automatic recovery, so the chain stays down until a human intervenes, and no second producer exists to take over.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (single instance and fail-stop are stated; recovery behaviour is not).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — The second sealed chain has no mirror or faucet under supervision
- Topics: SR-01.4
- Evidence: The supervision diagram asserts the mirror and faucet edges connect only to `sidestr:dreamlab`; the producer connects to both chains (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:111`, `:117`, `../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/dreamlab/chain.json:5`, `README.md:11`). `sidestr:dreamlab-txbt4` receives only the producer's edge.
- Failure: the txbt4 chain's history apparently exists in one place — the producer. Loss of that node's storage loses the chain's only copy, with no mirror to rebuild from. The same pattern, carried into production, makes every new chain a data-loss SPOF by default.
- Confidence: medium (taken from the asserted diagram; a mirror elsewhere is not mentioned but not disproved).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — Forward records are keyed by (downstream channel, payment hash), which collides on a repeated payment
- Topics: SR-04.3
- Evidence: forwards are "keyed by downstream channel and payment hash, so a restart or a second payment in flight cannot lose them (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:173`)"; the record is written "before the downstream `add`" (`route.rs:108`).
- Failure: the same invoice paid twice (user retry, duplicate relay delivery) through the same channel produces two distinct forwards with one key. The material asserts no loss but never states collision behaviour: an overwrite loses the first record; a merge can misattribute one settle to both payments. Either way upstream settlement can pay the wrong hop or the wrong amount.
- Confidence: low, inferred (the keying is stated; the collision semantics are not).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — The Rust port silently drops malformed points where the reference replies, desynchronising peers
- Topics: SR-04.5
- Evidence: "a point off the curve is refused at deserialisation and dropped without a `reject`, where Hitch replies with a reject at the proof-of-possession check (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:63`)".
- Failure: a Hitch JavaScript peer and a Rust peer disagree on the same malformed input. The Hitch side transitions (reject sent, state freed, retry timers armed); the Rust side waits for a well-formed message. The two state machines are now in different places for the same conversation — stalled channels or divergent retry behaviour that neither implementation's tests, run alone, will surface.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-13 — The deepest consensus cross-check runs only when an environment variable is set
- Topics: SR-04 (header)
- Evidence: "Since 0.2.1 a chain-consensus suite… has `sidestr-core`'s `Chain` validate every spend, with siding replaying to the same tip hash **when the oracle environment is set**."
- Failure: the only test that replays Rust block production against the real engine to an identical tip is conditional. A developer running the ordinary suite gets green without that assertion ever executing; a regression in transaction construction or block assembly ships in the next release having "passed tests". Whether CI reliably sets the variable is not stated in this material.
- Confidence: low, inferred (the gating is stated; CI's setting of it is not).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — ADR-0001 names four reference repositories; CI pins six
- Topics: SR-01.2
- Evidence: the record claims CI pins "all four reference repositories" while the workflow pins spec, schema, blaketestnode, Hitch, Reef and teller (`../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0001-reference-oracle-is-the-compatibility-contract.md:27` vs `../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:14`).
- Failure: the document that *is* the compatibility contract misdescribes the contract's parties. An engineer implementing to the ADR builds against four oracles and misses two — including the teller that defines the reserve attestation bytes (F-15). The decision process is not being maintained as the oracle set grows, so every ADR's factual claims are suspect until checked.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-15 — A "private project contract" has its canonical bytes defined by an external, re-pinnable repository
- Topics: SR-01.2, SR-05.2
- Evidence: the reserve attestation format is declared "a private project contract" (SR-05, business), yet its signed bytes are held "to the JCS of the solidpayorg teller `7c00cea` (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-reserve/src/lib.rs:60`)". References demonstrably move: Reef was "repinned from `648487a` to `2bd3cb8`" (`../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:25`).
- Failure: the meaning of "the bytes that were signed" is anchored to a third-party repository the estate does not control. A future teller repin that changes a JCS edge case changes what the cross-check suite enforces; attestations signed under the old pin and verified under the new one can disagree, and the "private" label gives no stability guarantee to fall back on.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (the anchoring is stated; the impact of a future repin is reasoning forward).
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

## Not judgeable from this material

1. Whether CI applies `patches/peg-script.patch` to the reference checkout before regenerating fixtures — this single fact decides whether F-02 is a real certification gap or already handled.
2. The peg model itself: what a "peg announcement" authorises on its own, who holds the single-signer key, and whether block signers provide any independent check — decisive for F-01's severity.
3. Sidechain reorganisation semantics: whether an applied sidechain block can be orphaned, and if so how committed rule state (EVM state committed by block hash per `sidestr-evm/src/state.rs:468`) and the mempool unwind.
4. Persistence and recovery of the producer's mempool and transaction queue across restarts, plus agentbox's restart behaviour and whether the two sealed chains are isolated from each other in one supervisor.
5. The `round`, `agent`, `nostr` and `header` crates: signers "order and validate every block" (SR-03.1), but no topic covers the federation-round crate's failure, recovery or key-ceremony behaviour — an unaudited consensus-critical surface.

## sidestr-rs / premortem

# Post-mortem: client loss twelve months after verified state

An operator lost a client to a custody/data failure. This document reconstructs the failure from the estate as the source material documents it: what is live, what is only source, and which boundaries the material itself flags as incomplete. Everything is cited to the material's own `path:line` references; reasoning beyond them is marked inferred. Each finding will be verified against code before action.

---

### R-1 — A partially built Hitch host never called the chain watch or the tick, so a peer's stale commitment went unpunished and the client's channel balance was taken
- Topics: SR-04.3, SR-04.4, SR-04.5
- Chain of events: A host service is finally stood up (ADR-2111 moves from proposed to implemented) with peer transport over Nostr kind 23600, snapshot storage and broadcasting, but the chain-lookup feed and the scheduler are partial. A client's channel carries value. The counterparty broadcasts an outdated commitment. The kernel's penalty logic exists — `on_spend` classifies the spend against every signed commitment of theirs and answers a revoked close with a penalty on each output — but it only runs if the host calls it; nothing in the sans-IO machine acts on its own. The host's watcher is not wired, or the tick is not scheduled. The peer's delayed output confirms and is spent before anyone reacts. The penalty opportunity is gone; the client's funds in the channel are gone; the client leaves.
- Evidence: The machine performs no IO and the host must carry messages, wallet funding, snapshot storage, chain watches and broadcasting (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:49`, `:105`). The scheduler — retries, proposal, funding and unfunded timeouts — is also a host duty, with only the constants exported (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:70`, `:121`). Penalties, `after_close` claims and `watch_outputs` republication all require host chain lookups and broadcasts (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/chain.rs:176`, `:364`, `:578`). The machine's tick drives claimable HTLCs to chain `delay + CLAIM_MARGIN` blocks before expiry (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/machine.rs:1306`) and the router's tick performs the protective force close (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:578`). The material states flatly that "no estate host supplies the complete service boundary", which 0.2.0 widened by the scheduler and chain-lookup duties, and that ADR-2111 remains proposed, unimplemented and inactive (`../project/docs/adr/ADR-2111-re-sequence-rgb-for-bridged-assets-and-delete-the-host-payment-store.md:95`), with adoption still open on the board (`../project/docs/TODO-unified.md:101`).
- Warning signs visible today: SR-04.5's Open explicitly says no estate host exists; every money-bearing reaction to an on-chain close was moved *into* the kernel in 0.2.0 precisely so a host would only need to supply lookups and broadcasts — a host that omits either silently disables all of it; the router also depends on the host persisting the ForwardRecord the action carries before proceeding (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:108`), the same class of omitted duty.
- Confidence: high that the incomplete host boundary is real and loss-capable (the material states it); the exact failing duty in this incident is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — A legacy 0.1 channel could not be restored after the 0.2.0 upgrade, and the 0.1 revocation-leaf flaw let the closer skip its own delay
- Topics: SR-04.1, SR-04.3
- Chain of events: A channel opened under 0.1 (before the two-party revocation key). The operator's host is upgraded to 0.2. On restart, restore refuses the version-1 snapshot outright. While the operator scrambles, the counterparty — still able to run 0.1 — broadcasts a stale commitment and, because the 0.1 key let a commitment's owner spend its own revocation leaf, takes its own delayed output immediately, inside what should have been the penalty window. Even re-installing 0.1 tooling arrives too late. The client's funds are taken; the client leaves.
- Evidence: Only version 2 snapshots restore; a version-1 snapshot is refused because its single-party revocation keys let the owner spend its own revocation leaf, "so such a channel must be closed with 0.1" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:29`, `:182`). The dev summary states a 0.1 channel cannot be restored by 0.2 and the release is breaking (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/CHANGELOG.md:45`, `:52`); the business summary states any channel opened with the earlier version must be closed with that version. The two-party revocation key is described at `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/lib.rs:740`, with the 0.2 invariant that an owner can never spend its own revocation leaf enforced by the oracle (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/tests/oracle.rs:465`).
- Warning signs visible today: a breaking release shipped with no migration path — refusal happens at restore time, i.e. discovered during recovery, the worst possible moment; "nobody in the estate runs one" today, so no operational drill for closing a legacy channel exists anywhere in the estate.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — requires that a 0.1 channel existed by incident time; the refusal behaviour and the 0.1 flaw are stated).
- Severity: high

### R-3 — `sidestr:dreamlab-txbt4` ran with a producer and no mirror; a producer storage failure destroyed the client's chain history
- Topics: SR-01.4
- Chain of events: A client is onboarded onto the second, newer chain. Per the live-boundary diagram, the JS producer feeds both chains, but the mirror and the faucet attach only to `sidestr:dreamlab`. The txbt4 producer hits an append or index-write failure; `SIDING_PERSISTENCE_FAILED` latches and the process exits before advertising an unsaved tip — correctly, but that leaves the chain with exactly one copy of its history. The disk is lost or inconsistent. There is no mirror to reconstruct from and no native producer to rebuild with. The client's records on that chain are unrecoverable; the client leaves.
- Evidence: The diagram shows `M --> D` and `F --> D` only, while the producer reaches both `D` and `D2` (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:111`, `:117`, `:11`). The persistence failure semantics — latch and exit before advertising or extending an unsaved tip — are at `../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:150-153`. Both chains are described as test-only chains produced by the upstream JavaScript engine (SR-01, "For the business"), and the material's own Open demands deploying and proving a native producer before describing the Rust engine as the running estate chain.
- Warning signs visible today: the second chain was sealed (2 Oct, `../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:11`) without the services the first chain has; the material insists publication, source completeness and deployment are separate facts — exactly the conflation this failure requires.
- Confidence: medium (the structural absence of a mirror for txbt4 is shown in the diagram; the failure scenario is inferred).
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — EVM state commits at apply where the reference commits at prepare
- Topics: SR-03.2
- Evidence: The crate "deliberately commits only an applied block, unlike the reference's `prepare`" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/lib.rs:84`), while ADR-0001 makes the reference the compatibility contract (`../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0001-reference-oracle-is-the-compatibility-contract.md:27`).
- Failure: On a chain with mixed implementations, a candidate is prepared then refused or reorganised; the reference's world state has already absorbed it, the Rust side discards it; the next accepted block's state root differs between implementations and the chain splits, stranding client funds on opposite forks.
- Confidence: medium (the divergence is stated; its observability on reorg paths is inferred).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — Forward records keyed by downstream channel and payment hash collide on a repeated hash
- Topics: SR-04.3
- Evidence: The router "keeps ForwardRecord … keyed by downstream channel and payment hash" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:173`) together with the claim that "a restart or a second payment in flight cannot lose them" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:7`).
- Failure: The same invoice (same payment hash) forwarded twice through the same downstream channel from two upstream payers collides on one key; on settle only one upstream is credited; the second upstream times out and reclaims while the hub settled downstream twice — hub loss and a failed client payment.
- Confidence: low (inferred; the material asserts safety, so the record may be a collection — unverifiable from here).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — An off-curve point is dropped without a reject while all timeouts are host duties
- Topics: SR-04.2, SR-04.5
- Evidence: Tension: "a point off the curve is refused at deserialisation and dropped without a `reject`, where Hitch replies with a reject at the proof-of-possession check" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:63`); proposal, funding and unfunded timeouts are the host's scheduler (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:70`, `:121`).
- Failure: A peer bug or relay corruption sends an off-curve point; the Rust side silently drops it and the channel stalls; without a host-enforced funding timeout the opening hangs, or an update stalls until an HTLC's `delay + CLAIM_MARGIN` window (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/machine.rs:1306`) expires.
- Confidence: medium (the departure is stated; the stalling consequence is inferred).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-04 — The documented ChannelSnapshot contents omit set-aside alternative states
- Topics: SR-04.2, SR-04.3
- Evidence: A set-aside signed state's HTLCs "stay bound until that state number is revoked or the funding is spent" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:20`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/machine.rs:561`); the snapshot is documented to contain "the signing key, the revocation basepoint and per-state secrets, and preimages" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:22`) — no alternative states are listed.
- Failure: Restore from a version-2 snapshot taken before a collision loses the set-aside alternative; the peer broadcasts that still-valid signed state; post-restore classification against "every signed commitment of theirs" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/chain.rs:176`) no longer includes it, so no penalty is constructed and the bound HTLCs are claimable by the peer.
- Confidence: low (inferred from the documented field list; the full serialised form is not shown).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — Scheduler constants are exported once; a Hitch repin can change the JS side's timeouts
- Topics: SR-04.5
- Evidence: "Hitch keeps failed broadcasts, retry timers and the proposal, funding and unfunded timeouts on its channel document; here they are the host's scheduler, with the constants exported" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:70`, `:121`); the Hitch pin is currently fixed at `62f8e39` (`../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:22`).
- Failure: Hitch is repinned past a timeout change; the JS peer enforces the new funding/HTLC deadline while the Rust host schedules the old exported constant; the peer force-closes or the claim margin passes before the host acts.
- Confidence: medium (inferred).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — Durability of the producer's persistence latch across supervisor restarts is unstated
- Topics: SR-01.4
- Evidence: "an append or index-write failure latches `SIDING_PERSISTENCE_FAILED` and the producer exits before advertising or extending an unsaved tip" (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:150-153`); agentbox supervises the producer (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:111`, `:117`).
- Failure: If the latch is process-local (or the failed write partially persisted), a supervisor restart re-enters a producer whose store and in-memory tip disagree; it advertises or extends a tip it cannot reproduce, and the dreamlab mirror propagates the bad tip.
- Confidence: low (inferred).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — The peg-script patch rides on a moving upstream pin
- Topics: SR-01.4
- Evidence: "`patches/peg-script.patch` keeps single-signer peg announcements" against the baked spec pin (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:162-165`); the baked pin moves with upstream PRs (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:150-153`).
- Failure: A pin bump rebases or drops the patch silently; peg-announcement acceptance differs between builds or between nodes built at different pins — one key's peg messages accepted by some signers and rejected by others, splitting peg validity.
- Confidence: low (inferred).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — Released crates are validated against one oracle revision; repins reveal drift only in the tree, not the release
- Topics: SR-01.2, SR-01.3
- Evidence: Reef was "repinned from `648487a` to `2bd3cb8`" as the oracle for wallet payment requests (`../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:25`); releases `sidestr-wallet` 0.5.1 and `sidestr-hitch` 0.2.0 are recorded on the closed N-9 row (`../project/docs/TODO-unified.md:114`).
- Failure: Upstream Reef later fixes a BIP 21 parsing defect; the already-released 0.5.1 running in clients diverges from the now-current contract; a payment request in the changed form is misparsed (amount or address) and funds go to the wrong destination. Nothing re-tests released versions against new pins.
- Confidence: medium (inferred).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — ADR-0001 says four reference repositories; CI pins six
- Topics: SR-01.2
- Evidence: The ADR says CI pins "all four reference repositories", while the workflow pins spec, schema, blaketestnode, Hitch, Reef and teller (`../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0001-reference-oracle-is-the-compatibility-contract.md:27`, `../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:14`).
- Failure: An engineer auditing compatibility from the ADR reviews four oracles and misses the teller/Reef surfaces; a drift in an unlisted oracle ships unreviewed.
- Confidence: high (stated).
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-10 — Fixed overlay order versus a future chain document that enumerates rules
- Topics: SR-02.1, SR-02.4
- Evidence: The compositor installs assets before pool before markets and the invariant says order "cannot be rearranged as an implementation detail" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/overlays.rs:1`, `:65`); the live chain document reaches `containmentDigest` with no `rules` field at all (`../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/dreamlab/chain.json:28`).
- Failure: The first chain to activate rules lists them in another order; if the JS engine honours document order while the Rust compositor is fixed, mint authorisation diverges and the implementations split on the same document.
- Confidence: low (inferred).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, SR-02.4)

### F-11 — 1 sat = 1 gwei leaves wei dust and withdrawal rounding undefined
- Topics: SR-03.1
- Evidence: "One sat maps to one gwei inside the overlay" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/lib.rs:5`); withdrawal obligations are paid by the coinbase in sats (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/lib.rs:21-25`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/rule.rs:62`).
- Failure: Contract balances below gwei granularity accumulate dust that can never be withdrawn, or rounding pays more sats than the obligation — in the overpay case breaking the withdrawal-equals-coinbase invariant itself.
- Confidence: low (inferred).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — EVM snapshot restore refuses on any configuration change
- Topics: SR-03.3
- Evidence: Restore "verifies version, configuration, root and time, then clears transient execution state" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/state.rs:305`).
- Failure: An operational config bump makes every retained snapshot unrestorable; EVM state must be rebuilt from genesis while the chain runs, and in-flight withdrawals cannot be reconciled against retained receipts during the rebuild.
- Confidence: low (inferred).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-13 — One-credit-one-mint is enforced only inside a single attestation
- Topics: SR-05.1, SR-05.2
- Evidence: Construction "sorts credit ids through a set, refuses duplicates" and the stated rationale is that "a credit must not support two mints" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-reserve/src/lib.rs:499`); neither crate implements the consuming `bridge` rule (SR-05.1 dashed edge; `../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0003-keep-reserve-attestations-origin-neutral-and-private.md:32`).
- Failure: When the bridge rule is finally written, it accepts the same replay-keyed credit from two attestations at different origin tips and mints the bridged asset twice; the neutral crate's duplicate check does not span attestations.
- Confidence: medium (inferred).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — The Rust faucet is the only Rust code on the live JS chain and is covered by no named oracle
- Topics: SR-01.4
- Evidence: The live boundary shows a "Rust faucet" feeding `sidestr:dreamlab` while the Rust libraries are drawn as "validated components; native producer not deployed" (SR-01.4 diagram, `../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:111`); the oracle ladder runs crate tests (`../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:207`), not the faucet service.
- Failure: After a spec or peg-script pin bump, the faucet emits transactions the baked JS engine rejects — or accepts with unexpected peg semantics — stranding or draining faucet-held sats a client was promised.
- Confidence: low (inferred).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — A maturity-refused channel sweep depends on the host classifying the refusal as retryable
- Topics: SR-02.4, SR-04.5
- Evidence: Admission refuses immature BIP 68 / `nLockTime` so that the transaction "can be submitted again once their height arrives" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/state.rs:631`, `:721`); the router "expects the host to retry a retryable action (Hitch tries twenty times)" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/route.rs:13`).
- Failure: A channel sweep broadcast before its lock matures is refused by the mempool; if the host does not treat that refusal as retryable-at-height, the sweep is dropped, the counterparty's HTLC-success confirms first, and the local claim is never made.
- Confidence: low (inferred).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

## solid-pod-rs / critical

### F-01 — Every payment-state and trail lock is process-local: two live processes over one pod store corrupt money state
- Topics: SP-07.15, SP-10.4, SP-07.7, SP-07.12
- Evidence: `PAYMENT_STATE_LOCK` is "PROCESS-wide. Two replicas serving the same pod storage share no lock, and the README's audit line records 'non-atomic payment state' as a reproduced critical finding" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:208`, note at `handlers/pay.rs` DIVERGENCE). "The storage API has NO cross-resource CAS primitive, so the guard must be held across the replay check, the ledger/order/pool/trail mutation and the persist." SP-10.4 W8: "trail and epoch locks are in-process and held across network calls — a known gap that a second replica does not share" (`CHANGELOG.md:222`); W4 repeats it for the payment lock (`lib.rs:204`).
- Failure: any deployment that runs two instances against the same storage — horizontal scaling, a failover that overlaps, or the restart race in F-15 — loses the lost-update protection entirely: double credits by outpoint receipt can be raced, debits and ledger writes interleave, epoch/trail anchors can double-spend the same head. This is silently wrong; nothing detects the second process.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in SP-07.15; W4/W8 in SP-10.4) — but presented as a multi-replica caveat, not as "the normal restart path can trigger it" (see F-15).

### F-02 — The live money routes cannot be switched off, and the authors' own record says not to use them
- Topics: SP-07.14, SP-07.15, SP-07.18, SP-07.13
- Evidence: "the whole /pay/* surface is registered with the SAME gating as /pay/.info — always on, with no payments feature flag (`lib.rs:5262`). The README's status section says not to carry value through these routes until the audit findings are fixed" (SP-07.14 N2). ADR-2008 D3 "declares MRC20 'retired as a token rail' … yet `/pay/.buy` and `/pay/.withdraw` still execute token trades and withdrawals against the stored ledger (`pay.rs:1396`, `pay.rs:1485`)". The ledger is "still a stored number" — D4/D5 not built (SP-07.13, SP-07.18).
- Failure: an operator deploying the binary for LDP storage alone ships `.deposit`, `.buy`, `.swap`, `.withdraw`, `.withdraw-sats` anyway. There is no feature flag, config key or CLI switch to remove them; the only mitigations are "don't carry value" and the process lock that F-01 shows is per-instance. Combined with F-01 this is a money-loss incident waiting for a second process.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE SP-07.14; Tension SP-07.18)

### F-03 — The edge tier's ACL resolver fails open: invalid/unreadable policy treated as absent, exactly the escalation this repo fixed
- Topics: SP-10.6 (D5), SP-04.3, SP-04.4, SP-09.7
- Evidence: D5: "the edge ACL resolver is out of repo and still treats a failed or invalid read as a miss (`wac/resolver.rs:265`)". In-repo, the fix is invariant 4b: "Invalid → DENY, never inherit … collapsing Invalid or Unavailable back into Ok(None) re-opens the escalation where a malformed RESTRICTIVE policy is silently replaced by a permissive inherited one" (SP-04.3). The forum is pinned at `0.5.0-alpha.14` caret, and "wiring the edge ACL, audience and replay seams is separate work" (SP-09.7 N).
- Failure: on the wasm edge (the forum worker serving pod-backed data), an oversized, depth-bombed or unreadable `.acl` — including one an attacker can arrange by writing a broken ACL where they hold Control, or a transient storage fault — resolves as "no policy", ascends to an ancestor, and can inherit a permissive ACL. Private pod data is served on the edge tier. The in-repo evaluator cannot help because the consumer never adopted the seam.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE D5) — but recorded as an out-of-repo note, not as a live exposure of this system's data.

### F-04 — The replay store's fail-closed capacity is a cheap denial-of-authentication primitive
- Topics: SP-05.4, SP-05.5, SP-05.1, SP-10.10 (B5)
- Evidence: at capacity the store returns `CapacityExhausted — REFUSE`, "fail closed on authentication, not on replay" (`replay.rs:275`, `replay_store.rs:75`). Defaults: TTL 120 s, 10 000 entries (`replay.rs:78-82`). The record path runs after successful verification (`lib.rs:636` then `lib.rs:641`, SP-05.1), and NIP-98 accepts any self-generated keypair — anyone can mint unlimited valid kind-27235 events with unique event ids.
- Failure: an attacker signs ~10 000 fresh events with throwaway keys and fires them at any NIP-98 endpoint. Each passes Schnorr verification and occupies a slot; within the TTL every subsequent credential — including legitimate users — hits `CapacityExhausted` and is "treated as anonymous" (SP-05.1), i.e. total authenticated-access outage for up to 120 s, repeatable forever. `sizing_floor` exists but the shipped default is the vulnerable size.
- Confidence: medium (store semantics and call order are stated; the flood path is inferred — nothing in the material shows a pre-verification throttle or per-key quota)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — the fail-closed refusal is the marked ADR-2006 invariant (SP-05.4, SP-10.1 I2); the exhaustion-lockout consequence is not marked anywhere.

### F-05 — `acl:origin` restrictions are parsed but silently never enforced in a stock build
- Topics: SP-04.8, SP-01.6 (O1), SP-10.5, SP-04.13
- Evidence: "acl-origin is NOT in any default feature set, so a stock build ignores the Origin header entirely and a cross-origin write is gated only by the agent's credential" (SP-04.8 note; same in SP-10.5 N and SP-01.6 O1). Meanwhile the parser fully supports origin conditions — `extract_origin_patterns` pulls `acl:origin` triples (`wac/origin.rs:218`) and the gate exists behind the feature (`evaluator.rs:341`).
- Failure: an operator writes ACLs restricting writes to specific origins; the document loads and parses with no warning; the deployed stock binary enforces nothing. Every origin restriction in every ACL on the deployment is dead configuration, and nothing — no log, no metric except the unused `ACL_ORIGIN_REJECTED_TOTAL` counter — reveals it. This matters for browser-held-key flows (NIP-07-style signing), where a page is the cross-origin actor.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE O1, SP-04.8) — but the "parsed yet unenforced, no error" shape is not spelled out.

### F-06 — `acl:agentGroup` appears silently nonfunctional in the shipped server
- Topics: SP-04.15, SP-04.7
- Evidence: the class diagram labels `NoGroupMembership — always false, the server default` (`evaluator.rs:28`); `StaticGroupMembership` is "used by tests and pods that resolve group documents eagerly" (`evaluator.rs:38`). The note says "an unwired GroupMembership resolver (NoGroupMembership) makes every acl:agentGroup clause deny silently — a server that forgets to pass a real resolver … loses group grants without any error" (SP-04.15 N1). No topic shows the server passing any resolver.
- Failure: any ACL granting via `acl:agentGroup` denies for all members on a standard deployment — 403s indistinguishable from a legitimate denial, no error, no log line naming the cause. Group-based access (the WAC-spec feature the evaluator implements at `evaluator.rs:139`) is dead config; whole-user lockouts are debugged as "WAC says no".
- Confidence: medium — "the server default" may be the default type parameter that the server overrides; the material never shows an override, so the inert-server reading is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — the trap is described generically (N1); that the shipped server is affected is nowhere stated.

### F-07 — The 401 challenge advertises DPoP and Bearer, but a production build treats every Bearer header as anonymous
- Topics: SP-04.14, SP-05.1, SP-05.8 (D1, D1b, D3), SP-10.1 (I6)
- Evidence: `acl_denial` sends "WWW-Authenticate: Nostr, DPoP, Bearer — all three realms" (`lib.rs:1160`, SP-04.14). But the auth extractor has exactly two paths, and "production build: every Bearer header is anonymous" (`lib.rs:617`, SP-05.1); the dev-bearer path exists only behind a cargo feature. So the advertised DPoP/Bearer schemes cannot authenticate on a stock server. Adjacent marked drifts compound the incoherence: the verifier accepts EdDSA that discovery does not advertise (invariant 6 "currently BROKEN", `oidc/mod.rs:184` vs `:549`), aud handling is ahead of ADR-2003 (D1), and "without verify_webid_issuer_binding an unrelated IdP can assert any WebID" (D1b) — nothing shows the server ever calls that binding.
- Failure: an OIDC client doing the correct thing — following the challenge, minting a DPoP-bound token, sending it — is silently downgraded to anonymous and 401-loops forever. Separately, any consumer wiring `verify_access_token` per the library API without knowing about the separate binding call accepts forged WebID assertions from arbitrary issuers.
- Confidence: high for the challenge-vs-anonymous contradiction (both are stated directly); the D1b consumer impact is stated as a conditional.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partly — D1/D1b/D3 are marked drifts; the contradiction between the advertised challenge and the production Bearer path is not marked.

### F-08 — Default CORS echoes any Origin with credentials semantics, contradicting "a bare binary opens no back door"
- Topics: SP-06.10, SP-02.3, SP-02.8
- Evidence: "an empty allowed_origins list means every Origin is echoed back — a local-dev default, not a production one" (`lib.rs:3505`, SP-06.10 N2); `allowed_origins` defaults to unset (SP-02.3); wildcard+credentials "degrades to echoing the concrete Origin" with `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials` (`cors.rs:241-243`). Yet the SP-02.3 note claims "a bare `solid-pod-rs-server` opens no back door".
- Failure: every stock deployment reflects any attacker-chosen Origin alongside credentials-enabled CORS. Practical exploitation is limited today because auth is per-request signatures rather than cookies, but any consumer that adds cookie/session auth, or any mashlib/HTML flow a page wants to read cross-origin, inherits a fully permissive default. The "no back door" claim and the "local-dev default" note cannot both be true.
- Confidence: medium (the permissive default is stated; browser-exploit specifics are inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (SP-06.10 N2 note) — but not reconciled with the SP-02.3 claim.

### F-09 — New trails cannot be started: nothing outside tests calls genesis, and the failure message misdirects
- Topics: SP-07.3 (N4, N5), SP-07.20 (N2), SP-07.1
- Evidence: "nothing outside the tests calls `GitmarkAnchorer::genesis` (`mempool.rs:1400`), and no route, CLI verb or boot step writes a git-mark trail file (`trail_store.rs:354`). A new pod therefore has no way to reach the git-mark branch … from this repository" (SP-07.3 N5; repeated SP-07.20 N2). Meanwhile the `_prov/anchor` handler still answers "no pay-token / trail not minted" though `build_anchorer` no longer requires a pay-token (`handlers/prov.rs:501` vs `:195`, SP-07.3 N4).
- Failure: an operator enables the anchoring feature set, buys a funding voucher, tries to start anchoring — and cannot, from any surface this repo ships. The error tells them to mint a pay-token, which since alpha.12 is not the requirement, so they route effort/money into a token they don't need. The flagship "anchored to Bitcoin" custody property is unreachable for new pods.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN N5; DRIFT N4)

### F-10 — A default build records zero provenance while the documentation narrative sells git-marked, anchored writes
- Topics: SP-07.2 (N3), SP-10.6 (D4), SP-10.5, SP-01.7
- Evidence: "with the server's empty default feature set this whole function is the no-op shim (`lib.rs:4110`), so a default build records ZERO marks. Every provenance claim carries a --features git caveat" (SP-07.2 N3); the README's "every write is a git-mark commit" needs `--features git` to be true (SP-10.5 N); the README now says "compiled features, configured service and activation are separate" (SP-10.7 R5).
- Failure: a deployment built from defaults serves LDP normally and never records a mark or raises any error. The gap surfaces only when someone later needs proof — a dispute, an audit, a custody claim — and finds nothing exists. The absence is observable only by noticing responses lack `X-Provenance`.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE SP-07.2 N3; SP-10.6 D4)

### F-11 — Crash and restart leave mismatched body/meta pairs, unmarked writes, and a reopened replay window
- Topics: SP-06.4, SP-10.3, SP-10.4 (W1–W3), SP-10.8 (F5)
- Evidence: "The rename is atomic per file, but the body and its sidecar are two renames — a crash between them leaves a mismatched pair" (`fs.rs:111`, audit F5 still open). "write-then-mark is NOT atomic — a crash between the storage put and the git commit leaves an unmarked resource" (`lib.rs:1518`, W1). "replay protection is process-local — … a restart reopens the window for one TTL" (`lib.rs:196`, W3); TTL is 120 s (SP-05.5).
- Failure: a crash mid-write serves a body with another version's ETag/content-type afterwards (silent corruption clients will cache); a crash after `put` but before the mark loses the provenance record for a stored write; and every restart leaves any NIP-98 event captured in the last two minutes replayable.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (W1–W3; audit F5)

### F-12 — The material contradicts itself on whether MCP is safe to enable
- Topics: SP-08.14, SP-10.8 (F2), SP-02.3
- Evidence: SP-08.14: "the README's status section records 'anonymous MCP reads / WAC sidecar bypass' as a reproduced CRITICAL finding and says to keep MCP disabled." SP-10.8 F2: the same finding is "CLOSED in 0.5.0-alpha.13: one path canonicaliser feeds permission check and storage on every route, MCP enforces the dotfile allowlist and the REST quota path (`CHANGELOG.md:169`, `:178`)", and N says the README "no longer … says to keep MCP disabled". Both cite the same README at the same verified commit.
- Failure: an operator deciding whether to raise `--mcp` gets opposite instructions from two governing sections of the same record. If the SP-08.14 reading is current, enabling MCP re-exposes a critical read/sidecar bypass; if SP-10.8 is current, the feature is fixed and the caution is stale. Either way the decision cannot be made from the documentation, and the alpha.13 operator guidance (rotate trail keys, audit ledgers) suggests prior exposures were real.
- Confidence: high that the two sections contradict; which is accurate is not determinable from this material.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: each half carries a divergence marker; the contradiction between them is not marked.

### F-13 — CI never runs what production would run: forge, git runtime, TLS, the examples, and PR coverage are all outside the gate
- Topics: SP-09.10, SP-01.13 (N3), SP-09.2 (N2B), SP-09.11
- Evidence: the matrix runs core-crate feature sets; the workspace job runs siblings "at DEFAULT features, and every sibling default is empty (SP-01.9)" — so "the forge feature … the git feature end-to-end … the tls feature … deployed behaviour" are ungated (SP-09.10 NG1–NG4). The examples check lines "neither … is a CI job" (SP-01.13 N3) although "These seven ARE the integration contract" (SP-01.12). Coverage runs only on main/schedule/dispatch, so "a pull-request run reaches green without the 60 percent gate having run" (SP-09.2 N2B). Fuzz and bench are "not in the needs list" (SP-09.11).
- Failure: a runtime regression in git provenance, the forge/mirror, TLS, or any example compiles green and ships; the 0.5.0-alpha.13 security fixes lived exactly in these under-tested seams (path canonicalisation across routes). Nothing in the pipeline would catch their reintroduction at runtime.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (each gap individually marked; the aggregate is not)

### F-14 — The estate's shipped build runs forge-full from an untagged commit while ADR-2010 records activation as inactive
- Topics: SP-09.7 (AB), SP-08.18 (Tension), SP-01.8
- Evidence: "that Nix pin is still version 0.5.0-alpha.17 but its rev is now 45c73aa, alpha.17 plus the bounded Jev server integration, no longer the tag commit 55bfec1 (`../project/agentbox/lib/solid-pod-rs.nix:54-57` and `:18`, forge-full compiled in)" (SP-09.7). ADR-2010 "records `activation_status: inactive` because 'no consumer builds it' … while agentbox's pin lists `forge-full` among the features it enables" (SP-08.18 Tension).
- Failure: the binary actually deployed in the estate includes the forge, mirror sockets and anchoring from a commit that is not any release tag, under a decision record asserting nobody consumes it. The mirror activates at boot when `FORGE_MIRROR_RELAYS`/`FORGE_MIRROR_FOLLOW` name anything (SP-01.8 FMI) — relay-following and auto-pull behaviour no ADR reviewed as active. Supply-chain review, key handling (`f1.` token key under the plugin dir) and the mirror's outbound behaviour all run ahead of their governance.
- Confidence: high (both facts stated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension SP-08.18; the off-tag rev is noted in SP-09.7 but not flagged as a problem)

### F-15 — Busy-port shifting can silently produce two live instances over one store
- Topics: SP-02.7, SP-02.1, SP-07.15
- Evidence: on `AddrInUse` the server retries up to 11 times and binds a shifted port with only a warning ("warn port busy — shifted listener", `main.rs:206`); the listener is handed to actix and the process reports healthy. Shutdown drains for `shutdown_timeout(30)` (`main.rs:485`). The payment/trail locks are process-local (F-01, `lib.rs:208`).
- Failure: a supervised restart where the old instance is still draining its 30-second window holds the configured port; the new instance silently binds port+1 and both processes now serve the same pod store simultaneously — precisely the unprotected two-writer configuration of F-01, entered through normal operations with no error, only a warning line. Clients and health checks aimed at the old port keep talking to the draining instance while writes land on the new one.
- Confidence: medium — each element is stated; the chained scenario (restart overlap → shifted bind → concurrent writers) is inferred from them.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — the port shift is documented as a convenience behaviour; its interaction with the process-local locks is flagged nowhere.

## Not judgeable from this material
