1. **Account-store persistence.** The server registers `/login/password`, `/account/password/reset`, `/account/password/change` unconditionally (SP-02.9), and the only `UserStore` implementation the material names is `InMemoryUserStore` (`solid-pod-rs-idp/src/user_store.rs:70`), with the same question open for the OIDC `SessionStore` codes (SP-05.16). Whether accounts and sessions survive a restart — or vanish, making password login cosmetic — needs the server's actual wiring.
2. **ActivityPub keyId-to-actor binding.** The inbox resolves the signing key from a URL in the untrusted signature header under an SSRF guard (SP-08.5), but nothing states the resolved key is checked against the activity's claimed `actor`. Without that check the inbox accepts identity assertions from anyone's key; with it, it doesn't. The code must be read.
3. **Deployment posture.** Every finding conditioned on a flag (`--mcp`, `open_edit`, `acl-origin`, CORS origins, TLS, quota) depends on the runtime configuration of the specific deployment, which this material cannot show. Sign-off needs the production config and the exact feature set compiled into the shipped binary.
4. **Current advisory status of the two RUSTSEC exceptions** (`RUSTSEC-2026-0258` h2, `RUSTSEC-2023-0071` rsa, `deny.toml:47,56`). The material itself says the README's refresh "did not establish current dependency-advisory status" and that historical greens are no claim — an up-to-date `cargo audit` against the shipping lockfile is required.
5. **Whether deposit credit amounts are bound to chain value.** Whole-trail verification states "the amount is not checked on this path … The key and the spend order are the whole check" (SP-07.19). Whether the credited amount derives solely from the verified MRC20 state payload (safe) or anywhere touches unverified on-chain values needs the credit-amount code path in `handle_mrc20_deposit`.

## solid-pod-rs / premortem

# Post-mortem — custody loss on the pod payment surface

The incident under investigation is a custody failure: value recorded in a client's pod ledger was paid out or double-counted in a way the stored ledger could not reconcile against the chain, and the client terminated. Root cause has not been reproduced; the three candidates below are ranked from defects the repository's own records already carry at the verified head. Every claim is drawn from the diagrams-as-code material and its `path:line` citations; where I reason past what the material states, I say so.

---

### R-1 — Process-local payment and trail locks mean a second process over the same pod store can double-spend the ledger and the on-chain trail head
- Topics: SP-07.15, SP-07.12, SP-07.18, SP-10.4, SP-09.10
- Chain of events:
  1. An operator carries token value for a client through `/pay/*` — the surface is registered unconditionally, with no payments feature flag (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:5262`), despite the README recording that value must not be carried until audit findings are fixed.
  2. For availability the deployment runs a second process over the same pod storage. Nothing in CI or the evidence base covers deployed behaviour, a real cache, or crossing a restart (`SP-09.10` NG4).
  3. Both processes hold their own `PAYMENT_STATE_LOCK`; the storage API has no cross-resource CAS primitive, so the replay check, ledger mutation and persist are only serialised *within* one process.
  4. A ledger mutation on both replicas interleaves read-state → mutate → commit; one commit overwrites the other (lost update), so a balance is spent twice or a credit applied twice.
  5. Independently, the per-trail locks are also in-process and held across network calls: two concurrent anchors on the same trail each build a spend of the same head outpoint; one transaction is orphaned after the pod has already recorded both advances.
  6. Balances and the trail diverge from the chain with nothing to reconcile them — the quota `reconcile` operator command exists, a payments one does not appear anywhere in the material.
- Evidence: `PAYMENT_STATE_LOCK` is process-wide and "the storage API has NO cross-resource CAS primitive" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:208`); the divergence note records "non-atomic payment state" as a *reproduced critical* audit finding; W4 repeats it (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:204`); W8 records trail and epoch locks as in-process, held across network calls, "a known gap that a second replica does not share" (`CHANGELOG.md:222`); the per-trail lock serialises only one process (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/mampool.rs` — `solid-pod-rs-server/src/mempool.rs:755`); ADR-2008 makes fixing non-atomic state (D7) a precondition of the port, and nothing states what replaces the lock (`adr/ADR-2008-...md:72`); the whole `/pay` family is always on (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:5262`).
- Warning signs visible today: the repo's own audit line calls the payment state non-atomic and critical; ADR-2008 gates its chain-view work on fixing this first and the fix is not built; no CI job starts the binary or crosses a restart.
- Confidence: high that the defect exists exactly as recorded; medium, inferred, that it is this incident's cause — it requires a two-process deployment, which the material does not state occurred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Whole-trail verification accepts an unconfirmed mempool head, and the deposit credit it gates is irreversible: credit-then-double-spend
- Topics: SP-07.19, SP-07.9, SP-07.17, SP-07.13, SP-07.18
- Chain of events:
  1. A depositor submits an MRC20 deposit whose anchor chain head transaction is still in the mempool.
  2. `verify_mrc20_anchor` walks every mark back from the head; a head still in the mempool is accepted — "intact, not verified" (`solid-pod-rs/src/mrc20.rs:1034`).
  3. The deposit handler passes a replay guard on the anchor outpoint and credits the caller's ticker balance via `credit_by_outpoint` (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/pay.rs:655`).
  4. Credits have no reversal path: `reverse_payout` undoes only payout receipts (`solid-pod-rs/src/payments.rs:536`); a repeated credit for the same outpoint is a no-op (`AlreadyApplied`), but nothing claws a credit back.
  5. The credited balance is spent through the still-live token routes — `/pay/.swap` (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/pay.rs:918`), `/pay/.buy` (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/pay.rs:1396`), `/pay/.withdraw-sats` (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/pay.rs:1588`) — which ADR-2008 D3 says should already be retired and which the record concedes are live "in part" pending D4/D5.
  6. The head transaction never confirms or is double-spent (inferred — the material does not discuss replacement or eviction); the chain never carried the value, but the pod already paid out against it.
- Evidence: `is_verified` needs every mark confirmed, `is_intact` also accepts a mempool head, and `verify` answers with `is_intact` (`solid-pod-rs/src/blocktrail.rs:841`, `:849`; `solid-pod-rs-server/src/mempool.rs:856`); the head-in-mempool acceptance is stated at `solid-pod-rs/src/mrc20.rs:1034`; credit-by-outpoint as the only credit at `solid-pod-rs/src/payments.rs:450`; issuer/ticker binding on deposits (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/pay.rs:549`) constrains *who* can deposit but not *whether the head is confirmed*.
- Warning signs visible today: the material itself distinguishes intact from verified and states verify answers intact; an irreversible credit gated on a zero-confirmation chain event is visible on the page today.
- Confidence: medium — the acceptance and irreversibility are stated; the double-spend/eviction step is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — A restart or second replica reopens the NIP-98 replay window: a captured kind-27235 event re-executes a payment or write
- Topics: SP-05.4, SP-05.5, SP-05.1, SP-10.4, SP-10.6
- Chain of events:
  1. A client signs a state-changing request (a `/pay/.buy`, `/pay/.swap`, or an LDP POST/PUT/PATCH) with a NIP-98 event.
  2. The pod restarts — a rolling deploy or a crash — within the event's freshness window; the process-local `Nip98ReplayCache` loses every entry.
  3. The same event is re-submitted within its 60-second two-sided tolerance (`solid-pod-rs/src/auth/nip98.rs:28`); `NIP98_REPLAY.check_and_record` (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:641`) sees an empty store and records it as fresh.
  4. The request re-executes: a second purchase, swap or withdrawal debits the client's balance again, or a POST mints a duplicate resource.
  5. On a second replica the window is not 120 seconds but permanent — the replay store is shared by nothing (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:196`).
- Evidence: SP-05.5 states the contract plainly — "A process-local store loses every entry on restart, so each restart reopens the window for one TTL", default TTL 120 s (`solid-pod-rs/src/auth/replay.rs:78`); W3 (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:196`); W5 records that only the single-use store closes the freshness gap (`solid-pod-rs/src/auth/nip98.rs:28`); the ReplayStore seam has exactly one implementor and the second tier lives out-of-repo in nostr-rust-forum (`solid-pod-rs/src/auth/replay_store.rs:90`; SP-10.6 D6), so nothing in this repo closes it.
- Warning signs visible today: the restart-reopen property is stated as the store's own contract note; the estate's edge consumer still pins a caret range and has not adopted a tier-2 store.
- Confidence: high that the window exists as described; low-to-medium, inferred, that it caused this particular loss — it needs a restart or replica inside a 60-second window.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Filesystem body and `.meta` sidecar are two separate atomic renames; a crash between them leaves a mismatched pair
- Topics: SP-06.4, SP-10.4, SP-10.8
- Evidence: the write sequence asserts the `.meta.json` sidecar is renamed first, then the body (`solid-pod-rs/src/storage/fs.rs:111`); the audit records that filesystem writes "still violate the advertised atomic-storage contract... a crash between them leaves a mismatched pair" (SP-06.4 divergence note; SP-10.8 F5).
- Failure: after a crash, a resource is served with a metadata sidecar (ETag, size, content type) that does not describe the stored body; a client or cache validating by ETag accepts or serves the wrong bytes.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes

### F-02 — `verify_access_token` does not check the WebID↔issuer binding; any IdP can assert any WebID unless every caller remembers the second call
- Topics: SP-05.8, SP-10.6
- Evidence: "verify_access_token does not check that the webid profile lists iss as solid:oidcIssuer (`solid-pod-rs/src/oidc/mod.rs:833`), so without verify_webid_issuer_binding an unrelated IdP can assert any WebID" (SP-05.8, DIVERGENCE D1b).
- Failure: a future consumer of the library (a forge session injector, an embedding host, the IdP userinfo route) verifies a token without the binding call; an attacker-run IdP mints a token for the victim's WebID and the WAC/cross-identity path treats it as the victim.
- Confidence: high that the seam is opt-in; inferred that a caller omits it.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes

### F-03 — Forge `open_edit = true` removes authorisation entirely: any caller may edit any repo
- Topics: SP-08.18
- Evidence: "with it true no authorisation check runs at all and any caller may edit any repo, so the service logs a warning when it is on" (default false at `solid-pod-rs-forge/src/config.rs:131`, warning at `solid-pod-rs-forge/src/lib.rs:217`, gate at `solid-pod-rs-forge/src/web_edit.rs:226`).
- Failure: an operator flips the flag for a demo or internal convenience and forgets; anonymous internet commits into any namespace's repository — a data-integrity and content-custody loss mitigated only by a log line.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes

### F-04 — The MCP critical finding was "closed" in alpha.13, but the disable-only posture and the absence of any runtime CI gate leave the closure unverifiable
- Topics: SP-08.14, SP-10.8, SP-09.10
- Evidence: SP-10.8 F2 says the anonymous-MCP/WAC-bypass finding is CLOSED in alpha.13 via one canonical path canonicaliser (`CHANGELOG.md:169`, `:178`); SP-08.14 still records the README instruction to keep MCP disabled; SP-09.10 states the CI matrix never exercises MCP runtime behaviour and no job starts the binary.
- Failure: twelve months on, MCP is enabled in the belief the finding is closed; a regression in one tool's `wac_check` or the SSE subscription path — none of which any required check runs — reopens the bypass against client data.
- Confidence: medium (the doc tension is real; the regression is inferred).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes

### F-05 — The git-mark trail's base *secret* is written under `/.well-known/gitmark/`, and the guarded-path list as documented may not cover it
- Topics: SP-07.20, SP-04.1
- Evidence: `save_gitmark_trail` writes the secret-bearing trail at `/.well-known/gitmark/name.json` (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/trail_store.rs:208`); `guarded_acl_target` elevates "trail secrets, /.well-known/prov/ state and /.well-known/webledgers/" to `acl:Control` (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:826`) — the material never enumerates `/.well-known/gitmark/`, and gitmark trails are new in alpha.12 while the guard list's other entries read as pre-existing.
- Failure: if the guard does not cover the gitmark paths, the file holding the key that derives every on-chain mark is governed by whatever ordinary ACL the pod carries there; a pod with a permissive inherited ACL serves the secret, and whoever reads it controls the trail key — able to spend the head or forge future anchors.
- Confidence: low — inferred; the guard phrase "trail secrets" may well include it, and nothing in the material shows the actual code list.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — Default CORS configuration echoes any Origin
- Topics: SP-06.10, SP-02.3
- Evidence: "an empty allowed_origins list means every Origin is echoed back — a local-dev default, not a production one" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:3505`; `solid-pod-rs/src/security/cors.rs:243`), and every CLI knob defaults to OFF/None (SP-02.3).
- Failure: a default deployment reflects arbitrary origins; the signature-bound NIP-98 surface resists cross-site driving, but the account and IdP-adjacent POST routes registered in SP-02.9 have no signature binding shown, so a malicious page can make credentialed cross-origin calls succeed CORS-wise against them.
- Confidence: medium — the echo is stated; cookie/credential use on those routes is not.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes

### F-07 — Pending anchors keep the debit, and failed refunds are parked in a file nothing in the material drains
- Topics: SP-07.6, SP-07.12, SP-07.3
- Evidence: once the intent is recorded the broadcast may be on-chain, so a failure returns "anchor pending" and the caller is not refunded (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/mempool.rs:717`); the `_prov/anchor` route answers 202 and does not refund a pending anchor (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/prov.rs:527`); a failed refund is "retained in /.well-known/prov/refunds-pending.json" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/prov.rs:683`) with no reprocessor stated anywhere.
- Failure: a run of broadcast failures silently accumulates customer debits; refunds require manual action the system does not define, and during an incident nobody knows the file is the ledger of owed money.
- Confidence: medium — retention is stated; the absent reprocessor is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — The git-mark anchorer is unreachable: no route, CLI verb or boot step ever starts a trail
- Topics: SP-07.3, SP-07.20
- Evidence: "nothing outside the tests calls GitmarkAnchorer::genesis (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/mempool.rs:1400`), and no route, CLI verb or boot step writes a git-mark trail file (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/trail_store.rs:354`)" (SP-07.3, OPEN).
- Failure: an operator sells Bitcoin-anchored provenance; every new pod's `Always`/`Epoch` policy quietly degrades to marks-only — writes still return 201 and receipts carry anchor errors nobody surfaces as a contract breach.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes

### F-09 — Epoch accumulation silently drops commits past 4× the epoch size, and failed batch anchors are swallowed
- Topics: SP-07.7
- Evidence: "Past 4 times the epoch size a commit is no longer accumulated, but the close is still attempted" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/prov.rs:289`); "A failed batch anchor is swallowed — it never fails the write nor the git-mark" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:4066`).
- Failure: a prolonged mempool outage means every commit beyond the 4× ceiling is permanently unanchored with no surfaced error; the custody guarantee a client believes they hold silently does not exist for those writes.
- Confidence: medium
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — The SPARQL-update fuzz target asserts no panics on an authenticated write surface, but runs 60 seconds weekly outside ci-required
- Topics: SP-09.11, SP-09.12, SP-03.5
- Evidence: the fuzz contract encodes "in-cap input must either succeed or error — never panic", reachable "from an authenticated write (SP-03.5)"; the job is schedule/dispatch only and "not in the needs list" (`../solid-pod-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:292`, `:309`).
- Failure: a panic-inducing PATCH lands between weekly runs and reaches production; an authenticated caller repeatedly crashes a worker — a denial of service on the write path.
- Confidence: medium — the coverage gap is stated; an actual panic is not.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes

### F-11 — The RUSTSEC-2026-0258 exception's justification ("no HTTP/2 listener enabled") is invalidated the moment the tls feature is turned on
- Topics: SP-09.4, SP-01.8
- Evidence: the advisory exception for h2 0.3 via actix-http is justified by "no HTTP/2 listener enabled" (`../solid-pod-rs/deny.toml:47`; SP-09.4 N4); a `tls` feature exists and is compiled by nothing in CI (`crates/solid-pod-rs-server/Cargo.toml:136`; SP-09.10 NG3).
- Failure: an operator enables tls for production HTTPS; the ignored advisory becomes live on a listener the exception's premise excludes, and nothing — not CI, which never builds tls — catches the change in exposure.
- Confidence: medium — inferred link between the tls feature and an HTTP/2 listener.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — The `acl:origin` gate is off by default: a stock build ignores the Origin header entirely
- Topics: SP-04.8, SP-01.6
- Evidence: `acl-origin` is not in any default feature set (`crates/solid-pod-rs/Cargo.toml:173`; SP-01.6 O1), so "a default build ignores the Origin header entirely and a cross-origin write is gated only by the agent's credential" (SP-04.8).
- Failure: combined with F-06's origin echo, any browser context holding a non-signature credential can be driven cross-site against the pod; the WAC origin policy an operator believes they have is not compiled in.
- Confidence: high that the default is off; medium on exploitability.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes

### F-13 — The anchor handler's failure message misdiagnoses: "no pay-token / trail not minted" when a pay-token is no longer required
- Topics: SP-07.3
- Evidence: the handler still answers "no pay-token / trail not minted" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/prov.rs:501`) while `build_anchorer` no longer requires one (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/prov.rs:195`) — DRIFT N4.
- Failure: during an anchoring outage an operator chases a missing pay-token that is not the problem, extending the outage and the period of unanchored writes.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes

### F-14 — The 60% coverage gate never runs on pull requests
- Topics: SP-09.2
- Evidence: coverage runs only on main pushes, weekly schedule and dispatch (`../solid-pod-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:201`), and ci-required explicitly accepts a skipped coverage result for that job alone (`../solid-pod-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:347`).
- Failure: regressions in exactly the code paths only tests cover land green on PRs; the gate the number promises is advisory for the changes most likely to introduce the defects in this document.

- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes

### F-15 — The examples are the integration contract and nothing in CI compiles them
- Topics: SP-01.13, SP-09.10
- Evidence: "neither cargo check line is a CI job — SP-09.10 shows the matrix never builds --examples, so this gate is documentation an author must run by hand" (SP-01.13, DIVERGENCE N3).

## visionclaw / critical

### F-01 — Payment handler debits users for resources it never delivers
- Topics: VC-04.27
- Evidence: The handler confirms and charges (`store.debit(&did, cost)` returns remaining balance) but "resource proxying is a STUB — this confirms and charges but never forwards to the underlying resource handler (pay_handler.rs:503-505)". ADR-2111 which would delete this is "PROPOSED, NOT LIVE".
- Failure: A user with PAY_ENABLED=true pays sats for inference/image-gen/analytics, receives a 200 with `X-Balance` and `X-Cost` headers showing the debit, and never receives the resource. Sats are lost with no delivery and no refund path (`/pay/.deposit` returns 501).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (the stub is stated plainly, not flagged as DEBT/DRIFT/OPEN)

### F-02 — Ingest writeback route bypasses the X-Agent-Key gate the decision core otherwise requires
- Topics: VC-05.7
- Evidence: The enrichment-proposals decide route enforces `require_agent_key :141 — header must equal VISIONCLAW_AGENT_KEY`. The git-bridge writeback route `POST /api/ingest/writeback` calls the SAME `apply_decision` core but the material states "(no X-Agent-Key required, GOV-4)".
- Failure: Any authenticated Editor-level user can inject decisions into the enrichment store through `/api/ingest/writeback` without possessing the shared service secret, bypassing the service-level authentication the primary route requires. The body-supplied `broker_pubkey` is also trusted for attribution without proof of key ownership.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — client-messages WebSocket token contents never verified, any non-empty string passes
- Topics: VC-04.25
- Evidence: "the token's CONTENTS are never verified against NostrService, only its presence, so any non-empty string passes" (client_messages_handler.rs:131-142). The code rejects only an empty token with 401.
- Failure: A client connects to `/ws/client-messages` with `Authorization: Bearer x` — a one-character garbage token — and receives the full outbound agent message stream including session IDs and agent IDs. The in-code comment says "allows but logs unauthenticated connections" which is also false; the code does reject empty tokens.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (the DOC-DRIFT marker covers the stale comment, not the verification gap)

### F-04 — NIP-98 replay cache is process-local, allowing token replay across replicas
- Topics: VC-03.4, VC-03.5
- Evidence: "Process-local scope — no cross-replica replay protection (SECURITY-profiles.md)" (nip98.rs REPLAY_CACHE note). The replay cache is a `Mutex<HashMap>` inside each process.
- Failure: Deploy two replicas behind a load balancer. Send a valid kind-27235 event to replica A (consumed). Replay the same bytes to replica B (accepted, because B's cache has no entry). A single-use credential is now used twice, breaking the non-replay invariant that the identity authority chain asserts.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high (in multi-replica deployments; single-replica is unaffected)
- Marked by authors: no (documented as a limitation in SECURITY-profiles.md but presented as an INVARIANT note, not as an open defect)

### F-05 — Settings persistence failure is warn-only, actor state diverges from disk
- Topics: VC-06.3
- Evidence: After the actor round-trip succeeds and GPU params propagate, the SQLite write runs: `set_setting("physics", ...)` with "alt persist fails / DB-->>H: warn only — the response still succeeds" (settings_routes.rs:667-673).
- Failure: A disk-full or SQLite lock causes the physics settings write to fail silently. The user receives 200, the in-memory actor and GPU run with new values, but a restart reverts to the last persisted snapshot. The operator has no alert, only a warn-level log line.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high (silent data loss on restart)
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — Decision attribution trusts caller-supplied broker_pubkey without verification
- Topics: VC-05.7, VC-05.17
- Evidence: `apply_decision` builds attribution from "64-hex broker_pubkey -> did_nostr+kg URN, else unattributed" with no signature check. The REST path writes `decision_event_id:None` (enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:558), meaning no signed kind-31403 correlation exists for any REST-originated decision.
- Failure: A caller with the agent key (or any authenticated user via F-02) attributes a decision to an arbitrary did:nostr by including that hex in the body. The provenance trace (`GET /api/trace`) and KPI computations then report decisions that the named agent never made, and no evidence distinguishes them from genuine ones.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high (provenance integrity, not just metadata)
- Marked by authors: no (the `decision_event_id:None` divergence is marked, but the trust-the-body attribution is not)

### F-07 — Constraints settings write commits but GPU push fails silently
- Topics: VC-04.7
- Evidence: `POST /api/constraints/define` merges `{computeMode = 2}` into app_settings BEFORE "a best-effort GPU push (constraint failures there are only warn-logged, never fail the request, constraints_handler.rs:100)".
- Failure: The user defines ontology constraints, receives 200 with the settings confirmed, but the GPU actor never received them. The physics simulation continues without the constraints. No error reaches the client; the only trace is a warn log. Every subsequent graph layout is incorrect relative to what the user believes is configured.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (described as "best-effort" without flagging the silent failure mode)

### F-08 — PTX .version downgrade is a text splice that does not verify instruction compatibility
- Topics: VC-12.5
- Evidence: "the rewrite is a declared-ISA text splice only — it does not prove every instruction is supported by that ISA, only a real driver load settles that (ADR-2030 Consequences)".
- Failure: A CUDA 13.x nvcc emits PTX using instructions not in ISA 9.0. The build-time rewrite changes the `.version` directive to 9.0, the build succeeds, and at runtime the driver JIT either fails to compile the module (system degraded) or produces incorrect code (silent data corruption in physics positions). The build gives no warning.
- Confidence: medium (the failure requires an actual instruction mismatch, which is version-dependent)
- Severity: high (could cause silent incorrect computation)
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-09 — ForceComputeActor self-init creates a CUDA context invisible to supervision and health
- Topics: VC-10.6, VC-10.3
- Evidence: "self-init is a second, unsupervised path to a CUDA context that bypasses ResourceSupervisor timeouts and backoff" and "a context built this way is invisible to ResourceSupervisor accounting and GetSubsystemHealth" (ADR-2053 addendum). The boot log "GPU context self-initialized successfully" means the supervisor path FAILED.
- Failure: ResourceSupervisor reports the GPU subsystem as Failed/Degraded (the supervised init failed) while ForceComputeActor is actually computing with its own context. An operator monitoring GetSubsystemHealth sees degradation and may restart the process, but nothing is actually wrong. Conversely, if the self-init context has a subtle device error, no supervision path detects or retries it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, RECORDED as deliberate by ADR-2053 addendum)

### F-10 — Two decision producers converge only at a SQLite table, no complete human-approval journey
- Topics: VC-05.17, VC-05.18
- Evidence: "the two producers only converge at the SQLite table, no handler route calls into either actor, and case authority/failure/restart receipts are not modelled here" (elevation_actor.rs vs enrichment_proposals_handler.rs). BASELINE-architecture.md:357 states "Current source review does not certify a complete human-approval journey."
- Failure: A decision approved by the forum (elevation actor path, with a signed 31403 event) and a decision made via REST (no signed event) are indistinguishable in the broker inbox. If the elevation actor crashes mid-approval, the case remains in an ambiguous state with no restart receipt. The governance guarantee ACSP claims (human approval with signed evidence) does not hold end-to-end.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium (governance and audit trail integrity, not runtime availability)
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-11 — 16ms receive gate acknowledges chunks the worker never processed
- Topics: VC-13.7, VC-13.4
- Evidence: "handleBinaryFrame returns normally when a frame arrives within 16 ms of the last accepted one, so the ack is still sent for a chunk the worker never saw" (wsClient.ts:26-28, binaryProtocol.ts:392-408). The server's ClientStream then records those nodes as held.
- Failure: Client receives chunk B within 16ms of chunk A. B is dropped before reaching the worker. B is acknowledged. Server marks B's nodes as current for this client. If those nodes do not subsequently move past `streamMoveThreshold`, the client displays stale positions for them indefinitely — the server believes they are delivered.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION in VC-13.7)

### F-12 — Two unreconciled naming grammars for the same GitHub coordinates
- Topics: VC-09.11
- Evidence: "GITHUB_REPO_OWNER, GITHUB_REPO_NAME and GITHUB_BASE_BRANCH are read only by github_pr_service.rs and duplicate GITHUB_OWNER, GITHUB_REPO and GITHUB_BRANCH read elsewhere — two naming grammars for the same coordinates, unreconciled".
- Failure: An operator sets GITHUB_OWNER=acme and GITHUB_REPO=repo for the sync service. The PR service reads GITHUB_REPO_OWNER (unset) and GITHUB_REPO_NAME (unset) and operates against the wrong or empty repository, creating PRs against a nonexistent target or silently failing to create PRs at all.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-13 — Position streaming halts entirely if node classification fails to load
- Topics: VC-03.9
- Evidence: "alt policy_on and node classification not yet loaded (!cx.nta.loaded) / CC-->>CC: warn and send nothing — fail closed, 'no classification' must not read as 'nothing private'" (client_coordinator_actor.rs:408-413).
- Failure: GraphStateActor fails to push node-type arrays (e.g., graph data corruption, actor mailbox failure). The ClientCoordinator's `cx.nta.loaded` stays false. Every pump call hits the fail-closed branch. No client receives any position frame. The system serves REST and health checks normally — it appears healthy but delivers nothing over WebSocket. No canary fires because no frames are expected.
- Confidence: medium (inferred: the material shows the gate but does not state what happens if classification never arrives)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (described as correct fail-closed behavior without noting the operational consequence of a total streaming halt)

### F-14 — Settings hot-reload watcher is disabled due to database deadlocks
- Topics: VC-06.1
- Evidence: "the settings hot-reload watcher is DISABLED, src/app_state.rs:1110-1113 — reason recorded in code: it was causing database deadlocks".
- Failure: Settings changes made by one process (e.g., the sync binary or a direct SQLite write) are never picked up by the running server. The actor's in-memory settings diverge from disk indefinitely until a restart. Combined with F-05, a restart may then load values that differ from both what the API user set and what the actor was running with.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-15 — MultiMcpVisualizationActor is defined but never started
- Topics: VC-02.19
- Evidence: "MultiMcpVisualizationActor has no start() call found in src/app_state.rs or src/main.rs — coverage gap, reported not diagrammed further (see report)".
- Failure: Any consumer that expects this actor to be running (e.g., the multi-MCP visualization WebSocket feature) receives no data. The actor's message handlers exist and compile but nothing processes them. If a `/multi-mcp/ws` client sends a message that routes to this actor, it is silently dropped or errors depending on whether an Addr is held anywhere.
- Confidence: medium (inferred from absence; the material states the gap but does not describe a consumer that depends on it)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no (described as "coverage gap" without a formal DEBT/DRIFT marker)

---

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Actual runtime behaviour of the PTX splice under a real driver**: whether any instruction emitted by CUDA 13.x actually uses an opcode absent from ISA 9.0 — requires a build-and-load test on target hardware.

2. **Whether the SQLite role store and the enrichment store share a database file or connection pool**: if they share a `tokio_rusqlite::Connection`, a deadlock in one domain could freeze RBAC lookups system-wide (fail-closed to Viewer for all users).

3. **The actual contents of `SECURITY-profiles.md` and `IDENTITY-authority-chain.md`**: these are cited as governing documents but their full text is not in the material — the multi-replica replay limitation and the NIP-26 deferral scope may have additional caveats or mitigations not visible here.

4. **Whether any consumer actually calls `/pay/{resource}` in production**: the severity of F-01 depends on whether the pay gateway is reachable by real users with funded ledgers, or whether PAY_ENABLED is universally false in deployed environments.

5. **The agentbox side of the broker-bridge**: the material references `agentbox/management-api/routes/broker-bridge.js` but does not show its authentication model — whether it adds its own gate before forwarding to `/api/ingest/writeback` (which would partially mitigate F-02) cannot be determined.

## visionclaw / premortem

# Post-mortem — visionclaw estate

Written against the diagram register at commit `e74bcddc772d47a45b1971156a2bab540eaa027c` (agentbox `0b0f7918961e629280faf86f88227535abf514b5`). Every claim below carries the citation the register gives; where I reason past what a diagram asserts, the confidence line says "inferred". Ordering of steps within a diagram is only claimed where the diagram claims it.

---

### R-1 — The payment routes debit a customer's sats balance, return 200 success, and never deliver anything
- Topics: VC-04.27, VC-09.13, VC-01.5 (feature default), ADR-2111 as cited in VC-04.27
- Chain of events: the `/pay/*` route set is compiled in by default (`solid-pod-embed` is in the default feature set, Cargo.toml:278, cited in VC-01.5) and mounted unconditionally (src/main.rs:1105 per VC-04.27; src/main.rs:1097-1105 per VC-09.13) → operator flips `PAY_ENABLED=true` to monetise a tier → client calls `GET /pay/{resource_path}` with a NIP-98 header → handler resolves `cost_for_endpoint` (inference = cost×10, image-gen = cost×100, analytics = cost×5) → `FsPaymentStore.debit(&did, cost)` succeeds → handler returns `200 {resource, charged, balance}` with `X-Balance`/`X-Cost` headers → the underlying resource handler is never invoked → the customer has paid and received nothing, and the only top-up path (`POST /pay/.deposit`) is a 501 stub directing them to "contact the server operator for manual funding". The ADR that would have deleted this ledger (ADR-2111) was re-qualified 2026-10-01 to keep `FsPaymentStore` and `/pay/*` host-owned, so the defect persists into any deployment that enables the flag.
- Evidence: VC-04.27 states verbatim "resource proxying is a STUB — this confirms and charges but never forwards to the underlying resource handler (pay_handler.rs:503-505)" and "POST /pay/.deposit → 501 Not Implemented (:487-492)"; routes "mounted UNCONDITIONALLY at src/main.rs:1105 … inert until PAY_ENABLED=true" (pay_handler.rs:96); `PAY_LEDGER_DIR` is a local directory (pay_handler.rs:103, :958); ADR-2111 "decision_status proposed, implementation_status none" and the 2026-10-01 re-qualification "FsPaymentStore and /pay/* stay host-owned and the record stays proposed" (ADR-2111 doc :38, :83, :95-96).
- Warning signs visible today: a reachable, charged happy path whose delivery half is unimplemented; a working debit beside a 501 deposit; a ledger on local disk with no stated backup; an ADR describing the removal sitting in "proposed" for over a year.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high

### R-2 — "Revoke" does not revoke: deleting a role row drops the principal to ambient authority, so a removed key keeps write access
- Topics: VC-03.8, VC-03.7, VC-03.6, VC-05.1, VC-04.4, VC-09.9
- Chain of events: a compromised or departing user's key must be cut → operator calls `DELETE /api/admin/rbac/users/{pubkey}/role` → 200 response with a "note" warning → `effective_role` resolution now runs with no explicit row: Admin if the pubkey matches `POWER_USER_PUBKEYS`, else the configured default → `RBAC_DEFAULT_ROLE` unset parses to **Editor** (ADR-2010 compat default, role_store.rs:195) → Editor maps to `AccessLevel::Authenticated` (rbac.rs:84-90) → every mutating route outside `/api/settings` requires only `WriteGraph` at the RbacGate (VC-03.6 mutating classification, rbac_gate.rs:262) → the "revoked" user can still `POST /api/files/process` and `POST /api/files/update_graph` — which VC-04.4 notes carry **no RequireAuth wrap**, only the scope-wide mutating classification — triggering a full corpus re-fetch and `AddNodesFromMetadata` graph rebuild, and can still `POST /api/decisions/record`, appending to the insert-only provenance store. If the compromised key is listed in `POWER_USER_PUBKEYS`, the deletion changes nothing at all: ambient Admin.
- Evidence: VC-03.8 — "removal is NOT revocation: deleting the row drops the target to their AMBIENT authority (Admin if power-user, else configured_default), never denies outright. Genuine revocation requires an explicit Viewer assignment" (remove_checked role_store.rs:500); VC-03.7 precedence (role_store.rs:359) and `parse_default_role` unset → Editor (role_store.rs:195); VC-05.1 — the removal response itself carries a "note" warning "the target may still hold effective_after by ambient default" (:203); VC-04.4 — files::refresh_graph/update_graph have "no RequireAuth wrap here (only the scope-wide RbacGate mutating classification applies)"; VC-09.9 — `POWER_USER_PUBKEYS` "maps to Admin when unassigned" (nostr_service.rs:134).
- Warning signs visible today: an API whose success response must carry a warning that the requested action did not occur; a default role (Editor) that carries `WriteGraph`; a second, parallel grant channel (power-user env list) that role deletion cannot touch.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Success is reported while the second store silently failed: no cross-store transaction, warn-only persistence, and no verified restore across Oxigraph + per-domain SQLite
- Topics: VC-01.1, VC-05.7, VC-06.3, VC-05.17, VC-07.9, VC-07.7
- Chain of events: a governance decision arrives → `repo.record_decision` commits to the enrichment SQLite → `append_derived_summary` to Oxigraph fails → `writeback_committed=false`, warn DEGRADED — yet the route returns `200 DecideResponse{success:true, …}` → the decision row exists without its provenance quads and nothing reconciles them later. Independently, `PUT /api/settings/physics` updates the actor and GPU, then `set_setting` fails → "warn only — the response still succeeds" → after any restart the SQLite row is stale and physics silently reverts. The register states outright that the persistence layer has "no cross-store transaction, no actor reload consistency, no restore correctness", so after disk loss or a restore attempt the decision store, provenance graph, settings and KPI tables can be mutually inconsistent with no detection path. The two decision producers (elevation actor with signed kind-31403 correlation, REST path without) converge only at the one SQLite table, so the divergence is also invisible downstream.
- Evidence: VC-01.1 DIVERGENCE — "shared Oxigraph ownership establishes no cross-store transaction, no actor reload consistency, no restore correctness" (BASELINE 2026-09-04 persistence closeout); VC-05.7 — writeback failure path sets `writeback_committed=false`, warn DEGRADED, then returns `DecideResponse{success:true,…}` (record_decision enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:561; StoredDecision built :543-560); VC-06.3 — "alt persist fails / DB-->>H: warn only — the response still succeeds" (settings_routes.rs:667-673); VC-05.17 — "the two producers only converge at the SQLite table" and the REST path "writes decision_event_id:None every time" (:543-560) versus the elevation actor setting it from the signed event (elevation_actor.rs:1749), with BASELINE-architecture.md:357 quoted: "Current source review does not certify a complete human-approval journey"; VC-07.7 — missing settings silently fall back to defaults (api_handler/mod.rs:68-73).
- Warning signs visible today: success responses that depend on warn-only second writes; an append-only guarantee (ADR-2016 `commit_quads`) enforced per-path but not across the decision+writeback pair; an explicit register statement that restore correctness was never established.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — `/ws/client-messages` authenticates on token *presence only*: any non-empty string passes
- Topics: VC-04.25, VC-03.2 (contrast), VC-05.12, VC-05.13
- Evidence: VC-04.25 states "token present (NOT actually verified against NostrService here) … the token's CONTENTS are never verified against NostrService, only its presence, so any non-empty string passes" (client_messages_handler.rs:131-142), and flags the in-code comment as DOC-DRIFT (:114-116). The sibling WS routes received exactly this fix under ADR-2090 — mcp-relay resolves the token through `get_session` (mcp_relay_handler.rs:445-452, VC-05.12) and multi-mcp likewise (multi_mcp_websocket_handler.rs:783-790, VC-05.13) — while client_messages_handler is not listed among them.
- Failure: anyone connecting with `Authorization: Bearer x` receives the streamed `{type:client_message, content, session_id, agent_id}` frames from `app_state.client_message_rx` without ever holding a session; whatever those messages carry is disclosed.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT, though the drift note concerns the comment — the unverified-token behavior itself is stated as fact)
- Severity: high

### F-02 — Dropped position chunks are acknowledged anyway, so the server permanently believes stale nodes are delivered
- Topics: VC-13.7, VC-13.4, VC-14.10
- Evidence: TENSION recorded twice — "ADR-2141 decision 5 says a chunk that was not applied is not acknowledged, but handleBinaryFrame returns normally when a frame arrives within 16 ms of the last accepted one, so the ack is still sent for a chunk the worker never saw" (wsClient.ts:26-28, binaryProtocol.ts:392-408, ADR-2141 doc :32); the dispatcher's 256-frame backstop discards oldest frames (binaryFrameDispatcher.ts:27-28, 71-77), which the register itself calls "safe only because the server rebuilds an unacknowledged chunk after the timeout" — but the wrongly-sent ack means the server never rebuilds.
- Failure: a node whose only movement was in the dropped chunk is committed as "held" by that client (`acknowledge` commits the chunk's nodes, position_stream.rs:225-240); unless the node later moves past `streamMoveThreshold`, that client's view of it is permanently stale while the server records it as current — two users see different graphs and neither is told.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)
- Severity: medium

### F-03 — The KG watchdog counts a "degraded" backend as UP, so the primary liveness gauge cannot fire on degradation
- Topics: VC-08.2, VC-04.16, VC-08.3
- Evidence: verdict rule — `2xx with status present and not "unhealthy" → UP` (liveness_harness.rs:505-507), and the canary fires only on state *change* (:421, :429-433); the health composer's degraded states are labelled `degraded`, not `unhealthy` (VC-04.16: `health_status = degraded` when `get_degraded_reason()` is Some or issues are non-empty). No diagram shows `status: "unhealthy"` ever being emitted — inferred gap.
- Failure: Oxigraph or a subsystem fails → `/api/health` still returns 2xx `degraded` → `kg_backend_up` stays UP → `CANARY-VC-RESA-KG` never fires → the outage runs until a human notices; this amplifies R-3 and F-10.
- Confidence: high for the verdict rule; the "unhealthy is never emitted" half is inferred
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-04 — No request-size or content-type middleware exists on the live request path
- Topics: VC-03.1, VC-04.19
- Evidence: VC-03.1 DEBT — "no request-size or content-type layer exists in this .wrap() chain. The nearest remaining guard, RequestSizeLimit (src/utils/validation/middleware.rs:17, default cap 16MiB at src/utils/validation/mod.rs:14), is not registered by any .wrap()"; VC-04.19 — the sanitizer/validator family is "reachable only via this explicit dry-run test route, never automatically on the real request path".
- Failure: oversized or malformed bodies hit handlers directly across 4 workers; memory/CPU exhaustion or unguarded parsing on routes that accept raw payloads.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: medium

### F-05 — Concurrent settings PUTs lose each other's writes via full-snapshot read-modify-write
- Topics: VC-06.2, VC-06.3
- Evidence: the route fetches one full `GetSettings` snapshot (settings_routes.rs:527, explicitly to avoid TOCTOU), mutates one section (`full_settings.visualisation.graphs.knowledge.physics = new_physics`, :577), then writes the **whole** snapshot back via `UpdateSettings{settings: full_settings}`. The register does not assert concurrency between two PUTs; the lost-update hazard is a direct implication of the shown sequence.
- Failure: two operators PUT physics and rendering concurrently; the later commit's snapshot was read before the earlier commit and silently reverts the other section — persisted, GPU-propagated, and reported 200.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (the snapshot write-back is stated; the interleaving is not)
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-06 — NIP-98 replay protection is process-local; the stated deployment has no cross-replica claim
- Topics: VC-03.4, VC-01.7
- Evidence: "Process-local scope — no cross-replica replay protection (SECURITY-profiles.md)" (REPLAY_CACHE at nip98.rs:221; TTL 120s :178; per-signer quota 1000 events per 120s). VC-01.7's invariant describes a single `visionclaw_container`, so today this is latent, not live.
- Failure: the moment the estate is scaled horizontally behind a load balancer, a captured kind-27235 event replays successfully on a second replica inside its 60s freshness window.
- Confidence: high that the limitation exists; the incident scenario is inferred (requires a deployment the material does not show)
- Marked by authors: no (stated as a plain scope note, not flagged)
- Severity: medium

### F-07 — A misconfigured local corpus silently falls back to GitHub, and two unreconciled env grammars name the same repo
- Topics: VC-01.2, VC-07.13, VC-09.11
- Evidence: "a misconfigured local source logs an error and falls back to GitHubSource rather than failing boot" (corpus_source/mod.rs:157-171); VC-09.11 DIVERGENCE — `GITHUB_REPO_OWNER`/`GITHUB_REPO_NAME`/`GITHUB_BASE_BRANCH` (github_pr_service.rs:131-143) duplicate `GITHUB_OWNER`/`GITHUB_REPO`/`GITHUB_BRANCH` (github/config.rs:72-97), "two naming grammars for the same coordinates, unreconciled".
- Failure: a bad `VAULT_ROOT` or a stale duplicate variable set syncs the wrong corpus into the knowledge graph; enrichment decisions and the ontology are then derived from content the client never wrote, with only a log line distinguishing it.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence, for the duplicate grammar; the silent fallback is stated as designed behavior, unmarked)
- Severity: medium

### F-08 — REST-recorded decisions carry no signed correlation and are indistinguishable from panel-approved ones
- Topics: VC-05.17, VC-05.7, VC-05.6
- Evidence: "the agentbox/operator/git-bridge REST path writes decision_event_id:None every time, since it never carries a signed 31403 event" (enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:543-560) while the elevation actor retains the signed event id (elevation_actor.rs:1749); "the two producers only converge at the SQLite table" and BASELINE-architecture.md:357 is quoted verbatim: "Current source review does not certify a complete human-approval journey."
- Failure: in any dispute or audit, the store cannot prove which decisions were human-approved versus merely asserted over the X-Agent-Key REST path; a compromised agent key mints decisions indistinguishable from panel verdicts.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)
- Severity: high

### F-09 — The cross-process contract crate has zero consumers; each side ships its own envelope
- Topics: VC-07.12
- Evidence: "visionclaw-server does NOT list visionclaw-contracts under [dependencies] … zero current consumers on either side this crate names" and `src/agent_events/schema.rs:61` defines "its OWN, INDEPENDENT AgentActionEnvelope struct — same name, not the crate's type"; no TS package references `@visionclaw/contracts` (VC-07.12, N1-N4).
- Failure: agentbox and visionclaw evolve their envelopes independently; a field added or retyped on one side breaks the other silently at runtime, because nothing shares the definition the crate was built to hold.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)
- Severity: medium

### F-10 — GPU compute is mandatory with no CPU fallback anywhere; init failure degrades quietly and the stream freezes at last-known-good
- Topics: VC-10.10, VC-10.2, VC-10.6, VC-13.3
- Evidence: "There is NO single system-wide CPU fallback — each actor degrades differently"; orchestrator warns once "CPU physics fallback not implemented - GPU compute is mandatory" (physics_orchestrator_actor.rs:585-587); init timeout logs "system will continue in degraded mode" (resource_supervisor.rs:458); ComputeForces with no context drops the frame; bad-frame path broadcasts "the last-known-good layout" (force_compute_actor.rs:2166-2189); ForceComputeActor self-init is "invisible to ResourceSupervisor accounting and GetSubsystemHealth" (ADR-2053 addendum, force_compute_actor.rs:649).
- Failure: a CUDA/driver change leaves the server booting, REST serving, and clients receiving frozen last-known-good positions presented as live — coupled with F-03, nothing alarms.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence for the no-fallback posture; the self-init gap is RECORDED as deliberate in the ADR-2053 addendum)
- Severity: medium

### F-11 — Multi-MCP discovery never resets its cycle counter, so a post-idle reconnect pushes an empty frame as if it were real data
- Topics: VC-05.13
- Evidence: DEBT — "cycles is never reset by stop_discovery (multi_mcp_agent_discovery.rs:437-450, counter :413) while a new socket starts last_frame_cycle at 0 (multi_mcp_websocket_handler.rs:137) … the first 1s tick can push a frame while the caches are still cleared … that frame carries empty servers and agents"; the same diagram records `request_agents` never reaching the discovery service (Handler only logs, :669-671) and no client consuming the discovery frame (OPEN).
- Failure: an operator reconnecting after idle sees "no connected MCP servers" that is an artefact of counter skew, and reacts to a phantom outage — or trusts a simulation path (`request_agents`) as live agent data.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt and Open)
- Severity: low

### F-12 — Presence handshake replay protection is a 4096-entry LRU; no freshness check on the signed challenge itself is shown
- Topics: VC-05.4
- Evidence: replay detection is only `seen.put(nonce)` against a SeenNonces LRU capped at 4096 (presence_handler.rs:51, check :266); the timestamp-skew check is shown as `opt client ts present` (:224) — i.e. optional; no diagram step verifies the *signed challenge's* `timestamp_us` against the current clock.
- Failure: capture a victim's auth message, let 4096 later joins evict the nonce, replay the signed handshake — the signature still verifies and the nonce is no longer seen, joining the room as the victim's DID.
- Confidence: low, inferred (the LRU cap and optional skew check are stated; the absence of a signed-challenge freshness check is an absence in the diagram, not a stated fact)
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-13 — Rate limiting keys on `realip_remote_addr` behind an nginx front, which may collapse all clients into one bucket
- Topics: VC-03.12, VC-01.7
- Evidence: identifier is `realip_remote_addr` when `use_user_id` is off (rate_limit.rs:242-263); nginx fronts the backend on 3001→4000 (VC-01.7 invariant). The register does not state how nginx propagates the client IP.
- Failure: if the real IP is not propagated, every visitor shares one 60/min bucket on `/api/settings` (collective lockout) and per-client limits elsewhere key on the proxy address.
- Confidence: low, inferred
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: low

### F-14 — The PTX ISA downgrade is a text splice, and the runtime symbol check is bypassable by a cached build artefact
- Topics: VC-12.5, VC-12.6
- Evidence: "the rewrite is a declared-ISA text splice only, it does not prove every instruction is supported by that ISA, only a real driver load settles that" (ADR-2030 Consequences, VC-12.5); "get_function by name, ONLY symbol check, unguarded if build-time symbol gate bypassed e.g. a cached OUT_DIR artefact" (VC-12.6).
- Failure: a driver upgrade or stale `OUT_DIR` yields PTX that parses, passes the build-time symbol gate, and fails at `Module::from_ptx` or at a missing kernel — boot-time GPU failure feeding straight into F-10's no-fallback posture.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)
- Severity: low

### F-15 — The boot ontology pull is fail-open, so the embedded pod can serve a stale ontology indefinitely and silently
- Topics: VC-01.15, VC-05.6
- Evidence: "fail-open throughout — any error is logged (log_outcome, src/services/ontology_pull.rs:416) and the pod keeps whatever it already held"; fetch failures return Err with nothing written (:205-208); re-check interval defaults to 3600s. The behavior is stated as design; the downstream consequence is my inference.
- Failure: the release pipeline breaks for weeks; the pod keeps the old `ontology-latest`, decisions continue to be recorded and Whelk-gated against outdated classes, and no signal distinguishes "current" from "frozen" ontology to any client or operator.
- Confidence: high for the stated behavior; the operational consequence is inferred
- Marked by authors: no (stated as designed fail-open)
- Severity: medium

## visionclaw#2 / critical

### F-01 — Shipped compose resolves unassigned pubkeys to Editor role, inverting the code's fail-closed RBAC
- Topics: VC-22.9
- Evidence: The material states code fails closed: `public_reads_enabled() ends .unwrap_or(false)` (rbac_gate.rs:135-141) and `main.rs:809-818` refuses to start owner-less unless `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS` is set. But "the shipped compose inverts both (docker-compose.unified.yml:121, :122, with the :-1 default), so an unassigned pubkey resolves to Editor (role_store.rs:359)."
- Failure: Any anonymous client in the default deployment has Editor-level write access to mutating routes, because the shipped compose file overrides the code's fail-closed posture. An operator deploying with the supplied compose gets an open system without realising it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (CORRECTED ADR-2070, raised by estate ADR-2087)

### F-02 — Erasure gap: `deleteAgentMemory` leaves embedding rows searchable in RuVector
- Topics: VC-22.6
- Evidence: "DIVERGENCE: no reverse tombstone to RuVector - embedding row persists and stays searchable (docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md:87)" and "this Pod-side delete never calls mcp__claude-flow__memory_delete or any RuVector endpoint - RuVector is external Postgres reached only via MCP, no delete-propagation hook exists in this path." Additionally the server "now also holds a direct read-only RuVector client (tokio-postgres from RUVECTOR_PG_CONNINFO)" which means the stale embedding has a second consumer.
- Failure: A user exercising their right to erasure deletes agent memory from their Pod; the semantic embedding persists in the vector store and remains retrievable via similarity search indefinitely. Under GDPR this is a compliance incident.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-03 — Agent-event provenance: "Attributed" means a well-formed pubkey was asserted, not that a signature was verified
- Topics: VC-27.13
- Evidence: "SECURITY: ProvenanceStatus::Attributed means a well-formed pubkey was asserted, NOT that a signature was verified - the wire carries no sig field (provenance.rs:29-38)." Events on this socket are published to the hub which the KPI tap persists (VC-25.3).
- Failure: A compromised or unauthorised agent-box pushes events claiming to be any valid 64-hex pubkey. Downstream KPI computations (Trust Variance, HITL Precision) and governance decisions treat those events as genuinely attributed to that identity, fabricating audit evidence.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (SECURITY note)

### F-04 — Full-sync destroys governance-approved ontology additions that exist only in the store
- Topics: VC-22.10, VC-20.7
- Evidence: "this CLEAR wipes the ENTIRE assert graph including runtime OWL classes/axioms added via the governed write door (github_sync_service.rs:952-954)" and "a class added via add_owl_class/add_axiom between full-syncs is NOT itself in GRAPH_PROVENANCE-protected history for the assert graph - only re-derivation from the corpus...restores it after the next rebuild."
- Failure: A governance decision approves adding a new OWL class via `POST /api/ontology/classes`. `FORCE_FULL_SYNC=1` runs during routine maintenance. The class is silently deleted from the assert graph; any downstream consumers (physics constraints, DAG ranker, ontology validation) lose it without error.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (mentioned in note, but severity is understated — presented as a property of the design rather than a data-loss path)

### F-05 — Oxigraph GRAPH_PROVENANCE has no point-in-time backup; it is restore-only but cannot be restored
- Topics: VC-22.11, VC-22.4
- Evidence: "Oxigraph still has NO point-in-time backup" and "GRAPH_PROVENANCE is restore-only because it cannot be re-derived." The backup script covers only the four SQLite databases; Oxigraph is absent from both REQUIRED_DBS and OPTIONAL_DBS.
- Failure: Disk failure or corruption destroys the append-only provenance graph. Because it cannot be re-derived from any source (it is a record of what happened, not derived state), the entire audit trail for governance decisions, erasure compliance and dispute resolution is permanently lost. There is no recovery path.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (mentioned in DriftNote in VC-22.11)

### F-06 — A throttled chunk is acknowledged without being applied, violating the acknowledged-streaming invariant
- Topics: VC-32.8, VC-30.10
- Evidence: "TENSION: handleBinaryFrame returns without applying when under 16ms have passed since the last frame (wsClient.ts:26-27), updateNodePositions then resolves and the chunk is acknowledged (binaryProtocol.ts:393,407), while ADR-2141 decision 5 acknowledges a chunk only after it has been applied." The server's `ClientStream.acknowledge(seq)` then marks the nodes as delivered: "the chunk's nodes become the client's held state, never moving it backwards (position_stream.rs:225)."
- Failure: Under high frame rate, the client's 60fps throttle causes a chunk to be silently discarded (not applied to the worker), but the server records it as acknowledged. That chunk's node positions are now permanently stale on this client because the server will not resend them—the client's held state says they are current.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION)

### F-07 — Dual-write between SQLite and Oxigraph without 2PC and without any reconciliation mechanism
- Topics: VC-22.8
- Evidence: "no cross-store 2PC - the durable decision commits in SQLite first (writeback_triggered=true) - the Oxigraph write is best-effort, writeback_committed flips only on Ok (enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:576, :589)." No reconciliation, retry or repair process is described anywhere for the state where SQLite says committed but Oxigraph was never written.
- Failure: An enrichment decision commits in SQLite with `writeback_triggered=true` but the Oxigraph `append_derived_summary` fails transiently. The two stores permanently diverge: the decision is authoritative in SQLite but absent from the knowledge graph. No background process repairs this, and the material describes no operator tool for detecting it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (presented as a design property, not flagged as unrecoverable divergence)

### F-08 — force_full_sync clears GRAPH_KNOWLEDGE before rebuilding, with no transactional boundary
- Topics: VC-21.1, VC-22.10
- Evidence: "SYNC->>KGREPO: clear_graph() wipes GRAPH_KNOWLEDGE (github_sync_service.rs:406-410)" then batches process sequentially ("chunks(BATCH_SIZE=50)...github_sync_service.rs:60, chunks at :418"). No rollback or transaction wrapping the rebuild is described.
- Failure: The process crashes or the pod is killed mid-rebuild after clear_graph but before all batches complete. The knowledge graph is left in a partial state—missing nodes and edges. The system does not detect this on restart and does not automatically resume. Users see a broken graph until another full sync is manually triggered.
- Confidence: medium — the material does not state whether the store has internal transactionality that would make this atomic, but the batched processing described implies it does not
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — ElevationActor has no boot reconciliation: a case lost between publish and persist is never recovered
- Topics: VC-24.5
- Evidence: "STILL OPEN on this path: the ElevationActor poll shown here persists set_status but has no boot reconciliation, so a case lost between publish and persist is not reloaded on restart. ADR-2101 adds that to DecisionElevationActor only - see VC-24.6."
- Failure: ElevationActor publishes a kind-31404 `concept_elevated` receipt to the forum, then crashes before `set_status` persists. On restart the case is not reloaded from the store, so the tracked PR is abandoned, the in-memory `elevating` map is empty, and the forum shows a resolved case that the system has no record of.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (STILL OPEN)

### F-10 — Whelk EL++ reasoning has no timeout or axiom-count cap; a pathological ontology blocks the sync cycle indefinitely
- Topics: VC-20.3
- Evidence: "DOC-DRIFT - module doc claims complete EL reasoning (whelk_inference_engine.rs:5) but infer() lines 412-467 has no tokio timeout or axiom-count cap." This is called from the post-sync reasoning cycle (github_sync_service.rs:1245) which runs in the server's sync path.
- Failure: A large or cyclic ontology causes Whelk to enter a long-running or non-terminating subsumption computation. The entire sync cycle stalls, blocking graph updates, and there is no mechanism to kill the reasoning or proceed without it. The server appears healthy but stops ingesting.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT)

### F-11 — Governance decisions from first-contact bridge requests are ungated because no case row exists yet
- Topics: VC-24.3
- Evidence: "OPEN: a case with no row yet carries no recorded tier, so a first-contact bridge decision that forwards no effective_tier is still ungated. handlers/enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:502-508."
- Failure: The agentbox bridge sends a POST `/api/enrichment-proposals/{id}/decide` for a case the server has never seen, with `outcome=approve` and no `effective_tier`. Because the case has no stored `risk_tier`, the rationale gate (`MIN_RATIONALE_CHARS 20`) does not fire. A high-risk approve slips through with no reasoning and no audit trail.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN)

### F-12 — Feature flags are wholesale-replaced, process-global and unpersisted; a partial POST silently resets omitted flags
- Topics: VC-18.2
- Evidence: "WHOLESALE REPLACE - no field merge, a partial POST silently resets omitted flags to the body's values" and "OPEN ADR-2059 - FEATURE_FLAGS remains process-global, unpersisted and shared across all users."
- Failure: Operator A sets `ontology_validation=true` via a POST containing only that field. The POST body replaces the entire struct. In the same window, operator B's concurrent update is silently lost. A process restart resets to defaults. The `ontology_validation` gate silently opens after a restart, admitting unvalidated ontology mutations.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (explicitly described; OPEN ADR-2059)

### F-13 — Three independent AcspClient instances run concurrently under three panel identities sharing one bridge secret
- Topics: VC-24.11
- Evidence: "this is the SAME AcspClient type ElevationActor and DecisionElevationActor each construct independently at their own startup (elevation_actor.rs:1056, decision_elevation_actor.rs:521) - three separate relay connections under three different panel identities can exist concurrently, all using the same bridge secret."
- Failure: Three connections to the forum relay under three identities means kind-31403 decisions consumed by one actor's subscription may be missed by another's if the relay's subscription filters overlap. The shared secret means any of the three can impersonate the others. Message ordering across the three is unguaranteed, so a decision receipt can be published before the corresponding case is visible to all consumers.
- Confidence: medium — the material describes the topology but does not state the relay's fan-out semantics
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (noted as a factual observation, not flagged as a problem)

### F-14 — Analytics GET endpoints are unauthenticated by design; the material asserts they are "harmless" without evidence
- Topics: VC-18.1
- Evidence: "INVARIANT ADR-2009 mutations_only() means every POST needs an authenticated pubkey while GETs stay public as harmless operational telemetry." The public GETs include `/analytics/params` (system configuration), `/analytics/constraints` (constraint definitions), `/analytics/gpu-metrics` (GPU execution telemetry including per-kernel GPU-vs-fallback counters), `/analytics/insights` (derived AI insights from graph data and anomaly state), and `/analytics/cycles` (graph structure analysis).
- Failure: An unauthenticated attacker enumerates the system's internal topology, constraint definitions, GPU kernel execution patterns and performance telemetry. This is reconnaissance material: knowing which kernels fall back to CPU (from `total_cpu_fallbacks`) tells the attacker when the system is degraded and most vulnerable to resource exhaustion.
- Confidence: medium — the material calls them harmless; the risk is inferred from the nature of the exposed data
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (presented as an INVARIANT, not as a risk)

### F-15 — Multiple analytics diagrams show direct-addr routing while resolution notes claim supervisor routing; the documentation is internally contradictory
- Topics: VC-15.6, VC-15.8
- Evidence: VC-15.6's diagram shows `CS->>SPA: ComputeSSP{source_idx,max_distance,delta}` sent directly to `ShortestPathActor (data.shortest_path_actor)`, and the section title says "direct-addr." But the resolution note says "the route now sends ComputeSSP to data.gpu_manager_addr, forwarded GPUManagerActor to GraphAnalyticsSupervisor to the SUPERVISED ShortestPathActor." VC-15.8 has the same pattern for ConnectedComponentsActor. Neither diagram was updated.
- Failure: An engineer or operator troubleshooting a production SSSP or connected-components failure reads the diagram and sends a test message directly to `data.shortest_path_actor`, which no longer receives messages from the route. The actual path goes through two supervisors. Debugging effort is wasted on the wrong actor; supervision-tree restart semantics (which the supervisor provides) are invisible.
- Confidence: high — the text of the resolution note directly contradicts the diagram's message flow
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (the notes say RESOLVED but the diagrams were not updated)

---

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Actual runtime behaviour of the Redis session cache** — VC-22.1 says Redis is "not enabled by default; deployment not verified." Whether the shipped compose enables it, and whether session restore across restarts works in practice, cannot be determined.

2. **The ComfyUI failure mode under concurrent job submission** — VC-28.4/28.5 describe a submit-poll-retrieve pipeline with 60 polls at 5s intervals, but there is no evidence of what happens when multiple agents submit simultaneously or when ComfyUI returns inconsistent history state.

3. **The XR client's actual behaviour on chunk loss** — VC-32.12 describes the server's acknowledged-chunk rebuild, but the XR (Godot/Rust) client's handling of a rebuilt chunk that arrives out of order relative to a later chunk is not shown.

4. **Whether the `vault certify` whelk-parity check (VC-20.14) runs in CI on every change or only on demand** — the material says it is "measured" and "pinned" but does not state whether a regression in parity would block a merge.

5. **The actual GPU kernel numerical stability at production scale** — VC-15.13 shows oracle tests pass at test-fixture scale (two-clique, triangle, star), but the material gives no evidence of conformance at the stated production scale (e.g. the 16,000+ communities and 351,421 asserted triples mentioned in VC-20.14).

## visionclaw#2 / premortem

# Post-mortem: VisionClaw production incident (client data believed private/deleted remained exposed, and governed ontology edits were lost)

Preamble: the incident class is a custody/confidentiality failure — a client's data that the system's own documentation said was private or deletable was in fact published, undeletable, or destroyed. Three ranked root-cause hypotheses follow, then latent defects found during investigation. Every claim carries the citation the material gives; where I reason past the material I say "inferred". All findings need verification against code and tests before action.

### R-1 — Erasure is structurally impossible: Pod deletes never reach RuVector, provenance is append-only with no crypto-shredding, and Oxigraph (which holds provenance) has no backup at all
- Topics: VC-22.6, VC-22.4, VC-22.7, VC-22.11, VC-22.1
- Chain of events: 1) Client demands deletion of their agent memory. 2) Operator runs `deleteAgentMemory`; the Pod-side JSON-LD resource is deleted (2xx or 404 both count as success). 3) No reverse tombstone to RuVector exists — the embedding row persists and stays searchable; this path never calls `memory_delete` or any RuVector endpoint. 4) The server's direct read-only RuVector client is a second consumer of that same orphaned row. 5) The client's activity also lives in `GRAPH_PROVENANCE`, which is append-only by invariant — retraction only ADDs `dl:validTo` and deletes nothing. 6) The crypto-shredding remedy (ADR-2102) that would preserve the append-only invariant while satisfying erasure is still PROPOSED. 7) Even a manual intervention has no safety net: Oxigraph has no point-in-time backup, and `GRAPH_PROVENANCE` is explicitly restore-only because it cannot be re-derived. 8) Audit finds the data still searchable and still attributed; the client leaves.
- Evidence: "DIVERGENCE: no reverse tombstone to RuVector - embedding row persists and stays searchable (docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md:87)" and "this Pod-side delete never calls mcp__claude-flow__memory_delete or any RuVector endpoint" (`agentMemory.ts:191-198`, `ldpClient.ts:203-212`); second read consumer at `Cargo.toml:143-145`; "append-only - no DELETE, DROP or CLEAR issued against this graph (ADR-2016, provenance_emitter.rs:32-33)" and "Retraction only ADDS dl:validTo and deletes nothing - history never removed (provenance_writer.rs:218,478-502)"; "PROPOSED ADR-2102: keep the append-only invariant and satisfy erasure by crypto-shredding … exists to force that choice rather than leave it to omission" (VC-22.4); "Oxigraph still has NO point-in-time backup" and "GRAPH_PROVENANCE is restore-only because it cannot be re-derived" (VC-22.11 DriftNote, PROPOSED ADR-2103 at VC-22.7); `backup-sqlite.sh:75` — the backup set is SQLite only.
- Warning signs visible today: three separate author-flagged gaps (DIVERGENCE in VC-22.6, PROPOSED-not-accepted ADR-2102, PROPOSED ADR-2103) whose combination makes compliance with any erasure request impossible by construction; none has an owner or a date.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high

### R-2 — A full corpus sync wipes the entire assert graph, silently destroying ontology classes added through the governed write door
- Topics: VC-22.10, VC-21.1, VC-20.7
- Chain of events: 1) Operators author ontology classes/axioms through the sanctioned governed write door (`POST /api/ontology/classes`, `add_axiom` — power_user gated), landing in `GRAPH_ONTOLOGY :assert`. 2) Later, a full sync runs — reachable via `FORCE_FULL_SYNC=1`, `POST /api/admin/sync?force_full=true`, the boot sync task, or the `sync_corpus` binary. 3) `rebuild_assert_graph` performs `CLEAR GRAPH assert then INSERT DATA` (`github_sync_service.rs:1026`). 4) That CLEAR wipes the ENTIRE assert graph including the runtime OWL classes/axioms added via the governed door (`github_sync_service.rs:952-954`). 5) Those classes exist nowhere else: they are not in the corpus and are explicitly NOT in `GRAPH_PROVENANCE`-protected history for the assert graph — "only re-derivation from the corpus … restores it after the next rebuild". 6) `rematerialise_decisions` re-adds only `dl:DecisionRecord` quads and is non-fatal on failure (`github_sync_service.rs:1042-1048, :1105`). 7) The client's governed ontology work is gone without an error surfaced to them; Whelk reasoning, the class index and every downstream consumer now run on the reduced set.
- Evidence: VC-22.10 notes: "this CLEAR wipes the ENTIRE assert graph including runtime OWL classes/axioms added via the governed write door (github_sync_service.rs:952-954)" and "a class added via add_owl_class/add_axiom between full-syncs is NOT itself in GRAPH_PROVENANCE-protected history for the assert graph"; sync entry points at VC-21.1 (`admin_sync_handler.rs:66`, `app_state.rs:585`, `sync_corpus.rs:25`); `kg_repo.clear_graph()` on force (`github_sync_service.rs:406-410`).
- Warning signs visible today: the author note says the destruction is by design of the rebuild and that the governed door's output has no durable home outside the corpus; the governed door is nonetheless advertised as the live write path (VC-20.7) and ADR-2114 made the corpus the authority, stranding anything written only through the door.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Shipped compose inverts the RBAC fail-closed posture: public reads on, owner-less boot, and any unassigned authenticated pubkey resolves to Editor
- Topics: VC-22.9, VC-18.1
- Chain of events: 1) Operator deploys with the shipped `docker-compose.unified.yml`. 2) Compose sets `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS` on (`:121`) and `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS` with a `:-1` default (`:122`); no Owner is assigned. 3) Boot logs a warning and runs owner-less (`main.rs:797-802`) instead of the code's fail-closed refusal (`main.rs:809-818`). 4) Anonymous GETs pass through (`rbac_gate.rs:172-175, :264-269`) — graph data and all `/analytics` GET telemetry are public. 5) Any authenticated nostr pubkey with no role assignment resolves to `default_role` Editor (`role_store.rs:359-366`); the material does not enumerate which mutation routes accept Editor, but the default role exists to authorize, and separately every `/analytics` POST authorizes on the presence of any authenticated pubkey alone (`analytics/mod.rs:171`, ADR-2009 `mutations_only()`). 6) An external key holder mutates settings/constraints/layout and reads everything public; the client's data is altered or exposed.
- Evidence: "the CODE fails closed: public_reads_enabled() ends .unwrap_or(false) (rbac_gate.rs:135-141) and main.rs:809-818 refuses to start owner-less … The shipped compose inverts both (docker-compose.unified.yml:121, :122, with the :-1 default), so an unassigned pubkey resolves to Editor (role_store.rs:359). The open posture is ADR-2027's deliberate demo default and stays."
- Warning signs visible today: the VC-22.9 diagram's own red rect; the persisted ADR-2027 demo default in the only compose file the material cites; public telemetry surface enumerated at VC-18.1.
- Confidence: high that the posture ships exactly as described; medium, inferred, that this specific posture was this incident's trigger rather than a co-cause.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — A page explicitly marked `public: false` is still ingested and published if it carries an `owl-class` marker, contradicting the governing doc
- Topics: VC-21.2, VC-21.12
- Evidence: the gate is the disjunction `self.public || self.owl_class.is_some()` (`vault/mod.rs:162-163`, reached via `github_sync_service.rs:2114`); the governing doc says every `knowledge/` page with valid frontmatter is a KG node with "no `owl-class` bypass and no OR" (`docs/VAULT-corpus-format.md:236-238`); VC-21.12 records the DIVERGENCE that "an explicit public false plus a class marker remains included by policy".
- Failure: an author marks a page private but uses an owl-class for internal typing, relying on the documented contract; the page becomes a KG node and is served through graph reads.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift and DIVERGENCE, both recorded)

### F-02 — `working/` notes are ingested into the governed graph although the doc says they never are
- Topics: VC-21.2
- Evidence: "docs/VAULT-corpus-format.md:244 says `working/` is never ingested into the governed graph, while the local source's default base paths are `knowledge,working` (`corpus_source/local.rs:21`) and the sync keeps a working page as its own node unless a knowledge page owns the same id (`github_sync_service.rs:2156`)".
- Failure: an operator's scratch notes under `working/` become governed-graph nodes and ship through the same public reads as curated knowledge.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)
