### F-03 — A first-contact broker decision on an unregistered case bypasses the rationale gate entirely
- Topics: VC-24.3
- Evidence: OPEN note — "a case with no row yet carries no recorded tier, so a first-contact bridge decision that forwards no effective_tier is still ungated" (`enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:502-508`); the gate is `gate_tier(declared, effective)` — stricter of recorded case tier and forwarded tier (`:70, :382, :508`); the in-code scope note still claims effective_tier has not landed (`:308-314`).
- Failure: the agentbox bridge posts an approve for a high-risk case it has never registered, forwarding no tier and no rationale; no stored tier exists to tighten the gate; the decision is recorded and, if attributed, written into the summary graph unvetted.
- Confidence: high (the gap is stated verbatim)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-04 — Agent-event ingest treats an asserted pubkey as "Attributed" without any signature, allowing forged attribution into durable KPI and provenance
- Topics: VC-27.13, VC-25.3, VC-22.5
- Evidence: "SECURITY: ProvenanceStatus::Attributed means a well-formed pubkey was asserted, NOT that a signature was verified - the wire carries no sig field (provenance.rs:29-38)"; the declared intent is persisted verbatim on `kpi_agent_events` so "a later reading cannot soften it" (`migrations/sqlite/0006_kpi_agent_event_intent.sql:1`, `sqlite_kpi_repository.rs:245`); Trust Variance and HITL Precision are computed from these rows (`kpi_compute.rs:140, :143`).
- Failure: the holder of any valid session token posts `agent_action` events naming another agent's DID; forged attribution lands durably in `kpi_agent_events` and in provenance crossings, poisoning the KPIs and the `/api/trace` audit trail that governance decisions rely on.
- Confidence: high that no verification exists; the exploit precondition (a valid token) and poisoning effect are inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (flagged inline as SECURITY, but none of the four markers)

### F-05 — Feature-flag update is a wholesale replace of process-global, unpersisted, all-user state — a partial POST silently resets the ontology gate
- Topics: VC-18.2, VC-18.9
- Evidence: "WHOLESALE REPLACE - no field merge, a partial POST silently resets omitted flags to the body's values" (`feature_flags_handlers.rs:28-29`); "OPEN ADR-2059 - FEATURE_FLAGS remains process-global, unpersisted and shared across all users"; `ontology_validation` is "the ONLY flag read as a real GATE" (`api_handler/ontology/mod.rs:332`).
- Failure: one power user posts a body naming only `sssp_integration`; the omitted `ontology_validation` is deserialized to its default and the ontology validation gate silently changes state for the entire process and every user, with no persistence and therefore a different value after restart.
- Confidence: high on the replace semantics; medium, inferred, on which value omitted flags land at (the Default is not stated in the material).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-06 — ElevationActor's PR-tracking is memory-only with no boot reconciliation on the poll path; terminal states and PRs can be lost or duplicated across a restart
- Topics: VC-24.4, VC-24.5, VC-24.6
- Evidence: "STILL OPEN on this path: the ElevationActor poll shown here persists set_status but has no boot reconciliation, so a case lost between publish and persist is not reloaded on restart" (VC-24.5); `elevating.insert` is an in-memory map (`elevation_actor.rs:1572`); on the DecisionElevation path, "a resumed approval can open a duplicate PR, recorded as follow-on 1 in ADR-2101" (VC-24.6); publish-Err before `create_or_update` writes no row at all (`elevation_actor.rs:1300-1301`).
- Failure: the process restarts while an elevation PR is open or between a 31404 publish and `set_status`; the case's terminal event never lands or lands only in the forum; on resume a second PR is opened for the same case, splitting the governance record.
- Confidence: high (stated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open; follow-on recorded in ADR-2101)

### F-07 — Dual-writes fan out to two stores with no 2PC and no re-drive; a crash strands `writeback_triggered=true, writeback_committed=false` permanently
- Topics: VC-22.8
- Evidence: "no cross-store 2PC - the durable decision commits in SQLite first (writeback_triggered=true) - the Oxigraph write is best-effort, writeback_committed flips only on Ok (enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:576, :589)"; content-sync fan-out writes GRAPH_KNOWLEDGE and the assert graph in a `critical` block with no compensation (`github_sync_service.rs:1587, :1026`).
- Failure: the process dies or Oxigraph errors between the SQLite commit and the summary write; the decision is durable but the KG fact never lands, and nothing in the material re-drives the stuck flag; a sync crash mid-fan-out leaves KG and assert graphs from different corpus generations.
- Confidence: medium (the missing compensation is stated; the permanence of the stuck flag is inferred — no retry path is shown).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — Whelk inference runs with no timeout and no axiom cap despite its doc claiming otherwise
- Topics: VC-20.3
- Evidence: "DOC-DRIFT - module doc claims complete EL reasoning (whelk_inference_engine.rs:5) but infer() lines 412-467 has no tokio timeout or axiom-count cap".
- Failure: a pathological or adversarial class hierarchy makes `reasoner::assert` run unbounded inside `run_post_sync_reasoning` (`github_sync_service.rs:1205-1255`), stalling the post-sync pipeline and everything sequenced after it.
- Confidence: medium (the absence is stated; the hang is inferred).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT)

### F-09 — The NIP-98 replay cache is an in-process `Mutex<HashMap>`; restart or multi-instance deployment reopens the 60-second replay window
- Topics: VC-26.4, VC-32.1
- Evidence: `REPLAY_CACHE` is a `Mutex<HashMap>` process-global (`nip98.rs:215`); `claim_event_id` deduplicates only within 2x the window (`nip98.rs:178, :234`); tokens are valid for `TOKEN_MAX_AGE_SECONDS (60s)` (`nip98.rs:169, :362`); no persistence or cross-instance sharing is shown anywhere in VC-26.4.
- Failure: the server restarts (or a second instance sits behind the same proxy) while an attacker holds a captured token; the claim state is gone/unshared and the token replays within its window, authenticating as the victim's pubkey on `/solid/*` and on the `/wss` signed upgrade.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (single-process structure is implied by the static `Mutex`; no persistence is cited).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — The STT side retains a live on-demand third-party edge: `SetSTTProvider(OpenAI)` sends users' raw audio to api.openai.com
- Topics: VC-28.7, VC-28.9
- Evidence: "DRIFT: … STTProvider declares Whisper and OpenAI … but SetSTTProvider(OpenAI) is live: ProcessAudioChunk then POSTs the audio to https://api.openai.com/v1/audio/transcriptions when settings.openai holds an API key, so a third-party speech edge exists on demand" (`speech.rs:71-74`, `speech_service.rs:75, :689-699, :722`); the same note stresses every TTS edge is now LAN-local, so this is the only voice egress off-estate.
- Failure: a voice operator switches provider (or a settings write does); participants' raw meeting audio leaves the estate to a third party with no consent gate shown in the material.
- Confidence: high (the path is stated); the trigger is config-dependent.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-11 — Broker inbox is capped at 500 newest-updated rows; older pending cases become invisible and `total` lies about the table
- Topics: VC-24.13
- Evidence: "DEBT: total is proposals.len() after store::all, which is capped at ALL_LIMIT 500 rows newest-updated first, so total is not the table size once more than 500 proposals exist (enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:738-745, broker_inbox_handler.rs:194)".
- Failure: once the table passes 500 proposals, an older pending case falls outside the returned window; the broker never sees it; it expires at the 14-day OPEN_CASE_TTL with a 31404 receipt and no human decision while every dashboard reports a steady total of 500.
- Confidence: high (the cap and misreported total are stated); the starvation scenario is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-12 — Three ACSP panel identities share one bridge secret, NIP-26 delegation is not wired, and connect-Ok does not prove relay reachability — forum attribution and visibility are unreliable
- Topics: VC-24.8, VC-24.11, VC-24.9
- Evidence: "NIP-26 delegation not wired, service-signing only (nostr_bridge.rs:29,65,169)"; "three separate relay connections under three different panel identities can exist concurrently, all using the same bridge secret" (`elevation_actor.rs:1056`, `decision_elevation_actor.rs:521`, boot client at `app_state.rs:1307-1334`); "connect Ok does not prove the relay is reachable … forum_projection=failed at decide time" (`acsp/client.rs:87-89`, `enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:654`).
- Failure: forum events carry the bridge bot's signature rather than a panel's or a human's, so consumers cannot cryptographically tell which of the three producers emitted a 31402/31403/31404; simultaneously a decision can be durable locally and absent from the forum (or vice versa after a publish-failure warn), splitting the governance record across two audiences.
- Confidence: medium
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence and Debt)

### F-13 — Wire-id overflow handling: a stamped id whose base exceeds 26 bits but carries a class pattern bypasses the bounds check entirely and aliases silently
- Topics: VC-23.7, VC-32.6
- Evidence: "An over-range id is still masked and therefore aliases, but never silently" (VC-23.7, `binary_protocol.rs:194-204`); the 2026-09-08 invariant: "an id that ALREADY carries a recognised class pattern is forwarded unchanged by the encoder's last branch … Only a high bit that is NOT a class pattern is still an overflow, handled by the untyped branch (`src/utils/binary_protocol.rs:475-484, :490`)".
- Failure: a caller stamps ontology flags onto an id whose numeric base already exceeds `NODE_ID_MASK`; the encoder forwards it unchanged with no log; on the wire it is indistinguishable from another node's id and the two nodes' positions/analytics merge.
- Confidence: low, inferred (this edge sits beyond the stated invariant; the material does not show such a caller).
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — FileService re-seeds Oxigraph from local `metadata.json` whenever the store is empty — deleted or cleared graph content can resurrect on boot
- Topics: VC-25.13, VC-22.10
- Evidence: the seed path runs when the store is empty and `metadata.json` is non-empty — "Phase 1 - build AppNode per file … Phase 2 - wikilink regex … save_graph" (`file_service.rs:1095-1213`), with `data/metadata/metadata.json` listed as a source; a force-full sync clears `GRAPH_KNOWLEDGE` (`github_sync_service.rs:406-410`).
- Failure: after a KG clear (or any future erasure-driven wipe), the next boot detects an empty store and silently rebuilds nodes and edges from the on-disk snapshot — removed content returns without operator action.
- Confidence: low, inferred (the seed condition is stated; no erasure flow for the KG is shown to interact with it).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — npub-vs-hex mixing at the UserContext boundary: the primary user identifier is the display form while identity verification compares hex
- Topics: VC-23.4
- Evidence: "DIVERGENCE npub-vs-hex mixing risk -- user_context.rs:16 documents npub as the primary user identifier, no automatic hex conversion enforced at that boundary", with hex kept separately (`user_context.rs:20`); the DID check compares `verified.pubkey_hex to payload_pubkey` (`nostr_identity_verifier.rs:105`).
- Failure: a consumer passes an npub where hex is expected; comparisons fail-closed to 401s for a legitimately signed user, or any path keyed on `user_id` misattributes actions to the display form rather than the verified key.
- Confidence: low-medium, inferred beyond the stated mixing risk.
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)

## visionclaw#3 / critical

### F-01 — Dev-mode full auth bypass is armed by default and peer-agnostic on the unified compose
- Topics: VC-36.15, VC-36.3
- Evidence: `docker-compose.unified.yml:105` sets `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE: "${VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE:-1}"` — ADR-2108 flipped the default to armed on 2026-09-08 ("the operator opts OUT with VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE=0"). When active, "Every request granted as DEV_MODE_PUBKEY = dev-mode-local-admin" (`src/utils/auth.rs:96`, `project/src/main.rs:299`) and the REST chokepoint is a "LAN-local FULL bypass, peer-agnostic: no NIP-98, no token, no peer check" (`src/utils/auth.rs:203-218`). The same stack is served to the LAN (nginx :3001, VC-36.3 note; XR client targets `ws://<lan-host>`, VC-36.16). The material's own caveat: "the var must never sit in a .env shared with prod - sharper now that the compose default supplies it unasked" (`docker-compose.unified.yml:102-104` note).
- Failure: anyone on the LAN reaches every write endpoint (settings PUTs, graph writes, drag/pin) as `dev-mode-local-admin` with no credential at all. The only protections are conventions: "dev service only", not in the common-environment anchor, and a release binary that refuses the var.
- Confidence: high on the stated posture; whether this compose is the production vehicle is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — the .env caveat and the default flip are recorded (ADR-2108 discussion in VC-36.15), but the peer-agnostic armed default is presented as a decision, not a defect.

### F-02 — Codescape's main port runs with no authentication while its HTTP path is LAN-published
- Topics: VC-39.2, VC-39.5, VC-39.7
- Evidence: `codescape-server/src/auth.rs:120-124` is OpenAccess, "reported honestly as auth open" (`codescape-server/src/routes/mod.rs:69`); `nginx.dev.conf:63-65` publishes `/codescape/` on port 3001 "LAN-published in dev by owner decision". The same surface reaches the typed command executor `run_command_inner` (`codescape-server/src/routes/mod.rs:289-354`), the voice floor and ASR (`codescape-server/src/routes/voice.rs:192`+). Only the ingest listener is token-gated (`codescape-server/src/main.rs:175-189`). Register R1: "OPEN until P6".
- Failure: any LAN peer issues commands, claims the voice floor, and drives focus/layout effects with no credential; the only per-peer control is a rate-limit key that, with trust off, derives from the TCP peer — and the port is open regardless.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN, register R1 in VC-39.7)

### F-03 — Shipped RBAC posture contradicts the code default: public reads on, every unassigned key becomes Editor
- Topics: VC-33.6
- Evidence: structural default is fail-closed (`RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=false`, `src/middleware/rbac_gate.rs:141` `unwrap_or(false)`), but "docker-compose.unified.yml:121 sets RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=1 in the dev/unified compose service. An unassigned authenticated pubkey resolves to Editor via UserRole::default_authenticated()" (`src/models/rbac.rs:68-70`, `src/services/role_store.rs:188,196-198`). The authors mark this DIVERGENCE: "two different postures depending whether you read the binary default or the shipped compose env". The governing doc's citations have also drifted (BASELINE:271/272 cite compose :93/:94; actual :121/:122).
- Failure: on any deployment built from the shipped compose, a stranger generates a fresh Nostr key, authenticates, and holds Editor in the Owner>Admin>Editor>Viewer lattice (BASELINE-architecture.md:267). If Editor carries write permissions (the material ties rejected writes to WriteGraph/Admin), this is self-service write access for any key that shows up.
- Confidence: high on the divergence; medium (inferred) that Editor grants writes — its exact permission set is not shown.
- Severity: high (conditional on Editor write scope)
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE; DOC-DRIFT for the stale citations)

### F-04 — Codescape presence shim accepts Schnorr signatures unverified: any DID is claimable
- Topics: VC-39.7, VC-39.2
- Evidence: "/ws/presence shim accepts the Schnorr signature unverified, the claimed DID is only a label, bounds 64 members per room, 256 rooms" (`codescape-server/src/xr_presence.rs:20-23`, `:65-67`; register R2).
- Failure: a participant impersonates any `did:nostr` in a room — co-presence rosters and the 0x44 agent gaze/attention overlays render for the wrong identity; in a shared scene this poisons avatar attribution, and the room-count bounds are global, so impersonation also feeds a cheap exhaustion vector.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN, register R2)

### F-05 — Proxy trust enabled with an empty peer list trusts every peer's forwarded headers
- Topics: VC-39.6
- Evidence: the decision tree reads "trust enabled? → peer list empty? → yes, any peer → ON" (`codescape-server/src/proxy.rs:271`). Once ON, the same-origin host is "first X-Forwarded-Host if a bare authority" (`proxy.rs:283-291`) and the rate-limit key is the last X-Forwarded-For hop (`proxy.rs:295-308`). Only the combination `--trust-proxy-from` without `--trust-proxy` is a boot error (`codescape-server/src/config.rs:237-239`); trust-on with no listed peers is accepted and trusts everyone. The shipped compose narrows the list to the service alias (`docker-compose.unified.yml:506, :510-511`) — the empty-list semantics itself is unguarded.
- Failure: an operator enabling `--trust-proxy` "for the nginx path" without listing peers lets any host on the compose network set X-Forwarded-Host — steering the origin every NIP-98 verify rebuilds (`codescape-server/src/routes/mod.rs:427-430`) — and rotate X-Forwarded-For to defeat rate limits. Moot today only because the port is OpenAccess; it bites the moment P6 adds auth.
- Confidence: high on the stated semantics; the exploitation path is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — The server still accepts a legacy session-bearer realm no client has ever constructed
- Topics: VC-33.3, VC-33.9
- Evidence: ADR-2009 fallback is "a SERVER-side acceptance path in src/utils/auth.rs and src/services/nostr_service.rs:500" — "UUID minted at login, plain-equality check against token_expiry" — while "No client code in this tree ever constructs an X-Nostr-Token session header — nostrAuthService.getSessionToken() always returns null" (`nostrAuthService.ts:435`). ADR-2009 even lists client files as dependents of this realm; VC-33.9 verified none of them builds the header.
- Failure: an entire accepted auth realm is dead code in practice — untested by real traffic, outside the NIP-98 single-use replay protections (ADR-2002), with a plain-equality comparison. Any future token leak or regression in this path ships with zero legitimate exercise, and audits anchored on ADR-2009's dependent list are mis-scoped.
- Confidence: high on the acceptance path and absence of constructors; exploitability is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — VC-33.9 marks it "DOC-DRIFT-adjacent"; the standing accepted-realm exposure is not flagged as a security concern.

### F-07 — Voice socket gives up permanently after five fixed 2-second reconnect attempts
- Topics: VC-35.4, VC-35.14
- Evidence: "reconnectAttempts capped at maxReconnectAttempts=5, fixed reconnectDelay=2000ms between tries - no backoff growth" (`client/src/services/VoiceWebSocketService.ts:44-46`, `:110`, `:461-468`). Deliberate `disconnect()` avoids the path only by pre-setting attempts to max (`VoiceWebSocketService.ts:505-513`); no re-arm path is described.
- Failure: a server restart or ~10-second network blip leaves voice dead for the whole session — STT, TTS, governed PTT dispatch and barge-in cancel all ride this socket (`/ws/speech`). The remedy is a page reload, and the fixed 2 s hammering gives a struggling server no backoff relief.
- Confidence: high on the cap; recovery-only-by-reload is inferred from the absence of any retry-reset path.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — The XR client never learns whether its NIP-98 authenticate was accepted
- Topics: VC-36.3, VC-36.4, VC-36.11
- Evidence: "the client sends the NIP-98 authenticate message and never reads an acknowledgement, so a rejected signature leaves the session anonymous with no signal (`xr-client/rust/src/transport.rs:117`); the visible symptom is the write-denied notice of VC-36.15" (Open note, VC-36.3). The reconnect state machine likewise "has no Authenticated state" (VC-36.4).
- Failure: an expired or rotated `XR_NOSTR_SECRET`, or a server-side verification change, produces a session that reads the graph fine but fails every drag, pin and settings write. The socket stays connected indefinitely in a half-working state; diagnosis depends on a user noticing the HUD bottom strip.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, VC-36.3)

### F-09 — Agent polling halts permanently after three failed requests
- Topics: VC-34.9
- Evidence: the error path of the polling loop: "retryCount++, retryDelay backoff; break retryCount >= maxRetries (3) → stop() - polling halted, error surfaced via errorCallbacks" (`client/src/features/bots/services/AgentPollingService.ts`, VC-34.9). The loop restarts only when the subscriber count returns to zero and a new first subscriber calls `start()`.
- Failure: a transient API outage of a few seconds (three polls at the 2 s active interval) permanently freezes the bots/agents views — statuses, action feeds and the interrupt control keep operating on stale data for the rest of the session unless the consuming panel is unmounted and remounted.
- Confidence: medium — stop-at-3 is stated; the absence of any restart while still mounted is inferred from the loop description.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — Memory-route ordering compares unrebased wall clocks, so clock skew silently drops routes
- Topics: VC-38.8, VC-38.12
- Evidence: "sent_at is Unix milliseconds on both paths, the relay's sentAt passed through unrebased and the headset's own Time.get_unix_time_from_system() (`memory_cloud_layer.gd:491`), so a headset clock ahead of the desktop's makes desktop frames Stale until the desktop catches up... nothing states the unit or bounds the skew" (VC-38.8, Open). The headset's own query routes stamp the headset wall clock, so "a later desktop relay replaces it only while the two clocks agree" (`memory_route.rs:1605` note).
- Failure: a headset a few seconds fast never shows any desktop-relayed memory route (every frame judged Stale); one a few seconds slow starves its own routes the moment a desktop relay arrives. No error surfaces — the feature simply does nothing on that device.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-11 — NodePositionContext is a mutable module singleton with no invalidation for stale readers
- Topics: VC-37.7
- Evidence: "NodePositionContext is named a context but is a module-level mutable singleton, not a React context. GraphManager publishes the live buffer on every frame (`GraphManager.tsx:452`) and the id map on every node-set change (`GraphManager.tsx:78`), and the agent overlay reads it directly. Nothing invalidates a stale reader after a remount." (`NodePositionContext.tsx:13`; VC-37.7 Note3; `BotsVisualization.tsx:29` reads it directly).
- Failure: after a graph reload, remount or backend switch, a still-mounted consumer can index the current position buffer with a stale nodeId→index map — placing agent overlays on the wrong nodes or reading out of range, with no error path.
- Confidence: medium-high — the hazard is stated; the concrete wrong-node rendering is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT)

### F-12 — The passkey fallback's key backup is a single downloaded file, by design the only copy
- Topics: VC-33.4, VC-33.9, VC-33.5
- Evidence: "fallback for authenticators without PRF — downloadKeyBackup() generates a random key and forces a file download, marking it the ONLY copy" (`passkeyService.ts:349-377`). The derived key material is otherwise held only in memory/sessionStorage and wiped on logout and tab close (VC-33.5, VC-33.9).
- Failure: a user on a non-PRF authenticator who loses the file (or never saves it) permanently loses the Nostr identity and everything bootstrapped from it — including the Solid WebID and pod contents (VC-33.8 derives the WebID from the pubkey). No server-side recovery mechanism appears anywhere in the material.
- Confidence: high on the single-copy design; the absence of any recovery path is inferred from silence.
- Severity: medium (custody)
- Marked by authors: no

### F-13 — Runtime voice/LLM dependencies are unverifiable from this tree: broken symlinks and a dirty untracked nested checkout
- Topics: VC-35.6, VC-35.12
- Evidence: "Whisper-WebUI is a BROKEN SYMLINK in this tree... so the STT container source is not checked out here" (VC-35.6). "Kokoros, Whisper-WebUI and xinference are UNTRACKED symlinks at the repo root... All three dangle in this container. git ls-files returns nothing for any of them... xinference has live compose consumers (`docker-compose.unified.yml:149`, `:396`, `agentbox/docker-compose.yml:86`), so Xinference is a runtime endpoint dependency" (VC-35.12, DIV). "voice-stack/unmute is a NESTED checkout that the VisionClaw repo does not track... its working tree is dirty: read the declared revision, never the files on disk" (UNPIN).
- Failure: STT, LLM inference and the web voice stack run on code that cannot be audited, diffed or reproduced from the repository at this stamp. An incident investigation or a rebuild-from-source of those services has nothing to verify against, and the on-disk unmute state may not match the declared revision.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in VC-35.6; DEBT/UNPIN in VC-35.12)

### F-14 — The client's generated settings types are gitignored: a fresh checkout has a missing source file
- Topics: VC-34.2
- Evidence: "client/src/types/generated/settings.ts — build output written at `src/bin/generate_types.rs:18`, gitignored (.gitignore:151), not in the repository at this stamp" (VC-34.2). The registry and manifest pipeline assume this file; it is produced only by running the Rust generator.
- Failure: cloning the repo and building the client fails (or type-checks against nothing) until `cargo run --bin generate_types` has been executed with the Rust toolchain present. A recovery build or CI job that assumes a self-contained client checkout breaks, and nothing stated checks that the generated file matches the current Rust structs.
- Confidence: medium — the file's absence from the repo is stated; the concrete build failure is inferred (no CI description is given).
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — Jev voice options are flagged enabled in compose but accept nothing: config and behavior contradict
- Topics: VC-35.7, VC-34.14, VC-34.19, VC-39.7
- Evidence: VisionClaw: the Jev option is "default off, fail-open, Compose flag on: docker-compose.unified.yml:8" but "the outcome-changing threshold is unset in Compose (docker-compose.unified.yml:14), which accepts nothing automatically, so neither Jev call alters a turn yet, and activation is staged until recreation (docs/BASELINE-architecture.md:49-50)" (VC-35.7). Codescape: "enabled in compose but accepts nothing: the threshold defaults to 1.0 and compose leaves it empty... no measured uplift, and BASELINE says activation is staged until the host is recreated" (register J1, `codescape-server/src/voice/jev.rs:166-171`).
- Failure: operators reading the compose believe semantic clarification/ranking is live; it is inert, and even the "enabled" flag is not in the running hosts' environment until they are recreated — so two hosts with identical files behave differently. The fail-open Jev paths (command-palette ranking VC-34.14, CommandInput VC-34.19) silently do less than configured, with no runtime signal of the discrepancy.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN, in both VC-35.7 and register J1)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Actual production topology.** Which compose files/services constitute the production deployment, the network exposure of ports 3001/4000/4100, and whether `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE` / `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=1` ship as in the unified file. Sizing F-01/F-02/F-03 requires this.
2. **The real permission sets of the RBAC tiers** (does Editor write?), the full route inventory under `RbacGate`/`RequireAuth`, and tests covering the NIP-98 replay cache and freshness window. Needed to confirm F-03 and the middleware-ordering assumption behind `verify_access` reusing "the signer an outer RbacGate or RequireAuth already verified" (VC-33.3 note).
3. **Persistence, backup and restore for the data plane** — Solid pods under `/vault`, Postgres, RuVector, Oxigraph — and what a container recreation or disk loss actually loses. Nothing in this material covers backups or memory custody.
4. **Multi-instance behavior.** Presence caps (64 members/room, 256 rooms), the in-memory clarification gate, the session relay, and single pocket-tts/whisper/xinference services: whether any of this is expected or safe to run as more than one instance, and what failover looks like.
5. **Secret handling.** Provisioning of `XR_NOSTR_SECRET` to headsets, the panel signing key (`load_panel_secret`), LiveKit JWT key/secret, the Codescape ingest token file, and `POWER_USER_PUBKEYS` management — storage, rotation and leak response are not described anywhere in the material.

## visionclaw#3 / premortem

# Post-mortem — twelve months on

Premise accepted as given: an incident occurred that cost a client, of the data/custody/security class. What follows reports only what the distillation states or directly implies, plus suspected defects flagged for verification. Where a chain steps beyond the material it is marked inferred.

---

### R-1 — The shipped compose posture is open by default: public reads, Editor-for-any-authenticated-key, and an auth bypass armed unless opted out
- Topics: VC-33.2, VC-33.3, VC-33.6, VC-36.15, VC-39.2
- Chain of events:
  1. A client environment is stood up from `docker-compose.unified.yml` — the single file that defines the whole estate (VisionClaw services, the Codescape peer, pocket-tts, xinference consumers at `docker-compose.unified.yml:149, :396`).
  2. That compose sets `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=1` (`docker-compose.unified.yml:121`), so reads are open to unauthenticated callers, and any authenticated-but-unassigned pubkey resolves to **Editor** via `UserRole::default_authenticated()` (`src/models/rbac.rs:68-70`, `src/services/role_store.rs:188, :196-198`) — a write-capable role.
  3. The same file's dev service arms `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE` by default (`"${VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE:-1}"`, `docker-compose.unified.yml:105`); with it on, `verify_access` grants **every** request as `DEV_MODE_PUBKEY = "dev-mode-local-admin"`, peer-agnostic — "no NIP-98, no token, no peer check" (`src/utils/auth.rs:203-218`).
  4. Nothing user-side resists this: a browser build with `VITE_DEV_MODE_AUTH=true` presents the fixed literal `Bearer dev-session-token` on every request (`authInterceptor.ts:62-69`), and the XR headset "can be empty" on `XR_NOSTR_SECRET` against an armed backend and still write (`graph_scene.gd:488-493`).
  5. Whoever could reach the published port read and wrote the client's data as power user. Step 5 is inferred: the material states the defaults and the caveat, not which ports were published in the incident environment.
- Evidence: the explicit DIVERGENCE "compose ships public reads open and unassigned-pubkey-is-Editor by default, while the Rust struct-level default is fail-closed — two different postures depending whether you read the binary default or the shipped compose env" (VC-33.6); "ADR-2108 flipped the default from :-0 to :-1 … the operator opts OUT" (VC-36.15); the flagged caveat "both services load env_file .env, so the var must never sit in a .env shared with prod — sharper now that the compose default supplies it unasked" (`docker-compose.unified.yml:102-104`, VC-36.15).
- Warning signs visible today: the VC-33.6 DIVERGENCE note; the opt-out-required default; the .env caveat; one artefact deploying the entire estate.
- Confidence: high that the defaults and dual postures exist as described; the path from compose defaults to a client-facing exposure is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Codescape's main listener has no authentication while its compose peer mounts ten member repositories into it
- Topics: VC-39.2, VC-39.5, VC-39.7
- Chain of events:
  1. The codescape service mounts "read-only views of the estate scene and ten member repositories" (`docker-compose.unified.yml:482, :530-611`) — multiple principals' source code.
  2. Its main listener (REST, `/wss`, `/ws/events`, `/ws/voice`, `/ws/presence`, port 4100) runs **OpenAccess** — "reported honestly as auth open" (`codescape-server/src/auth.rs:120-124`, `routes/mod.rs:69`).
  3. The port defaults to 127.0.0.1 (`docker-compose.unified.yml:633`) but direct LAN access exists whenever `CODESCAPE_PUBLISH_ADDR` is set — exactly what a second machine or headset on the LAN needs (VC-39.2); the nginx `/codescape/` route on :3001 is itself "the owner's dev-only waiver" (`nginx.dev.conf:63-65`).
  4. With the port reachable, an unauthenticated caller reads the code graph of all mounted repositories and, through `run_command`, executes typed commands on it — VC-39.5's executor path shows no auth step, and VC-39.7 R1 records "OPEN until P6".
  5. Presence adds identity spoofing: the `/ws/presence` shim "accepts the Schnorr signature unverified, the claimed DID is only a label" (`xr_presence.rs:20-23, :65-67`), so activity in the shared scene is attributable to anyone.
- Evidence: as cited; ADR-2143 is `implementation_status: partial`, `activation_status: inactive`, "P0 done and P2 to P6 open" (ADR-2143:296).
- Warning signs visible today: register entries R1 and R2; the publish-addr escape hatch; the dev-only nginx waiver; the estate-mount DEBT below (F-15).
- Confidence: high that OpenAccess, the unverified presence signature and the mounts are as stated; the exposure event is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Passkey custody: the Nostr identity that owns the Solid pod can become unrecoverable
- Topics: VC-33.4, VC-33.5, VC-33.8, VC-33.9
- Chain of events:
  1. At registration the Nostr key is HKDF-derived from the authenticator's PRF output (`deriveNostrKey`, `passkeyService.ts:60-73`); for authenticators without PRF, `downloadKeyBackup()` generates a random key and the forced file download is "the ONLY copy" (`passkeyService.ts:349-377`).
  2. The WebID and pod are bootstrapped **from** the Nostr pubkey (`solid:oidcIssuer = did:nostr:hex-pubkey`, `pod-provisioning.test.ts:104`); every pod operation "re-derives podUrl/webId/structure from the current NIP-98 identity" (VC-33.8) — pod contents are reachable only by signing as that pubkey.
  3. The derived key exists only in memory, wiped at logout and `beforeunload` (VC-33.5, VC-33.9); sessionStorage holds only a marker cross-checked "against derived pubkey on restore".
  4. If the PRF output changes (different authenticator, firmware/browser change to PRF evaluation, profile loss), login re-derives a **different** key and the identity no longer matches the server's bound record; for the no-PRF path, losing the single file is total. This step is inferred from the stated mechanisms.
  5. The pod under the old WebID persists server-side but is signed-for by no one: a custody loss of the user's stored data. No recovery or escrow path appears anywhere in the material.
- Evidence: `PRF_SALT` fixed constant (`passkeyService.ts:9-11`); re-derivation on every login (VC-33.4 authentication branch); "No separate WebID session token is cached client-side" (VC-33.8); the "ONLY copy" marking.
- Warning signs visible today: the material itself names the backup the only copy; key material is memory-only by design; nothing states key rotation, escrow or pod recovery.
- Confidence: medium; the mechanism is fully stated, the loss event is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Server still accepts a legacy session-bearer realm that no client mints, with a plain-equality check
- Topics: VC-33.3, VC-33.9
- Evidence: "ADR-2009 legacy session-bearer fallback (UUID minted at login, plain-equality check against token_expiry) is a SERVER-side acceptance path in `src/utils/auth.rs` and `src/services/nostr_service.rs:500`. No client code in this tree ever constructs an X-Nostr-Token session header"; `getSessionToken()` always returns null (`nostrAuthService.ts:435`); the VC-33.9 note records the mismatch with ADR-2009's client-dependent list.
- Failure: any captured or replayed session UUID is honoured until expiry; the comparison is not constant-time; and because no in-tree client exercises the realm, its server behaviour is untested by the client suite.
- Confidence: high that the acceptance path exists; exploitability inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT-adjacent, VC-33.9)

### F-02 — Codescape presence DID spoofing via unverified signature
- Topics: VC-39.2, VC-39.7
- Evidence: "/ws/presence shim accepts the Schnorr signature unverified, the claimed DID is only a label, bounds 64 members per room, 256 rooms" (`codescape-server/src/xr_presence.rs:20-23, :65-67`).
- Failure: any peer reaching the socket impersonates another principal's DID in the shared scene; agent/avatar attribution and co-presence become untrustworthy evidence.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-03 — Egress allowlist is configuration, not an invariant; voice transcripts and command text leave to Loom/ASR when configured
- Topics: VC-39.2, VC-39.5, VC-39.7
- Evidence: "TENSION: I8a egress allowlist and no-/vault are REJECTED as invariants, compose non-publication is not an egress boundary, open until P2 ships an egress test" (ADR-2143:82, :144); per-URL check at `egress.rs:77`; Loom receives command text with `LOOM_SYSTEM` (VC-39.5).
- Failure: one configured LLM URL sends client command text (voice-derived) off-estate; no test would catch a boundary violation because none exists.
- Confidence: high on the missing invariant; the exfiltration scenario is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension/Open)

## visionflow / critical

### F-01 — Replay protection for signed requests is process-local, so a restart or a second instance re-admits a captured authorization
- Topics: VF-08.2
- Evidence: every signed NIP-98 request is verified against VisionClaw's "process-local single-use cache — the event id is recorded atomically after validation (`nip98.rs:164`)", and the panel's own limit says "Restart, replicas, URL semantics and caller wiring require separate proof… the cache is declared single-process by invariant in VisionClaw itself (`nip98.rs:210`)". The same signed request is also consumed by the pod, the forum/worker verifiers and the agentbox proxy, each with its own contract; only a "Native Solid replay-store seam" is named, not a shared store.
- Failure: an Authorization header captured once (proxy log, referer, transcript) is replayed after a VisionClaw restart, or sent to a second replica or to the pod/forum verifier, and is accepted again. The single-use guarantee holds only within one process lifetime.
- Confidence: high (the limitation is stated; the replay scenario is its direct implication)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — drawn as a "LIMIT" node in the panel, but not tracked as Debt/Open

### F-02 — The copyright guard is bypassable by directory depth or file extension in a public repo whose tooling reads copyrighted sources
- Topics: VF-05.9, VF-07.8
- Evidence: the invariant is that the book's transition chapter "reads copyrighted sources that must never enter this PUBLIC repo" (`copyright-guard.yml:3`), but the guard enumerates only "every tracked ROOT-LEVEL *.txt" (`copyright-guard.yml:36`) and its own divergence note says "A copyrighted .txt committed one directory down passes, and texput.log at the repo root is a .log, outside the pattern entirely". VF-07.8 confirms stray root artefacts already survive (`texput.log:1`).
- Failure: an operator or an automated night commits a copyrighted excerpt as `docs/notes/x.txt`, a `.log`, or any non-.txt file; the guard stays green; the text is published to the public repository and potentially the site.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE)

### F-03 — The settlement substrate is a custodial single-signer chain whose activation guard is still only proposed
- Topics: VF-01.14
- Evidence: "the root chain is LEVEL 1 with one signer we operate and is CUSTODIAL; the k-of-n federation is rung 2 of the hardening path, not reached" (`ADR-2012…:84-87`); "agentbox still produces sidestr:dreamlab with upstream JavaScript and no overlay is activated" (`:113-128`); the record itself is proposed/partial/inactive (`:5-7`) while the roster publishes all eight crates (`roster.json:73-80`) and the page says "testnet research only" (`index.html:630`, `:1125`).
- Failure: if this is activated past testnet — which P1 permits once implementing records are accepted and receipts filed — one lost or compromised signer key, or the un-audited upstream JavaScript wallet path in agentbox, can halt, rewrite or lose settlement funds; there is no second signer until a hardening rung that has not been reached.
- Confidence: high (custody posture stated; production activation is inferred from P1's own activation gate)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (stated plainly in P5, no marker)

### F-04 — A governance decision can be acknowledged but never applied, and nothing correlates the two
- Topics: VF-08.4
- Evidence: the fan-out planner is "gated by MESH_FEDERATED_KINDS (`mesh.rs:59`)" with "Deferred outbound connector and accept-path joins"; broker stages end at "signed / accepted / projected / received / applied"; the panel's own note is "Relay acknowledgement does not prove external application" and "Complete correlated applied-decision proof remains open"; agentbox's journalled fast path "Does not mediate all tools or nightly egress".
- Failure: a governance event is signed and relay-acknowledged, the submitting side records it applied, the consuming substrate never applies it (deferred connector, dropped fan-out), and no evidence path exists to detect the divergence — governance silently forks with both sides believing they are current.
- Confidence: medium — the gap is stated; whether acks are treated as application anywhere is inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (drawn as open/dotted in the panel, no prose marker)

### F-05 — Estate health launders failures into exempt/none/unreadable, and the check ignores exempt rows
- Topics: VF-03.4, VF-03.5, VF-03.6, VF-03.11
- Evidence: knowledgeGraph's "last Build and verify run failed on an older commit, so the HEAD rule reports none and ci_by_design turns that into exempt" (`estate-health.json:468`, `:488-489`); "check() has no branch for exempt, so an exempt row prints nothing and moves no verdict (`estate-health.mjs:968` and `:978`)"; solid-pod-rs "reads none because its tip commit has no run and its older runs were red"; one unfiltered page of 100 runs makes a busy repo's row "read none rather than its real state" (`estate-health.mjs:609`, `:614`); the private visionGraph row is unreadable without ESTATE_READ_TOKEN (`estate-health.yml:86`) and a maintainer's local PAT masks that (TRAP, `:92`); "no mechanism records why a row flips".
- Failure: a substrate hard-down for weeks presents as exempt, none or unreadable — not red — so ESTATE-HEALTH-OK is attainable with failing repositories, the dream estate-health deep forms no hypothesis, the public estate section understates reality, and no audit trail explains a flip.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially (Debt on flips and the 100-run page; HOLE/TRAP marked; the check()-exempt blindness is unmarked)

### F-06 — The anti-drift gate fails open on the publication path, anchors one axis to a deleted component, and covers none of the page's headline figures
- Topics: VF-01.4, VF-02.8, VF-05.2, VF-05.3, VF-05.4, VF-02 (closing invariants), VF-01.12
- Evidence: on deploy the agentbox checkout is optional ("continue-on-error true — `deploy.yml:64`") and in Reported mode "this axis is never actually measured on the publication path, yet the page still ships" (VF-02.8 DIVERGENCE); the counter resolves agentbox by requiring `mcp/servers/ontology-bridge.js` (`drift-counter.mjs:77`) and counts its TOOLS array (`:165`), but "the bridge is retired in current Agentbox" (`allowlist.json:32`); ADR-2005 is implementation partial, "2 of 4 axes unenforced" (`ADR-2005…:46`) — ontology-classes unavailable (`allowlist.json:73`), roster planned (`:90`); "The drift counter still carries no CUDA axis" while the page states 83 kernels / 6,302 lines tied to VisionClaw at a moving commit (`index.html:296`, `:1089`), against the invariant that every count needs one queryable source (`BASELINE-visionflow.md:316-317`); and unpoliced spec contradictions persist — `PRD-website.md:124` still requires two WASM modules with a 300 KB budget (`:132`), `site-verification.md:10`/`:17` claim wasm-pack builds, and `BASELINE-visionflow.md:238-246` records README sentences that no longer exist (`./README.md:82`).
- Failure: a sibling changes (agentbox already deleted the bridge); the pinned checkout ages into measuring a ghost file, or the checkout fails and the gate reports instead of blocking; the site keeps publishing stale counts and no gate anywhere fails.

- Severity: medium

## visionflow / premortem

# Post-mortem — incident of <date>, twelve months after stamp `124512b8`

Scope note: everything below is grounded in the diagrams-as-code corpus at the stamps it cites (VisionFlow `124512b8f5e8c33c247065c58901dcfdc18988a2`, VisionClaw `e74bcddc…`, agentbox `0b0f791…`, nostr-rust-forum `eb5f232…`). The corpus is the only source; where I reason past it, I say so. Each finding names the defect an engineer should re-verify before acting.

---

### R-1 — Replay protection for authenticated requests is process-local, so a restart or second replica accepts a captured signed request
- Topics: VF-08.2, VF-08.7, VF-08 (header)
- Chain of events: 1) A client-side actor signs a NIP-98 request; VisionClaw validates it and records the event id in a cache to make it single-use. 2) That cache is declared single-process by invariant in VisionClaw itself — there is no cross-process or cross-replica store. 3) The process restarts, or the deployment grows a second replica; the id is no longer known. 4) The same signed request is replayed; validation passes; the authenticated action executes a second time under the client's authority. 5) Data is read or mutated by a replayed credential; the client attributes the breach to the operator and leaves.
- Evidence: "VisionClaw process-local single-use cache — the event id is recorded atomically after validation" (`src/utils/nip98.rs:164`); "the cache is declared single-process by invariant in VisionClaw itself" (`nip98.rs:210`, see VC-03); the matrix attaches the limit to every verifier path: "Restart, replicas, URL semantics and caller wiring require separate proof" (VF-08.2, LIMIT node); the review header states it never "certifie[d] … a replica-wide authentication contract" (VF-08 preamble); pod-tier "replay semantics" sit under a CHECK node that points at consumer-level fixtures "and deployment evidence" that the matrix does not claim to hold (VF-08.7).
- Warning signs visible today: the limitation is printed in the compatibility matrix itself; the forum/worker verifiers are described as separate contracts with the same unproven boundary (VF-08.2, EDGE node); nothing in the corpus shows a cross-process replay store anywhere in the estate.
- Confidence: high that the boundary is exactly as limited as stated; the multi-replica/restart incident path is inferred — the material proves the gap, not the deployment shape that exploits it.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — The canonical corpus exists in exactly one place, and nothing polls for divergence, so the estate serves stale or contradictory knowledge (or loses the vault) with no detector
- Topics: VF-01.15, VF-03.1, VF-03.5, VF-01.13 (context)
- Chain of events: 1) ADR-2013 consolidates the corpus into one vault and deletes every other copy — "vault, vault-working, the logseq symlink and its host bind are all deleted, no second copy anywhere" (`ADR-2013-sovereign-corpus-is-ecosystem-canon.md:41-43`). 2) That one repo is private and cross-owned (`scripts/estate-health/roster.json:17`), and the nightly collector cannot read it without a token, so the row sits unreadable (`estate-health.yml:86`). 3) Invariant 8 makes count divergence between Loom, VisionClaw and `vault build --stats` a defect (`BASELINE-visionflow.md:327-340`), but "nothing in this canon repo currently polls the three counts to detect one" (VF-01.15, Open; `ADR-2013…:12`). 4) A bad build or a divergent producer ships: the baseline records Ontomark as producer of the shared build and VisionClaw as consumer (`BASELINE-visionflow.md:53`) while ADR-2013 D3 still names VisionClaw/crates/vault as the only implementation (`ADR-2013…:52`) — unamended. 5) Loom and VisionClaw answer clients from different corpora for weeks, or the single vault's history is damaged; there is no second copy and no detector, so recovery is partial at best.
- Evidence: as cited above, plus the original failure mode this decision was meant to close — four doors disagreeing, "no one can say which number is right" (`ADR-2013…:22-26`) — which demonstrates the estate has already lived this failure once; the `review_trigger` names exactly this divergence as a forcing event (VF-01.15, Open).
- Warning signs visible today: the four-doors problem is recorded as history; the detection for its recurrence is recorded as absent; the producer/consumer tension is recorded as unamended; the one repo holding the corpus is recorded as unreadable by monitoring.
- Confidence: high that the single-copy state and the missing detector are both true as stated; the loss/divergence event is inferred. Whether the private remote has backups outside the estate is not addressed by the material.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — The only inbound client channel holds no server-side record, loads its code path only on submit, and is covered by no gate that executes JavaScript — inquiries can be lost silently and at length
- Topics: VF-02.3, VF-02.6, VF-02.11, VF-02.2
- Chain of events: 1) The contact form gift-wraps the message (NIP-17) in the browser and publishes it to the DreamLab relay; "nothing is stored by this site"; the vendored transport "loads only on first submit" (`contact.js:5-6`, `contact.js:112`, `assets.manifest.json:13-14`). 2) The publication path is browser-free by design — every blocking gate greps sentinels or checks file presence/bytes, none executes the page (`deploy.yml:83-129`; `website-assets.mjs:177`, `website-assets.mjs:250`). 3) The local browser spec asserts structure and zero console errors at load (`site.spec.js:31`); a submit-time failure in a module that loads only on submit cannot surface there. 4) A regression ships green — the 57 KB vendored minified transport drifts in a sibling rebuild (`nostr-contact.js:1-2`), or the relay is unreachable, or the recipient key mismatches. 5) Every inquiry in the window vanishes with no server-side record to detect the loss; a client who wrote twice and heard nothing takes the work elsewhere.
- Evidence: as cited; additionally the deep browser receipt is run "by hand, never by deploy.yml" and its three scenarios cover the mesh only — not the form (VF-02.11, DRIFT note: "The publication path has no browser gate at all").
- Warning signs visible today: the design states its own non-durability ("nothing is stored"); the coverage gap is stated in the VF-02.11 divergence note; the transport is minified, rebuilt elsewhere and committed here with only presence/byte checks against it.
- Confidence: medium — the durability fact, the load-on-submit fact and the coverage gap are all stated; the behaviour of `contact.js` on relay outage or transport failure is not stated, so the silent-loss mechanism is inferred and must be checked in code.
- Severity: high

### F-07 — The nightly verdict cannot go red on "none", "amber", "exempt" or "unreadable", so several broken substrates coexist with ESTATE-HEALTH-OK
- Topics: VF-03.4, VF-03.5, VF-03.6, VF-03.11
- Evidence: the HEAD rule turns known-red older runs into "none" (VisionClaw and solid-pod-rs in the current snapshot, `estate-health.json:116`, `:262`, `:292`); check has no branch for exempt rows and amber only prints (`estate-health.mjs:968`, `:978`); visionGraph is unreadable by design of the token setup (`estate-health.yml:86`) with the recorded TRAP that a local PAT masks it (`:92`); the one-page-of-runs debt can push real runs off the page (`estate-health.mjs:609`, `:614`).
- Failure: during a multi-substrate breakage the nightly returns OK; nothing escalates; the incident persists until a client reports it.
- Confidence: high on the rules; the masking-during-incident effect is inferred.

## visiongraph / critical

### F-01 — The four consumers use different inclusion gates: a page removed from the site stays live in VisionClaw's pod
- Topics: VG-01.3, VG-05.1, VG-01.4
- Evidence: The site publisher reads typed `public: true` alone (`../visionGraph/README.md:142-145`, `CLAUDE.md:10-11`); the code gate for KG ingest is `public || owl_class.is_some()` (`vault/mod.rs:162-163`, VC-21.2); the contract says VisionClaw's interface is "`public` or `owl-class`" (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:16`); agentbox has "a DIFFERENT top-level/private-page projection" (VG-01.3); the authors' own note: "changing one flag does not establish removal from every consumer" (VG-01.3 note2). Additionally, from 2026-10-01 to `bc4845c34` (2026-10-18) the agent rules stated the gate was `public` alone while the code always ingested `owl-class` pages — a 17-day window in which any agent-created owl-class page ingested under the belief it would not.
- Failure: A curator unpublishes a sensitive page (`public: false` or absent, `owl-class` retained). It disappears from narrativegoldmine.com but ships in the pod's `visionflow.ttl` / `ontology.jsonld` and appears in agentbox's projection. Un-publishing is not removable by one action anywhere.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, VG-01.3 note2) — but the concrete private-page-in-pod scenario and the 17-day agent-rules window are stated without being marked as risks

### F-02 — The only described private-reference filtering belonged to the deleted Python publisher; the audit boundary cases it addressed are still open
- Topics: VG-05.4, VG-01.4, VG-05.3
- Evidence: The 2026-09-07 execution update attributes "known-private-reference filtering before inference and before every export stage" to `public_projection.py` (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:39`); that Python publisher was retired 2026-10-01 (ADR-VG-003, banner at `PUBLICATION-contract.md:3-8`) and replaced by `ontomark`, a different program. Nothing in the material states ontomark or `vault` implement equivalent filtering. Meanwhile B2 (truthy string publication flags — `'false'` as a truthy value) and B3 (private-ancestor details leaking into derived public exports) are "unchanged since the 2026-09-07 audit" (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:18`), and the ADR that would fix them (ADR-VG-001, incl. requirement A5, a "deliberate, TESTED private-ancestor disclosure policy") is `proposed`/`inactive` (`docs/adr/README.md:7-8`), with a banner saying its acceptance conditions are not certified by the retirement (`ADR-VG-001-publication-policy-boundaries.md:18-21`).
- Failure: The privacy boundary the corpus relied on was a property of a pipeline that no longer runs. A `public: "false"` string or a public page deriving from a private ancestor publishes private content through the current producer, and no test or ADR currently guarantees otherwise.
- Confidence: medium — the open boundary cases and the retirement are stated; the absence of an equivalent filter in ontomark is inferred from the material's silence
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — the boundary cases and the historical banner are marked (VG-01.4, VG-05.4 note3); the implication that the described mitigation was retired with the Python publisher is unmarked

### F-03 — Omitting `--vault all` silently narrows the build to knowledge-only and every later gate stays green
- Topics: VG-03.5
- Evidence: "`--vault all` is required — omitting it silently falls back to knowledge-only and every later gate still passes green" (`publish.yml:152-157`, VG-03.5 note1). The substance gate floors are 1000 entries/lines (`publish.yml:293-302`) — easily met by knowledge alone; the BOTH guard (`publish.yml:189-198`) is inside the same build invocation that would no longer stage `/working`.
- Failure: A workflow edit (or a merge conflict resolution) drops the flag; every public working/ page silently vanishes from `/notes/` and the bundle on the next push to main, which auto-deploys. No gate, test, or alarm detects the content loss; discovery is by users.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (stated as an INVARIANT note in VG-03.5, i.e. a known hazard, not an enforced check)

### F-04 — Deploy authority is contradicted: a live "agents never push" invariant vs a workflow comment recording owner authorisation of agent pushes
- Topics: VG-03.1, VG-05.1
- Evidence: VG-05.1 lists as live invariant 7: "agents NEVER push this repo — a push to main deploys to narrativegoldmine.com (`CLAUDE.md:16`)". VG-03.1 records: "the workflow header says the owner authorised estate deployment 'during the ontomark resumption on 2026-10-11' (`publish.yml:17-18`) where the previous text said agents never push; the authorisation is recorded in a comment, not in a governing document." Both statements are presented as current at the same verified commit (`fd5269a55e`).
- Failure: Either the invariant is stale — then agents have licence (in a comment) to push to a branch that auto-deploys to production, with the authorisation recorded nowhere governing; or the comment is stale — then agents and operators act on contradictory rules about who may trigger production deploys. In an agent-operated estate, ambiguous push authority on the deploy-triggering branch is itself an incident vector.
- Confidence: high that the contradiction exists as stated; which side is currently true is not judgeable from this material
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — VG-03.1 marks the authorisation provenance as Open; the direct clash with invariant I7 is not drawn

### F-05 — Producer and checker binaries both fetched with one token; no rotation rule; no source fallback
- Topics: VG-03.5, VG-03.1
- Evidence: "`oo-cert` v2.0.1, sha256-pinned" is a separate pin from `ontomark` (`publish.yml:212-214` vs `publish.yml:49-51`), "but both are fetched with the same `ACCESS_TOKEN` and neither pin has a recorded rotation rule in this workflow (`publish.yml:117`, `publish.yml:215`)" — marked Open. Separately, "a run that cannot obtain the pinned release fails, because nothing else produces the site's artefacts" (`publish.yml:41-51`, `publish.yml:100-105`): there is no build-from-source path.
- Failure: One compromised token swaps both the producer and the "independent" certificate checker — the defence-in-depth of two pins collapses to one credential. Conversely, if the release assets or token become unavailable (release deleted, scope change, GitHub outage), the site cannot be deployed or rolled back at all, including during an incident.
- Confidence: medium — facts stated by the authors (Open); exploitation or outage is conditional
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, VG-03.5)

### F-06 — VisionClaw's source resolution falls back to a default repo and to `main`, bypassing the exact-sha pin the dispatch exists to provide
- Topics: VG-04.1
- Evidence: "source repo resolves inputs, then client_payload, then the jjohare/visionGraph default (`ontology-publish.yml:30`)" and the checkout is "at `client_payload.source_sha` or main (`ontology-publish.yml:89-95`)". The dispatch pinning (`publish.yml:525-531`) is therefore only one of three resolution paths, and workflow inputs outrank the payload.
- Failure: A manual `workflow_dispatch` run — or any run whose payload is missing — builds and publishes the pod release from whatever visionGraph `main` is at that moment, or from a different `ONTOLOGY_SOURCE_REPO` entirely, while the estate believes every pod release is traceable to a deploy-validated sha. Provenance that the 2026-10-01 change was specifically added to tighten ("the provenance of what the pod pulls", ADR-2106:152-154) is silently breakable.
- Confidence: high — the resolution order is stated
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — Same source sha, two different producers: unpinned default-branch `vault` vs digest-pinned `ontomark`; parity asserted only in a comment
- Topics: VG-04.1, VG-03.5, VG-03.1
- Evidence: visionGraph's publisher runs released `ontomark` pinned by tag and sha256 (`publish.yml:48-51`); VisionClaw's job "still builds `vault` from its own default-branch checkout (`ontology-publish.yml:145-146`, `:167-168`)". The only equivalence evidence is a workflow comment claiming ontomark reproduces the vault verbs "byte for byte against a parity corpus (`publish.yml:91-97`) — which this topic cannot re-check". The VisionClaw converter is additionally unpinned: it is whatever `main` is at run time.
- Failure: The site and the pod release — both presented as publications of the same corpus sha — are produced by different, independently evolving programs. Any divergence in fence handling, unknown-key policy, or projection silently forks the two publications; there is no test in the material that runs both on the same input.
- Confidence: high — the divergence is stated; its effect is a direct implication
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, VG-04.1)

### F-08 — Nothing binds the deployed artefact to the source revision that produced it
- Topics: VG-04.3, VG-05.2
- Evidence: "source, distribution and deployed artefact identities must NOT be collapsed into one revision (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:14`)" and ADR-VG-002's release manifest binding "source/corpus/pipeline/explorer/dependency/policy identity plus output hashes" is "proposed, NOT yet activation_status: live" (VG-04.3 note1; VG-05.2 B1). The dispatch (`publish.yml:525-531`) pins the VisionClaw side only, and only on one of three resolution paths (F-06).
- Failure: After any incident on narrativegoldmine.com, no one can prove which source commit, producer version, or dependency set produced the live bytes — the three identities cannot be reconciled post-hoc. Rollback is undefined; audit and forensics depend on reconstructing gh-pages history by hand.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (notes in VG-04.3, VG-05.2)

### F-09 — Deploy gates enforce domains and certificate steps whose activation the decision records declare not made
- Topics: VG-01.7, VG-03.5, VG-04.1
- Evidence: ADR-VG-004:35 says the space/Earth roots "exist as private draft classes" and "the changes are not deployed", while the gate fails unless each root is in the public search index (`check-space-domains.mjs:23-24`) and the workflow deploys on push (`publish.yml:309-310`, `:515-523`). ADR-VG-008 is accepted/complete but `activation_status: staged` (`ADR-VG-008...md:5-7`) while `publish.yml:312-313` runs its gate on every deploy — "the deploy enforces a domain whose activation has not been declared". ADR-2147 is likewise `staged` (`ADR-2147...md:7`) while both pipelines already run its certificate steps (`publish.yml:158-163`, `:210-227`, `ontology-publish.yml:176-208`).
- Failure: The decision trail is false in both directions: auditors read records saying domains/certificates are not live while production enforces and serves them; and anyone honouring the records (reverting a staged domain to actually stage it) fails the release gate — the pipeline holds the estate in a state its governance says it is not in.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift VG-01.7; Tension VG-03.5, VG-04.1)

### F-10 — The distribution repo's explorer copy cannot rebuild the site it mirrors: six domains, engine 0.1.1, no sub-path routing, built by nothing
- Topics: VG-06.1, VG-06.2, VG-06.5
- Evidence: The sibling `knowledgeGraph/explorer` is "built by nothing in that repo — see KG-04.6"; its `DOMAIN_SLUGS` stops at six (`explorer/modern/src/types/scope.ts:72-80`) against the shipping nine (`WasmVOWL/modern/src/types/scope.ts:75-86`); it pins vowl-wasm v0.1.1 (`explorer/modern/package.json:20`) against shipping 0.2.0 (`WasmVOWL/modern/package.json:25`), so "the sibling cannot be dropped in without the owned decode and encode bindings that 0.2.0 adds"; its router lacks the `readBaseUrl()`/basename sub-path support (111 vs 135 lines). 39 of 108 shared files differ, drifting in opposite directions (VG-06.4).
- Failure: The natural disaster-recovery move — rebuild the explorer from the distribution repository, the deploy target — ships a broken or regressed site: missing domains, an engine without required bindings, routing that breaks under the site's path layout. There is no sync discipline in either direction.
- Confidence: high for the stated facts; the DR scenario is directly implied by the authors' own "cannot be dropped in as a replacement"
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt/Drift throughout VG-06)

### F-11 — Same-commit contradictions about the vault layout: pages/ location and asset store described both ways, while a publish guard still requires `quartz/content/pages`
- Topics: VG-01.1, VG-01.5, VG-05.1, VG-05.4, VG-03.5
- Evidence: VG-01.5 note3: "page identity is the vault-relative path under pages/ … `CLAUDE.md:13-14`"; VG-05.1 I3: "pages sit at the vault root (the `pages/` folder was removed 2026-10-16) — `CLAUDE.md:13-14`" — the same lines cited for contradictory layouts, at the same verified commit. Likewise VG-01.1's diagram has `working/assets` as the 961 MB store with knowledge linking to it, while VG-05.1 I5 (restated 2026-10-17) says the store is repo-level `assets/`, `working/assets` is a symlink, and `knowledge/assets` is "a real synced subset" (`vault.toml:52-68`) — sync mechanism undescribed. The publish guard still requires "`quartz/content/pages` AND `/working` … both exist" (`publish.yml:189-198`).
- Failure: Page identity is path-based; anything resolving identities by the stale rule (consumers, ingest, link checks, the pages-count gate) computes wrong paths/IRIs after the 2026-10-16/17 restructurings. The two-restructurings-in-two-days pattern plus stale panels suggests not every gate and consumer was updated; whether `knowledge/assets`'s "synced subset" stays complete is unverified anywhere in the material.
- Confidence: high that the textual contradiction exists; the production effect is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — VG-05.4 notes the restatement, but the contradiction with VG-01.1/VG-01.5 and the guard at `publish.yml:189-198` is not drawn

### F-12 — The governing publication contract is banner-historical yet states retired mechanisms in the present tense, and every topic's frontmatter still cites it as governing
- Topics: VG-01.3, VG-05.4, VG-06.3
- Evidence: The banner marks the body as not current operating instructions (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:3-8`), yet `:16` still says the site publisher "reads JSON-LD Page vc:public" — a rejected format (`README.md:50`); `:39` states both publishers use `public_projection.py`; `:41` says the frozen notes export "receives only a scoped mobile compatibility stylesheet" whose file `publishing-tools/notes-mobile.css` was deleted 2026-10-01 (VG-05.4 Drift). All five topic files list `../visionGraph/docs/PUBLICATION-contract.md` under `governing:`. `CAPABILITIES.md:24` still instructs `python -m pipeline.build` for a pipeline that no longer exists (VG-06.3).
- Failure: Any integrator, agent, or new maintainer implementing against the governing document builds the wrong interface (JSON-LD fences, a projection filter that may not run — see F-02) or assumes guarantees the current producer does not provide. The contract is simultaneously the cited authority and disclaimed as current.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift notes VG-01.3 note1, VG-05.4, VG-06.3)

### F-13 — The README's publish-trigger list omits two trigger paths: explorer and static/ edits deploy to production unannounced
- Topics: VG-01.1
- Evidence: "`README.md:130-131` lists `knowledge/**`, `quartz/**` and `ontology/**` as the publish triggers; `publish.yml:24-30` also fires on `publishing-tools/WasmVOWL/modern/**` and `static/**`" (VG-01.1 note3, marked DRIFT). `static/ns/v2.jsonld` is a JSON-LD context — an interface other consumers may pin or cache.
- Failure: An editor changes the vendored explorer or a context document believing it is not deploy-triggering; the next push ships it straight to narrativegoldmine.com. A context change deployed this way can break every downstream consumer that resolves against it, with no release gate specific to context compatibility.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT, VG-01.1 note3)

### F-14 — The pod release goes silently stale whenever deploys fail; the pull model has no freshness signal
- Topics: VG-03.1, VG-04.1
- Evidence: "it runs only after the deploy step succeeds, so a failed deploy never refreshes the VisionClaw release" (`publish.yml:525-531`, stated as an invariant), and delivery is pull-model with no self-hosted runner (`ADR-2106...md:27`, `ontology-publish.yml:360`). No freshness check, staleness alarm, or divergence detector between site and pod appears anywhere in the material.
- Failure: A persistently failing site deploy (size gate, secrets gate, Pages outage — the pipeline has at least four hard-fail gates after build) leaves the pod serving an old ontology indefinitely while the site serves newer content, with nothing signalling the divergence; pod consumers reason over a stale graph believing it current.
- Confidence: medium, inferred — the ordering and pull model are stated; the absence of any freshness alarm is an absence in the material, not an asserted fact
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — The documents that govern agent behaviour contradict the pipeline they describe: pin v0.5.0 vs v0.6.0, and "the Rust `vault` CLI [is] the only parser"
- Topics: VG-01.7, VG-05.4, VG-01.5, VG-03.1
- Evidence: "`CLAUDE.md:23` says the pinned release is `v0.5.0` while `publish.yml:49` pins `v0.6.0`" (VG-01.7, VG-05.4 Drift). ADR-VG-003 "made … the Rust `vault` CLI the only parser (`ADR-VG-003...md:16-18`)" and is cited as live (VG-05.1), but the publisher runs `ontomark` (`publish.yml:98-117`); `vault.toml:30,71` comments still name "producer vault build" (VG-01.5 note5); the source-gate comment still names VisionClaw's spec and ADR-2112 as the contract (`publish.yml:119-120`).
- Failure: Agents and maintainers act on rules that misdescribe the production gate: an agent "correcting" the pin to v0.5.0 downgrades the producer; validation semantics are believed to be `vault`'s while the binary that actually gates is `ontomark`, whose equivalence rests on an unverified comment (F-07). In an agent-operated estate, wrong agent rules convert directly into wrong automated action.
- Confidence: high — each drift is stated; the mis-action vector is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, VG-01.7, VG-05.4, VG-01.5 note5)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. Whether `ontomark`/`vault` re-implement the private-reference filtering and truthy-string rejection that `public_projection.py` provided — the single most important input to closing F-02; I would need the ontomark source or its test suite against the B2/B3 boundary cases.
2. Whether the 961 MB asset store (`VG-01.1`) lands in `www/` and counts toward the 900 MB-warn / 1000 MB-hard-fail apparent-size gates (`publish.yml:364-388`) — i.e. how much deploy headroom actually remains.
3. Whether any gate enforces the review workflow (`review-before-publish.base`, `review-queue.base` are described only as saved Obsidian views, VG-01.6) before a working/ page flipped `public: true` ships to production on the next push.
4. The parity corpus and results behind the "byte for byte" claim at `publish.yml:91-97` — the only evidence bearing on whether F-07's two producers actually agree.
5. Whether the Quartz staging tree still contains a `pages/` directory after the 2026-10-16 vault-root move, and therefore what the BOTH guard at `publish.yml:189-198` actually proves today (bears on F-11).

## visiongraph / premortem

Post-mortem, written twelve months on, from the visionGraph distillation only. Every claim below carries the citation the material gives; "inferred" marks reasoning beyond what the material shows.

### R-1 — One `public` flag, four inclusion policies: withdrawing a page from the site does not withdraw it from VisionClaw's public pod
- Topics: VG-01.3, VG-05.1
- Chain of events: A page in `knowledge/` carries a governed `owl-class` (with or without `public: true`). The client asks for withdrawal; the operator removes or flips `public` and verifies the page is gone from `/notes/` and the site bundle — the only surface the operating rules describe. The next visionGraph deploy sends the `corpus-sync` dispatch naming that source sha (`publish.yml:525-531`); VisionClaw rebuilds with the gate `public || owl_class.is_some()` (`vault/mod.rs:162-163`), so the page re-enters `visionflow.ttl` and is PUT to the public pod path (`ontology-publish.yml:401-420`). The content remains publicly consumable after the operator believes it withdrawn; agentbox's projection differs again (VG-01.3, AB node).
- Evidence: VG-01.3 note2 verbatim: "these four consumers have DISTINCT inclusion policies — changing one flag does not establish removal from every consumer (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:16`)". The code gate is `public || owl_class.is_some()` (`vault/mod.rs:162-163`, VC-21.2); the site publisher reads `public` alone (`CLAUDE.md:10-11`); `/notes/` gates on typed `public: true` (`README.md:142-145`, `quartz/README.md:25-26`). The agent rules understated the gate as `public: true` alone from 2026-10-01 to 2026-10-18 (`bc4845c34`) — a 17-day window in which agent-authored pages could carry `owl-class` in the belief they were held back.
- Warning signs visible today: the standing four-consumer divergence itself; the 17-day wrong rule (corrected only via estate register ER-4); the contract body still describing a rejected format ("reads JSON-LD Page vc:public", `PUBLICATION-contract.md:16`) under a historical banner (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:3-8`).
- Confidence: high that the divergence and the 17-day mis-rule existed (both stated); the withdrawal-leak chain is inferred from note2's own warning.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Private-ancestor details leak through inferred exports; the disclosure policy ADR-VG-001 requires was never built or tested
- Topics: VG-01.4, VG-05.2, VG-05.3
- Chain of events: A public class is a subclass of a private concept (no frontmatter means private, VG-05.1 I2 — the gate fails closed for the *page*, not for the *inference*). The classifier's closure pulls the private ancestor's statements into the derived graph. `ontomark build` writes `ontology.ttl`, `api/`, the markdown mirror and the search index (`vault.toml:74-77`, `publish.yml:165-171` with `--with-markdown-mirror`). The deploy gates check artefact presence, substance floors, domain contracts and credential patterns — none inspect the visibility of inferred content; the certificate warrants that the closure is covered (`publish.yml:158-163`), not that its contents are publishable. The leak ships to narrativegoldmine.com and the pod.
- Evidence: B3 "private-ancestor details leaking into derived public exports" has stood since the 2026-09-07 audit (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:18`, VG-01.4); ADR-VG-001's acceptance condition A5 — "deliberate, TESTED private-ancestor disclosure policy across API/scaffold/Turtle" — is proposed/inactive (`docs/adr/README.md:7-8`, VG-05.2); VG-05.3 records "no real private-page disclosure is established". B4 (asserted vs inferred IRI representation diverging across outputs) can mask the leak in review of any single output.
- Warning signs visible today: an audit boundary case never closed; both closeout ADRs carrying the disclaimer "this record does not ratify the proposal or certify a deployment" (VG-05.2 note1).
- Confidence: high that the path exists untested (stated); the incident instantiation is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Two producers, one certified identity: the pod release is built by an unpinned `vault` at HEAD, not the digest-pinned `ontomark` the site's gates ran on
- Topics: VG-04.1, VG-03.5
- Chain of events: A visionGraph deploy succeeds; the dispatch pins the source sha only. VisionClaw's build job checks out visionGraph at that sha but builds the `vault` binary from its own default-branch checkout (`ontology-publish.yml:145-146`, `167-168`), builds `--vault knowledge` only (`ontology-publish.yml:167-174`, vs the site's `--vault all`), and certifies through its own engine (`vault certify`) with the same oo-cert v2.0.1 digest. Any behavioural drift between `vault`@HEAD and `ontomark` v0.6.0 ships a pod graph that never passed the site's gates — domain contracts, artefact secret scan, site substance floors — while carrying a certificate that attests only its own engine's closure. The byte-for-byte parity claim lives in a workflow comment the distillation itself "cannot re-check" (`publish.yml:91-97`).
- Evidence: VG-04.1 Tension verbatim: "the dispatch pins the corpus revision but not the converter… the site and the pod release can be produced by different producers from the same source sha"; the pin at `publish.yml:48-51` vs build-from-source at `ontology-publish.yml:145-146,167-168`; "through different engines" with the same oo-cert digest; ADR-2147 is `activation_status: staged` while both publish jobs already run its steps.
- Warning signs visible today: the unverifiable parity comment; two independently pinned binaries fetched with a single `ACCESS_TOKEN` and no recorded rotation rule (`publish.yml:117`, `publish.yml:215`).
- Confidence: medium — the split, the knowledge-only build and the unverified parity are stated; the divergence outcome is inferred.
- Severity: high

ADR-VG-008 is accepted/complete but `staged` (`ADR-VG-008:5-7`) while `publish.yml:312-313` enforces its gate on every deploy.
