## vowl-wasm / critical

### F-01 — Large ontologies are silently truncated at `max_classes`, so the rendered graph is quietly incomplete
- Topics: VW-02.1, VW-02.2
- Evidence: VW-02.2 states `ParserConfig` "has a `max_classes` cutoff enforced at src/ontology/parser.rs:62-64: a `class` array longer than the configured cap is silently truncated, not rejected." The `loadOntology` chain (VW-02.1) runs `validate(&ontology_data)` *after* `parse`, i.e. after truncation, and the validator that existed was deleted with `markdown-ontology` — nothing in the described path surfaces the truncation to JS.
- Failure: a user loads an ontology above the cap; classes vanish with no error, no warning, no partial-load signal; every downstream decision (layout, statistics, filtering, pinning) is made on a graph the user believes is complete.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — The zero-copy position contract can be invalidated by per-tick WASM heap growth (detached `Float32Array`)
- Topics: VW-01.7, VW-03.5, VW-04.6
- Evidence: VW-01.7 asserts the invariant "`NggExplorer` never allocates across the WASM boundary per frame — only the position pointer crosses (src/bindings/explorer.rs:9)". But VW-03.5 shows the default Barnes-Hut path does "one `QuadTree::build` per tick" (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:473-535) — allocation *inside* the wasm heap every tick, which can grow `wasm.memory`. VW-04.6 shows the consumer constructs `new Float32Array(wasm.memory.buffer, ptr, len)`; on memory growth the buffer detaches and any cached view silently reads `undefined`/stale data. The worker's mention of "SharedArrayBuffers or transferable double-buffer" hints at awareness but no stated contract or guard.
- Failure: a tier that grows the heap mid-session (quadtree churn, `loadCsr` of a second tier) detaches the worker's view; the explorer either throws per frame or, worse, reads a stale/empty view so the layout silently freezes while `tick()` reports success.
- Confidence: medium (inferred — depends on whether the worker re-reads `memory.buffer` every frame, which the material does not state)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-03 — Release pipeline wedges permanently if npm publish fails after the tarball is attached
- Topics: VW-05.3
- Evidence: VW-05.3 orders the steps: `npm pack` (release.yml:128-143) → GitHub release upload (release.yml:145-159) → `npm publish` (release.yml:165-173). A re-run "stops at the immutable-asset check (release.yml:86-90) once the tarball is attached", while the publish step separately "skips a version already on the registry (release.yml:168-171)". The publish-skip idempotency is unreachable dead logic, because the earlier immutability gate refuses re-entry first — the two mechanisms contradict each other.
- Failure: transient npm/OIDC failure after upload leaves the tag consumed, the GitHub release showing the new tarball, and the npm registry still on the old version; re-runs are refused at verify; remediation requires burning the version/tag.
- Confidence: high (mechanics fully stated; the failure ordering follows directly)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — No panic hook installed while release builds use `panic=abort`: production WASM failures are opaque traps
- Topics: VW-01.5, VW-05.5
- Evidence: VW-01.5: `init()` is "no-op today, reserved for panic-hook install (src/lib.rs:157)". VW-05.5: `[profile.release] ... panic=abort (Cargo.toml:90-95)`.
- Failure: any panic in the module aborts the whole wasm instance with a bare "unreachable executed" trap — no message, no stack, no Rust context — killing the physics worker mid-session and giving on-call nothing to triage; this compounds every other defect in this list by making its failure mode undiagnosable.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (the reservation is noted in VW-01.5 but the gap is not flagged)

### F-05 — The SIMD leaf-force path appears to lack the coincident-point guard, and no described CI job ever executes the real kernel
- Topics: VW-03.4, VW-05.1, VW-05.2, VW-05.4
- Evidence: VW-03.4's invariant enumerates exactly two paths that nudge coincident points: "both the scalar leaf path (src/layout/quadtree.rs:384-387) and the CSR tick" — the SIMD leaf path (`unsafe calculate_leaf_force_simd()`, src/layout/simd.rs:386) is conspicuously absent from the enumeration. Additionally: the non-wasm32 `is_simd_available()` "always returns `false`" (src/layout/simd.rs:525), so native benches never run it; the CI wasm32 build uses `--features ngg1,interaction` (no `simd`, VW-05.1); the feature matrix is `cargo check` only (VW-05.2, ci.yml:131-160); the published bundle excludes `simd`.
- Failure: a downstream build enabling the documented `simd` feature gets `NaN` forces when nodes coincide, propagating `NaN` into every position — from unsafe code that has never been executed by any test in the described pipeline.
- Confidence: medium (inferred from the invariant's omission; the kernel body is not in the material)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — Two physics engines with duplicated quadtrees and divergent same-named semantics
- Topics: VW-03.1, VW-03.5, VW-03.7, VW-03.10
- Evidence: VW-03.5 N1 (marked DEBT): two independent Barnes-Hut implementations, "a fix to one does not reach the other" (src/layout/quadtree.rs:126, src/layout/csr_sim.rs:152-161). Unmarked divergences on top: `reheat` clamps to `(0,1]` and *silently ignores* non-finite/non-positive input on `ForceSimulation` (src/layout/simulation.rs:71-75) but raises alpha and clears `finished` on `CsrSimulation` (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:618-621); `is_finished()` is a recomputed read in one and a latched bool in the other (VW-03.1 vs VW-03.5); and the sign conventions differ — `charge -30/-2000` in the presets (src/layout/mod.rs:99-111, 138-144) vs `charge 2000` in `SimConfig` (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:84-95).
- Failure: a numerics fix (e.g. the coincident-point nudge) lands in one engine only; a consumer using both `WebVowl.reheat` and `NggExplorer.reheat` gets different behaviour from the same-named operation; tuning intuition built on one engine's charge sign inverts on the other.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT for the quadtree duplication); no for the reheat/finished/sign divergences

### F-07 — NGG1 format custody moved outside the repo to a 0.1.0 crate; compatibility is evidenced by exactly one golden fixture
- Topics: VW-03.6, VW-04.5, VW-04.8, VW-05.3
- Evidence: the codec is `ontomark-ngg1` 0.1.0 from crates.io behind `ngg1` (Cargo.toml:45-46); "Header size, node stride, field offsets and the specification are therefore owned by ontomark, not by `vowl-wasm`" and the byte layout "cannot be re-read from this repository at 89d1a0a" (VW-03.6, marked Open). The only cross-artifact evidence is "golden tier decodes and re-encodes byte for byte" through one fixture (VW-04.5 CHECK4). The ADR's own caveat: "the plugin, the TypeScript reader consolidation and the wider acceptance evidence are not claimed" (ADR-001:89-93, per VW-04.8 N2).
- Failure: an `ontomark-ngg1` 0.2 changing header/stride makes already-published `@dreamlab-ai/vowl-wasm` versions in deployed consumers unable to read (or misread) newly produced tiers; this repo cannot even audit the format it renders, and the golden fixture proves nothing about other versions.
- Confidence: medium (drift scenario inferred; the ownership transfer and single fixture are stated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open for the byte layout; ADR caveat also author-noted)

### F-08 — Verification evidence is stale at the stamped commit and never covered the shipped optional paths
- Topics: VW-01 audit qualification, VW-05.2
- Evidence: the audit note: "`cargo test --locked --offline --lib` passed 136 default-feature tests at the earlier stamp; that count was not re-run at 89d1a0a", and that run "covers native logic, not optional `ngg1`, `interaction`, browser loading or a deployed consumer". Compounding: `ngg1_explorer_test` needs `required-features = ["ngg1"]` (Cargo.toml:109-111) so "a plain `cargo test` without that flag silently skips it" (VW-05.2) — the cited passing run skipped it by construction.
- Failure: the commit that deleted a feature and swapped the NGG1 dependency to crates.io has line-citation re-reads but no executed test run behind the `verified_commit` stamp; a regression in the default-invisible ngg1 path surfaces only after handover, in the browser.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (acknowledged in the audit qualification prose, but not as a marker)

### F-09 — Crate advertises `#!deny(unsafe_code)` while shipping an `unsafe` SIMD kernel
- Topics: VW-01.1, VW-03.4
- Evidence: VW-01.1: `src/lib.rs:136-137 #!deny(missing_docs, unsafe_code)`. VW-03.4: "unsafe `calculate_leaf_force_simd()` src/layout/simd.rs:386, target_feature simd128". Both cannot hold without a module-level `#![allow(unsafe_code)]` in `simd.rs` (inferred), which the material never mentions.
- Failure: an auditor or downstream consumer relying on the crate-root attribute as a "no unsafe code" guarantee is wrong in `simd` builds; the advertised hygiene invariant is silently feature-conditional, and the escape hatch is undocumented anywhere in the distillation.
- Confidence: high (both statements are in the material; the existence of an allow is inferred)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — Dependency-advisory checking runs weekly only and appears absent from the release path
- Topics: VW-05.1, VW-05.3
- Evidence: cargo-deny is a weekly cron job, explicitly because it checks "a live advisory database" (ci.yml:10-11, VW-05.1). The release sequence in VW-05.3 enumerates verify → build → gates → pin → notices → pack → release → publish with no advisory step anywhere (release.yml:79-173).
- Failure: an advisory published after the last weekly run and before a tag push ships the affected locked dependency to every browser consumer; the release gates (version, hashes, notices, symbols) all pass.
- Confidence: medium (inferred from the absence of a deny step in the distilled release sequence)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — Performance and size targets are aspirational only; the enforced bundle gate checks symbols, not size or latency
- Topics: VW-05.4, VW-04.5, VW-01.4
- Evidence: VW-05.4: phase3 targets (tick <10ms, `getGraphData()` <5ms, `checkNodeClick` <1ms/1000 nodes, bundle <1.5MB) are "aspirational annotations in the source, not enforced by any CI assertion". The only enforced gate is the eight-symbol `d.ts` check (VW-04.5, ci.yml:224-228). Worse, the <5ms target is measured for `getGraphData`, which is gated behind `debug-serde` (VW-01.4, bindings/mod.rs:176) and therefore not in the published bundle at all.
- Failure: a change doubles tick time or pushes the wasm module past the size budget (notably easy given `wasm-opt` is deliberately off, VW-05.5); CI stays green because the gate only proves the symbols still exist.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no (the aspiration is acknowledged in prose, not marked)

### F-12 — Error taxonomy is flattened to strings at the boundary and invalid-input policy is inconsistent across bindings
- Topics: VW-01.6, VW-03.6, VW-03.7, VW-04.3, VW-04.6
- Evidence: VW-01.6: "`VowlError` never crosses the boundary, only its rendered message"; `NggExplorer::load_csr` maps `Ngg1Error` "to a string" (explorer.rs:40-44). Meanwhile `checkNodeClick` validates input with `console.warn + None` (bindings/mod.rs:407-413), `reheat` silently ignores non-finite/non-positive input (simulation.rs:71-75), and `loadOntology` returns errors — three policies for bad JS input. The consumer's own defence — resolving names "permissively (snake_case per Rust, with camelCase fallbacks)" (physics.worker.ts:33-35) — signals it does not trust the export contract despite the frozen-surface invariant (VW-04.1).
- Failure: JS support code cannot branch on error kind (parse vs layout vs interaction) and must string-match; a silent `reheat(NaN)` no-op is misdiagnosed as an engine bug; every consumer must harden against naming drift that the freeze was supposed to preclude.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-13 — `SvgRenderer` is shipped but unreachable, while the governing ADR is titled a "rendering contract"
- Topics: VW-01.1, VW-04 (ADR-001 references)
- Evidence: VW-01.1: `render::SvgRenderer` (render/mod.rs:20) "is not wired to any `#[wasm_bindgen]` binding — a native-only diagnostic path ... unreachable from the published JS API". The crate's governing decision record is ADR-001 "shared ontology rendering contract" (every topic's `governing`/`adrs` front-matter).
- Failure: dead rendering code compiles into every build and rots unexercised from JS; the "rendering contract" the ADR names has no shipped rendering surface in this crate, leaving ownership of that half of the contract ambiguous between vowl-wasm and consumers.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — Visibility can be filtered but, on the listed surface, cannot be read back
- Topics: VW-04.1, VW-04.2, VW-01.4
- Evidence: VW-04.1's surface includes `filterByDegree/toggleDatatypes/toggleSetOperators/resetVisibility/filterHierarchy`, and guarantees `getNodeIds()`/`getNodePositions()` are index-stable — "filtering ... never reorder or remove nodes". No visibility/hidden accessor appears anywhere in the listed surface; the only candidate, `getGraphData()`, is `debug-serde`-gated (VW-01.4) and absent from the published bundle (VW-04.5).
- Failure: a bundle consumer filters, then zips `ids[i] <-> xy[2i]` per the documented contract — and cannot distinguish hidden from visible nodes, so it either renders filtered-out nodes or re-implements the filtering in JS, diverging from the Rust semantics.
- Confidence: low (inferred from the enumerated surface; a readback may exist outside it)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — Hit-testing uses a fixed world-space radius of 20.0 regardless of node size or zoom
- Topics: VW-04.3
- Evidence: VW-04.3: `NodeHitTest` is built per node with "radius=20.0 fixed (src/bindings/mod.rs:423-435)", with the in-code comment "In a real implementation, this could be configurable or per-node".
- Failure: on a zoomed-out canvas clicks miss visually large nodes; zoomed in, clicks land on the wrong neighbour; hit area is unrelated to rendered size, so interaction correctness silently varies with camera state.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no (acknowledged in the cited code comment, not as a marker)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. The full `physics.worker.ts` source — specifically whether the `Float32Array` view over `wasm.memory.buffer` is cached or reconstructed each frame, and whether SharedArrayBuffer/growable memory is actually used. Decisive for F-02.
2. The body of `src/layout/simd.rs` — coincident-point handling, tail-lane handling, and any module-level `#![allow(unsafe_code)]`. Decisive for F-05 and F-09.
3. `ParserConfig` defaults — the actual `max_classes` value and whether any load path overrides it. Decisive for how often F-01 bites in practice.
4. The raw `release.yml` and `ci.yml` — whether any advisory/cargo-deny step exists beyond the distilled sequence, and whether CI's wasm-pack/rustc versions match release's pinned 0.15.0/1.99.0. Decisive for F-10 and CI/release artifact parity.
5. The serialization shape of `getGraphData()` (debug-serde) and any undocumented bindings — whether per-node visibility or hidden-state readback exists outside the distilled surface. Decisive for F-14.

## vowl-wasm / premortem

# Post-mortem — vowl-wasm production incident review

Scope note: written twelve months after the verified stamps (`vowl-wasm` 89d1a0a, 2026-10-29; `visionGraph` fd5269a). Every finding below is drawn from the diagrammed material and its citations; where I reason past what the material asserts, the confidence line says "inferred". Order of steps inside a chain is only claimed where the material's diagrams assert it.

---

### R-1 — Ontologies above the parser's class cap are silently truncated, so consumers act on incomplete graphs with no error
- Topics: VW-02.1, VW-02.2, VW-02.3, VW-01.6
- Chain of events:
  1. A downstream consumer (main-thread `WebVowl` path) calls `loadOntology(json)` on an ontology whose `class` array exceeds `ParserConfig.max_classes`.
  2. `StandardParser::parse_classes` truncates the array at the cap — "silently truncated, not rejected" (src/ontology/parser.rs:62-64).
  3. `validate()` then runs on the already-truncated `OntologyData` (VW-02.1 sequence), so validation cannot detect the missing classes.
  4. `GraphBuilder::from_ontology` still iterates **all** properties (src/graph/builder.rs:38), including properties whose endpoints were truncated — the material states no behavior for edges to non-existent nodes (see F-01).
  5. `load_ontology` returns `Ok` and stores the graph (src/bindings/mod.rs:69-70); no `VowlError` variant exists for "input was cut" (VW-01.6), and even if one did, only its rendered string crosses to JS.
  6. The consumer renders, exports, or persists an ontology model that is silently missing classes; downstream work (graph statistics at src/graph/statistics.rs, position exports via VW-04.1) is computed on incomplete data.
- Evidence: src/ontology/parser.rs:17 (`ParserConfig` with `max_classes`), src/ontology/parser.rs:62-64 (silent truncation, stated explicitly in VW-02.2), src/graph/builder.rs:24-55 (classes and properties looped independently), src/bindings/mod.rs:56-70, VW-01.6 (`VowlError` never crosses the boundary as a type). The default value of `max_classes` is not stated anywhere in the material.
- Warning signs visible today: the material itself flags the behavior with the word "silently"; there is no stated test, CI check, log, or error variant covering the cap; only `ngg1` and `interaction` paths have named tests (VW-05.2), and none is stated for parser truncation.
- Confidence: high that the mechanism exists exactly as described (it is asserted, not inferred); the link to this specific incident is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — The zero-copy position pointer has no stated invalidation protocol: memory growth or a tier reload silently serves NaN or the previous tier's data
- Topics: VW-01.7, VW-04.2, VW-04.6, VW-04.8
- Chain of events:
  1. The rendering worker calls `loadCsr(bytes)` (src/bindings/explorer.rs:40), then builds a JS view `new Float32Array(wasm.memory.buffer, positionsPtr(), positionsLen())` over raw wasm linear memory (src/bindings/explorer.rs:82,89; VW-04.6 worker sequence).
  2. The explorer's invariant is per-frame only: "never allocates across the WASM boundary per frame" (src/bindings/explorer.rs:9). It does not cover sporadic operations that do allocate: a second `loadCsr` (new CSR buffers, src/layout/csr_sim.rs:353-361), `setParam(key, val)` with a String key (listed as an optional worker call in VW-04.6), or any string-returning call.
  3. If such an allocation grows wasm memory, the cached `Float32Array`'s buffer detaches — reads return `undefined`, which becomes NaN positions streamed to the renderer or persisted by the consumer.
  4. Alternatively, after a re-`loadCsr` replaces the simulation's `Vec<f32>` buffers (VW-03.9: re-load is a clean restart), a stale `positionsPtr()` still points inside linear memory — legal to read, but it yields the *previous* tier's positions with no trap and no error. There is no generation counter, length re-check requirement, or documented re-fetch rule anywhere in the material.
  5. The consumer renders or persists positions belonging to the wrong scope, or NaN, with zero diagnostics.
- Evidence: src/bindings/explorer.rs:9 (per-frame-only invariant), :82, :89 (raw ptr/len API with no invalidation contract stated), VW-04.2 ZeroCopy subgraph ("no copy at all"), VW-04.6 (worker constructs the view over `wasm.memory.buffer`; permissive name resolution noted at physics.worker.ts:33-35), VW-03.9 (`load` re-seeds and re-allocates buffers). The behavior of `positionsPtr()` *before* a successful `loadCsr`, and after a failed one, is also unstated.
- Warning signs visible today: the flagship API is a raw pointer + length with no epoch/generation; the material contrasts it with the copying paths precisely because it is unmanaged (VW-04.2); the one invariant protecting it is scoped to per-frame allocation only.
- Confidence: medium — the API shape and allocation facts are stated; the detach/stale-read event is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — NGG1 binary-format authority lives in an external 0.x crate pinned at 0.1.0 while every consumer holds a frozen bundle, so a producer-side format change makes user tiers unreadable (or silently misread)
- Topics: VW-03.6, VW-04.5, VW-04.6, VW-04.8, VW-05.3
- Chain of events:
  1. ADR-001 makes ontomark the single owner of the NGG1 format; this repo reduced `src/ngg1.rs` to `pub use ontomark_ngg1::*;` and pinned `ontomark-ngg1` 0.1.0 from crates.io (src/ngg1.rs:1-5, Cargo.toml:45-46; ADR-001:26).
  2. The producing side (Obsidian plugin / TypeScript writer — explicitly "not claimed" by ADR-001, docs/adr/ADR-001-shared-ontology-rendering-contract.md:89-93 via VW-04.8 N2) evolves independently and ships tiers written by a newer codec.
  3. Every deployed consumer holds a published npm bundle built against 0.1.0 (visionGraph pins the published package in its lockfile, never the source — VW-04.6; bundles are immutable per VW-05.3/VW-05.6).
  4. New tiers either fail `Ngg1::parse` — surfaced as a bare string via `NggExplorer::load_csr` (src/layout/csr_sim.rs:344, src/bindings/explorer.rs:40-44), leaving users' vault scopes blank — or, if the format has no strictly-checked in-band version (unstated in this repo; the stamp explicitly says the byte layout "cannot be re-read from this repository… not confirmed", VW-03.6), are misparsed into wrong positions/domains with no error.
- Evidence: Cargo.toml:45-46 and :67 (0.1.0, optional behind `ngg1`), src/ngg1.rs:1-5, VW-03.6 Open note (byte layout unconfirmed at the stamp), VW-04.8 N2 (reader consolidation and plugin out of ADR-001 scope), VW-04.5 CHECK4 (one golden fixture round-trip — this proves self-consistency with ontomark 0.1.0's own writer, not compatibility with any producer version), VW-04.6 (published-bundle-only dependency direction).
- Warning signs visible today: sole format authority is an external 0.x dependency; the repo's own verification disclaims knowledge of the layout; compatibility testing is a single golden file; the two repos are verified at different commits.
- Confidence: medium — inferred scenario; the structural facts (0.x external ownership, immutable bundles, disclaimed layout knowledge, single-fixture test) are all stated.
- Severity: high

## Further latent defects

### F-01 — Edges referencing truncated classes: builder behavior unstated, panic possible
- Topics: VW-02.1, VW-02.3
- Evidence: properties are iterated in full (src/graph/builder.rs:38-55) while classes may have been silently truncated (src/ontology/parser.rs:62-64); `graph.add_edge(source, target, edge)` with a missing endpoint has no stated behavior — drop, error, ghost node, or index-lookup panic.
- Failure: an internal panic on a missing node index aborts (see F-04) and kills the worker/main-thread instance mid-load; best case, dangling edges render false relations into the consumer's model.
- Confidence: low — the gap is an absence in the material, and the failure mode is inferred.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-02 — Coincident-node guard is stated for only two of the repulsion paths; the O(n²) batch path and the SIMD kernel are not covered by the invariant
- Topics: VW-03.2, VW-03.4
- Evidence: VW-03.4's INVARIANT enumerates exactly "the scalar leaf path (src/layout/quadtree.rs:384-387) and the CSR tick (VW-03.5)" as nudge-protected; `calculate_batch_repulsion` (src/layout/simulation.rs:180) and `calculate_leaf_force_simd` (src/layout/simd.rs:386) are not included. The batch path is the default for n ≤ 50 (src/layout/simulation.rs:158), i.e. most small ontologies.
- Failure: two nodes collapse onto identical coordinates on the batch path, distance² ≈ 0 divides, NaN forces propagate into positions and are exported verbatim via `getNodePositions`/`writePositionsInto` (VW-04.1).
- Confidence: low — inferred from the invariant's explicit enumeration.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-03 — `tick()` before `initSimulation()` runs on uninitialized all-zero positions; no guard is stated
- Topics: VW-03.1, VW-03.9, VW-04.1
- Evidence: seeding happens only in `ForceSimulation::initialize` (src/layout/simulation.rs:89, 269); the `WebVowl` binding exposes `tick()` independently (src/bindings/mod.rs:106) and the material states no ordering guard; default `VisualAttributes` x/y (src/graph/mod.rs:101) are unstated but typically zero.
- Failure: every node at (0,0) hits the batch repulsion path with all-coincident points — compounding F-02 — producing NaN positions on the very first tick of a misuse-prone sequence.
- Confidence: low — inferred.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-04 — No panic hook: `init()` is a no-op and panics abort opaquely
- Topics: VW-01.5, VW-01.6, VW-05.5
- Evidence: `init()` is "no-op today, reserved for panic-hook install" (src/lib.rs:157, 166); release profile sets `panic = "abort"` (Cargo.toml:90-95); all binding errors are pre-rendered strings (VW-01.6), so a trap is indistinguishable in kind from a handled error in JS logs.
- Failure: any internal panic (e.g. F-01) surfaces as an opaque `RuntimeError`/trap with no message, killing a worker silently and making the production incident far harder to diagnose.
- Confidence: high that the mechanism is as stated; the operational consequence is inferred.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-05 — A failed npm publish after tarball attach deadlocks the release: re-runs stop at the immutable-asset check
- Topics: VW-05.3, VW-05.6
- Evidence: "A re-run against an existing tag stops at the immutable-asset check (release.yml:86-90) once the tarball is attached, although the publish step skips a version already on the registry (release.yml:163-171)" — the skip logic never executes because the earlier check refuses first.
- Failure: a transient npm/OIDC failure after tarball upload leaves a GitHub release that advertises a version npm does not have; pinned consumers' installs fail while the release page claims the artifact exists.
- Confidence: medium — behavior stated, failure path inferred.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium
